Verified by unit test against real cached Space-Track data, including truncating a complete set by `CREATION_DATE` to synthesise early downloads at D+6h through D+96h and checking each is classified correctly; the dialog flow itself (owner detection, merge, save prompt) is not exercised end to end.

### Improvements
- **Orbit Image Set exports into a folder you choose, and warns before overwriting** (`01b21f25`, `ad642f0e`; `src/ExportImageSet.js`, `src/utils.js`). File → Export → Orbit Image Set → *Export Image Set* (and *Export Image Set (Fullscreen)*) now asks for an output folder up front — `window.showDirectoryPicker` in the browser, the native `chooseFolder` dialog wrapped in a directory-handle shim in the desktop app — and writes each image into it via `saveFileToDirectory` as it is captured, instead of accumulating a JSZip in memory and downloading a zip at the end. The zip remains only as the fallback where `supportsDirectoryPicker()` is false (Firefox/Safari); cancelling the picker aborts the export cleanly, with nothing yet saved or restored. The picker runs after validation (so a doomed export never prompts) but before any camera/time state is touched, while the button click's user activation is still valid. The fullscreen variant picks its folder BEFORE entering fullscreen, because an OS-level dialog opened from fullscreen kicks Chrome back out of it and frames would be captured at the wrong size; its enter-wait is hardened because `requestFullscreen` can be denied when the picker consumed transient activation (notably the desktop app's awaited IPC dialog), which fires no `fullscreenchange` — the wait now also settles on the request promise rejecting or a 3 s timeout, and the export proceeds at the current window size rather than hanging with the UI hidden. `ad642f0e` adds an overwrite pre-check: shot names are deterministic, so exporting into a folder holding a previous run of the same sitch replaces those files. The filename scheme is hoisted into a single `nameForShot` closure shared by the pre-check and the capture loop, `countExistingFiles()` lists the directory in one pass via `keys()` (probing name by name for the desktop shim, which has no async iteration), and an in-app `showConfirm` *Overwrite Existing Images?* dialog offers Continue/Cancel before anything is saved.
- **Double-clicking the frame slider outside the In/Out range resets that limit** (`45d3b702`, `src/nodes/CNodeFrameSlider.js`). A double-click on the bar left of the A marker resets A to frame 0; right of the B marker resets B to `Sit.frames - 1`. The listener is attached to `sliderDiv` rather than `sliderContainer` so double-clicks on the transport buttons can never trigger it (both the range input and the canvas bubble up to `sliderDiv`), double-clicks within `getNearLimit` grab distance of a limit line or handle are ignored because that zone belongs to dragging, a double-click inside the range does nothing, and the same `abFrameChanged` event the drag path fires is dispatched so every listener stays in sync.
- **Historical satellite sets are smaller, self-healing, and no longer able to exhaust the server** (`fca69699`, `78884c14`, `6969de16`, `b3b45689`; `sitrecServer/gpData.php`, `sitrecServer/proxyStarlink.php`, `sitrecServer/proxy.php`). Four measured changes to the Space-Track path, all landing together with the OMM CSV switch. **(1) Request only the columns we use.** Adding `/predicates/` to the query cuts a single LEO date from 56.6 MB to 21.9 MB raw (2.6×) and 9.19 MB to 3.92 MB compressed (2.3×) — back to within 1.15× of the 3LE zip it replaced — with coverage unchanged at 16,179 satellites, none gained or lost. Sixteen columns are requested: twelve are what SGP4 needs, and `OBJECT_TYPE`, `RCS_SIZE`, `CREATION_DATE` and `OBJECT_ID` are kept deliberately because the cache is permanent and rebuilding this archive against a rate-limited API is expensive, so a column dropped now costs a full re-fetch to recover. **(2) Re-fetch sets cached before the data settled.** Elements are published after their epoch and the queries span [D, D+2], so a set fetched too soon holds only what had been published by then — measured by re-fetching cached sets: fetch lag 0 holds 51.3% of the element sets that exist today (17% on another date) and misses 381 satellites outright, lag 1 92.3%, lag 2 99.7%, lag 3 100.0%. The publication lag is stable across seven dates spanning ten months (p50 0.26–0.39 d, p99.9 0.81–1.64 d), putting the settle point at 2.81–3.64 d once the +2 day window is included, so `GP_SETTLE_DAYS` is 4 (3 would have been too tight for 2025-11-08). `gpCacheIsUsable()` replaces `file_exists()` for the cache lookups: an entry fetched inside the settle window is re-fetched, but only once that window has actually passed, and the replacement's own mtime then falls outside it so each entry self-corrects exactly once; a request for today's date still serves the cache, because nothing better exists yet. Nothing is ever retracted, so this only ever adds — verified live, 2025-09-15 LEO (cached at lag 0) recovered 381 satellites and 25,839 element sets, losing none. **(3) Don't run PHP out of memory.** Three places treated a 35–57 MB payload as freely copyable and each blew the default 128 MB `memory_limit`: `gzencode()` returning the whole compressed payload as a second string (a real fatal — "tried to allocate 34994256 bytes" — which aborted mid-write and left a `.csv` in the cache with no `.gz` beside it), `isValidGPData()`'s `ltrim($dataBlob)` full copy, and `proxyStarlink.php`'s `trim($data)` plus `explode("\n", $data)` turning 35 MB into a 60,000-element array. The `.gz` is now streamed with `gzopen`/`gzwrite` in 1 MB chunks, validation examines a 64 KB prefix, and `getGPLine()` pulls a single line without copying. Verified against a real 56.6 MB response: cached, compressed to 9.2 MB (6.2×), served without error. **(4) One compressed copy per entry, and a failed refresh is not an error.** Cached GP data was being kept three ways at once — uncompressed payload, `.gz` beside it, and for a repaired historical date the legacy `.tle.zip` it replaced — which across 8 repaired entries turned 15.4 MB of zips into 188 MB on disk, an 11× blow-up projecting to roughly 3 GB across the remaining 133 legacy entries. Only the `.gz` is stored now; `writeGPCache()` compresses straight from the response via `deflate_add()` in 1 MB chunks so nothing large is held twice, `serveGPCached()` inflates on the fly for the (in practice non-existent) client that does not advertise gzip, and a superseded `.tle`/`.tle.zip` is removed only once the replacement is verifiably on disk and non-empty. Entries written before this change are still recognised — `gpCacheEntry()` prefers the `.gz` and falls back to a plain file. Local cache went 516 MB → 339 MB, with a full rebuild landing near 350 MB rather than ~3 GB. This exposed two bugs in `proxy.php`, both from paths assuming the plain file exists: freshness was computed from `file_exists($cachedFile)` on the uncompressed path, which with only the `.gz` stored reported "no cache" on EVERY request — re-fetching CelesTrak every time, which is precisely what earns its 403, and rewriting the cache each request so the ETag changed every time and conditional requests could never revalidate; and the failed-refresh backoff touched the uncompressed path, merely creating an empty file and leaving the backoff with no effect. Both now derive from `gpCacheEntry()`. Finally, the settle-window guard deliberately bypasses a provisional cache entry so the request falls through to Space-Track, and every failure path beyond that point was a `die()` — missing credentials, login failure, query failure, empty response, non-200, an HTML error page, a non-CSV body — turning a request that had been served from cache for months into a hard error purely because we decided to try to improve the file. Refreshing a provisional set is an improvement, not a requirement, and it is most likely to be attempted precisely when Space-Track is having a bad day, so the provisional file is remembered before the fetch and `gpFailSoft()` serves it if the refresh fails for any reason, logging why via `error_log()`; only when there is no cached copy at all does the request fail as before. (Recorded for future debugging: a wrong Space-Track password is NOT enough to simulate an outage — its login endpoint answers HTTP 200 even for bad credentials, and the data query then succeeds anyway on a still-valid session cookie in PHP-FPM's temp directory.)
- **Orbit rate scales with field of view** (`4e30a062`, `src/js/CameraControls.js`). The classic shift-drag/middle-drag orbit rate (a full view height of drag = 360°) was tuned for the default 30° main-view FOV, so on a telephoto view — a saved zoomed-in main camera, or one just out of orthographic mode — a few pixels of drag swept the entire visible field. `orbitFovScale = min(1, fov/30)` scales both `rotateLeft` and `rotateUp`; wider-than-default views keep the classic rate.
- **SitrecBridge: honour the requested tab, expose reload, and stop reaping attended sessions** (`f2bb266e`, `0c3dca5f`, `5ad993c6`; developer tooling — `tools/SitrecBridge/`, not part of the shipped app). Three silent ways a command could run against a tab the caller did not ask for, each producing normal-looking results describing the wrong page: `tabId` was not a recognised parameter (the schema names it `tab`, but `tabId` is what `chrome.tabs` uses and what `sitrec_list_tabs` reports as `id`), so unknown keys were dropped in silence and the command ran against the DEFAULT tab — it is now an accepted alias while any other tab-ish key (`tabID`, `tab_id`, `tabTarget`) is refused rather than ignored, extracted to `tab-target.js` so it can be unit-tested without importing `mcp-server.js`, which binds a port on import; a numeric tab ID sent as a STRING was treated as a URL substring, so `"78797930"` searched tab URLs for those digits and matched nothing (or could have matched an unrelated tab), and all-digit targets now resolve as an ID first with substring matching still available afterwards, because sitch URLs legitimately contain digits; and a closed tab was indistinguishable from a bad argument, so the error now says explicitly that Chrome tab IDs do not survive a tab being closed, tells the caller to re-run `sitrec_list_tabs`, and states that it is deliberately NOT falling back to another tab. `0c3dca5f` declares `sitrec_reload_tab` as a tool — the extension has implemented the `chrome.tabs.reload` action all along but the MCP server never exposed it, so the only way to reload was `location.reload()`, which silently does not execute: the page keeps running its previous bundle and a fix that IS deployed looks like a fix that did not work (the giveaway is `performance.now()` showing a document minutes old) — and documents the tab-targeting rules in the agent guide that `sitrec_guide` serves. `5ad993c6` corrects the idle-timeout added in 2.110.2: "no MCP tool call in an hour" was taken to mean "nobody is using this", which for a coding agent is routinely false — a session spends long stretches editing, building and running tests without touching the browser, and the longer and more productive the session the more certain it was to be killed mid-task (measured: 2h37m between browser calls inside a single refactor, and the bridge reaped itself exactly as designed). The DEFAULT timeout now only reaps a bridge whose PARENT has actually gone, tested with `process.kill(ppid, 0)` — a real existence check, strictly stronger than the existing ppid-change test, which cannot fire at all if we were already a child of init — while an EXPLICITLY configured timeout remains a caller instruction (tests, sandbox supervisors, CI) and is obeyed literally. Genuine port pressure was never this timer's job: `rankTakeoverCandidates` handles it, and only when the 9780–9799 range is really full. The decision is the pure function `shouldIdleExit`, so the cases are tested directly rather than through a five-second process spawn.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed the entire celestial sphere sitting ~22 arcmin out against the ground: precession is now applied, and annual aberration to the stars** (`47185a0d`, `src/CelestialMath.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeDisplayNightSky.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeStarChartView.js`, `src/nodes/CPlanets.js`, `src/nodes/CPointLightCloud.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeDisplaySkyOverlay.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeControllerVarious.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeView3DMouse.js`, `src/LongExposure.js`, `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `src/atmosphere/refraction.js`, `src/starTrack/StarTrackerUI.js`, `ThirdPartyNotices.txt`, `tests/celestialFrame.test.js`). The night sky was drawn from J2000/ICRS coordinates but rotated onto the Earth by GMST alone. GMST is referred to the mean equinox OF DATE, so pairing it with J2000 coordinates omitted precession entirely: the whole sky — stars, Sun, Moon, planets, equatorial grid, constellation lines — sat rigidly rotated against the terrain by the precession accumulated since 2000, ~50″/yr, which had reached **22.3 arcmin (0.37°) by mid-2026**. It went unnoticed for years because the sky stayed self-consistent with itself — stars agreed with planets, so star-to-star fits were unaffected and only sky-vs-ground alignment exposed it. It surfaced as a rising Moon photographed over Malmö from Copenhagen sitting 0.7 lunar diameters to the side of the rendered one, with the altitude matching only if the refraction pressure was dialled from a true 1017 hPa down to ~800. `CelestialMath.getEQJToECEFMatrix()` is now the single EQJ→ECEF transform — `Rz(-GAST) · Rotation_EQJ_EQD(t)`, memoised on the epoch because `Rotation_EQJ_EQD` runs a full nutation series and this is asked for once per view per body per frame — and is used by the celestial sphere, the day sphere, `getCelestialDirectionFromRaDec` (camera locks, Sun lighting, sky-overlay labels), the refraction zenith, and the Star Chart, which previously did its own local-sidereal-time projection and so could drift from the 3D sky independently. GAST rather than GMST: the equation of the equinoxes is only ~17″ but it is free here. Body positions keep `ofdate=false` — the precession now lives in the group matrix, so asking astronomy-engine for of-date coordinates as well would apply it twice. The refraction zenith is the subtle half: `uZenithECI` carries the observer's zenith into the sphere's frame and must be the EXACT inverse of the new matrix, so substituting GAST for GMST there looks right and is not — left alone it would have bent every refracted vertex about an axis 22 arcmin off and put back the altitude error just removed; `zenithECIFromLatLonGMST` is therefore now `zenithEQJFromLatLon`, taking `getECEFToEQJMatrix()` explicitly. Annual aberration (up to 20.5″) is now applied to catalog stars, which had none, as a shared uniform plus a GLSL chunk rather than baked into positions, because the star cloud is ~118k points and would otherwise re-upload on every time change; `getStarDirectionECEF()` is the one entry point for star-specific consumers (the Star Track camera sync, the "lock camera to star" celestial controller, star picking in the 3D view, the traverse analysis's star-matching hypothesis, and Long Exposure's star trails), keeping the star/planet distinction explicit so bodies that arrive already aberrated (astronomy-engine folds it in) cannot be shifted twice. The 3D constellation lines and equatorial grid deliberately stay unaberrated — the grid is a coordinate reference, and 20″ between a line and its stars is well under the line width. **Star Track note:** the blind solve is untouched (it is star-to-star in the catalog frame), but Sync Camera now goes through `getStarDirectionECEF` because the solve yields CATALOG RA/Dec and recovering real pointing needs the apparent direction — so any camera azimuth read off a Star Track sync for a 2020s clip before this release is ~22 arcmin wrong and needs re-deriving. Verified against astronomy-engine's independent topocentric `Horizon()` solve — sub-arcsecond for Moon, Sun, Mars and Jupiter at four epochs from 2000 to 2035 — and live in the app, where the reported Moon lands on 98.365890° / 0.179672° against a predicted 98.365890° / 0.179672°; the rendered Sun and the geocentric Sun used for satellite illumination, two independent paths that were 22.3 arcmin apart, now agree to 8.6″, which is solar horizontal parallax, i.e. exactly what should remain. `tests/celestialFrame.test.js` pins it, including a test that reproduces the old bare-`Rz(-GMST)` path and asserts it is 21–24 arcmin wrong, so a future simplification back to a sidereal spin fails with the reason attached. `ThirdPartyNotices.txt` corrected while here: the render catalog was credited to the Yale BSC5 but is a 117,955-entry Hipparcos/ICRS repack at epoch J1991.25 despite the "bsc" in the filename — now credited to ESA, with the Yale entry scoped to `data/nightsky/BSC5.bin`, which still ships. Stellar proper motion is still NOT applied: the shipped catalog carries no proper-motion columns, so that needs a regenerated data file and is left as separate work (residual median 1.2″, tail to 234″).
- **Fixed satellite refraction being bent about the geocentric radial instead of the local vertical** (`c9317dc9`, `e8293023`; `src/atmosphere/refraction.js`, `src/nodes/CSatellite.js`, `tests/refraction.test.js`). `applyRefractionFromObserver` used `observerECEF.normalize()` as the local zenith. That is the geocentric radial, not the local vertical: on WGS84 the two differ by up to 11.55′ (11.5′ at 45° latitude, 10.8′ at Copenhagen, zero only at the equator and the poles). Refraction is symmetric about the vertical, so the tilted axis both bent by the wrong amount and bent in a slightly wrong direction — near the horizon, where the Saemundsson curve is steepest, that is ~1.25′ of satellite altitude error at 0.5° elevation, ~1.0′ at 1°, 0.26′ at 5°, falling to nothing by 20°. The celestial path already used the geodetic zenith (`zenithECEFFromLatLon`); this one function was simply never moved over. New `zenithECEFFromPosition()` does the ECEF→geodetic step with Bowring's closed-form inverse, correct to well under a milliarcsecond at any altitude, kept inline rather than reusing `ECEFToLLAVD_radii` so `refraction.js` keeps its "three only" dependency (it is imported by the star/line/Sun/Moon shaders and by a unit suite that would otherwise drag in `Globals` and the node graph). `e8293023` then makes it track Sitrec's SELECTABLE earth model: `Sit.useEllipsoid` defaults to FALSE for legacy sitches, which makes `Globals.polarRadius` equal `Globals.equatorRadius` — a degenerate sphere on which the observer ECEF is built and the local vertical IS the radial, so a hard-coded WGS84 inversion would have reintroduced the same ~11.5′ tilt in the opposite direction. `zenithECEFFromPosition` now takes the two radii (defaulting to WGS84) and `CSatellite` passes `Globals.equatorRadius`/`polarRadius` alongside the pressure and temperature; equal radii short-circuit to the normalized radial rather than routing through a latitude, so the spherical case is exact rather than merely convergent. Tests cover geodetic recovery against positions built from known lat/lon/height (including 400 km altitude and near-pole), both earth models and the 11.5′-at-45° signature that distinguishes them, and that the lift is purely vertical in the local frame — refraction must never change azimuth.
- **Fixed mouse picking anchoring on the hidden terrain basemap under Google Photorealistic 3D tiles** (`4e30a062`, `src/raycastGround.js`). While Google 3D tiles are the rendered ground, `CNodeTerrainUI.updateTerrainAndOceanVisibility` sets the terrain group's `visible = false` — but it stays in the scene, and Three.js's raycaster ignores `.visible`. The elevation surface can sit tens of metres ABOVE the tile geometry, so as the nearer hit it silently won every pick: the orbit pivot, drag anchor and C-key camera placement all landed on invisible ground far closer than what is on screen (35 m against the visible 134 m, measured in Athens). That reads as shift-drag rotating about the camera rather than the point under the cursor, left-drag barely moving the world, and C appearing to do nothing. `raycastLocalGround` now tests the tiles FIRST and consults the terrain only when it is actually rendered, or as a fallback for rays that miss the tiles entirely (outside 3D-tile coverage, or before they stream in) — which also skips the terrain mesh's ~1 ms BVH-less raycast on every query while Google 3D is active.
- **Fixed the camera and tracked objects being clamped to the hidden elevation map rather than the visible tile surface** (`86c0f3cf`, `edd8a8f0`; `src/threeExt.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeControllerVarious.js`, `src/js/CameraControls.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeBuildings3DTiles.js`). Same root cause as the picking fix, on a different query. `CNodeControllerTrackPosition` clamps every tracked camera/object above the ground each frame via `clampAboveGround`, which measured against `getPointBelow` — the elevation map — so under Google 3D tiles it parked the camera in mid-air over the visible street: setting the look camera to 14 ft AGL left it stuck at 31 ft (clamped up 5.2 m onto an elevation surface 12 m above the tile ground, measured in Athens), with the height control appearing to stop responding while the AGL readout, correctly measured against the tiles, bottomed out well above zero. `86c0f3cf` re-ran the clamp against `getTilesPointBelow` when the basemap is hidden, but only where the tile ground sat BELOW the elevation map; `edd8a8f0` fixed the mirrored half, because `groundBelow()` accepts tile ground within its tolerance in EITHER direction and both occur — sampling 79 columns around the Athens camera, the tile surface was below the elevation map in 63 and ABOVE it in 15, by up to 5.7 m, and in those columns the early `pointAlt - groundAlt > height` return fired and left a camera/object up to 4.7 m BELOW the visible street. The clamp now works in both directions, and the cheap skip is made provably safe by widening it to the tolerance band (`tilesGroundTolerance()`, read off the tiles node rather than imported, because that module already imports `threeExt` and the reverse would be a cycle): no tile hit `groundBelow()` would accept can reach a point more than `height + tolerance` above the elevation ground. `groundBelow()` still rejects roofs and coarse-LOD tiles by returning null, in which case the elevation map remains the best available ground, so behaviour is unchanged wherever tiles are absent or unusable. Tile-aware clamping is **opt-in, default off**, because it costs a tile intersection — 0.157–0.196 ms measured live against ~0 for the elevation-map lookup — and `CNodeDisplayMoonShadow` clamps ~1,500 points per frame, all on the ground and so all landing in the near-ground path: ~235–294 ms per frame against a 16.7 ms budget, roughly 3 fps. Only the callers placing something the user positions or flies opt in: `CNodeControllerTrackPosition` (the camera and tracked objects, where the original bug lived) and `CameraControls.rotateUp` (once per mouse move). All three Moon-shadow sites keep the default and take the exact pre-existing path. Verified: the look camera sits at its requested position (clamped up by 0 m) at 14.08 ft above the tile ground, where it previously stuck at 31.2 ft.
- **Fixed globe drag losing sub-microradian steps and falling behind the cursor** (`4e30a062`, `src/js/CameraControls.js`). Left-drag computed its rotation angle as `acos(dot/(|a||b|))` on ~6.37 Mm Earth-centred vectors. At street level one mouse step subtends ~1e-8 rad, separating the vector tips by centimetres, so `cos(angle)` is exactly 1.0 in double precision and `acos` returned 0 — the step was silently dropped while the handler still consumed the mouse motion, with no catch-up, so a drag fell progressively behind the cursor. Now `atan2(|a×b|, a·b)`, which resolves arbitrarily small angles smoothly and stays correct for planet-scale drags; identical rays break out rather than normalising a zero-length cross product, and the NaN-patch warning it replaced is gone.
- **Fixed all mouse picking being broken while the camera is orthographic** (`4e30a062`, `src/threeExt.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeCamera.js`). Sitrec's orthographic view mode installs an orthographic projection matrix on a camera that still reports `isPerspectiveCamera === true` (so `.fov`/`.aspect` readers keep working). `Raycaster.setFromCamera` branches on that flag, so it unprojected cursor coordinates through the ortho inverse as if perspective, collapsing every screen ray toward the view-centre ray and breaking the orbit pivot, cursor, drag and context menus while ortho was on. `CNodeCamera._installOrthographicOverride` now stamps `__sitrecOrthoMatrixActive` in lockstep with whichever matrix it installs, and a `Raycaster.prototype.setFromCamera` patch builds correct parallel rays when that flag is set — guarded belt-and-braces on `projectionMatrix.elements[15] === 1` (1 only for an orthographic projection) so a stale flag can never divert a camera whose matrix is actually perspective.
- **Fixed the Orbit Image Set export and preview dumping garbage into the camera's Roll control** (`ad642f0e`, `src/ExportImageSet.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeControllerPTZUI.js`). Disabling the look-camera controllers for the export armed the custom sitch's camera→PTZ sync hook (`postApplyControllers`, which keeps the PTZ sliders warm while another source drives the camera), so every orbit pose was written into the *Pan (Az)* / *Tilt (El)* / *Roll* sliders — and the el = 90 nadir shot hits `syncFromCamera`'s gimbal-lock branch, flipping the controller into satellite mode and dumping a `lookAt`-degenerate heading into Roll (seen as ≈176.269°) that persisted after the camera pose was restored. The export and the orbit preview now save and suppress the hook for their duration and restore it in `finally`. Separately, roll extracted from the camera (`syncFromCamera` / `syncModeTransition`) snaps values below a micro-degree to a hard 0, so a zero-roll camera reads — and saves — as exactly 0 rather than `atan2` float noise (~1e-13°).
- **Fixed blind star identification refusing wide fields, refitting on contaminated matches, and gating on a fabricated denominator** (`4489aab9`, `src/starTrack/StarIdentify.js`, `tests/StarIdentify.test.js`, new `tests/StarIdentifyRealClip.test.js`, `tests/fixtures/rotatingStarfieldMap.json`). Three defects in one file, all in the shipping solve. **(1)** The solver's scale lives in GNOMONIC TANGENT UNITS per pixel — what `scalePriorFromFov` returns at the other end of the pipeline — but `projectAndMatch` read it as radians: `fovRadiusRad = width * scale * 0.75; if (fovRadiusRad > 1.2) return null`. Since half a frame spans `tan(fov/2)`, that rejected any centred gnomonic field wider than **77.3°**, however accurate, while claiming to allow 70° of RADIUS. Because it is an upper bound the error was one-directional and silent — never a wrong answer, only a missing one, which reads as "this clip just doesn't solve" — and it was also firing INSIDE the shipping solve: the reference clip cleared the guard at 1.199 against the 1.2 ceiling but refinement's own reprojection did not, so every round aborted and the shipped answer came from the UNREFINED provisional model. Fixed with the arctangent, plus the same correction to the reported `fovDeg` (its only consumer is a status string; `pxPerDeg` was already consistent with tangent units and is unchanged). **(2)** `finishSolve`'s refit is the one fit here whose input is a whole match set collected AT TOLERANCE rather than four hand-picked quad stars, so it is the only one guaranteed to be contaminated — and it was plain least squares, giving a chance pairing at the frame edge (longest lever arm) the same vote as a good one at the centre. Measured: 58 provisional matches refit to a model that could then find only 16, and the solve died reporting "refinement lost the match consensus" with most of its correspondences correct. New `fitSimilarityRobust` fits, drops the tail beyond 3× the MEDIAN residual, and refits — the median because it survives a minority of arbitrarily bad pairs, which is exactly the contamination present; never trimming below 60% of the points, because past that the "outliers" are more likely to be the model; and on a clean set nothing is trimmed, so an uncontaminated solve is left where least squares put it. **(3)** `nProjected` was fabricated: `finishSolve` initialised it to `min(deep.length, maxProjected)` — the whole SKY's verification pool clipped by the cap, a number no projection ever produced — and never rolled it back when a round did, while the final consensus gate divides by it. It now travels WITH the matches from the projection that produced them (`verifyHypothesis` returns it; the rollback triple carries it). Measured per label against an arbiter the identifier never sees — the spherical map's directions fitted to the matched catalogue directions by trimmed Wahba, then great-circle residuals, with the map built from pixel geometry only and no catalogue in it: **68 → 72 matched, 56 → 66 right, 7 → 3 grossly wrong, p90 error 0.723° → 0.253°, max 3.179° → 2.209°**. Five tracks changed HIP and all five moved CLOSER to where the map independently puts them (54 unchanged, 5 changed, 13 new, 9 that lost their names — of those 9, five were right and four wrong, three grossly). This also settles a wrong conclusion from `854f871a`'s investigation: the spherical map is accurate to ~0.15° (median 0.14, rms 0.18), not the 0.42° once reported — that figure came from a best-fit planar SIMILARITY between two gnomonic charts, which measures how unlike the charts are, not sky error, and the atmospheric-refraction hypothesis it prompted is unnecessary. The ~60 edge stars the lens fit recovers are still deliberately unnamed: feeding identification the improved star set now SOLVES, but mislabels those edge stars 37–75% of the time, which fails the bar that a wrong name shown confidently is worse than no name (`docs/dev/star-identify-edge-stars.md` carries the measured route from here). Adds a debug-only diagnostics channel (`solveField(..., {debug: true})`) recording quad counts, a `rej.*` counter per guard, and the full acceptance arithmetic of every refinement round — module-scoped because `solveField` is synchronous with no awaits, null unless requested, and never read by the solve, so a debug run takes bit-for-bit the same path as a normal one. `78965daa` fixes the same tangent-vs-radian trap in `identifyStars`' tier-order heuristic, which computed `scalePrior * maxDim * 180/PI` and so crossed its 35° threshold at a true 34.0° — small, but it decides which index tier is tried first.
- **Fixed the live CelesTrak feeds caching an error page as satellite data** (`0fd2bfb0`, `sitrecServer/proxy.php`, `sitrecServer/gpData.php`). CelesTrak answers a re-request made before it has republished with HTTP 403 and a "GP data has not updated since your last successful download" body. `proxy.php` ignored `http_status` and wrote that text straight into the cache as satellite data, so *Load CURRENT Starlink* / *Load ACTIVE Satellites* then parsed it as elements and produced an empty sky for the rest of the cache lifetime. Responses are now validated by `isValidGPData($dataBlob, $httpStatus, $ext)` before caching; when the fetch is invalid but a cache exists, the stale copy is served and the mtime is nudged so a refresh is re-armed after 10 minutes; when nothing is cached and nothing usable comes back, the client gets a plain `ERROR:`-prefixed message (the prefix `src/TLEUtils.ts` looks for) instead of silently loading nothing. The cache lifetime also moves from 1 hour to 2 to match CelesTrak's publication cadence rather than provoking the 403 hourly.
- **Fixed a dropped image overriding the view layout a dropped video would have got** (`4fc10cd0`, `src/EXIFUtils.js`, `tests/EXIFUtils.test.js`). Dropping an image and dropping a video into the same custom sitch produced different layouts — image bottom-right, video top-right — at the same landscape aspect. Both go through the same `videoLoaded` event, and it chose correctly for both; EXIF import then moved it again, because `applyImportedImageViewLayout` carried its own hardcoded table placing the video view at `0.55, 0.5, 0.45, 0.5`. A `.MOV` never passes through the EXIF importer, so only images were overridden. Three things were wrong with that: it is the wrong layer (everything else this module applies is DATA — GPS to camera position, heading/pitch/roll, FOV, capture time — whereas window geometry belongs to the view-preset system that already owns it); it is not metadata (the numbers are hardcoded, so no JPEG ever asked for that layout, despite the log line reading `Applied JPEG metadata … viewLayout=`); and it raced a decision already made correctly. It now bails out when a video view exists, keeping the remaining entries for view configurations the preset system does not reach (no video view at all), and keeping the UI-text refresh that used to ride along at the end of the function — the import still changed camera position, FOV and time even though it moved nothing.

## Version 2.110.4 (2026-07-31)

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed satellites showing stale Earth-shadow illumination after a time seek** (`src/nodes/CNodeDateTime.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeDisplayNightSky.js`). The per-satellite Earth-shadow/brightness test in `CNodeDisplayNightSky.updateSatelliteBrightness()` is staggered for performance — `satTimeStep = 10`, so only 1/10th of satellites are recomputed per rendered frame while the rest reuse `satData.lastScale`. Correct during continuous playback, but a discrete time set (dragging a Time-menu slider jumps sim time by minutes per rendered frame) left up to 90% of satellites displaying illumination computed at pre-jump times — visibly sunlit dots deep inside Earth's shadow, and vice versa, until the stagger caught up over the next 10 renders. Fix: `CNodeDateTime` gains `_bumpTimeSetSerial()`, called at the top of `setStartDateTime()` and `setNowDateTime()`, so every discrete time set increments `timeSetSerial` — the Time-menu sliders (Year through ms, via `updateDateTime` → `setNowDateTime`), the `;` / `'` keyboard time nudges (via `AdjustStartTime`), programmatic `setStartDateTime`/CSitrecAPI seeks, track sync, EXIF, and scenario setups. Live Mode's per-frame `setNowDateTime` call in `update()` is exempted via a `_liveTick` guard flag — that is continuous clock sync, not a seek. `CNodeDisplayNightSky.update()` watches the serial through its `startTime` input and on change latches `this.fullBrightnessUpdate = true`; `updateSatelliteBrightness()` skips the stagger entirely while the latch is set (the same bypass as `firstRenderTLE`), and the latch clears only when a full brightness pass actually completes — the early returns (satellites hidden, light cloud not ready) leave it pending. Keying off the serial rather than a time delta is deliberate: high-speed playback (e.g. 120× simSpeed × playbackSpeed) legitimately advances many seconds per frame but never routes through the setters, so it keeps the cheap staggered update. Verified live: 12-second and 15/30-minute slider seeks and direct `setStartDateTime()` API seeks each produced exactly one full pass with the brightness array bit-identical from the first post-seek frame onward (FP-bit hash over the light-cloud brightness array, 12,980-satellite LEO set); Live Mode ran ~76 frames with the serial unchanged.

## Version 2.110.3 (2026-07-31)

Internal release-infrastructure fix only — no user-facing changes. (The 2.110.2 tag exists but was never deployed to production because of the CI failure fixed here; 2.110.2's user-facing changes reach production with this release.)

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed CI failing on the SitrecBridge node:test suites** (working tree, `package.json` only). The 2.110.2 release added `tools/SitrecBridge/tests/*.test.js`, written for Node's built-in `node --test` runner. The root `test` script was `npx jest tests/` — but Jest's positional argument is a path *regex*, not a directory, so `tests/` also matched `tools/SitrecBridge/tests/` and Jest collected both suites. The `node:test` cases actually ran and passed inside Jest's process, but registered zero *Jest* tests, so each suite failed with "Your test suite must contain at least one test" and the 2.110.2 CI run went red (blocking deployment). Fix: `<rootDir>/tools/SitrecBridge/` is added to Jest's `testPathIgnorePatterns`, and the root `test` script becomes `npm --prefix tools/SitrecBridge test && npx jest tests/`, so root `npm test` — which is exactly what CI runs (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) — still executes the bridge lifecycle suites first, under their intended `node --test` runner, before the Jest suite. Verified: bridge suites 7/7 under `node --test`; full Jest suite 150 suites / 2663 tests green with the new ignore pattern.

## Version 2.110.2 (2026-07-31)

### New Features
- **`ride` camera command — sit ON a moving target** (`cb6558a0`, `src/scriptedVideo/ScriptCommands.js`, `docs/ScriptedVideo.md`). New scripted-video camera beat `ride(target, secs=5, lookAt?, height=1.6, back=0)`: the camera rides a moving target (a car, a `createWalker` marker) `height` m above it and `back` m behind its heading, looking at `lookAt` — another target or a `"lat,lon,alt"` literal — or, when `lookAt` is omitted, 500 m ahead along the direction of motion. This is the driver-POV shot `follow` cannot do: `follow` always looks at its own target, `ride` moves WITH one target while framing another (`ride car 4 sphere3`). `ridePose()` samples heading over a ±12-frame baseline like `followPose`, with the same deterministic stopped-target fallback (end-minus-start, then the start pose's look direction, then local north) so the "ahead" aim never degenerates; `sample()` eases out of the previous beat's pose over the first 25% of the beat. A *supplied* `lookAt` that doesn't resolve is a typo, not a request to look ahead: `prepare()` marks the beat invalid with `e.invalidReason` naming the bad id, and `_computeWarnings()` (`src/CScriptedVideo.js`) now prefers a command's own `invalidReason` over the generic `target "…" did not resolve` message — so the warning blames the bad lookAt, not the valid ride target. The `setScriptedVideo` API doc string now advertises `follow` and `ride`.
- **Walker markers persist with the sitch** (`cb6558a0`, `src/CSitrecAPI.js`, `src/CustomManagerSerialize.js`). `createWalker` records a sanitized spec in `Globals.walkerSpecs`; serialization saves only *live* walkers — the node must exist, actually be a walker (`_walkerTrackID`, so an unrelated node reusing the name doesn't count), and its synthetic track must still exist in `TrackManager` — so a walker deleted via any path stays deleted on reload. Deserialization recreates each spec via `sitrecAPI.call("createWalker", {...spec, reuseTrackID: spec.trackID})` before the mods pass (the appFlight contract: deterministic ids so mods can restore per-node state), and `reuseTrackID` rebinds the walker to the synthetic track `TrackManager.deserialize` already restored instead of creating a duplicate. Corrupt or hand-edited specs are skipped with a console warning rather than aborting the sitch load, and `Globals.walkerSpecs` is reset on every deserialize so a previous sitch's walkers can't leak into the next save.

### Improvements
- **Richer, safer `createWalker`** (`cb6558a0`, `src/CSitrecAPI.js`). Waypoints may now be `[lat, lon, alt]` (falling back to the `alt` param); a `fractions` array gives per-waypoint time fractions of `[startFrame, endFrame]` for uneven timing (hover-then-dash) — validated as non-decreasing finite 0..1, same length as waypoints, starting at 0 and ending at 1, via an index loop so sparse-array holes can't slip through; new `material` / `emissive` / `emissiveIntensity` options (material validated against `CNode3DObject`'s types *before* teardown, since `rebuildMaterial` asserts on unknown types), box `width`/`depth`, and `rotateY`. All numeric params go through a strict `num()` coercion — `Number()` alone takes `null`/`false`/`""` as 0 and would place waypoints on Null Island or scramble the frame range — and every validation runs BEFORE the destructive teardown, so a bad re-create can never destroy the existing walker and then fail. Name-safety guards: a name already held by a non-walker node is refused; a first creation is refused if `${name}_` collides with existing node ids or if the name sits inside another walker's namespace (which that walker's next re-create sweep would erase). Re-creates now sweep only `_walkerOwnedIds` — the `${name}_*` nodes recorded as appearing during this walker's own construction — never a blind prefix sweep that could take unrelated nodes created in the namespace later. Track knots are kept strictly increasing, and a waypoint that would land past `endFrame` is an error rather than a silently late finish. The walker's path display line is hidden by default (authoring noise in a scripted shot; re-showable from the track's GUI folder). Pinned by a validation/persistence suite in `tests/CSitrecAPI.test.js` and `tests/scriptRideCommand.test.js`.
- **Captions: word-wrap, shrink-to-fit, hard-break, never drop lines** (`cb6558a0` + review follow-up `09289a68`, `src/CScriptedVideo.js` `_layoutCaption` / `_drawCaptions`). Scripted-video captions previously drew as a single line that could clip at the frame edge. Now: wrap to at most 3 lines at the base size (5.5% of frame height, max width 92% of frame width), stepping the font down by 0.92 per iteration to a hard floor of half the base size until the block fits; concurrent captions stack upward from the same 0.85 h bottom anchor as the old single-line behavior. The follow-up fixed two review findings in the same release: a single token wider than the frame (a long URL) was accepted as one overflowing line — tokens no word-wrap can fit are now broken at character level (`breakWord`, measured chunk by chunk) so no line can exceed maxWidth; and a caption still over 3 lines at the half-size floor lost its remaining words to `slice(0, 3)` — at the floor all wrapped lines are now kept and the block stacks higher. Captions may shrink or stack, but never clip or drop words.
- **Scripted-video `groundClearance` serializes with the scripts** (`cb6558a0`, `serializeScriptedVideo` / `deserializeScriptedVideo` in `src/CScriptedVideo.js`, scriptless-load reset in `src/CustomManagerSerialize.js`). The camera's minimum height above terrain now saves alongside the script tabs — a driver-POV sitch needs its low clearance back on load. The manager is a singleton, so the load paths must actively reset it: a save without the field, and a scriptless sitch load, both restore the 3 m default rather than inheriting whatever the previously loaded sitch set.
- **Star Tracker: full bridge-anchor search on re-acquisition** (`33b7258d`, `src/starTrack/StarMatch.js`). After an outage (blur, obstruction), `solveChainOnce` previously accepted the first *strong* re-acquisition outright — but "strong" is relative to the anchor's OWN size, so a 6-of-6 lock on a sparse degraded anchor could clear its local gates and preempt a 24-inlier recovery waiting one anchor further down the list, purely by search order. Every candidate anchor is now evaluated; across anchors the comparison is absolute — a strong fit beats any weak one, and among fits of equal standing the one explaining the most sources wins. This changes the composed cumulative transform chain, so the star map, identification, and camera sync downstream all benefit.
- **Star Tracker: contest requires transform disagreement** (`33b7258d`, `src/starTrack/StarMatch.js`). A frame is now reported contested only when a challenger (the best of `matchByInvariants` / `matchByOffsetVote`) matches or beats a strong prediction fit's inlier support AND proposes a materially different transform — measured as `motionMagnitude(challenger ∘ predicted⁻¹)` at the frame corners exceeding 2× `inlierThreshold`. Previously any challenger that displaced a strong prediction flagged the frame, even when both proposed essentially the same transform (agreement, not contest); conversely an inlier *tie* — where the prediction is kept for continuity — now does count when the transforms genuinely disagree. This corrects the `weakFrames` diagnostic returned by `solveFrameChain` (no UI consumer yet — arbitration is honest but is an arbitration, so the frame is reported weak for downstream use). A new dense-random-field refusal test in `tests/StarIdentify.test.js` pins the strong-count path.
- **SitrecBridge MCP server: lifecycle overhaul** (working tree, developer tooling — not part of the shipped app; `tools/SitrecBridge/mcp-server.js`, new `tools/SitrecBridge/lifecycle.js`, `tools/SitrecBridge/tests/`, `README.md`, `package.json`). Host-fallback bridges now exit after an idle timeout — no MCP stdio message for `SITREC_BRIDGE_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS` (default one hour; `0` disables; invalid values fall back to the default via `parseIdleTimeout`) — checked on an interval and only while not busy (no pending extension requests or Local Compute jobs); paired sandbox bridges (`SITREC_BRIDGE_PAIRED_ORIGIN`) never idle-exit. Activity is timestamped on every MCP stdio message via a `transport.onmessage` hook set before `server.connect` (the MCP SDK chains a pre-set handler, so this works), and `sitrec_status` now reports `lastMcpActivityAt` / `idleTimeoutMs`. The HTTP server gains two loopback-only control endpoints: `GET /__sitrec_bridge/status` (service, pid/ppid, pairedOrigin, busy, lastMcpActivityAt, per-process random control token) and `POST /__sitrec_bridge/shutdown`, authenticated by the `X-Sitrec-Bridge-Token` header. When every fallback port (now env-configurable via `SITREC_BRIDGE_FALLBACK_PORT_MIN`/`MAX`, default 9780–9799) is occupied, a newly launched bridge no longer aborts: it queries each occupant's status and reclaims the least-recently-active non-busy, non-paired bridge (`rankTakeoverCandidates` — LRU by `lastMcpActivityAt`, preferring a same-parent sibling on ties) by requesting its shutdown and polling the freed port for up to 2 s. `startServer` is promisified with bind-failure rejection (EADDRINUSE continues the scan), shutdown also triggers on stdout EPIPE and MCP transport close, `shutdownGracefully` is idempotent, and `npm test` runs new `node --test` suites (`tests/lifecycle.test.js`, `tests/server-lifecycle.test.js`).

### Bug Fixes
- **Multi-touch-safe star toggle** (`33b7258d`, `src/starTrack/StarTrackerUI.js`). Toggling a star's inclusion previously fired on any pointerdown/pointerup pair within 4 px — including right-clicks and the fingers of a pinch gesture, since every touch contact reports `button === 0`. The click tracker now arms only on a primary-button primary pointer with no pointer already down; a second finger arriving mid-press VOIDS the pending click rather than replacing it (neither finger's release toggles, and the void can't be re-armed by further fingers of the same gesture); `pointermove` beyond 4 px sets a sticky `moved` flag so a drag that returns to its origin still doesn't count; `pointercancel` voids; and `pointerup` must match the arming `pointerId`. Pinch-zooming over the video can no longer change star exclusions.

## Version 2.110.1 (2026-07-31)

### Improvements
- **Star Tracker — one-click Measure/Analyze/Identify/Sync, tidier menu** (`78ffd8a4`, `src/starTrack/StarTrackerUI.js`). A new **Measure/Analyze/Identify/Sync** button at the top of Video → Star Tracker runs the whole pipeline via `runFullStarTracker()`: calibrate on the current frame, analyze the In/Out range, identify against the catalog, sync the camera. Each stage gates the next on what it actually produced, not on shared module state: `detectStarSize()` now returns whether the clicked context still stands — false when the sitch or video changed during the decode wait, a newer calibration click superseded this one, or there was nothing to run against; true otherwise, *including* a failed measurement, which keeps the previous parameters by design so analysis stays runnable. A failed or aborted `runStarTracker()` returns null and stops the chain, and `syncCameraToStarTrack()` only runs when the analysis it chained from carries `identify?.solved?.ok`. So each stage's own failure status stays on screen instead of being overwritten by the next stage's complaint, and a click's analysis and camera sync can never land on a video the user swapped in after clicking. The sliders and per-stage buttons (*Detect threshold (sigma)*, *Min blob area (px)*, *Detect Star Size (current frame)*, *Min detections per track*, *Moving: significance*, *Moving: min drift (sigma)*, *Analyze In/Out range*, *Identify Stars (catalog)*, *Sync Camera to Star Field*, *Make Star Chart (PNG)*) move into a closed **Star Tracker Tweaks** subfolder; *Status*, the five display toggles (*Show stars* … *Show rejected*), *Chart: object tracks*, and *Clear* stay top-level, with the toggles moved up under Status.
- **Star Tracker overlay — clump-wide click-to-toggle** (`78ffd8a4`, `src/starTrack/StarTrackerUI.js`). Clicking the overlay now toggles ALL star circles containing the click point, not the smallest one: `starHitsAt()` (replacing `starHitAt()`) collects every hit, and `toggleStarsEnabled()` unifies them to one outcome — if any clicked circle is still enabled the click disables them all, otherwise it re-enables them all. A tight clump of overlapping circles goes in one click instead of one per circle, and mixed states can't flip forever without ever reaching "all off". Only star-class circles are clickable; movers and clusters are not registered in `overlayStarHits`.
- **Fullscreen suppression is now a snapshot; views opened during fullscreen show on top** (`src/CViewManager.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeView.js`, `src/CLayoutManager.js`, `src/CSitrecAPI.js`, `src/CustomSupport.js`, `src/index.js`, `src/LongExposure.js`, `src/scriptedVideo/ScriptRenderer.js` — committed with this release). `ViewMan.setFullscreenView(view)` is now the sole assignment point for `fullscreenView` (all former direct writes in `CNodeView.doubleClick`, `CSitrecAPI`, `CustomSupport`, `index.js` `disposeEverything()`, and `restoreFullscreenFromMods` route through it). On entry it snapshots exactly the independent views visible at that moment into `ViewMan.fullscreenSuppressed`, and `_computeEV` hides only members of that set — so a view opened DURING fullscreen (Tab → chat, a graph) is not suppressed, renders on top of the fullscreen view, and survives its exit; exiting restores the covered set, whose own `visible` flags never changed. Dependent children (`relativeTo`, non-`separateVisibility` overlays) are excluded from the snapshot because they inherit their parent's effective visibility; popped-out (`windowed`) views are excluded because they live in a separate browser window. A show during fullscreen is treated as a deliberate reveal: `CNodeView.setVisible(true)` lifts the view's suppression via `ViewMan.unsuppressView()` — *before* the same-value early-out, since a suppressed view still has `visible === true` — and `unsuppressView` cascades to `separateVisibility` overlay descendants so a re-shown video brings its enabled tracking/annotation overlays along. The offline-render machinery that mechanically forces and restores visibility flags (`LongExposure`'s lookView save/restore, `ScriptRenderer`'s per-frame force) now uses a new intent-free `setVisibleRaw()`, so those writes never lift suppression mid-render. Fullscreening view B while A is fullscreen un-doubles the incumbent first, so exiting B's fullscreen can no longer reveal A still stretched over the whole screen.
- **Closing a full-screened view restores the layout; tiled views exit rather than close** (`src/nodes/CNodeView.js`, `src/CLayoutManager.js` — committed with this release). `closeViewWithUndo` on the fullscreen owner first runs `undouble()` — the extracted exit half of `doubleClick()`, split out without its visibility guard so hide paths can use it — restoring the saved pre-fullscreen geometry, then consults a new `LayoutMan.viewSharesEdge(id)` seam test computed fresh from `_collectRects()`/`_computeSeams()` (not the possibly-stale `_seams`): a view that sits edge-to-edge with others is structural, so its ✕ reads as "exit fullscreen" and the tiles come back intact; only a floating view actually closes (with the usual undo record).
- **Star Chart has a single visibility authority** (`78ffd8a4`, `src/nodes/CNodeDisplayNightSky.js`). The Star Chart view sets `excludeFromViewsMenu: true`, so it no longer gets a second toggle under Show → Views that would fight its own Show → Star Chart folder.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed the Sky Plot drawing at 2x with its centre at the bottom-right on high-DPI displays** (`fa0316da`, `src/nodes/CNodeSkyPlotView.js`). The plot (Show → Celestial → *Sky Plot*) drew using `canvas.width/height` — backing-store pixels — on a context the tabbed-canvas base class had already scaled by `devicePixelRatio`, landing the whole plot at 2x. It now draws in CSS units (`this.widthPx`/`this.heightPx`) like every other canvas view.
- **Fixed the Sky Plot showing no satellites unless the Satellite Ephemeris table was open** (`fa0316da`, `src/nodes/CNodeViewEphemeris.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeSkyPlotView.js`). `CNodeViewEphemeris.update()` consumed its throttle window (setting `lastUpdateTime`) *before* the visibility check, so while hidden it burned every refresh window without computing `filteredSatData`, and the Sky Plot — which reads that array — stayed empty or frozen. The az/el compute now lives in `ensureSatData()` on the shared throttle; `update()` checks visibility first and no longer touches the throttle when it declines, and `plotSatellites()` calls `ephemerisView.ensureSatData()` itself, so the plot drives refreshes while the ephemeris view is hidden.
- **Fixed hiding a full-screened view leaving a blank screen — from any path, and from legacy saves** (`fa0316da` originated the ✕-button fix; generalized by the working-tree changes committed with this release — `src/nodes/CNodeView.js`, `src/CViewManager.js`). Previously `ViewMan.fullscreenView` kept suppressing every other view after the one view it showed was hidden. Now `setVisible(false)` is the choke point: hiding the fullscreen owner by ANY route — header ✕, Show → Views checkbox, title-menu close, an API `hide()`, or the redo of a close — runs `undouble()` first, restoring the pre-fullscreen layout, so a hidden owner can never strand a blank screen. `restoreFullscreenFromMods()` gets the matching load-time guard: a legacy save that captured `doubled: true` with `visible: false` (closing a full-screened view used to hide it without un-doubling) is un-doubled instead of restored to fullscreen, so it comes back windowed at its saved pre-fullscreen rect on a later re-show.
- **Fixed a same-sitch video swap feeding one video's plate-scale calibration into another's analysis** (`fa0316da`, `src/starTrack/StarTrackerUI.js`). *Detect Star Size* now records which video it measured (`calibrationVideoData`); `dropCalibrationForOtherVideo()` runs at the top of both `detectStarSize()` and `runStarTracker()` and drops the calibration — together with its measured `minArea`, restored to `STAR_DETECT_DEFAULTS.minArea` only when `minAreaCalibrated` — when the current video differs, exactly as `disposeStarTracker()` does and for the same reason: keeping one without the other hands the analysis a half-calibrated mismatch. A hand-edited `minArea` is a preference, not a measurement, and stays.
- **Fixed moving-object circles pinned to the sky outside their observed range** (`78ffd8a4`, `src/starTrack/StarTrackerUI.js`). `drawStarTrackerOverlay()` skips a mover's circle and label outside `[c.first, c.last]` — the nearest-observation mapping otherwise carried its position to every other frame, pinning a red circle to the sky before the object appeared and after it left. Movers now also draw at 50% alpha so the marker and label flag the object without painting over it.

## Version 2.110.0 (2026-07-31)

### New Features
- **Star Tracker — night-sky video analysis** (`45841f96`, new `src/starTrack/` — `StarDetect.js`, `StarMatch.js`, `StarSolve.js`, `StarCluster.js`, `StarSynthetic.js`, `StarTrackerUI.js`; `src/CustomManagerSetup.js`, `src/index.js`). A four-stage pipeline over the In/Out frame range that separates stars from moving objects in noisy night-sky video, under a new **Video → Star Tracker** folder (`setupStarTrackerMenu()` runs from `CCustomManager.setup()`, so it appears in the custom sitch and any editable sitch with a Video menu; labels are inline `.name()` strings, no i18n keys). `StarDetect`: sigma-clipped tile background, matched-filter detection, per-channel saturation handling, green/laser rejection, aperture photometry, and `calibrateDetection` behind the **Detect Star Size (current frame)** button, which derives the pixel-scale detection parameters from the current frame's measured blobs. `StarMatch`: frame-to-frame rigid fits (|A|=1 — stars are at infinity), predicted matching, triangle-invariant re-acquisition, camera-fixed artifact removal, and a chained solve with bridging. `StarSolve`: a global alternating solve of star map + per-frame transforms with temporally regularised rotations, exact min-cost (Hungarian) detection-to-track assignment, velocity-predicted association, split-star merging treated as a falsifiable hypothesis, and sigma-scaled classification into star / moving / cameraFixed / incoherent with instrumental magnitudes. `StarCluster` groups the lights of one moving object (an aircraft's flashing cluster) by shared motion — a fixed-effects path fit with F-tested curvature, strobe attachment, and distinct-light counting. `StarTrackerUI` runs it all with non-blocking progress and abort, draws per-class overlay circles on the video view (**Show stars / Show moving / Show light clusters / Show rejected** toggles), and **Make Star Chart (PNG)** downloads a magnitude-scaled chart of the solved star map. `disposeStarTracker()` is called from `disposeEverything()` in `index.js` — the solve is tied to the old video and the GUI folder to the old menu bar, so both must go on sitch change. `StarSynthetic` renders ground-truth clips (bloomed stars, a laser, hot pixels, a mover) for the Jest suites; every regression test was verified to fail under the code it guards against.
- **Blind star identification, and object tracks on the star chart** (`f6f67595`, new `src/starTrack/StarIdentify.js`, `src/starTrack/StarTrackerUI.js`). **Identify Stars (catalog)** names the solved star map with no location, time or pointing assumed — blind astrometric calibration by quad geometric hashing (Lang et al. 2010, the astrometry.net method). `StarIdentify.js` is pure: it parses Sitrec's shipped 118k-star Hipparcos repack (`data/nightsky/sitrec_bsc_lite.bin`, fixing the IAU-CSN HIP column and the catalog's trailing-record bound along the way), builds a two-tier quad-code index at runtime, and solves by hypothesis-and-verify — a code match proposes a field via a four-point similarity onto the catalog quad's tangent plane, and the whole in-field catalog must then land on the detected stars to be believed; refinement keeps basis, transform and match set mutually consistent, rolling a round back atomically if it degrades. The app layer lazy-loads the catalog (2.5 MB) and the IAU name file (`data/nightsky/IAU-CSN.txt`) on first press — cached-promise loaders assigned before the body can throw and cleared by identity on failure, with names cached separately so a transient failure retries. Identified stars are labelled on the overlay and the chart; the status line reports field centre, size, roll and rms. The chart also gains object tracks — movers as red paths, cluster ensembles as dashed orange, arrowheads at true endpoints — behind the **Chart: object tracks** toggle. Verified on the test clip: the field blind-solves to the head of Draco, 34 of 38 stars identified, Eltanin and Rastaban named, RA 17.70h Dec +46.8°, 15.2° field, rms 1.8 px.
- **Still images and real astrophotos** (`eaa1eab4`, `src/starTrack/StarDetect.js`, `StarIdentify.js`, `StarTrackerUI.js`). A still loaded as video (`CVideoImageData`) gets its own path: one detection pass however many frames the timeline claims, every point presumed a star (nothing can move in a single exposure), and one identity transform held across the range so the overlay registers at any slider position. Detection now survives real photographs: the too-big-to-be-a-star bound scales with sensor area, green rejection requires laser shape (non-round or large relative to the frame) so compact green dots — aircraft navigation lights — are kept, and a white-clipped round disk is read as a bright star, not a beam; what was rejected and why is tallied onto the result as `rejectCounts` / `rejectSamples`. The decisive identification change: verification re-centres its tangent point on the image centre and refits the quad before projecting — a pinhole camera is exactly gnomonic-about-axis plus a similarity, so verification becomes distortion-exact at any field of view (correct wide-field hypotheses had been dying with tens of pixels of pure projection error). Around it: two-stage acceptance, a consensus denominator of min(image, projectable), a depth-adaptive refinement pool that names faint stars, a scale prior from camera optics metadata, a wide index tier for phone-lens fields, and a clutter filter so lit foliage cannot hijack the quad anchors. Stars with proper names label in white, one point larger, on both the overlay and the chart. Verified end to end: a 12-megapixel iPhone twilight photo with a lit tree and a contrail blind-solved to the Summer Triangle (RA 18.90h Dec +21.8°).
- **Sync Camera to Star Field** (`989ecc08`, `src/starTrack/StarTrackerUI.js`, `StarIdentify.js`). Drives the look camera through the blind solve: a `CNodeControllerStarTrack` samples the frame centre and a point above it through the per-frame transform and the identify calibration (`refToSky`, exported from `finishSolve` as the bridge from any reference-frame pixel to RA/Dec under the final model), converts both to ECEF at each frame's own instant, and rebuilds the full orientation — boresight and roll — idempotently at apply time. Registered via `replaceOption`/`selectOption` as a **"Star Track"** option on the **Camera Heading** switch (`CameraLOSController`) and the FOV source switch (`fovSwitch`, a constant vertical FOV from the fitted plate scale), so it round-trips freely with Manual and the other sources; clearing the analysis detaches both options *before* the solve they rest on goes away. Identify robustness in the same commit: the scale prior is converted through `scalePriorFromFov` (short-axis tangent units, portrait-safe), projections are confined to the image bounds before the cap and the consensus denominator, and the top-5 provisional candidates are finalized best-first rather than first-only. Verified live on the Draco clip: boresight exact at the frame centre, and the rendered sky registers with the video stars.
- **The overlay registers in decoded pixel space** (`200aca0b`, `src/starTrack/StarTrackerUI.js`). The analysis runs on the decoded frames, whose size is capped by **Settings → Performance Tweaks → Max Resolution** — a 4K source may be analysed at 720P. The overlay mapped through `videoToCanvasCoordsOriginal`, which expects ORIGINAL source coordinates, so on a 4K clip decoded at 720P every circle was compressed threefold toward the top-left; the two spaces only coincide at or below the cap, which is what hid it on 720P footage. The overlay now rescales from the frame size the analysis ran at to the *current* decoded size and maps through the display-space conversion, so it stays registered at any Max Resolution setting, including one changed after the analysis.
- **Wide-range solve robustness, and click-to-toggle stars** (`6b992c73`, `src/starTrack/StarMatch.js`, `StarIdentify.js`, `StarTrackerUI.js`). Long panning ranges no longer lose the solve — on the test clip, extending In/Out from 179 to 379 frames previously killed the frame chain at a camera jerk (196 of 379 frames unregistered) and star tracks shattered into fragments and fake movers. `matchByOffsetVote` adds translation-consensus re-acquisition (every cross-frame source pair votes for the offset it implies; binned histogram, median-seeded refit, invariant-grade corroboration gates) — triangle invariants pick vertices by brightness rank, so motion blur starves them exactly when re-acquisition is needed, and position consensus does not care. Bridging tries a pool of recent STRONG anchors, newest first, not only the last accepted frame — the frame at an outage's edge is the tail of the blur that caused it. All three matchers now run on every frame pair and the corroborated fit explaining the most sources wins, closing a reachable hole where a coherent decoy cluster (stationary glints) formed a strong-looking wrong lock. `pairWithinGate` pairs through a gate-sized spatial hash (near-linear, identical pairs, order-invariant tie-break): 197 failed frames → 7 on the real clip. Identify on wide maps: solver bounds are the union of the video rectangle and the star map's bounding box (a panning clip carries the map far beyond frame 0); a strong-count acceptance path (≥ 25 matches at a ≥ 0.35 fraction floor) admits similarity-stitched wide mosaics whose intrinsic gnomonic warp caps the within-tolerance fraction near half however correct the solve is; and identify input keeps only stars with min(25, 0.15 × frames) observations, stills exempt — the still photo now identifies 518/531, up from 372. Click-to-toggle: clicking inside a star circle in the video view toggles it — disabled stars draw at 30% alpha and are excluded from the next Identify run; hit-testing uses the circles as drawn this frame, stationary clicks only, capture-phase listener so the view's own drag handling is untouched. Review hardening: analyze/clear detach the Star Track camera options and disable the controller before the solve dies (a dormant enabled flag could silently override Manual on the next solve), and re-identification refreshes the cached Star Track FOV so heading and zoom always come from the same solve.
- **Star Chart — Heavens-Above style whole-sky chart** (`ee0979e3`, `a8c68b22`, new `src/nodes/CNodeStarChartView.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeDisplayNightSky.js`). `CNodeStarChartView` (a `CNodeTabbedCanvasView`, draggable/resizable, clamped fully on-screen) draws the visible hemisphere as an azimuthal-equidistant disk — zenith centre, east on the left — with BSC catalog stars sized by magnitude, constellation lines and names, the naked-eye planets, and an azimuth ring with cardinal letters. Created in the `CNodeDisplayNightSky` constructor (not on `tleLoaded` — stars and constellations need no TLE data), so any sitch with the night sky gets it, hidden by default. A **Show → Star Chart** folder holds **Show Star Chart**, a **Color Scheme** dropdown, and a **Satellite Track** toggle that plots the *Satellite to Track* across the chart with boxed local-time labels and a direction arrow, clipped at the horizon. The chart renders for the current frame time and look-camera location using J2000 planet coordinates so it matches `CPlanets` and the 3D sky; redraws are skipped via a dependency signature (time, observer, size, scheme, track endpoints). `a8c68b22` then made the printable **White** scheme the default and renamed the pale-blue heavens-above.com-style scheme to **Lavendar** (spelled that way in the UI) — the shipped dropdown reads *Lavendar / Black / Night Vision / White*.
- **BotBench — bulk traverse analysis over many files** (`eb698802`, new `src/analysis/BotBenchUI.js`, `BotBenchRunner.js`, `BotBenchIngest.js`, `src/TraverseBattery.js`, `src/MISBSightline.js`; `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `src/analysis/AnalyzeVideoFile.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeLOSTrackMISB.js`). **File → File Analysis → "BotBench..."**, beside **Analyze Video FMV Data...**: point it at a folder of BOT interchange scenarios or MISB FMV clips and it runs the shipping traverse analysis on each in turn, filling a table as results land. Each row keeps its full results object, so **Gallery** opens the same candidate gallery the Analyze button produces with no recomputation and **Report** builds the same HTML report on demand. The fit battery moved out of `AnalyzeTraverse.runTraverseAnalysis` into `TraverseBattery.js` with every scene-coupled piece injected (`buildHypotheses`, `sampleWindPrior`, `searchSatellites`, `kalmanNoise`, `afterHypotheses`, `familyScreen`, `groundPrior`), so the live button and BotBench drive the same code — verified byte-identical on the live path by FNV hash over every hypothesis track, residual, sweep grid and provenance field, A/B against the pre-extraction build. `MISBSightline.js` holds the platform/gimbal rotation order extracted from `CNodeLOSTrackMISB`, so a caller with MISB rows and no node graph builds the identical sightline (`bf2bac0b` removed accidental angle defaults the extraction had introduced — a missing angle is an UNKNOWN attitude, not a level one, so live behavior is preserved exactly; validation belongs to the caller, `BotBenchIngest.misbHasSightline`). The table has two column groups because they answer different questions: SOURCE is measured before any fit (frames, duration, sensor baseline, LOS sweep, CV-family rcond, and pointing noise estimated from the sightlines themselves — 1.01× the declared sigma across the 16 white-noise files of the shipped 19-scenario set, with the 3 correlated-wobble files at 0.37× excluded from the ratio rather than pooled into a false claim); ANALYSIS is what the run concluded. Ranking is blind: `rankAllHypotheses` is called with `{useTruth: false}` explicitly, because with truth attached the "top interpretation" was being chosen by the same truth it was then scored against (median relative separation read 0.036; the honest figure is 0.455). Continuity is judged on timestamps, not array adjacency; ground sits at Z=0 for a BOT scenario but at the geoid for FMV; the range anchor is fixed across a run so the bracket is never a function of the answer; and what a bulk run cannot produce is listed in `ABSENT_HYPOTHESES` and printed with every result, so a missing interpretation is never read as negative evidence. The gallery's **Use exact result** is disabled for bulk rows with an `applyDisabledReason` explaining itself — the track is in some other file's local ENU frame at its own epoch, and applying it would silently corrupt the loaded sitch (`bf2bac0b`). `AnalyzeVideoFile` gained an opt-in `includeMisb` that returns the decoded MISB records (note in code: `MISB.UnixTimeStamp` is MICROSECONDS from KLV; the CSV importers write milliseconds into the same column).
- **BotBench ingest fails closed, with a verified clock** (`f4419c37` → `c122100c` series, `bf2bac0b`, `920d584c`, `d386246b`; `src/analysis/BotBenchIngest.js`, new `src/analysis/BotBenchClock.js`, `tests/BotBenchClock.test.js`). The FMV timebase is chosen on QUALITY, not finiteness — a wall clock can be finite on every record and still jumping, and PES PTS repeats when several KLV records ride one packet — so each clock is scored for strict increase and regularity and the wall clock wins ties only because it alone carries an epoch (cadence from PES PTS, epoch only ever from `UnixTimeStamp`). The encoder clock is verified against the wall clock step-by-step before being trusted; the clock logic lives in `BotBenchClock.js` with its own regression suite, whose rules landed one honest correction at a time: a rate is a span-weighted mean, not a median and not an average of anchor pairs (the shipped Truck.klv had been read 4.1 s short); outlier rejection must not fail open and must not centre on a mean; a majority of intervals must agree, with steps rejected before weighting, intervals measured from a stale anchor dropped, a stalled clock distinguished from one that jumped backward, and trust finally measured as COVERAGE of the record, not interval count. A declared cadence must agree with the timestamps in BOTH directions — the gap test alone accepts a stream arriving twice as fast, which then replays at nominal rate and halves every speed; on disagreement the timestamps win, because they are the data, and the conflict is reported. A BOT sidecar must STATE its frame, not merely avoid contradicting it (missing `frame.type`, a non-1.x `specVersion`, or a coercible-string origin previously sailed through on v1 ENU defaults it never claimed); a sidecar that exists but does not parse is refused naming the parse error rather than silently falling back to the shipped set's default origin; a Time column that never advances is refused on both paths rather than having the declared FPS applied to a dead clock. Sparse truth backfills BEFORE its first valid sample as well as holding after the last (a rewrite had dropped the backfill half, plotting a dive to the ENU origin); `Math.max(...array)` spreads were replaced with loops, since past ~65k elements they throw RangeError and any FMV clip over about half an hour at 30 fps crashed ingestion; the row and CSV record which Earth model was actually in force; per-row option sets keep a mixed-anchor batch honest in the exports; and a verdict whose top candidate contradicts the file's declared MaxRange no longer counts as "Resolved".

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed "Open Full Report" throwing for any traverse analysis whose fixed-wing fit failed** (`920d584c`, `d386246b`, `buildReportHTML` in `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`). `fitAircraft` is wrapped in a try/catch that records a typed failure and leaves `aircraft` null, but `buildReportHTML` dereferenced `aircraft.params`, `.series`, `.track`, `.errDeg` and `.runs` unconditionally — so the gallery's **Open Full Report** button threw deterministically, with no way to read the other candidates. A pre-existing fault on the live button; a bulk BotBench run just meets it far more often. Each site now degrades: the time-series charts drop the aircraft series, the plan view drops the aircraft track, the chart caption stops claiming to show "the aircraft fit", and the empty runs table is replaced by prose saying the fit returned no solution and pointing at the recorded reason — an empty table reads as "the search found nothing interesting", which is a different statement. The report HTML is also no longer cached on the results object: it is ~10 MB, the window it is written into keeps its own copy, and the cached second copy was pinned for as long as the results lived — for a bulk run, one per file for the whole session.
- **Fixed Point Track coming back enabled on every reload of a saved sitch** (`4703ff6f`, `src/CObjectTracking.js`). `serializeAutoTracking()` wrote the tracked positions and parameters but not whether the tracker was switched on: disabling leaves the tracker alive with its `trackedPositions` intact, so a "disabled" save serialized identically to an enabled one, and `deserializeAutoTracking()` called `enable()` unconditionally for any non-null data — disabling was therefore unsaveable as long as a track existed. The `enabled` flag is now serialized and honoured on load; saves predating the flag have no field, and a missing value means true — the behaviour those saves were made under (the **Enable/Disable Point Track** menu label follows the restored state). Companion fix: `createOverlay()` seeded the cursor to the middle of the video unconditionally on first call, harmless only because `enable()` ran it just before the deserializer overwrote the position — a track loaded disabled does not create its overlay until the user's first enable, long after the restore, so the seeding would have discarded the saved position. A `trackPositionSet` flag now guards the seeding — deliberately not a keyframe count, since **Clear from Here** can empty the track without touching the cursor.

## Version 2.109.1 (2026-07-30)

### New Features
- **Satellite context menu — *Sat Track 1* / *Sat Track 2*** (`e58c806e`, `src/nodes/CNodeView3DMouse.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeSatelliteTrack.js`, `src/i18n/en.js`). `showCelestialObjectMenu()` (reached from `onContextMenuInner`, i.e. right-click in a 3D view) already showed a satellite's NORAD number and name as read-only rows; it now also adds two action rows for `celestialObject.type === 'satellite'`, labelled from the new i18n keys `view3d.celestial.satTrack1` / `satTrack2` ("Sat Track 1" / "Sat Track 2"). They point the `satelliteTrack` / `satelliteTrack2` nodes — the **Satellite to Track** and **Satellite to Track 2** fields in the **Satellites** menu, defined in `data/custom/SitCustom.js` — at the clicked satellite. The assignment logic that lived inline in the text field's `onFinishChange` is extracted to `CNodeSatelliteTrack.setSatelliteText(nameOrNumber)`, so the menu items take exactly the same path as typing a name in by hand: `checkSatelliteTrackValid()`, the "*x* not found" fallback, `updateUI()`, `recalculateCascade()` and `setRenderOne()` — meaning the track, the camera/target track switches and the **Contents** folder all update. The menu passes `celestialObject.number` (the NORAD number) rather than the name, because TLE names are not unique (SAPPHIRE is both 26932 and 39088) and a name lookup returns the first match, so clicking one satellite could have started tracking another; `setSatelliteText()` resolves the number and writes the resolved *name* back into the field, so the field still reads e.g. "SEASAT 1". Each slot is looked up with `NodeMan.get(nodeID, false)` and skipped when absent, so sitches that do not define the satellite track nodes get no extra rows.

### Improvements
- **Slider drags use pointer capture and are bound to the one input that started them** (`7d4b468a` plus uncommitted review follow-ups, `src/js/lil-gui.esm.js`, `NumberController._initSlider`). The mouse path is rewritten from `mousedown` + window-level `mousemove`/`mouseup` to `pointerdown` + `this.$slider.setPointerCapture(e.pointerId)` with `pointermove`/`pointerup`/`pointercancel` on the slider itself. Note this is **not** what makes a drag track off the slider — the old window-level listeners already did that anywhere inside the document; what capture adds is that delivery is bound to one pointer id and routed to the slider directly, so it cannot be stolen or suppressed by whatever the cursor happens to be over (see the floating-panel entry below). The drag remains bounded by the edge of the screen: once the OS pins the cursor there `clientX` stops changing and a wrapping slider stops wrapping.
