Pointer Lock would remove that bound but Chrome answers every lock outside fullscreen with an unsuppressible "to show your cursor press Esc" bubble, so it was tried and deliberately not used — **do not describe these sliders as unbounded or off-screen draggable, and do not claim the drag survives leaving the browser window; that was asserted from the spec, never tested, and the old path very likely did the same.** `pointerdown` returns early for `e.pointerType === 'touch'` (touch keeps going through `touchstart`, which is the path that can distinguish a slide from a scroll — committing a value on touch-down would move the slider under anyone trying to scroll the menu) and early-returns while `dragging || touchDragging`, so a second pen or mouse press cannot take over `dragPointerId`; `pointermove`/`pointerup` ignore other pointer ids. The review follow-ups make the teardown safe: `endDrag()` is split out of `pointerUp` and runs *before* `_callOnFinishChange()`, so a throwing callback cannot strand the listeners or the `lil-gui-dragging` body class; the listeners and `dragging`/`dragPointerId` are claimed *before* the first `setValueFromX()`, whose `onChange` can rebuild the menu or throw; `document`-level `lostpointercapture` and window `pointerup`/`pointercancel` backstops are registered in the **capture phase** (bubble-phase `stopPropagation` exists elsewhere in Sitrec — `blockViewEvents()` in `src/DragResizeUtils.js:368` and `Chart3D.js:291` — and would otherwise suppress cleanup) to cover the slider being removed from the DOM mid-drag, which silences its own listeners; and `setPointerCapture` is wrapped in try/catch, aborting the drag via `endDrag()` when it throws (slider already detached), because the element's bounding rect would then be all zeros and `setValueFromX()` would map the press to an endpoint or `NaN`. Verified in Chrome with trusted events (Playwright): the drag keeps tracking with the pointer off the slider but inside the window; the screen-edge bound is real; a second pen or mouse press cannot steer or end a drag; and the teardown holds both when `onChange`/`onFinishChange` throw and when the slider is removed from the DOM mid-drag.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed the wrap carry on drag counting only ±1 per move instead of every slider width crossed** (`7d4b468a`, `src/js/lil-gui.esm.js`, `setValueFromX`). The date/time chain in `src/nodes/CNodeDateTime.js` (`guiMenus.time`, the **Time** menu) is a ladder of `wrap()` receivers — ms → Second → Minute → Hour → Day → Month → Year — and the old carry was a sign heuristic (`this.deltaX > 0 && newValue < oldValue` ⇒ +1) that could only ever move the receiver by one. A single move can cross the end of the slider several times — a fast flick, or a `pointermove` the browser coalesced — so a quick spin of *Second* left *Minute* running behind it. The carry is now `wrapIndex(clientX) - wrapIndex(clientX - this.deltaX)`, where `wrapIndex()` counts whole `sliderWidth`s past `rect.left`/`rect.right` and deliberately mirrors the branch tests of the value mapper above it (`x > rect.right`, `x < rect.left`), so its steps land exactly where the mapped value jumps; the ends are inclusive, so `clientX === rect.right` is index 0 — still an un-wrapped `_max` that does not carry. Guarded by `Number.isFinite(this.deltaX)`. Note this affects only wrapping sliders that were given a **receiver**: `CNodeControllerPTZUI.js:144`'s *Pan (Az)* and `CNodeWind.js:48`'s *Wind From* call `.wrap()` with no argument, so their value still wraps but there is nothing to carry into. Verified in Chrome with trusted events (Playwright): a three-slider-width move carries +3 days, and `clientX` exactly `=== rect.right` carries 0.
- **Fixed touch drags never carrying into the slider above them, and a touch inheriting a stale carry from the last mouse drag** (`7d4b468a`, `src/js/lil-gui.esm.js`). The touch path never maintained `this.deltaX`, which the wrap carry reads: on a touchscreen with no prior mouse drag it was `undefined` (no carry ever), and after a mouse drag it held that drag's leftover value, letting a touch invent a carry it never made. `onTouchStart` now seeds `this.prevDragX`/`this.deltaX = 0` at finger-down, `beginTouchDrag(e, touch)` measures from that origin rather than from itself — important in a scrollable container, where it runs on the *first* `touchmove` after the scroll/slide test, so the finger has already travelled and that first step has to carry like any other — and `onTouchMove` updates `deltaX`/`prevDragX` on every move. Verified with CDP-synthesised touch events: a touch drag now wraps and carries +1.
- **Fixed a second finger ending or aborting a slider drag** (`7d4b468a`, `src/js/lil-gui.esm.js`). The touch handlers are on `window` and saw every contact: `onTouchEnd` fired on *any* `touchend`, so lifting an unrelated second finger ended the drag, and during the scroll/slide test `onTouchMove` read `e.touches[0]`, so a second finger moving while the tracked one was still produced `dx === dy === 0`, which the test read as a vertical scroll and used to abort a drag that had not started yet. The drag now records `Touch.identifier` in `touchDragId` at `touchstart` and resolves it out of `e.changedTouches` via `findDragTouch()`, so only the contact that started the drag can steer or end it — verified: a second finger can neither steer nor end a drag. The touch and pointer paths also now exclude each other (`if (dragging || touchDragging) return`), as they share `prevDragX`/`deltaX` and the value itself. `touchcancel` is handled and `endTouchDrag()` is factored out (running before `_callOnFinishChange()`), so the new per-drag flags cannot be stranded by a cancelled touch or a throwing callback and lock the slider out of all future drags.
- **Fixed a slider staying stuck to the pointer when the button was released over a floating panel** (`7d4b468a`, `src/js/lil-gui.esm.js`). **Derived from the code, not reproduced** — recorded here for future debugging, and deliberately kept out of the user-facing changelog for that reason. The old `mouseup` listener sat on `window` in the bubble phase, and `blockViewEvents()` (`src/DragResizeUtils.js:368`) registers bubble-phase `e.stopPropagation()` for `mouseup` on Sitrec's floating panels — the EXIF info panel (`EXIFInfoPanel.js:228`), the track filter dialog (`TrackFilterDialog.js:217`), and the script timeline's own note at `ScriptTimelineWidget.js:350` records hitting exactly this problem — so a release over one of them would never reach the window handler: `mouseMove` would stay installed, `_setDraggingStyle(false)` and `_callOnFinishChange()` would never run, and the slider would go on following the mouse with no button held. With pointer capture the `pointerup` is delivered to the slider itself, bypassing the panel entirely, and the window-level backstops are registered in the capture phase for the same reason.
- **Fixed spline control-point markers sitting at an arbitrary tilt away from lat/lon 0,0** (`83e56af2`, `src/PointEditor.js`, `src/SplineEditor.js`). The render frame is ECEF, so a control-point cube left at the default identity rotation is aligned to the Earth-centred axes and only looks level near 0,0 — at Aguadilla it sat at an arbitrary tilt. It used to look right because the render frame was a local EUS tangent plane centred on the sitch, and nothing was updated when that changed. New `PointEditor.updateCubeOrientations()` builds each cube's basis from the ellipsoid normal at its **own** position (`getLocalUpVector`, the same way `updatePositionIndicator()` orients the altitude cone) crossed with the local direction of travel, via `basis.makeBasis(right, up, forward)` with `right = up × forward` so the matrix is a pure rotation (determinant +1, not a mirror), then multiplies in a 45° `cornerTwist` about local up so the track runs through two opposite corners instead of through the middle of two faces (a cube has 90° symmetry about that axis, so ±45° are the same orientation). Heading is horizontalised by `forward.addScaledVector(up, -forward.dot(up))`; a vertical-only segment or a lone point falls back to `getLocalNorthVector`, and — because that returns zero at the poles, which `SplineInterchange` can reach since it rejects only `Math.abs(lat) > 90` — then to whichever cardinal axis is not parallel to up, avoiding a singular basis and a non-unit quaternion. Direction comes from the curve that is actually drawn: `SplineEditor.getControlPointDirection()` overrides the base chord with `spline.getTangent(i / (n-1))`, because the tangent of a chordal or centripetal Catmull-Rom is *not* the chord between a point's neighbours (only uniform parametrisation makes those equal; on the Aguadilla ground spline they differ by up to 16.6°, median 1.8°). In `'linear'` mode the base chord is kept, since that class interpolates control points directly and is exact there. Orientation is view-independent so it runs on change, unlike `updateCubeScales()` which stays in the render loop. Verified on the Aguadilla ground spline (33 points, chordal): tilt from local up 0.0000°, track-to-corner 0.0000°, track-to-edge 45.0000°, quaternion norm error 2.2e-16; both poles finite and unit.
- **Fixed the drawn spline line and the curve it reports drifting apart when control points moved as a group** (`83e56af2`, `src/PointEditor.js`, `src/SplineEditor.js`). Refresh now runs through a new `refreshEditingGraphics()` hook called from `load()`, `updateSnapping()`, `adjustUp()` and `updatePointEditorGraphics()`, rather than only from the drag path. `updateSnapping()` (called inside `CNodeSplineEdit.recalculate()`, which then immediately samples `getPoint()`) and `adjustUp()` both move every control point and previously synced nothing, so the cubes moved while the curve behind `getPoint()` stayed on the pre-move points, and the rotations went stale — 44° stale after a 400 m move. The old `SplineEditor.load()` override synced `localPositions` but never rebuilt the mesh, leaving the drawn line up to 398 m from what `getPoint()` reported. `SplineEditor.refreshEditingGraphics()` now does `syncLocalPositions()` → `super.refreshEditingGraphics()` (orientations, read off the now-current tangent) → `updateSplineMesh()` in that fixed order, so the curve, the orientations and the visible line cannot disagree; `updateSplineMesh()` is split out of `updatePointEditorGraphics()` and the old `load()` and `updatePointEditorGraphics()` overrides are gone. The hook deliberately does **not** set `this.dirty`: `CNodeSplineEdit.update()` kicks off another `recalculateCascade()` whenever dirty is set, so setting it inside a path that runs from `recalculate()` would schedule a fresh recalculate every frame forever.

## Version 2.109.0 (2026-07-29)

### New Features
- **WESCAM MX gimbal OSD overlay** (`9e373936`, new `src/nodes/CNodeWescamMXUI.js`, `src/CustomSupport.js`, `src/CustomManagerSetup.js`). `CNodeWescamMXUI extends CNodeViewUI`, modelled on `CNodeMQ9UI`: an MX-15/MX-20 style character-grid display drawn over the look view. The grid is 80 x 37 cells, taken from measuring a real 2670x1494 MX frame (~33 x 40 px cells), and it is anchored to the **video rectangle** rather than the whole view — `renderCanvas` reads `dx/dy/dWidth/dHeight` from the `mirrorVideo` (else `video`) view via `getSourceAndDestCoords()`, exactly as `CNodeMQ9UI` does, and falls back to the look view clamped to 16:9 when there is no video. A picture narrower than `REF_ASPECT` (16:9) pulls the blocks *in* towards the centre (`boxW * (aspect / REF_ASPECT)`) and shrinks the font and row pitch with them, so compressed columns never overlap. Colours come from `getHUDColor()` / `getHUDColor(0.55)`, so the overlay follows the global **HUD Color** control (`setupHUDColor(guiMenus.view)` in `index.js`); grid entries tagged `'#888888'` are the fields Sitrec does not simulate and are drawn dimmed as static placeholders (`AUTO`, `VIC`, `EON`, `COL`, `LI:DISARM`, `LI:LOW`, `MAN`, `NONE`, `CENT`, `LOW`), the same convention `CNodeMQ9UI` uses. Live fields: date / time / UTC offset from `GlobalDateTimeNode` (shifted by `getTimeZoneOffset()` and read back with `getUTC*`, matching `CNodeDateTime`'s own display conversion); a 35mm-equivalent focal length derived from the camera's vertical FOV as `(sensorHeightMM/2) / tan(vFOV/2)` with `sensorHeightMM` defaulting to 24 and overridable per sitch; ACFT lat/lon in truncated DMS, heading and altitude; and TGT lat/lon/altitude with LOS bearing and slant range in NM. The target position is `targetTrackSwitchSmooth` if the sitch has one, else the LOS/terrain intersection via `TerrainModel.getClosestIntersect`, else `intersectSurface` on the reference ellipsoid; with none of those the TGT and LOS fields read `-`.
- **Tape and needle conventions, measured rather than assumed** (`src/nodes/CNodeWescamMXUI.js`). The **azimuth tape is airframe-relative** (`cameraHeadingDeg - airframeHeadingDeg`) while the **elevation tape is absolute below the local horizon** (`getAzElFromPositionAndForward(camera.position, forward)[1]`, i.e. `90 - angleTo(localUp)`), because the MX gimbal is inertially stabilised and its elevation readout references the stabilised horizon, not the airframe. Checked against a real frame: 10737 ft over a 1663 ft target at 4.4 NM is 19.8 degrees of depression, and the tape's centre pointer sits on `-02`. Both tapes have a **fixed centre pointer and scroll past it**, labelled in tens of degrees with a minor tick every 5; scales are `AZ_WIDTH_PER_10DEG = 0.042` / `AZ_HALF_WINDOW = 0.10` of box width and `EL_HEIGHT_PER_10DEG = 0.074` / `EL_HALF_WINDOW = 0.095` of box height. The **north needle is a flat compass needle driven by azimuth alone** and deliberately does *not* project the north vector through the camera — measured on a real MX frame at azimuth 193 / depression 19.8, a true 3D projection puts the needle 146 degrees clockwise from up, azimuth-only puts it at 167, and the frame shows about 164.
- **Airframe heading derivation, with an honest "no heading" state** (new `src/AirframeHeading.js`, new `tests/AirframeHeading.test.js`). `airframeHeadingFromVelocity(position, groundVelocity, windVector)` returns degrees true, or **`null` when the track gives no usable direction of travel**; the node then prints `---°` in ACFT HDG and skips `drawAzimuthTape` entirely rather than drawing a tape around a fabricated bearing. It is split out of the node purely so Jest can load it — `CNodeWescamMXUI` pulls in `three/addons` through `threeExt`, which the test environment cannot. Two decisions carry the correctness: (1) **everything works on the horizontal component**, because heading is a horizontal quantity — testing full 3D speed would let a purely vertical track (a balloon climbing) through the guard, and `getCompassHeading` projects onto the horizontal plane, so a vertical vector projects to zero and `atan2(0,0)` hands back a fabricated `000`; (2) the speed gate is tested **before** the wind is removed, because a fixed camera and an aircraft holding station in wind produce identical (zero) ground tracks and the wind-corrected direction cannot tell them apart — the Custom sitch's default camera is a fixed one, so with a ~1.2 m/frame `localWind` this previously read as a confident heading that was really the wind direction. A wind that exactly cancels the ground track is rejected by the same test applied a second time. Threshold `MIN_HORIZONTAL_SPEED_SQ = 0.001` m²/frame² = 0.0316 m/frame, under 2 knots at 30 fps. 13 unit tests cover the cardinal headings, climbing-while-travelling, crosswind (`atan2(-2,5)` = 338.2), every null case, and non-mutation of the caller's vectors.
- **Altitudes are MSL, not HAE** (`altitudeMSLFeet` in `src/nodes/CNodeWescamMXUI.js`, `meanSeaLevelOffset` from `src/EGM96Geoid.js`). An MX reads feet above mean sea level; Sitrec positions are ECEF, so `ECEFToLLAVD_radii` gives an ellipsoidal height and the geoid separation is taken back off — `(lla.z - meanSeaLevelOffset(lla.x, lla.y)) * 3.28084` — for both the ACFT and TGT readouts, the same way `CNodeLabels3D` and `CNode3DObject` report altitude. This is not cosmetic: over Baja California the EGM96 separation is about -34 to -38 m, so an uncorrected readout would sit ~112-125 ft low. The grid is always resident by first render because `SituationSetup.js` awaits `ensureGeoidLoaded()` before any node is built.
- **Created in setup, not in the sitch definition, so existing saves get it** (`CCustomManager.setupWescamMXUI()` in `src/CustomSupport.js`, called from `CustomManagerSetup.js`). `MQ9UI` is a node in `data/custom/SitCustom.js`; `WescamMXUI` deliberately is not, because the Custom sitch is fully serialized and a node added to the definition would never reach a sitch a user had already saved. Instead the hook runs inside `CCustomManager.setup()`, which `index.js` invokes for **every** `Sit.isCustom || Sit.canMod` sitch, guarded by `NodeMan.exists("WescamMXUI")` and by the presence of `lookView` + `lookCamera`. The consequence to know: that hook runs **before `Sit.setup()`**, so sitches that build their tracks there (SitAguadilla's `jetTrack`) have none at construction time — hence `cameraTrack` and `target` are optional inputs resolved **lazily on first render**, with `findCameraTrackID()` walking `["cameraTrackSwitchSmooth", "cameraTrackSwitch", "jetTrack", "cameraTrack"]`, the same candidate chain and rationale as the wind fit in `CustomManagerSetup.js` (the smoothed switch first, because noise suppression steadies the heading). Note the ACFT lat/lon/alt come from `camera.position` while the heading comes from that resolved track, so a camera not riding its track will show the two from different sources. The view is created with `relativeTo: "lookView"` (not `overlayView`) and no `excludeFromViewsMenu`, which is what puts it in **Show → Views** via `CNodeView`'s `guiShowHideViews.add(this, 'visible')`; `passThrough: true` keeps pointer events going to the 3D view underneath, and `visible` is in `toSerialCNodeView` so a user's show/hide choice persists in a saved sitch. **No i18n key was added** — `name = v.menuName ?? this.id`, so the Views entry is currently labelled with the raw node id `WescamMXUI`; adding a `menuName` (and an `en.js` key) is the obvious follow-up.

## Version 2.108.1 (2026-07-29)

### Security
- **`webpack-dev-server` declared floor raised `^5.2.4` → `^5.2.6`** (`package.json`, `package-lock.json`). This was the last direct dependency range that still *admitted* a vulnerable version: `webpack-dev-server` <= 5.2.5 carries GHSA-f5vj-f2hx-8m93 / CVE-2026-14620 (CSRF via internal developer endpoints) and GHSA-m28w-2pqf-7qgj / CVE-2026-14631 (denial of service via a malformed `Host` or `Origin` header), both medium, both first patched in 5.2.6. **No installed version changes** — `package-lock.json` already resolved `node_modules/webpack-dev-server` to 5.2.6, which is why `npm audit` never flagged it; the only edited line in the lockfile is the root `packages[""].devDependencies` mirror of the `package.json` range. The lockfile pin was therefore an incidental guarantee rather than a structural one: any fresh install that regenerated or discarded the lock could legally resolve back to 5.2.4/5.2.5. Raising the floor makes it structural. **Scope: nothing user-facing, and no `src/` file is touched.** `webpack-dev-server` is a devDependency executed only by `npm start` (`npx webpack serve --config webpack.dev.js`) and the contributor-only `docker-compose.dev.yml` / `Dockerfile.dev` path — `npm run build` uses the same `webpack.dev.js` config but invokes plain `npx webpack`, so the package is never loaded, and the production `Dockerfile` is multi-stage with the final `php:8.4-apache` image copying only `/build/dist` out of the `node:22` build stage, so `node_modules` and this package never reach the shipped image at all. Absent from `dist/`, `dist-serverless/` and the desktop runtime; both advisories require an attacker to reach a developer's *running* dev server. Hence no entry in `docs/WhatsNew.md`. Secondary motivation for shipping it as its own release: GitHub's dependency-graph SBOM for the repository is stale — it still reports pre-2.107.0 versions (`sharp` 0.34.5 vs 0.35.3 actual, `fast-uri` 3.1.2 vs 3.1.4, `hono` 4.12.25 vs 4.12.32, `app-builder-lib` 26.8.1 vs 26.15.3), so the open Dependabot alerts describe versions that are not installed. Every one was semver-checked against the committed lockfiles and is already patched by `55f9950b` (shipped in 2.107.0); a manifest-touching commit is the trigger that gets the graph re-parsed. Still not fixed, unchanged from the 2.107.0 note: GHSA-mh99-v99m-4gvg (`brace-expansion` unbounded-expansion OOM DoS, range `<=5.0.7`). `npm audit` continues to report `fixAvailable: false` — the 1.x and 2.x lines top out at 1.1.16 / 2.1.2 with no patched release, and 5.0.8 is not a drop-in — and it reaches the tree only through dev/test tooling (jest → `test-exclude`/`glob` → `minimatch`). It accounts for the whole of the current `npm audit` result (19 high, all one cluster), which is what a "clean" audit looks like for this repo.

## Version 2.108.0 (2026-07-28)

### New Features
- **Spline interchange format — hand-drawn tracks as droppable files** (`dce67b9d`, `5823b7f0`, new `src/SplineInterchange.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeSplineEdit.js`, `src/TrackManager.js`, `src/CFileManagerParse.js`, `docs/Tracks.md`, `tests/SplineInterchange.test.js`). A hand-authored solution used to be reachable only by hard-coding an `initialPoints` array into a `Sit*.js` — the legacy Aguadilla Lantern/UAP splines are exactly that. `.spline.json` is the droppable equivalent: `{fileType: "sitrec-spline", version, name, sourceSitch, fps, frames, curveType, constantSpeed, extrapolateTrack, altitudeLock, altitudeLockAGL, altitudeDatum: "HAE", columns: ["frame","lat","lon","alt"], points, color?}`. Points are **geodetic** `[frame, lat, lon, alt]` rather than the raw ECEF arrays the old sitches embed, so a file survives a change of Earth model or sitch origin; altitude is HAE so the round trip needs no geoid lookup and is exact. Written by `CNodeSplineEditor.exportSplineJSON()` (via `makeSplineJSON`), surfaced as an **Export Spline** button in two places — the synthetic track's own folder under **Contents** (`t("trackManager.exportSpline")`, new i18n key `trackManager.exportSpline`) and the spline editor's `guiMenus.physics` folder — both of which write the same file, because `exportSplineJSON` lives on the unsmoothed spline node where the control points actually are. `importSplineJSON` routes through `TrackManager.addSyntheticTrack()`, so an imported spline is indistinguishable from one made with **Add Track**: editable control points, same folder, same controls. `isSplineJSON` is tested in `parseMethods` *before* `detectTrackFile` so the generic JSON track handlers cannot claim it. `validateSplineJSON` refuses a bad file by name — in particular **frame numbers must strictly increase**, because the spline's frame-to-curve mapping divides by the gap between adjacent control points and a repeated frame writes NaN into every frame of the track — and `curveType` must be one of `linear`/`catmull`/`centripetal`/`chordal`. Names go through `safeTrackName` (a file called `constructor.spline.json` is as dangerous as a `"name": "constructor"` field) and then `makeUniqueName` against `takenTrackNames()`, with the SAME uniqued value used for both `name` and `shortName` so a track displayed as "Lantern 2" cannot export itself as `Lantern.spline.json`. On success `fileManagerEntry.skipSerialization = true`: the control points now belong to the synthetic track, which `TrackManager.serialize` already saves, so keeping the file would import a second copy on every reload (same reasoning as FEATURES files). A rejected file is also marked `skipSerialization` — embedding a file that cannot be loaded would raise the same error on every future open, and would be an unsaved change the sitch was never marked dirty for.
- **FOV interchange format — camera zoom as editable keyframes** (`dce67b9d`, new `src/FOVInterchange.js`, new `src/FOVUtils.js`, `src/index.js`, `docs/Tracks.md`, `tests/FOVInterchange.test.js`). **Camera ▸ FOV (Zoom) ▸ Export for FOV Editor** samples whatever is *currently* driving the camera FOV — the selected Camera FOV source in a custom sitch, or the camera itself in a legacy sitch that computes zoom in code — over every frame, then reduces the samples to the fewest keyframes that reproduce them under the FOV Editor's linear interpolation to within `FOV_EPSILON_DEGREES` = 1e-4°. The reduction is an O(n) slope-interval fit (each straight run collapses to its two endpoints), and Aguadilla's 7028 per-frame samples come out as 12 keyframes replaying to within one ULP. An **instant** zoom step — a real camera changing discrete zoom levels — emerges as two keyframes one frame apart (e.g. `[6, 0.8], [7, 4]`), which under linear interpolation is a step and not a ramp. The button is registered in `initializeOnce()` alongside the permanent `cameraFOV` folder and marked `.perm()` so it survives sitch loads and is present in legacy sitches too — those, whose zoom track is locked up in code (Aguadilla reads a CSV column in a `preRenderFunction`), are precisely what it is for. `importFOVJSON` feeds the keyframes into `fovEditor` (`CNodeCurveEditor2`), rescales the editor's y axis to the data (the default 0..40 renders Aguadilla's sub-degree track as a flat line pinned to the bottom), and then **selects "FOV Editor" on `fovSwitch`** — without that the import appears to do nothing. It refuses to reinterpret: `units` must be `degrees`, `axis` `vertical`, `interpolation` `linear`, since reading a radians/horizontal/stepped file as degree-linear-vertical yields a plausible but wrong zoom track; a file with no `fovEditor` in the sitch errors with "Load it into a custom sitch". Keyframe frame numbers are absolute, so a frame-count mismatch against `Sit.frames` logs a warning. `extractFOV` moved from `CNodeControllerVarious.js` to a new leaf module `src/FOVUtils.js` (importing only `MISBFields` and `assert`) because `CNodeControllerVarious` reaches `three/addons` via `threeExt` and so cannot be imported from a Jest-tested module; it is re-exported from its old home, so no call site changed.
- **MX-style (WESCAM) azimuth-elevation turret camera roll** (`dce67b9d`, new `src/TurretRoll.js`, new `src/nodes/CNodeControllerMXRoll.js`, `src/CustomSupport.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeControllerCameraBankRoll.js`, `tests/TurretRoll.test.js`). Sitrec's existing pod model is a Raytheon ATFLIR — a **roll-nod** gimbal whose first axis is the aircraft's *longitudinal* (nose-tail) axis. A WESCAM MX ball turret is an **az-el** mount: its first axis is the aircraft's *vertical*, which tilts with bank and pitch, and because the turret is bolted to the airframe the rotations compound, so the visible image roll is the combined result of jet bank/pitch AND turret azimuth/elevation — not the bank alone. The previous approximation (`CNodeControllerCameraBankRoll`, **Roll View with Bank**) applies only the bank; that happens to agree looking straight ahead and is wrong everywhere else, by up to ~92° of image roll for one banked, off-axis line of sight. `azElTurretImageRoll` derives the roll coordinate-free from three cross products: for an az-el mount, camera-right has zero component along the aircraft's vertical, so image "up" always lies in the plane containing the line of sight and the aircraft's vertical — the same thing you see turning your head in a cockpit — which makes the turret's image roll identical to the human-horizon angle at every azimuth and elevation (verified against the roll-nod chain, against `getHumanHorizonFromPitchRollAzEl`, and against two independent derivations, agreeing to ~1e-6° over hundreds of attitudes). It is done coordinate-free deliberately: `getHumanHorizonFromPitchRollAzEl` and `extractRollFromMatrix` hard-code world up as `(0,1,0)`, valid only in the legacy flat local frame the `Jet*` code runs in, and the custom sitch renders in ECEF where up is `getLocalUpVector(position)`. Verified consequences: wings level and level pitch gives **exactly zero** roll at every azimuth/elevation (a ball turret in level flight needs no derotation; a roll-nod pod does), and looking abeam is zero *at zero elevation only* — depress past world-nadir (measured: null exactly at el −60 for bank 30 / az 90) and the roll flips to 180°, which is correct because the image really is inverted, but the "abeam is zero" rule must not be quoted without the elevation qualifier. `CNodeControllerMXRoll` (id `mxRollController`) drives the look camera from it, added to `lookCamera` **last** so it runs after `TrackToTrack`'s `lookAt()` leaves the horizon level for it to tilt. It takes no target input — the line of sight is read off wherever the heading controllers have already aimed the camera — which keeps it compatible with every heading mode (To Target, Manual, Celestial, Custom Az/El) instead of overriding them. It sets the pose absolutely so it is idempotent under repeated controller passes, and changes roll only. **Camera ▸ Heading** gains **Roll From MX-Style Camera** (`lookCameraMXRoll`, `CNodeGUIFlag`, default off) and **MX Roll Amount** (`lookCameraMXRollScale`, 0–1, default 1): 1 = an unstabilised two-axis turret (the full geometric roll), 0 = a perfectly roll-stabilised turret. That scale is not a convenience — what `TurretRoll.js` computes is the *un-derotated baseline* stabilisation is measured against, and a real MX-10 is an actively stabilised four-axis system, so the honest instruction is to set this by matching the horizon tilt in the actual video. Turning the flag **off** takes explicit work and the code says why: `CNodeControllerPTZUI.syncFromCamera` reads the rendered camera back into `ptzAngles.roll` after every pass, so once the camera has been rolled, *our* roll sits in `ptz.roll` and `TrackToTrack` keeps applying it forever with the controller disabled — and it has already overwritten the user's manual roll. Restoring `ptz.roll` alone does not work, because `apply()` runs before `syncFromCamera`, which re-reads the still-rolled camera; so on the enable→disable transition the controller runs its normal path once with an effective scale of zero to level the CAMERA, restoring the user's pre-takeover roll (using `H(M·Rz(d)) === H(M) − d`) rather than snapping to zero. The transition is gated on `objectNode.camera === NodeMan.get("lookCamera").camera`, because `CNodeLOSFromCamera` updates a DUMMY camera to sample sightlines and one of those passes consuming the disable transition would leave the real camera rolled forever; and the disable path deliberately needs nothing from the track, so a stationary track, the last frame, or singular geometry cannot strand it pending. The bank estimate itself was extracted from `CNodeControllerCameraBankRoll` into an exported `coordinatedTurnBankAngle(smoothedTrack, f)` (`bank = atan(angularVelocity · speed / g)`, positive = right wing down) so both controllers derive the aircraft's attitude from exactly the same signal — two different bank numbers driving one camera would be a subtle and very confusing bug. Its two failure outcomes are distinct and load-bearing, mirroring the original inline code exactly: `null` = no usable estimate (missing/duplicate samples), caller must leave the roll alone because snapping level would be a visible jolt; `0` = wide-baseline samples unavailable but the track is moving, so "no measurable turn" is honest and should be applied. Guards are written `!(x >= k)` rather than `x < k` so a NaN coordinate is rejected rather than sailing through into the camera pose.
- **Solution families — per-class range bands in the traverse analysis** (`276f36e7`, new `src/TraverseFamily.js`, `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `src/LOSFitting.js`, `src/Chart3D.js`, `docs/TraverseMethods.md`, `tests/TraverseFamily.test.js`, `tests/LOSFittingParamLocks.test.js`). LOS-only traverse is under-determined in a provable way: for **any** range function R(t), the path `S(t) + R(t)·D(t)` reproduces the sightlines exactly, so range is pinned only once target dynamics are imposed and then only as far as those dynamics constrain it. The gallery has always drawn ONE representative track per interpretation, which reads as a confident answer even when the evidence supports a wide family. Enabled from **Traverse ▸ Traverse Analysis Tweaks ▸ "Solution families (range bands)"** (`analyzeTweaks.solutionFamilies`, **default off** — it is several extra fits per model). `buildSolutionFamilies` traces one band per interpretation CLASS, on the independent member only: the free-wind balloon (the wind-pinned variant is conditioned on an external prior and would answer a different question), the free quadcopter (the drone-control fit is a seeded refinement of one path, not a family), and the fixed-wing fit. `buildRangeLadder` builds a log-spaced ladder of `DEFAULT_LADDER_POINTS` = 11 rungs over the searched bracket, clipped to the model's own `initialRange` envelope — a lantern cannot be fitted at 45 NM when its own bound stops at 30 km, and pretending otherwise reports an envelope limit as a data limit. The headline fit's own solved range (`anchorM`) is carried EXACTLY and this is load-bearing, not a nicety: the caller labels the anchor rung with the headline's track, so an anchor beyond the bracket EXTENDS the ladder and a near-duplicate collision resolves in the anchor's favour, never the evenly-spaced rung's — otherwise that track would be published under a distance it does not have. A bracket with no overlap at all with the model envelope returns an EMPTY ladder and the family is skipped, because a rung emitted anyway would pass straight through as a `paramLocks` value, which `fitPhysicsModel` writes into the parameter vector **without clamping** (the locked coordinate is removed from the search, so nothing else bounds it) and the model would be integrated outside its own envelope and published as an admitted range. `rangeConditionedFamily` marches the ladder continuation-seeded (each rung solved from its neighbour, far cheaper than a global search per rung) plus a global DE basin probe at BOTH ends because these landscapes are multimodal; if the probe finds a better basin the band is re-traced from there and the tile says so. `fitPhysicsModel` gained `options.paramLocks`, implemented as a **reduced search vector** rather than a zero-width bound, because Nelder-Mead maintains n+1 points spanning n-space and every move is a linear combination of existing vertices, so a simplex born flat on a zero-width bound stays flat forever. `fitAircraft` needed no change — DE and pattern search tolerate a zero-width dimension, and `assessBoundPins` already skips zero-span coordinates, so a deliberate lock can never be misreported as a load-bearing capability limit. `makeFamilyScreen` applies a physical screen on top of the LOS residual (`FAMILY_MAX_G` = 9, `FAMILY_MAX_SPEED_KT` = 900, plus `trackGroundStats` underground and ground-contact-mode checks) so a member that threads the sightlines perfectly while buried in a hillside or pulling 40 g does not widen a band meant to describe REAL solutions; the ceilings are the rank-0 boundaries `plausibilityRating` already uses, so an admitted member is one the gallery would not call kinematically extreme. Presentation: `hypothesisVolumeScene` draws admitted members as faint tracks in the hypothesis's own colour (alpha 0.28), pushed BEFORE the representative track so the headline overlays its family and the bundle reads as one interpretation with a width rather than competing answers; members that follow the rays but fail the physical screen are dashed and dimmer (alpha 0.14) — visible, because "the rays allow this and physics does not" is exactly what the analyst needs to see, but never mistakable for part of the answer. `Chart3D`'s `line` series gained an `alpha` field applied to the polyline AND its end markers so a faint member fades as one object. `hypothesisStats` adds a **"Range band (model-conditional)"** row deliberately next to the slant range (which is one solution's extent over the clip and says nothing about whether the distance was determined), worded "3.0–3.6 NM (2 of 12 sampled)" — "of N **sampled**", never "of N tested", and never a bare distance. `familyDetailHTML` states four cautions the design refuses to drop: the band is conditional on THIS model and is not a general uncertainty on the object's distance; the acceptance cut is empirical (relative to the model's own best fit — there is no calibrated sightline noise floor here); a band reaching the searched bracket's edge is a bound, not a result; and a re-seeded basin means bands elsewhere on the ladder may still be conservative. **Standing rule enforced in code and asserted in tests: a ladder is a SAMPLE.** A band edge is where the sampling changed answer, never a measured boundary, and only a rung that was evaluated and REJECTED can bracket one — so non-contiguous results report as SEPARATE intervals via `gapDisclosure` (which owns the wording so it can be unit-tested), distinguishing "every sampled rung in this gap was rejected" (those samples are excluded; the untested ground between them is not) from "a rung produced no fit" (untested, not ruled out — a failure to solve is not evidence the range is unavailable). Bands are **reporting only and never enter ranking** — a model with a tighter band does not sort higher, because "the more determined model wins" would be exactly the calibrated object-probability claim this analysis declines to make; a test in `tests/TraverseRanking.test.js` locks that. `analyzeTweaks.solutionFamilies` is folded into `computeAnalysisFingerprint` so toggling it cannot serve the other setting's cached results, and a compact audit record (counts, extents, acceptance cut, disjoint intervals, `basinReseeded`) is serialized into `manifest.solutionFamilies` — the member tracks live once, on the hypotheses. **K (band width) is not yet calibrated**: the smoke run gave truth coverage of 80% balloon / 40% fixed-wing / 0% quadcopter at K = 1.5, and picking the smallest K reaching 90% needs the pilot sweep.
- **BOT interchange files import as a sensor track with measured sightlines plus a truth track** (`215c196e`, `eb6ce808`, new `src/TrackFiles/CTrackFileBOT.js`, `src/CFileManagerParse.js`, `src/TrackFiles/CTrackFile.js`, `src/TrackManager.js`, `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `tests/BOTInterchangeImport.test.js`). The BOT interchange format (the bearings-only benchmark's published CSV/JSON release, spec in `benchmarks/botbench/BOT-Interchange-Format.html`) was revised to v1.1 — three folders per release rather than four loose files, with frame-neutral column names: `Input/` (`TrackID,TrackSource,Time,SensorPositionX/Y/Z,LOSUnitVectorX/Y/Z,MaxRange,LOSUncertainty`), `Truth/` (`TrackID,Time,TruePositionX/Y/Z`) and `All/` (the two joined column-wise, plus both JSON sidecars). `All/` is answer-key material, so a sealed release keeps it with `Truth/` and ships only `Input/`; the sealed-release test asserts the challenge tree contains no `All/` directory and no truth column. `Valid` and `MinRange` are gone (invalid frames moved to `scenario.json`; the range floor is the solver's business) and truth carries position only, with a direction-only target's columns empty and the bearings in `truth.json`. `scenario.json` gained `axisOrder`, because reading X/Y/Z as North/East mirrors the scene. Sitrec now reads all three shapes: `detectCSVType()` gains a `BOT_CSV` classification (`isBOTCSV`) and `CTrackFileBOT` emits a sensor track (role `camera`) whose MISB rows carry the sightlines as sensor azimuth/elevation — so `TrackManager` builds the `"<name> angles"` LOS through the existing path — and a truth track (role `target`). One scenario per file, which is what the format emits; a concatenated file keeps the first and says so. Two honest costs are documented in the class header rather than hidden: (1) the CSV carries **no georeference** — origin, datum and epoch live in the `scenario.json` sidecar, which this importer deliberately does not require, applying `BOT_DEFAULT_ORIGIN` (35°N, 125°W, ground elevation 0 — the site the shipped release is generated at) and `BOT_DEFAULT_EPOCH_ISO`, so a file generated elsewhere imports with correct SHAPE at the wrong place on the globe; and (2) BOT scenarios are generated on a **flat plane** (`altitude = Z + groundElevationMSL` at any horizontal distance) while Sitrec's world is the WGS84 ellipsoid — the spec's rule is honoured, so Z is read as altitude directly and only the horizontal offset derives lat/lon, making altitudes match the generator exactly at the cost of a straight line bending by the curvature term (X²+Y²)/2R ≈ 2 m at 5 km, 31 m at 20 km, 196 m at 50 km. Sightline directions are unaffected because they come from the LOS column rather than from differencing positions. The LOS column is expressed at the scenario origin but Sitrec applies az/el at the sensor, so it is re-based through ECEF — 0.45° at the 50 km cell, fifteen times the declared pointing noise. Verified in-app: the camera flies the measured bearings exactly (0.0000°) and misses truth by 0.046°, which is the noise the file declares. Four new `CTrackFile` predicates carry the semantics rather than inferring them from names: `anglesAreMeasurement(i)` (true for the Sensor sub-track) forces the file's own sightlines to be selected as the camera heading even in an established sitch, because otherwise the heading stays on "To Target", aims at the Truth track, and substitutes sightlines re-derived from the answer — every bearing then passes exactly through truth, the declared noise disappears, and a range fit run from that state is fitting its own input, a failure that is invisible because the picture looks *better* than the real one; `anglesSmoothing(i)` (0 for BOT, default 120) because `RollingAverage` shrinks its window symmetrically at the ends, so a 120-frame average over a 61-frame track collapses its middle to the mean of the whole track; `hasRedundantLOSReferenceTracks()` (default false, true only for STANAG's platform/ground pair); and `cpaCandidate(i)` (default `!isSupplementaryTrack(i)`, always false for BOT) so several independent primary tracks that merely share a coordinate frame stay primary and keep their platform models while never re-timing the sitch to a closest approach with no physical meaning. `TrackManager` gained matching pairing guards for role-declaring files: a file nominates BOTH a camera and a target so its pair describes ONE scenario and must stay together — dropping two such files at once interleaves their tracks, and the last write to each switch could otherwise come from a different file, leaving the camera on one scenario and the target on another's object, which looks perfectly normal on screen while every range readout and the traverse analysis measures a sensor against something it never saw. A role-declaring file supplying no target of its own therefore declines to take the camera from a file that already has a coherent camera+target pair, because `fixedTarget` is not an absence but a real point (measured at 667 km from the camera after a BOT import) that would quietly appear in every range and altitude readout. Separately (`eb6ce808`), `CTrackFile.isGroundTruthTrack(trackIndex)` (default false) lets a file declare that a track is the known ANSWER rather than a measurement of it, and `AnalyzeTraverse.fileDeclaredTruthTrackIDs()` auto-selects such a track in the **Truth Track** dropdown. Previously the dropdown auto-selected on a `/^truth_/i` test of the track's NAME, and a BOT sub-track is labelled "bot-0002 (Truth)", so it had to be picked by hand for every scenario. `CTrackFileBOT.isGroundTruthTrack` keys on the sub-track's KEY rather than its index, because truth is index 1 in an `.all` file and index 0 in a `.truth` file and an index test would quietly pick the Sensor in one of them. It is deliberately **not** `trackRoleHint("target")`: a STANAG target track is also the observed object, but its positions are somebody else's ESTIMATE, and auto-selecting it would score our analysis against another answer and report the difference as error — "what is this track for" and "does this file know the truth" are different questions. The name test is kept alongside the new predicate rather than replaced, since a name is a convention the user can break by renaming a track while the predicate is a statement by the importer, and neither subsumes the other.

### Improvements
- **The Truth Track is now applied only on request, via a "Use Truth Track" toggle** (`5823b7f0`, `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `src/TraverseRanking.js`). Selecting a truth track used to silently reorder and reword the whole gallery, which hides what the analysis concluded **on its own** — the thing being validated. `showResultGallery(results, uiState)` now defaults `useTruth` to false and adds a `tg-toggle` **Use Truth Track** button (shown only when `results.truth.usable`), with the header text stating which state it is in ("selected but NOT applied — candidates are in the ordinary screening order and no rank basis uses it" vs "APPLIED"). Toggling costs **no re-analysis**: `truthSortScore(x, useTruth)`, `makeComparator(crossCategory, useTruth)`, `rankHypotheses(h, {useTruth})`, `rankAllHypotheses`, `groupAndRankHypotheses` and `rankingExplanation` all take the flag, and the comparisons themselves were computed during the run and ride on the hypotheses — so each tile still shows its Truth Δ and the dashed pink truth line either way. The executive assessment strip follows the same gate (`!useTruth && results.executiveAssessment`), so a usable-but-unapplied truth leaves the ordinary evidence reasoning in force and the gallery matches the report. The gallery is rebuilt rather than patched because tile order, the Extras split and set-aside indices all move together; reader state is carried across by **hypothesis identity** (`dismissed: new Set(Array.from(dismissed, i => tiles[i].h))`, `selected`) because tile INDICES change with the ordering, and the selection is restored instead of jumping back to the top. Three teardown hazards are handled: the click is queued through `queueAnimation` (a set-aside in flight is two animation beats and only reaches `dismissed.add(i)` between them, so re-presenting synchronously would capture pre-click state and silently drop it); a `representing` latch refuses further tile actions once a re-present is queued; and `rerenderWithTruth` checks BOTH `removed` and `!overlay.isConnected` before rebuilding, because this is the one deferred action that CREATES DOM and a sitch reload would otherwise be followed by a gallery rebuilt from the previous sitch — in the detached-without-`remove()` case it runs the teardown it owns (keydown capture, resize handler, ResizeObserver, every `Chart3D`) rather than merely declining to re-show.
- **Sitch-built reference paths can be chosen as the Truth Track** (`5823b7f0`, `src/nodes/CNodeTrack.js`, `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `src/sitch/SitAguadilla.js`). `truthTrackOptions()` was sourced only from `TrackManager` data tracks, so a hand-authored reference path built by a sitch could never appear in the dropdown. A `CNodeTrack` can now opt in with `v.truthTrackCandidate` — a string to name the menu entry, or `true` to use its own `menuText`/`id` — and `NodeMan.iterate` adds those alongside the data tracks without ever shadowing a data track that already claimed the label. `resolveTruthTrack` falls back to such a node with `dataNode: null`, so `buildTruthReference` gets no start/end time and treats the whole analysis window as covered, which is correct here: a spline editor is defined over all of `Sit.frames`. Applied to the legacy Aguadilla sitch, whose hand-authored solutions are now selectable as **UAP Spline** and **Lantern Spline** (and whose three spline editors gained `menuText` so they export and display under readable names).
- **A fit pinned at one of its model's own limits is labelled "Not fully tested"** (`5823b7f0`, `plausibilityRating` in `src/TraverseRanking.js`, `tests/TraverseRanking.test.js`). A bound-pinned fit was labelled through `tierForRank`, whose words — "Moderate", "Low" — describe how ordinary the OBJECT's motion is. A pinned fit has not been judged on its motion at all: its search ran into a limit and stopped. That is the exact fit-vs-ordinariness confusion the comment above `plausibilityRating` exists to prevent, with pins as a third binding dimension that had no vocabulary of its own. Measured on the BOT Bench interchange set: bot-0009's balloon — the closest candidate to truth on that scenario at 0.037° residual — was badged "Moderate", and bot-0006's genuine high-altitude balloon was badged "Low". The rank cap (`capRank = pins.length >= 2 ? 1 : 2`) is unchanged, so **ordering and eligibility are identical** — only the label moves, and only when the pin is what binds (`if (result.rank > capRank)`). A model that also fits badly or requires extreme motion keeps "Poor fit" / "Kinematically extreme", because that is a real measured failure and the stronger statement; relabelling on every pin (as a first version of this did) would overwrite and bury those. Both directions are locked by tests.
- **The verdict strip discloses what was never modelled, and stops styling a non-result as an alert** (`5823b7f0`, `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `NOT_MODELLED_DISCLOSURE` in `src/TraverseRanking.js`). `"unresolved"` was rendered in amber — the same alert palette as a wind-evidence "tension" warning — while `"insufficient"` was neutral grey. Both are statements that nothing was concluded and neither is a finding about the object, so giving the alert styling to the one whose headline reads "no conventional model passes" ranked the more alarming-sounding of two non-results above the calmer and more informative one, and in a UAP tool that is the sentence most likely to be screenshotted and read as "unidentified". Both now use one `NEUTRAL` style (and so does the unknown-code fallback); amber is reserved for the tension strip, where it flags a genuine conflict in the evidence. On an `unresolved` verdict the strip additionally appends "Not modelled here, and therefore not excluded: birds and insects; airborne debris; helicopters and rockets; reflections, glare, and bokeh; video-processing artefacts." That list was already computed and already on the record but appeared only in the HTML report, not on the strip carrying the headline — and it is the context that stops "no conventional model passes" being read as an anomaly claim.
- **A whole-recording track file sizes the sitch to its full length, not just its start** (`215c196e`, `src/TrackManager.js`, `CTrackFile.syncsSitchDuration()` on `CTrackFileSTANAGBase` and `CTrackFileBOT`). A STANAG or BOT file is a complete recording rather than a clip of one, so an **unestablished** sitch now runs `GlobalDateTimeNode.syncDurationToTrack()` — the same operation as the **Sync Duration to** entry in the Time menu — before the existing start-time sync. Previously a 60 s scenario loaded into the 30 s default window with its second half off the timeline. Duration runs FIRST because it changes `Sit.frames` and rebuilds, so doing it before the start-time sync leaves that cascade (the one that gives good initial terrain positions) as the last word. It is bounded **only in this automatic caller**, at 24 hours: `syncDurationToTrack` and its menu entry are a deliberate click on a track the user chose and still sync to any length, whereas this runs unattended on whatever was dropped, and every per-frame array in the graph is sized from `Sit.frames` — a track with a broken timestamp (a row left at the Unix epoch spans decades) would take the tab out. Over the bound it logs a warning and leaves the timeline alone; the track still loads either way. Formats that do not define the method are unaffected.
- **KML track export is available on any per-frame position array, and its inspect probe is now O(1)** (`dce67b9d`, `exportTrackKML` moved from `src/nodes/CNodeTrack.js` to `src/nodes/CNodeArray.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeTrackClosest.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeManager.js`). `CNodeTrackClosest` and the raw LLA track arrays are plain `CNodeArray`s, not `CNodeTrack`s, so they could not emit a `gx:Track` at all. The exporter now lives on `CNodeArray` and works for any array of per-frame `{position}` or `{lla}`. Two structural constraints came with the move. First, `FileManager.makeExportButton` probes every export function with `inspect = true` **at node creation**, and the old implementation built the entire KML string — including a `new Date(...).toISOString()` per frame — before checking the flag, so every `CNodeSmoothedPositionTrack`, `CNodeTrackFromMISB` and `CNodePositionLLA` paid a full 7000+-frame KML build on construction; the new probe is capability-based (`return {desc: "KML Track Export"}`, rejecting only scalar arrays such as FOV or heading) and does no work, and it deliberately does **not** probe by data because an empty array means a lazily-recalculated track that still deserves its button. Second, registration is gated to `this.constructor === CNodeArray`: a base-class probe on a subclass override runs against a half-built object — `CNodeMISBDataTrack.exportTrackKML` reads `this.misb`, which that subclass assigns *after* `super()` returns — which threw and left a registered-but-unconstructed node in `NodeMan` when importing an exported KML. Nothing is lost, since a `CNodeEmptyArray` subclass has `array = []` at that point and the subclasses register their own buttons once their data exists. `CNodeTrackClosest` calls `addArrayExportButtons()` again after `recalculate()`, and `CNodeManager.addExportButton` now dedupes on a `button._exportFunction` tag (tagged rather than counted, so a probe that returned null for missing data does not block a later successful retry). Both hazards were introduced and closed inside this commit and never reached a shipped build.
- **Alt offset on hand-drawn (synthetic) tracks** (`dce67b9d`, `src/TrackManager.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeSplineEdit.js`). Imported data tracks have had a non-destructive **Alt offset** slider from `CNodeDisplayTrack`, which only builds it when there is a `dataTrack` input — so synthetic tracks never saw one. They now get the same control in their **Contents** folder, matched label, ±1000 range and `unitType: "small"`, ordered before Alt Lock as there. `CNodeSplineEditor.applyAltitudeAdjustments()` applies it in the same order `CNodeMISBDataTrack.adjustAlt` uses: an active altitude lock pins the altitude and the offset does not stack on top of it, otherwise the offset shifts the whole track. It is deliberately kept out of `applyAltitudeLock`, which is also used to conform the CONTROL POINTS to the lock — baking the offset into those would mean returning the slider to zero no longer restores the original altitudes exactly. It serializes with the spline and is carried by `.spline.json`.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed exported KML re-importing into Sitrec as "Unnamed Track"** (`dce67b9d`, `src/nodes/CNodeMISBData.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeArray.js`). The KML track exporter rooted its output in `<Folder>`. `CTrackFileKML.legacyTrackName` resolves a name for `Document>Placemark` but deliberately leaves it unset for `Folder>Placemark` for back-compatibility, so a Folder-rooted export lost its name on re-import. Both exporters now emit `<Document>`, which is also the conventional KML root container.
- **Fixed sparse angle data being interpolated the long way around the 0/360° seam** (`215c196e`, `ExpandMISBKeyframes` in `src/utils.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeArrayFromMISBColumn.js`). Every angle column is created with `degrees: true` (see `makeLOSNodeFromTrackAngles`), but the flag reached only the smoothing step, not the keyframe **interpolation** — `ExpandMISBKeyframes` did not take it and passed nothing through to `ExpandKeyframes`. An azimuth or heading stepping 0.03° → 359.3° between two sparse keyframes is a 0.7° change, but interpolated as plain numbers it sweeps 359° the other way and every frame in between points somewhere wrong. Per-frame MISB from 30 fps video hides this entirely, because consecutive keyframes are adjacent frames with nothing to interpolate; a 1 Hz source resampled to 30 fps puts ~30 frames inside each step, where the error was measured at 126°.
- **Fixed a multi-track import landing thousands of km off when the first track was deselected** (`215c196e`, `src/TrackManager.js`). The sitch start-time sync was gated on `trackIndex === 0`, so whenever the multi-track picker deselected the first track the sync silently never ran. The sitch timeline then stayed where it was while the loaded tracks kept their own timestamps, so every track was resampled far outside its own time range and the extrapolated positions landed thousands of km from the site — a wrecked import with no error anywhere. The gate is now a per-file `syncedFromThisFile` latch that fires on the first track **actually loaded**; supplementary tracks share their primary's timeline, so syncing on one when the primary is absent gives the same answer the primary would have. The sonde exemption (a fetched sounding must never re-time the sitch to the balloon launch) and the caller suppression for legacy sitches with an explicit `startTime` are unchanged.
- **Fixed the Alt Lock control vanishing from a hand-drawn track when any other track was removed** (`dce67b9d`, `src/TrackManager.js`). The synthetic track's `<trackID>_altitudeLock` GUI value carried `pruneIfUnused: true`. It drives its track through an `onChange` callback rather than a graph edge, so it has no inputs and no outputs, and `pruneUnusedFlagged()` — which runs whenever *any* track is removed — deleted it, taking the control off a spline that still existed. The flag is removed (and deliberately not set on the new Alt offset value for the same reason); `disposeSyntheticTrack` removes both by name instead.
- **Fixed a hand-drawn track's short name being taken over by a later import** (`dce67b9d`, `TrackManager.addSyntheticTrack`, `src/TrackManager.js`). `disposeSyntheticTrack` and `CMetaTrack.dispose` already RELEASE a short name from `usedShortNames`, but `addSyntheticTrack` never CLAIMED one — so the pair was one-sided and a KML or balloon track loaded afterwards could pick the same short name and take over the synthetic track's drop-target switch options. The name is now reserved after the early returns and before anything is built, so a failed creation cannot leave an orphaned reservation, covering every route in: the **Add Track** menu, spline import, and deserialization of a saved sitch.
- **Fixed a hand-drawn track being truncated when the sitch duration changed** (`dce67b9d`, `src/TrackManager.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeSplineEdit.js`). `addSyntheticTrack` passed `frames: Sit.frames`, snapshotting whatever count happened to be current at creation. The normal order of work is to draw or import a track and THEN load the video that sets the real duration, so the track stayed stuck at the old length — and the new automatic duration sync made this visible immediately. It now passes `frames: -1`, which `CNode` maps to `Sit.frames` **and** sets `useSitFrames`, the flag that makes `NodeMan.updateSitFramesChanged()` re-expand the spline when the duration changes. `CNodeSplineEditor`'s constructor no longer clobbers that flag by overwriting `this.frames` with the raw value.
- **Fixed the spline editing line staying visible outside edit mode** (`dce67b9d`, `src/SplineEditor.js`). The curve mesh is an EDITING AID, like the control cubes — the track itself is drawn by a separate `CNodeDisplayTrack` with a user-controllable colour — but `setEnable` lives on `PointEditor`, which knows nothing about the mesh, so leaving it on double-drew every spline track. `SplineEditor` now extends `setEnable` to toggle `this.spline.mesh.visible`, guarded because the base constructor calls `setEnable` before `this.spline` exists, with the current state applied to the mesh once it is created.

### Documentation
- **Spline and FOV interchange formats documented** (`dce67b9d`, `docs/Tracks.md`). Two new sections — *Sitrec Spline (.spline.json)* and *Sitrec Camera FOV (.fov.json)* — with annotated sample files, the strictly-increasing-frame and curve-type validation rules, both **Export Spline** locations and the **Camera ▸ FOV (Zoom) ▸ Export for FOV Editor** path, and two warnings a user will otherwise hit: frames are VIDEO frames, so load the video before dropping a spline (control points are expanded across `Sit.frames` at import and not re-expanded later), and the file is CONSUMED rather than kept, because the control points then belong to the synthetic track and are saved with the sitch. Both formats are added to the export table.
- **Solution families documented in the traverse methods guide** (`276f36e7`, `docs/TraverseMethods.md`, new section "Solution families — the range band a model admits"). States the under-determination result up front (`S(t) + R(t)·D(t)` reproduces the sightlines for any R(t)), how the held-range ladder is marched, how admitted and screened-out members are drawn, the four cautions the tile repeats, the two ways a gap is described, and — explicitly — that the band never enters the ranking, because "the more determined model wins" would be the calibrated object-probability claim this analysis declines to make.
- **Legacy ATFLIR pod conventions documented** (`dce67b9d`, `src/SphericalMath.js` +69, `src/JetHorizon.js` +58, `src/JetUtils.js` +27). The roll/pitch conventions of the existing roll-nod pod code are written down alongside it, including the `(0,1,0)` world-up assumption that makes `getHumanHorizonFromPitchRollAzEl` and `extractRollFromMatrix` valid only in the legacy flat local frame and unusable in ECEF — which is the reason `TurretRoll.js` redoes the geometry coordinate-free rather than reusing them.

### Internal
- **BOT Bench: the traverse analysis run over the published interchange set** (`62e500b5`, `276f36e7`, `benchmarks/botbench/`). No `src/` behaviour is changed by `62e500b5`. `lib/verdictRunner.js` runs the SHIPPING analysis headless; `lib/readInterchange.js` turns an `.all.csv` plus sidecar into a scenario with truth quarantined; `interchangeVerdict.bench.test.js` sweeps both search configurations; `scoreInterchangeVerdict.mjs` scores verdicts, bands and matched pairs; `probeNoiseFloor.mjs` asks whether a noise floor can be estimated from the data. `lib/classProbability.js` turns evidence signatures into group-weighted frequencies with a cluster bootstrap over `truthContentKey` groups — these are MEASURED FREQUENCIES over the benchmark population, never a cross-model likelihood, which preserves the no-global-winner law at `TraverseRanking.js:7`. Two limits are reported rather than papered over: the benchmark **cannot** reach "Probably a wind-blown balloon", because a scenario's wind is a hand-set constant that `rateBalloonWindEvidence` correctly rates inconclusive and injecting it as a measured sounding would manufacture the corroboration that licenses the verdict; and no multirotor or stationary target exists in the 855, so those classes report "not calibrated", never 0%. Four harness defects were found and fixed, and **all four had made results BETTER than they should have been** — a pattern worth naming, because a degenerate input is systematically the flattering one: the search bracket was anchored on `spec.initialHorizontalRangeM`, the target's TRUE starting range, so every earlier result was obtained with a search window centred on the answer; `adaptiveRangeList` was documented as "the production range bracket" and was not (production is linear over 0.1c..max(8,2c) NM, this was log over c/12..4c), so it measured a search the app never performs; `runVerdict` hard-coded `expand: true`, so the "pinned wide" configuration silently widened itself per scenario; and `familyCoverage`'s union collapsed every class into one lowest-to-highest span, crediting coverage across gaps no model admits — the same merge-across-a-gap error the band feature exists to avoid, in the function that reports on it. The scorer now refuses input it cannot trust (no verdict, missing id/config, unrecognised or contradictory truth labels, duplicate `(scenario, config)` pairs, runs missing scenarios), and the runner writes to a `.partial` file promoted only after validation — both because **absence reads as agreement downstream**: two crashed pair members compare equal and would report "INDISTINGUISHABLE", turning a harness failure into a finding, and stripping one `.truth.json` turned a balloon `miss` into an `honest-unknown` pass. Scoring is asymmetric on purpose: only three of six truth classes have a Sitrec model, so for bird, aerostat and the true anomalies, declining to name a class is the RIGHT answer and naming one is the worst outcome, while the two anomalous CONTROL cells are the opposite; the real control test is pairwise, since each pair shares geometry and pointing error and differs only in whether the manoeuvre is achievable. New script `npm run bench-bot-verdict`; `docs/temp/` is now gitignored for local working notes.

## Version 2.107.0 (2026-07-27)

### New Features
- **STANAG 4676 CSV exports import as full three-track sets** (`5e962a77`, `src/TrackFiles/CTrackFileSTANAGCSV.js`, `src/TrackFiles/CTrackFileSTANAGBase.js`, `src/CFileManagerParse.js`, `docs/Tracks.md`). GXP InMotion also emits STANAG 4676 track data as a flat CSV (`UTC0`/`FRM`/`UTC`/`t` plus `GLAT`/`GLON`/`HAE`, `SLAT`/`SLON`/`SHAE`, `TPLAT`/`TPLON`/`TPHAE`). Those headers already satisfied the generic Custom1 header lists, so such a file imported as a single target track and the Platform and Ground line-of-sight endpoints were silently discarded. The container-independent STANAG semantics — track enumeration, ground-locked de-duplication, MISB conversion, **(Target)**/**(Platform)**/**(Ground)** naming, camera/target role hints, HAE datum and import-track count — are factored out of `CTrackFileSTANAG` into a new `CTrackFileSTANAGBase`; subclasses supply only normalised `{time, target, platform, ground}` points, so the XML and CSV flavours make the same decisions in one place (`CTrackFileSTANAG` drops 282 → 117 lines with no behaviour change, its 45 existing tests unmodified). `detectCSVType()` gains a `STANAG_CSV` classification tested *before* `isCustom1()` — deliberately narrow, requiring the `TP*` family plus at least one of the `S*`/`G*` families, so a target-only CSV keeps taking the Custom1 path exactly as before. Header matching is case- and order-insensitive with extra columns ignored, and a missing `UTC` column falls back to `UTC0` + `t`. `cellNumber()` maps blank cells to `NaN` rather than letting `Number("")` place a point at 0°N 0°E or the Unix epoch. `data/test/elevated_track.csv` is the CSV export of the existing XML fixture and the suite asserts both load identically — track count, names, roles, supplementary flags, datum, and per-point MISB positions and timestamps.
- **Fit diagnostics for the live global-fit traverse methods** (`5f724e53`, new `src/nodes/LOSFitDiagnostics.js`, `assessLinearFitConditioning` in `src/LOSFitting.js`, `CNodeLOSFitCV/CA/Kalman/MonteCarlo/MonteCarlo2.js`). Bearings-only data determines range only through parallax, so when the sensor flies a path the constant-velocity model can represent (straight cruise exactly, a gentle arc approximately), the sensor's own trajectory is a near-zero-residual solution to every perpendicular-distance fit and the CV family collapses onto the camera — the residual looks fine while the range is an artifact. The live nodes used to publish that degenerate track with nothing flagging it. Each of the five methods now builds a lil-gui folder in `guiMenus.traverse` (the **Traverse** menu) titled *Constant Velocity / Constant Acceleration / Kalman Smoother / Monte Carlo 1 / Monte Carlo 2 Fit Diagnostics*, with two disabled rows — **CV-family conditioning** (`good`/`marginal`/`POOR` plus the rcond value) and **Fitted location** (median range, or "ON/BEHIND the camera — the fit collapsed; its range is meaningless" / "near the camera under weak geometry — range likely an artifact"). The folder auto-opens only when there is something the analyst must see (collapse, or poor conditioning). `assessLinearFitConditioning` computes the reciprocal condition number of the column-equilibrated CV design system over the sightlines by cyclic-Jacobi eigenvalues, with thresholds `LINEAR_RCOND_POOR` = 10^−2.5 and `LINEAR_RCOND_MARGINAL` = 1e−2 taken from the BOT Bench benchmark, where the CV collapse rate was 82% in the log10(rcond) ≈ −3 bin, 72% at ≈ −2.5, and 0% at ≈ −2 and above. Two deliberate limits are enforced in code: it is **CV-family-local** — never OR-ed into the analysis's global `rangeUnobservable` evidence veto, because a stationary-point, ground, ray-constrained or physics fit can succeed where CV collapses (benchmarked at ~8% error where CV sat at 100%) — and it is a **one-way warning**, since pointing noise can inflate apparent conditioning, so "good" is explicitly rendered as "not a guarantee of range". The fits themselves are byte-identical: the problem is surfaced, never forced. A per-frame lifecycle in `update()` reconciles the folder with visibility so it survives method switching (the deselected input is not recalculated, so the folder must be removed), gallery pre-computes (a node computed while hidden must surface its cached assessment without a recompute), and hidden invalidation (a cached assessment describing a LOS that no longer exists is cleared, never resurrectable); `_doCompute` clears the rows up front so early returns cannot leave a previous compute's values showing.

### Improvements
- **Collapse cautions on linear-family tiles in the traverse gallery** (`5f724e53`, `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `src/TraverseRanking.js`). `methodNodeHypothesis` attaches `linearConditioning` metadata to global-fit tiles (`meth.key.startsWith("gf")`), and the polynomial/Monte-Carlo sweep attaches `sweepConditioning` — the MC pair samples only near the CV seed and the alternating fit seeds from it, so all three inherit the linear family's range degeneracy. Collapsed fits append a reason-specific caution to the tile's notes: "this fit has collapsed onto the sensor path — its range and speed are artifacts, not measurements" for the demonstrated on/behind-sensor verdicts, and the weaker "near-camera result under poor CV-family conditioning — high artifact risk" for `near-camera-weak-geometry`, which is a risk classification and must not be stated as an accomplished collapse. The cautions are **rank-neutral** — notes only; `linearConditioning` is never read by `TraverseRanking.js`, so gallery ordering is unchanged. The same diagnostic is recorded once for the clip in `provenance.linearFitConditioning` and serialized into the results manifest (`rcond`, `log10Rcond`, `conditioning`), kept deliberately separate from `rangeUnobservable`.
- **Audio Spectrum trace responds much faster** (`7fbb52ac`, `src/nodes/CNodeAudioSpectrumView.js`). `SMOOTH` 0.5 → **0.99**. The blend is `dst[i] = dst[i] + (v - dst[i]) * SMOOTH`, i.e. a lerp *toward* the new spectrum, so a higher constant means less smoothing and a snappier trace — the opposite direction to the Web Audio `AnalyserNode.smoothingTimeConstant` convention the neighbouring "analyser-like" comment alludes to. At 0.99 each update moves 99% of the way to the newly computed spectrum instead of halfway, so transients and sweeps track the audio essentially without lag, at the cost of showing more frame-to-frame noise. The existing snap-on-jump behaviour (`jump ? v : …`, which bypasses smoothing entirely when the playhead moves further than the FFT window) is unchanged. Note the commit subject records this as "0.5 -> 0.9"; the committed value is 0.99.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed a settled Sky Lantern / Balloon fit being badged "Optimizer incomplete"** (`5f724e53`, `settledButUnidentifiable` in `src/TraverseRanking.js`, `lanternHypothesis` in `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `src/NelderMead.js`). A Nelder-Mead `iteration_limit` stop is not always an unfinished search: the LOS fits require BOTH a flat objective AND a collapsed simplex, so a model parameter with no effect on the trajectory over this particular clip legitimately holds the simplex wide forever. When a lantern's solved flame-out (`tBurn`) lies at or beyond the clip end, its sink and cool-down parameters (`vSink`, `tauCool`) never influence the fitted window, and the fit was badged incomplete for that reason alone — measured on a genuine easy balloon that Constant Altitude recovered to the exact range and drift speed. `NelderMead` now reports per-parameter simplex spreads, extremes and the final cost spread (`parameterSpreads`, `parameterMins`, `parameterMaxs`, `costSpread`, `tol`, `xTol`, `paramNames`, plumbed through `fitPhysicsModel`), and `settledButUnidentifiable()` reclassifies the stop **only** when the objective has settled (cost spread within `tol`) and every still-wide dimension is one the caller declares unobservable for this clip. The gate additionally requires the WHOLE final simplex's `tBurn` to sit at or beyond the clip end (`requireMinAtLeast`), not merely the best vertex — a simplex straddling the clip end is a real still-burning/already-cooling ambiguity and stays a genuine incomplete — and refuses conservatively when the minima metadata is missing. In that case the ordinary `localFitCompletionWarnings` entry is suppressed and replaced by an `identifiabilityNote` carried in the tile subtitle, worded as an identifiability statement ("unconstrained by this clip — not measured — an identifiability limit, not an optimizer failure") and never as convergence success for those parameters. Because `optimizerWarnings` drives `eligible: !boundaryLimited && !optimizerWarnings.length && result.rank >= 3` and `incomplete:` in `rateHypothesis`, an affected Sky Lantern / Balloon tile also becomes eligible and complete, and can therefore rank higher in the gallery — this fix is *not* purely cosmetic.
- **Fixed a STANAG 4676 file with no usable position being imported as an empty track** (`d8616930`, `src/TrackFiles/CTrackFileSTANAGBase.js`, `CTrackFileSTANAG.js`, `CTrackFileSTANAGCSV.js`). `doesContainTrack()` is the gate in `CFileManagerParse.handleParsedFile` (line ~784) that decides whether a parsed file is handed to `TrackManager.addTracks()`. The base class implemented it as "has at least one track point", so a CSV whose rows carry a timestamp but blank or non-numeric position columns reported `true` while `getTrackCount()` was 0 and every `toMISB()` returned `false` — the file was imported as a track that yields nothing, surfacing only later as a `makeMISBDataTrack: No data in file` console warning. `doesContainTrack()` is now defined as `_distinctTracks().length > 0`, exactly the condition behind `getTrackCount() > 0`, so the two can never disagree; the CSV parser drops rows carrying a timestamp but no position at all rather than carrying them as empty points (safe because all three position sequences derive from that one array, so they stay aligned and equal-length for the duplicate check), and its skip warning now reads "no usable timestamp or position". The XML override now ANDs its structural check with the base — previously an empty-but-valid `<track>` element was accepted as a track — keeping both containers on one contract while the structural half still short-circuits so malformed data is rejected without attempting a parse. Found by the Codex stop-time review.

### Security
- **All 26 open Dependabot alerts resolved across the three lockfiles** (`55f9950b`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `tools/SitrecBridge/`, `apps/video-viewer/`). Most were a stale lockfile rather than a blocked range — the patched versions already sat inside each parent's semver range, so `npm update` reached them. Root: `sharp` 0.34.5 → 0.35.3 (the one direct `package.json` bump, libvips CVEs), `fast-uri` 3.1.4, `hono` 4.12.32, `body-parser` 2.3.0/1.20.6, `linkify-it` 5.0.2 via `markdown-it` 14.3.0, `shell-quote` 1.10.0, `webpack-dev-server` 5.2.6, `brace-expansion` 1.1.16/2.1.2, `postcss` 8.5.23. `apps/video-viewer`: `tar` 7.5.22 (critical), `js-yaml` 4.3.0, `builder-util-runtime` 9.7.0. `@hono/node-server` is forced to ^2.0.5 via `overrides` in both root and SitrecBridge because `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` pins ^1.19.9 — verified safe, as both MCP servers use only `StdioServerTransport` so the Hono HTTP adapter carrying the advisory is never loaded. **Scope note for the record: none of this reaches the running application.** Every bumped package is build, test or developer tooling — `sharp` is a devDependency used by `tools/equirect2tiles.js`, `markdown-it` runs only in `webpack.common.js` at build time, `apps/video-viewer` declares zero runtime dependencies (electron/electron-builder only), and the Hono/MCP packages belong to the SitrecBridge developer extension. The shipped browser bundle, the PHP backend and the desktop app runtime are unaffected, which is why this carries no user-facing entry. Not fixed: GHSA-mh99-v99m-4gvg (brace-expansion OOM DoS) — no Dependabot alert exists, there is no fix for the 1.x/2.x lines, and 5.0.8 is not a drop-in (its `require()` returns an object with `.expand` rather than a callable, breaking minimatch and with it jest and electron-builder); build tooling only, absent from `dist/` and `dist-serverless/`.

### Documentation
- **Range observability documented in the traverse methods guide** (`5f724e53`, `docs/TraverseMethods.md`, new section "Range observability and collapse (CV-family conditioning)"). Explains why bearings-only range depends on parallax, which methods share the CV family's degeneracy (CV, CA, the Kalman smoother whose process model is CV, and the Monte Carlo/polynomial fits seeded from CV), the bin-scoped benchmark collapse rates, and the two honest limits (method-local, one-way). Adds Kalman noise-colour tuning guidance from the benchmark: real operator tracking error is autocorrelated, and at matched noise power it hurts stiff trackers far more than white noise — CV degraded ~4.7× under simulated operator wobble versus matched-power white noise, the default smoother (process noise 1e-4) ~2.8×, while a soft smoother (1e-3) was essentially immune. Corrects four overclaims in the existing text: soft range constraints *discourage* impossible solutions but do not prevent them; Constant Velocity is "first thing to try — *after* checking the Fit Diagnostics folder"; the Kalman seed is only as good as the CV fit it seeds from; and the *Measurement Noise* parameter is a variance on the projected positional pseudo-measurement, not an angular noise in degrees, with *Process Noise* flagged as an implementation tuning quantity rather than a calibrated physical variance.

### Internal
- **`buildHypotheses()` extracted to a dependency-free module** (new `src/TraverseHypotheses.js` +1258, `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js` −1197). The function that turns traverse fit results into the ranked hypothesis gallery was private inside the GUI-coupled `AnalyzeTraverse.js`, which pulls in `threeExt`/three-addons, lil-gui and `Chart3D` and therefore cannot be imported headless — so the benchmark had to re-implement the ranking and could silently drift from what the app actually does.
