Three things stay behind in `AnalyzeTraverse` because they genuinely need the live scene, injected at exactly the point in the sequence they previously occupied so hypothesis ORDER — and therefore tie-breaking — is unchanged: `extraHypotheses` (the two astronomical-catalogue searches plus the user's live LOS-fit method nodes, spliced where sections 8–9 sat), the `signedAGL` + `localGroundZ` terrain probes (they reach `getPointBelow`/`calculateAltitude` in `threeExt`), and `clipStartMs` (absolute clip start, previously read from `GlobalDateTimeNode`/`Sit`). GUI state (`aoFixedPoint`, `groundMode`) is now passed in rather than read off the module-level `analyzeTweaks`. Behaviour-preserving for the app: the moved code differs from the pre-refactor original on 54 lines, all intentional (signature, injected parameters, three null-guards, the injection splice, one added argument), with every hypothesis-producing line byte-identical; the app always passes non-null probes, so the new guards are no-ops there. Two hazards were caught and closed **during the extraction, before it was committed — neither ever affected a shipped build**: (1) `simSpeed` was momentarily applied twice in the extracted clip-time helper, because `buildAnalysisDataset` already defines `dataset.fps` as `Sit.fps / simSpeed` while the draft also multiplied by `simSpeed` — invisible at the default `simSpeed` 1, but a 10× sitch would have placed satellite hypotheses at 100× the elapsed time. The shipped `dateAtFrame` (`dateStart + f * 1000 * simSpeed / Sit.fps`) was always correct and is unchanged; the arithmetic is now an exported `clipFrameDate()` carrying no `simSpeed` term at all, with a regression test at simSpeed 1/2/10/0.5. (2) The two terrain probes were independently defaulted, so a caller supplying `localGroundZ` but not `signedAGL` would build the ground hypotheses while `trackGroundStats` returned null, leaving the underground and ground-contact rejections inert — a trajectory diving below the surface would rank eligible headless but ineligible in the app. `buildHypotheses` now throws unless both probes are supplied or neither, and a new exported `flatTerrainProbes()` returns a correct pair for flat-plane datasets. Verified by `tests/Traverse*` (95 tests, 7 suites) plus the full suite; new coverage in `tests/botbench/clipFrameDate.test.js` and `tests/botbench/headlessHypotheses.test.js`.
- **BOT Bench: 855-scenario bearings-only tracking benchmark** (`6e73e687`, `benchmarks/botbench/`). Deterministic scenario generator (6 fixed-wing platform paths, 9 target families including balloons/bird/aircraft/Venus/anomalous, 4 wind models, white and autocorrelated-wobble pointing error with FOV masking), 8 fast solvers, truth-aware metrics with paired noise contrasts and anomaly windows, a leakage-safe solver-selection protocol, a KML sitch bridge for in-app visual validation, and the round-1 report with figures and glossary. Round 1 totals 855 scenarios (the 765-scenario block matrix plus the RECOVERABLE-NOISE block). Contract and audit history in `benchmarks/botbench/PLAN.md`; `npm run bench-bot` / `bench-bot-classify` / `bench-bot-export`; smoke tests in `tests/botbench` run in normal CI; results directories are gitignored. This benchmark is the source of the CV-family conditioning thresholds and collapse rates shipped in `assessLinearFitConditioning` above.
- **BOT interchange format** (`benchmarks/botbench/lib/exportInterchange.js`, `lib/interchangeRelease.js`, `interchange.bench.test.js`, `benchmarks/botbench/BOT-Interchange-Format.html`, `npm run bench-bot-interchange`). A generic four-file CSV/JSON interchange per scenario — `<name>.input.csv` (sensor positions, LOS unit vectors, declared error), `<name>.scenario.json` (ENU origin, datum, surface model, epoch, timing), `<name>.truth.csv` and `<name>.truth.json` — all in ENU metres, so algorithms written outside Sitrec can be run on the same scenarios and scored against the same ground truth. Challenge and answer-key files are written to separate directories so a challenge set can ship without truth; because the default descriptive filenames themselves leak the target family, altitude and wind, a sealed set can be written with opaque ids (`bot-0001`) plus an opaque→descriptive mapping stored with the truth. Floats are written with `String(x)`, the shortest round-tripping decimal, because ENU positions span tens of km and the CV/stationary solves difference near-equal large values. Ships with a written spec document.
- **Additional BOT Bench tooling** (`physics.bench.test.js`, `run-physics-parallel.mjs`, `samplerate.bench.test.js`, `capability.bench.test.js`, `capability-validation.bench.test.js`, `lib/physicsSolvers.js`, `lib/capabilityDetect.js`, `lib/capabilityTargets.js`, `lib/envelopeFeasibility.js`, `tests/botbench/capabilityGate.test.js`, `analysis/BOT-Recovery-Paper.html`, plus `bench-bot-physics`/`-physics-par`/`-rate`/`-capability`/`-capval` scripts). Included in this release because the working-tree `package.json` scripts and two tracked sources reference them. **The capability / envelope-feasibility detector is deliberately NOT calibrated and makes no working detection claim.** Its α\* detector is fail-closed by construction — a binary detection claim is emitted only when a valid calibration artifact bound to the exact detector config is passed at the call site, there is no module-level enable flag to flip, and no such artifact exists yet, so every call in the repo today is **measurement-only**; a missing, malformed or unprovenanced calibration degrades to measurement-only rather than to an unsupported claim. Its S2 range-family verdict is **hard-disabled pending a parallax-based redesign** (`S2_POSITIVE_ENABLED = false` in `lib/capabilityDetect.js`, verdict `"held-pending-redesign"`), enforced in CI by `tests/botbench/capabilityGate.test.js`, which asserts the flag is false and that no exceedance lower bound is exported. Also new: `tests/botbench/interchangeReidentification.test.js` and `tests/botbench/venusEphemerisContract.test.js`.

## Version 2.106.1 (2026-07-22)

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed sitch teardown breaking when a view was popped out into its own browser window** (`src/nodes/CNodeView.js`). While popped, `popOut()` moves everything under the view's header into the popup's document via `adoptNode` — so when a new sitch was loaded in-page and `disposeEverything()` tore the views down, `CNodeViewCanvas.dispose()`'s `this.div.removeChild(this.canvas)` threw `NotFoundError` (the canvas was no longer a child of the in-page div), breaking teardown mid-way, and the popup was left orphaned with its 600 ms fallback poll (`_popPoll`) still running — the poll only docks once the popup is *closed*, and the main-window `pagehide` closer never fires on an in-page load, so the dead window just stayed. `CNodeView.dispose()` now checks `_poppedWindow` first and calls `dockWindow()` before any DOM teardown: the content is adopted back into the main document, the popup window closes, and the popup poll and `renderWhileWindowed` render loop stop; nothing happens when the view isn't popped. Fixes every poppable view — Notes (`CNodeNotes`), text views (`CNodeViewText`), the node-graph DAG (`CNodeViewDAG`), the script editor, and the Audio Spectrum analyzer added in 2.106.0. Verified by reproducing the popped state (content adopted into the popup's document) and loading a new sitch: clean teardown, popup closed, view rebuilt correctly.

## Version 2.106.0 (2026-07-21)

### New Features
- **Audio Spectrum analyzer view** (`src/nodes/CNodeAudioSpectrumView.js`, `src/workers/AudioSpectrumWorker.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeVideoView.js`). A classic real-time spectrum-analyzer panel for the soundtrack of the loaded video (or audio-only source): logarithmic frequency axis 3 Hz–20 kHz (capped at Nyquist), dB axis −18 to −90 with a 6 dB grid. `CNodeVideoView.setupSpectrumView()` creates it for the primary `video` view only, as a draggable/resizable/dockable free-floating panel (`menuName` "Audio Spectrum", hidden by default, toggled from Show → Views); it is `poppable` with `renderWhileWindowed`, so it can pop out into its own browser window and keeps animating there, and it costs nothing while hidden — the render loop only paints effectively-visible views, so no worker requests are made. Stereo sources draw one trace per channel (left bright cyan-white on top, right darker blue behind); mono draws one. Every spectrum comes from one path: a 32768-point Blackman-windowed FFT (~1.5 Hz bins at 48 kHz — needed to resolve the 3 Hz left edge) of the decoded `AudioBuffer`, with the window CENTERED on the playhead — a trailing window would effectively show the audio ~0.34 s early because Blackman weights its middle most heavily — so playback, scrubbing, paused stepping, and muted playback all behave identically and frequency sweeps track without lag. The FFT runs off-main-thread in `AudioSpectrumWorker.js` (in-place radix-2 Cooley-Tukey; `|X|/N` dB scaling matching the Web Audio `AnalyserNode` convention): channel data is transferred once per audio load (`init`, up to two channels), then each `compute` request returns one dB `Float32Array` per channel, transferred not copied. The main thread only draws. Latest-wins backpressure keeps at most one request in flight and drops intermediate playhead positions; a generation counter (`resetSpectrumState`) invalidates in-flight replies when the audio source changes, so a stale spectrum can never repopulate the display. Successive spectra are smoothed analyser-style (`SMOOTH` 0.5) but snap on playhead jumps larger than the FFT so scrubbing shows the new position immediately. The worker zero-pads samples outside the clip, which handles audio shorter than the FFT, playheads near the start, and positions past the end of shorter audio; for MP4 sources whose `AudioBuffer` is normally built lazily on first playback, `getScrubBuffer` builds it once decoding completes, so opening the analyzer before ever pressing play still works ("Waiting for audio to decode…" / "No audio" placeholders otherwise). Frequency-axis labels adapt to panel width via three-tier collision-based placement — majors (10/100/1k/10k) first, then 2/5 multiples, then every 1–9 step, each drawn only where it fits — so a wide panel automatically gains detail. The 3 strongest spectral peaks are labeled with their frequency in Hz to one decimal (parabolic sub-bin interpolation of the dB values around the peak bin); picking is done in bin space (so the set doesn't change with panel size) with two filters: a 6 dB prominence dip required between labeled peaks, and a Blackman sidelobe-leakage envelope (−58 dB at ~4 bins out, decaying 18 dB per octave of bin distance, 6 dB margin) that rejects candidates indistinguishable from a stronger peak's own window leakage. Overlapping peak labels stack upward.

### Documentation
- **Traverse gallery ranking fully documented** (`docs/TraverseMethods.md`, new section "How the tiles are ranked"). Documents the lexicographic ordering cascade behind the flat best-first gallery: truth separation (only when a truth track is selected), the broad-screen pass (deliberately decided BEFORE completeness, so a slow family that honestly reports touching a search edge is not buried under an extreme-but-cleanly-converged solution), eligibility, completeness, the combined fit-quality/kinematic-ordinariness tier plus load-bearing-limit count, category priority (Physically based → LOS Constrained → Geometric → Geometric Approximations → Known Object — needed because the next key is not commensurable across categories), the within-category secondary score, and the raw LOS residual tie-break. Also spelled out: the two independent 0–3 tier grades and their thresholds, the smoothness-composite secondary-score formula and its 0.05°-per-unit residual scale, the bounded symmetric balloon-consistency nudge (`6·(1 − 2C)`, at most ±0.3° residual-equivalent, unreachable across earlier cascade keys), three worked examples, and the design choices that keep genuinely anomalous solutions surfaced rather than ranked out of sight.

## Version 2.105.0 (2026-07-21)

### New Features
- **Printable light theme for the full analysis report** (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `buildReportHTML`). The report from Traverse → Analyze Traverse Methods... → *Open Full Report* gains a fixed `#theme-toggle` button (*Light view (print)* / *Dark view*) driving a complete `html.light` CSS theme designed to print clearly: dark text on white, `color-scheme: light`, and the inline-styled banner variants (amber/green/red/pink `.warning` boxes) flattened to ONE neutral light card via `!important` overrides — their meaning rides on the symbols and lead text, and solid dark ink blocks are exactly what a printout must not have. Printing ALWAYS uses the light theme regardless of the on-screen choice: `beforeprint` forces it, `afterprint` restores the previous mode. The preference persists in localStorage (`sitrec-report-theme`, try/catch-guarded — a saved copy opened from `file://` may have no storage), and the theme script seeds its initial mode from the document root's class rather than a hardcoded default, so a report downloaded via *Download this report* while in light mode reopens light even where storage is unavailable (a readable stored preference still wins). The charts and plots are pre-rendered dark PNGs; in light mode they are recoloured with `filter: invert(1) hue-rotate(180deg)` scoped to `html.light` — invert alone would turn every series hue into its complement, and the dark view ships the exact same untouched pixels as before. Because inversion flips brightness, the sweep-heatmap caption swaps its wording with the theme via `.dark-only`/`.light-only` spans (dark: "darker = lower score = more plausible"; light: "lighter"), keeping the value-encoding description true in both modes. `@media print` hides the toggle and the `#dl-report` download link.

### Improvements
- **3D volume graphs follow the user's unit system** (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `src/Chart3D.js`, `tests/Chart3D.test.js`). The traverse graphs previously hardcoded nautical miles on all axes. Geometry on all three axes now converts through `toBigUnits` (`Units.big2M`, the CUnits global; NM fallback when uninitialised) — one shared spatial scale, so the volume stays spatially true — and the axis labels read `East (<big>)`, `North (<big>)`, and `Alt (<small>)` from `Units.bigUnitsAbbrev`/`smallUnitsAbbrev`. Altitude TICKS are chosen and displayed in the SMALL unit while the geometry stays in big units, via a new per-scene `zTicks` `{toDisplay, fromDisplay}` transform: `Chart3D` now generates Z ticks as `{v, d}` pairs — nice values picked in DISPLAY space (5,000/6,000 ft rather than un-nice big-unit multiples), positioned in data space. `niceTicks` is exported for tests, and Alt tick labels at or above 100 display-units get thousands separators (`Math.round(v).toLocaleString()`).
- **3D volume graphs stand on real terrain** (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `src/Chart3D.js`, `tests/LLA-ECEF-ENU.test.js`, `tests/Chart3D.test.js`). The green ground plane was previously drawn at altitude 0 — wrong anywhere inland. A new `analysisGroundLevelM` samples the terrain (`getPointBelow`) on a 6×6 grid over the area of concern — the sensor path plus every finite candidate track — plus up to 40 probes directly under the sensor path, and returns the minimum in the LOCAL ENU-Z frame the graphs actually plot (`ECEF2ENU_radii(...).z`), NOT geodetic altitude: away from the ENU origin the two differ by the Earth-curvature drop (~70 m at 30 km — pinned by a new regression test in `tests/LLA-ECEF-ENU.test.js`), and a plane placed at geodetic altitude would float above the displayed tracks' ground. The sampling extent is anchored to the sensor-path centroid with a hard 100 km radius (`MAX_AREA_RADIUS_M`) and excludes at-infinity, identity, and non-physical tracks — a runaway polynomial-sweep fit reaching thousands of km would otherwise stretch the extent to where the curvature drop (half a megametre at 2,500 km) swamps the altitude axis. The result is frozen onto `dataset.groundLevelM` at analysis time, so cached results keep the ground they were graded against (0 fallback reproduces the old sea-level plane for older cached results and over ocean). A fold-in pass then lowers the ground to the lowest point of any credible terrain-hugging candidate — within `GROUND_FOLD_TOLERANCE_M` (50 m) below the sampled ground, not underground-flagged, not non-physical — so for every ordinary scene the floor equals ground and the plane IS the base of the box, including under Sync Scale's shared bounds; a rejected underground candidate must NOT redefine "ground" for the whole gallery. `padGraphBounds(b, groundZ)` starts the altitude axis AT the un-snapped ground level with nice ticks above it (new `niceTicks` test: floor 0.7343 → first tick 0.8). Underground-flagged candidates keep the plane at true ground (the floor follows the track down and the plane renders mid-box — what "passes below the terrain" should look like) and additionally carry `h.groundTrace`, the local terrain profile frozen from `trackGroundStats`' grading samples (`[x, y, z − AGL]`), drawn as a dashed line under the track in both the full and zoom views (it grows the zoom bounds, since "zoom to tracks" is exactly where burial evidence must not be clipped away) — on sloped ground this is what actually shows a burial that a flat area-minimum plane cannot. Helper LOS rays are clipped at ground level by `clipRayToGround`: a ray from an origin above ground gets a true intersection endpoint; a ray descending from an at/below-ground origin is omitted entirely, never fabricated. `Chart3D` draws the plane at the scene's `groundZ` — snapped exactly onto the box base when the floor equals ground, mid-box when the floor followed data down, and omitted when the box floats entirely above terrain (previously it drew only when `minZ` was exactly 0).
- **Results gallery: two full-height columns** (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `showResultGallery`). The former full-width header band — title row, explainer, truth/circular-LOS/advisory/failure/terrain banners, executive strip, and the Sync/Restore toolbar — moves into a `tg-tiles-head` block at the TOP of the scrollable tiles column, so it scrolls away with the list and the tile grid gets the whole screen height; the *Open Full Report* / *Close* footer moves to the BOTTOM of the details column, which is restructured as action bar / scrolling `tg-d-content` / footer (`tg-details` is now `overflow:hidden` with the content pane owning the scroll). `relayout()` now preserves the tiles column's `scrollTop` across set-aside/restore rebuilds — emptying the grid momentarily collapsed its height, clamping `scrollTop` to 0 and yanking the reader back to the top of the list. All gallery text is now selectable and copyable (`user-select:text` on `tg-panel`; the app root disables selection globally), with buttons and the 3D chart canvases kept `user-select:none` — a drag there rotates the chart.
- **The analysis always runs on the selected sightlines** (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`). The automatic measured-LOS substitution added in 2.102.0 (`3e61d42f`) is removed: when the selected LOS was the target-aimed camera boresight and the camera track carried recorded sensor angles, the analysis silently swapped in the measured `Track_<name>_LOS`. That overrode a deliberate selection — "To Target" sightlines (whose target may itself be valid recorded platform data, e.g. a MISB FrameCenter track) and "Use Angles" (recorded sensor angles) are BOTH legitimate evidence, and running the analysis on each and comparing is exactly how an analyst determines which source the data supports. The recorded-angles alternative is now an ADVISORY (`provenance.measuredAlternativeNote`), shown in the gallery banner, the verdict ("Alternative sightlines available"), and the report, that names the EXACT live selection to change: it resolves the JetLOS switch option whose node is the measured LOS (switch title read from the lil-gui controller name with the leading `*` stripped, option text through the `guiLabels` display overrides), else the Camera Heading switch's `Angles_<camera track>` option pinned to the camera track's own shortName via `cameraTrackSwitch.choice` — never a generic `Angles_*` pattern match, which could recommend the wrong track when several angle-bearing tracks are loaded. The advisory is suppressed when the current selection already delivers the recorded angles (`alreadyOnAngles`), and omitted entirely when no real selection path exists — never point at a control the user cannot find. Removed along with the substitution: the live-method-contender skip, the MC-sweep skip (`sweepPolynomialOrders` now always runs), the `measuredSubstituted`/`measuredLOSId` fingerprint entries, and the apply-toast divergence note; the results manifest's `measuredLOSSubstituted` field becomes `measuredLOSAlternative`. Circular-LOS detection and its banners are unchanged.

### Security
- **guzzlehttp/guzzle 7.12.1 → 7.15.1** in the PHP backend (`4973bf18`, `sitrecServer/composer.lock`, Dependabot). The window fixes five moderate advisories in the server's HTTP client: GHSA-g446-98w2-8p5w (cookie disclosure and injection via IP-address domains, fixed 7.12.3), GHSA-94pj-82f3-465w (Proxy-Authorization headers sent to origin servers, fixed 7.14.2), GHSA-f283-ghqc-fg79 (unbounded response cookies enabling denial of service), GHSA-h95v-h523-3mw8 (URI fragments disclosed in redirect Referer headers), and GHSA-wm3w-8rrp-j577 (host-only cookie scope not preserved) — the last three fixed in 7.15.1. Applies to deployments running the PHP backend (full server, standalone, Docker); serverless and desktop builds have no PHP backend and are unaffected.

## Version 2.104.0 (2026-07-21)

### New Features
- **Independent balloon wind evidence, frozen at analysis time** (`7dbe7e96`) (`src/TraverseAnalysis.js`, `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `tests/CompareWindVectorSeries.test.js`). Traverse → Analyze Traverse Methods... can now say — with evidence — whether the subject was a wind-blown balloon. The one independently checkable signal is the FREE balloon fit's solved wind: that fit never saw the loaded wind field, so agreement between the wind it *requires* and the wind independently measured/modelled aloft is genuine evidence for passive drift. A new pure metric, `compareWindVectorSeries` (`TraverseAnalysis.js`), compares the two as full E/N vectors — never mean speeds or a lone correlation, which both hide real disagreement: `vectorRMSE`, magnitude-weighted `alignment` and `meanAbsDirDiffDeg` computed ONLY over pairs where both winds exceed the calm threshold (default 2 m/s — a calm vector has no direction to agree with), `temporalCorrelation` withheld (null) unless BOTH centered series actually vary above `varianceFloorMs`, and a structured `{comparable:false, reason, coverage}` result below `minPairs` so the failure mode itself is recorded. A capture pass, `attachBalloonWindEvidence` (`AnalyzeTraverse.js`), runs once per analysis right after the hypotheses are built: it samples the free fit's solved wind at up to 25 points along the fitted track and reads the loaded wind field (`windField.sampleWindAtAltitude`) at each fitted point's OWN lat/lon — not the sensor origin — then FREEZES the comparison into `h.windEvidence`, because cached results are redisplayed while the live wind field can change underneath them. `rateBalloonWindEvidence` owns the deliberately asymmetric Supports / Compatible / Tension / Inconclusive vocabulary: agreement can SUPPORT a balloon, disagreement is only TENSION (the LOS range degeneracy makes fitted altitude partly model-selected, and a sonde can be far away in space and time), and "ruled out" is never produced. Mandatory-inconclusive gates: a hand-set constant wind (source class "assumption"), "Zero wind" analysis mode, unobservable range (a wind match at a model-chosen altitude is not evidence either way), and <50% coverage of the fitted trajectory. "Supports" quality caps (fail-closed): an incomplete fit (bound pins, model clamps, or optimizer warnings) caps at compatible; sounding evidence caps unless stations actually CONTRIBUTE at every sampled altitude (a station only vouches up to its measured wind top, `topWindAlt`) within ~300 km, and caps when station positions cannot be read at all. The wind-pinned balloon variant is tagged `windEvidenceRole: "externally-conditioned"`: it consumed the wind reference, so its agreement is circular — labelled "Not independent evidence", never rated. Surfaces: an **Independent wind match** badge on the free-fit tile (supports only), a colour-coded **Evidence — balloon wind check** strip in the gallery, a three-part "Wind-blown balloon check" reading in the verdict text (fit quality, motion character, wind evidence — kept as separate judgements), and the tile detail's inferred-wind table now renders from the frozen record, is titled "…vs loaded winds aloft" (previously "vs measured"), and omits the Loaded column entirely when no reference data exists — a column of em-dashes reads as "checked, found nothing", which is wrong. Plumbing: the analysis cache fingerprint now folds in `sondeProfileSignature()`, so soundings arriving asynchronously invalidate cached evidence instead of serving frozen "inconclusive" forever; `lanternHypothesis` now propagates `localFitCompletionWarnings(fit.params.optimizer)` into `optimizerWarnings`, matching the drone fit, so an unconverged balloon fit can never read "supports". Per the standing design rule, evidence surfaces as prose and badges only — gallery ORDERING is untouched.
- **Corroboration-first executive assessment** (`7dbe7e96`) (`src/TraverseRanking.js`, `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `tests/ExecutiveVerdict.test.js`). The analysis now opens with a frozen one-line verdict, licensed by independent evidence rather than gallery position. A pure, unit-testable layer in `TraverseRanking.js` — `NOT_MODELLED_DISCLOSURE`, `aggregateInterpretationClasses`, `assessExecutiveVerdict` — aggregates hypotheses into five interpretation CLASSES (balloon, fixed-wing, multirotor, stationary/ground, catalogued satellite/astronomical object), deliberately distinct from the gallery display categories: the free and wind-pinned balloon fits are one class; geometric/curve fits are compatibility screens and never appear; the date-sweeping `astroTime` diagnostic is excluded from identification; a direction-only geometric check (point at infinity) can never make a class viable however small its residual; and `judgeRepresentative` is STRICTER than gallery eligibility — "complete" also rejects load-bearing bound pins and model clamps. Catalogue identifications report the ANGULAR match separately from visibility: a satellite 0.05° away but in Earth's shadow is "matched and ruled out", the opposite of "no close match". Verdict codes: **"Probably a wind-blown balloon"** only when the free balloon fit is viable (complete + close + ordinary) AND its frozen wind rating is exactly "supports" AND balloon consistency >= 0.75 AND no catalogue identification is viable; **"Consistent with a/several …"** for viable-but-uncorroborated fits (a sole survivor is never "probably"); **"Insufficient evidence"** when the LOS provenance is circular or range is unobservable; and an **"Unresolved"** safety valve that is explicitly NOT an anomaly claim — it discloses that the sightline noise floor is uncalibrated and no exclusion audit was performed, plus the static not-modelled list (birds/insects, debris, helicopters/rockets, reflections/glare/bokeh, video artefacts). One record, one rendering per surface: a colour-coded headline strip at the top of the gallery (suppressed when truth ordering is active), an **Assessment** section leading the printable report with a per-class matrix (Tested / Complete / Close fit / Ordinary motion / Independent evidence / Status — "not checked" is never conflated with "checked, none found") plus the not-run and not-modelled disclosures, and a machine-readable `executive` gate audit (code, gates, per-class predicates, the judged consistency value) in the results manifest. Ranking is untouched: `tests/ExecutiveVerdict.test.js` asserts the assessment mutates nothing and leaves `rankAllHypotheses` order byte-identical.

### Improvements
- **Polynomial-order sweep tiles demoted below an "Extras" separator** (`7dbe7e96`) (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`). The order sweep produces one tile per swept order per strategy (5 by default, 15 with the Monte Carlo sweep on) and those tiles are a method diagnostic, not independent candidates — presenting them all up front buried the real contenders. The gallery now keeps each strategy's best-RANKED order in the main list (sweep tiles are identified by a finite `params.mcOrder`) and moves the remaining orders below an **Extras** separator at the end: still ranked exactly as they were, still selectable, nothing silently dropped. The separator describes them as a curvature diagnostic, appears only before the first visible demoted tile (so it vanishes when every extra is set aside), and the adjacent-tile keyboard/scan navigation no longer walks across it (`sameSide` guard). The gallery help text explains the split.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed stale-load races across the custom-sitch reload path** (`ed87df27`) (`src/CustomManagerSerialize.js`, `src/index.js`). Awaiting `handleParsedFile` (introduced in 2.103.2) widened the window in which a superseded custom-sitch load's continuations could write its tracks, mods, and flags onto the NEXT sitch. `deserialize()` now captures `Globals.loadGeneration` BEFORE any await, and every resumption point bails when superseded: the per-file load callback (entry and per parsed result), the post-file-loading continuation, after `buildAppFlightTrack`, after `ScenarioManager.activateForMods`, after the FINAL mod's `waitForPendingActions` (the loop-entry guard never runs again there), and inside `finishDeserialization` after its motion-analysis and auto-tracking awaits; the generation is threaded as a parameter into `deserializeMods`/`finishDeserialization` so their guards measure against the pre-load baseline rather than a too-late snapshot. Stale aborts touch nothing — `disposeEverything()` now resets the per-load globals (`dontAutoZoom`, `deserializing`, `dontRecalculate`) so a dead load can no longer leak them onto the next sitch (suppressed auto-centering, a stuck "deserializing" state, disabled recalculation). `newSitch()` bumps `loadGeneration` immediately, before its parsing/terrain/settle awaits, closing the pre-disposal window where a completing stale load could re-set `sitchEstablished(true)` over the false just set. And `finishDeserialization` restores `sitchData.globals` through a lifecycle-key blocklist (`loadGeneration`, `deserializing`, `disposing`, `dontAutoZoom`, `dontRecalculate`, `parsing`, `pendingActions`, `loadingTerrain`, `sitchEstablished`) — a hand-edited save could otherwise overwrite `loadGeneration` and defeat every guard; serialization only ever writes display toggles. Known residual, deliberately out of scope: the generation is not threaded INTO the `handleParsedFile`/`addTracks`/`activateForMods`/`deserializeMotionAnalysis` internals, so one in-flight mutation started before the supersede can still land — pre-existing behavior shared with the drag-drop path.

## Version 2.103.2 (2026-07-20)

### Improvements
- **Elevation-map sphere trace replaces the per-frame terrain-mesh raycast in MISB CSV export** (`src/raycastGround.js`, `src/QuadTreeMapElevation.js`, `src/QuadTreeMap.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeTrack.js`). The FrameCenter ground track of a MISB Compliant CSV export (File → Export, offered on LOS-type tracks — `CNodeLOSFromCamera`, `CNodeLOSTrackAzEl`, `CNodeJetTrack`, `CNodePositionLLA`, `CNodeTrackFromMISB`) intersected each frame's boresight with the terrain via `raycastLocalGround` — exact against the rendered polygons, but terrain tiles have no BVH, so Three.js brute-forces every triangle of every candidate tile at ~1 ms per ray: 25 of the 25.5 seconds of a 20,000-frame export. `CNodeTrack.frameCenterGroundPoint` now calls a new `raycastGroundElevationFast(origin, direction, maxDistance)`: a conservative sphere trace against the elevation quadtree's *served* surface — max(bilinear tile elevation, EGM96 geoid), the same clamp as `CNodeTerrain.getPointBelow` — at a few dozen sub-microsecond map lookups per ray. Each step advances by clearance/(1 + L), where L is a *measured* Lipschitz slope bound: `QuadTreeMapElevation.tileSlopeBound` computes the max clamped bilinear post-difference gradient of a tile's raster (posts clamped to the tile's minimum geoid height before differencing, conservative low-radius post spacing), cached keyed on the elevation array's identity; `subtreeSlopeBound` maxes it over every loaded servable descendant via `_getServedIndex`, an index rebuilt per tile-set epoch (`QuadTreeMap` now bumps `_tileEpoch` in `setTile`/`deleteTile`). Steps whose endpoints answer from different tiles are bisected to the border (~1 cm) and the far side's clearance is checked right at entry, so served-surface jumps at zoom transitions are treated as crossings; step length is capped at 1000 m to bound the lat/lon footprint bow of a straight ECEF ray. Hits are bisected to ~1 cm. Deliberate differences from the mesh raycast, all documented in-code: the hit is on the bilinear elevation surface, not the triangulated mesh (sub-meter deltas on ordinary terrain — exported FrameCenter values can differ slightly from previous releases), buildings / Google 3D tiles are never considered, and the no-data fallback is the EGM96 geoid rather than the camera-latitude geocentric sphere. Flattened terrain (flat-earth sitches, `flattening > 0`) and step-budget exhaustion (grazing rays, 30,000 steps) route to `raycastGroundMeshFallback`, the exact mesh raycast with the MAIN/LOOK layer mask.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed saved custom sitches dropping all mods on file-derived tracks** (`src/CustomManagerSerialize.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeTerrain.js`). The deserializer called the async `FileManager.handleParsedFile()` fire-and-forget inside each file's loading promise, so `Promise.all(loadingPromises)` could resolve before file-derived track nodes existed — for some file types the nodes are only created after a real network await (e.g. radiosonde soundings fetch the IGRA2 station list in `refineStationCoords` before `addTracks` runs). `deserializeMods` then hit "Node X does not exist … cannot apply mod" (observed: ~50 console warnings) and silently dropped those tracks' saved colors, color mode, smoothing, and wind-arrow settings on every load. Fix: `await FileManager.handleParsedFile(...)` inside the promise chain. `Globals.dontAutoZoom` is now hoisted across the whole file-load phase (set before the `loadedFiles` loop, cleared when `Promise.all` resolves) — it was previously set and cleared inside each load callback, i.e. cleared before the un-awaited `addTracks` actually ran, so loading a saved sitch could re-center the view on a track. Also new: nodes flagged `skipModSerialize` are excluded from the mods collector; the local-build `labelMainViewTerrain` debug overlay gets the flag at creation (it is created lazily on the first render frame, after `deserializeMods` has run, so its mod could never re-attach), and its legacy mod in older saves is dropped at load time.
- **Fixed synthetic (hand-drawn) tracks regenerating a different shortName on reload** (`src/TrackManager.js`). `CTrackManager.deserialize` never passed the saved `shortName` to `addSyntheticTrack`, which then regenerates `synth_NN_d` from the *current* track count. This was masked by the race above (the count was always 0 at that moment, so `synth_01_d` happened to match); with file tracks now correctly created first, the regenerated name drifted (e.g. `synth_01_d` → `synth_04_d`) and saved switch choices keyed by shortName (`orbitTargetSwitch`, `targetTrackSwitch`, …) could not re-attach. Fix: pass `shortName: trackData.shortName ?? trackData.menuText` (the serializer already stored it; menuText is the fallback for older saves, which kept the two identical).
- **Fixed a ~10-second frozen-UI pause loading a 20,000-frame saved sitch** (`src/nodes/CNodeTrack.js`, new test `tests/MISBExportInspect.test.js`). `CFileManager.makeExportButton` calls the export function with `inspect=true` at node CREATION time purely to build the button tooltip (header + one example row), and `exportMISBCompliantCSV` ran its FULL per-frame loop — including the per-frame FrameCenter ground intersection — in inspect mode: a single 10.1 s main-thread long task during load. The elevation-marcher change exposed this pre-existing bug (the old mesh raycast was coincidentally near-free at setup because no terrain tiles were loaded yet). Fix: inspect mode bounds the loop to one data row — loop bound only, the truth central-difference window clamp stays on `this.frames`, so the example row is byte-identical to row 1 of a real export. Measured on that load: total main-thread blocking fell from 12.2 s to 2.6 s. The new regression test pins the contract with a `frameCenterGroundPoint` spy: exactly 1 call in inspect mode, N calls in a real export, and byte-identical inspect row vs the real export's first row.

## Version 2.103.1 (2026-07-20)

### Improvements
- **No basemap imagery fetches under Google Photorealistic 3D Tiles** (`e9fd3f27`) (`src/nodes/CNodeTerrainUI.js`, `src/QuadTreeTile.js`, `src/QuadTreeMapTexture.js`). Google Photorealistic 3D Tiles fully replace the 2D basemap — `updateTerrainAndOceanVisibility()` hides the terrain group — yet every basemap tile still fetched its texture into the invisible group: a ground-level Google-3D scene was observed streaming ~605 ESRI tiles (20 at zoom 20), and the out-of-coverage zoom-20 tiles were the source of session-long "blocked by CORS / ERR_FAILED" console spam. A new `CNodeTerrainUI.suppressMapImagery()` predicate (true only when the buildings source is `google-photorealistic` — Cesium OSM buildings are an additive layer and keep their basemap) is consumed by `QuadTreeTile.applyMaterial()`, which early-returns with the shared placeholder `tileMaterial` (no allocation, no fetch) while still calling `addAfterLoaded()`, so geometry consumers — the ocean surface, ground-overlay drapes, terrain raycasts — are unaffected (the elevation quadtree is separate and untouched). `applyParentDataFallback()` is gated on the same predicate (optional-chained for unit tests without a terrainNode), since it was still building ~800 canvas-rescaled parent textures for the invisible group despite zero network. `updateTerrainAndOceanVisibility()` tracks the suppress→unsuppress transition via a new `mapImagerySuppressed` field and on un-suppress calls `terrainNode.reloadMap()` + `requestSubdivisionPass()`, so turning the buildings off re-textures the basemap without needing a camera nudge. Measured: ESRI fetches in that scene went from ~605 to 1 (the pre-buildings zoom-0 tile).
- **One retry for transient tile-texture failures** (`e9fd3f27`) (`src/QuadTreeTileMaterial.js`). Both texture-load call sites — the dynamic per-tile path and the static/shared-texture mipmap path — change from `loadTextureWithRetries(url, 0, 100, …)` to `maxRetries 1, delay 500`, so a single transient network failure (e.g. browser resource starvation while Google 3D tiles stream) no longer permanently dead-branches a tile. The static path matters most: that one shared URL serves all tiles, so a single blip previously blanked the entire layer. Deterministic failures (ESRI `PlaceholderTile`) skip the retry inside the loader (see below), so the retry adds no waste there.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed the Terrain Refresh button doing nothing on non-dynamic terrain** (`e9fd3f27`) (`src/nodes/CNodeTerrainUI.js`). With **Dynamic Subdivision** off, `doRefresh()` set `startLoading` and flagged a recalculation — but `recalculate()` early-outs when lat/lon/zoom/nTiles are all unchanged, which is exactly the state after clicking Refresh, so failed tiles were never rebuilt or re-fetched. `recalculate()` has a designed escape hatch for this — the `refresh` flag, checked in its early-out and cleared when consumed — but nothing ever set it. `doRefresh()` now sets `this.refresh = true` before `flagForRecalculation()`, forcing the full dispose-and-rebuild path; it also clears `badTextureUrls` and calls `ServiceAvailability.reset()` at the top of both branches (the dynamic branch gets them via `reloadMap()`, the non-dynamic branch bypassed it). Verified rebuilding the full 8×8 grid with 64 fresh fetches.
- **Fixed known-dead tile URLs being re-fetched on every subdivision churn** (`e9fd3f27`) (`src/js/map33/material/QuadTextureMaterial.js`, `src/QuadTreeMapTexture.js`, `src/QuadTreeMap.js`, `src/QuadTreeTileMaterial.js`). The `badTextureUrls` set was write-only — URLs were added on failure but never checked — so tiles pruned and later reactivated by subdivision churn re-requested the same dead URL every time (the observed repeat-fetch storm for out-of-coverage ESRI tiles at max zoom; the reactivation catch only silenced Aborted/cancelled). `loadTextureWithRetries` now pre-flights: if every candidate URL is in `badTextureUrls`, it rejects `'KnownBadUrl'` immediately with no network. Terminal failures are recorded centrally in the loader (`badTextureUrls.add(currentUrl)`); the `QuadTreeTileMaterial` catch no longer adds to the set, so mipmap/color-processing errors can't poison the URL blacklist. All four consumers treat `'KnownBadUrl'` like `'PlaceholderTile'` — silent `isDeadBranch = true` in `QuadTreeMapTexture`'s main and reactivated-tile catches, dead branch instead of `needsHighResLoad = true` (an infinite retry against the blacklist) in `QuadTreeMap`'s lazy high-res upgrade, silenced in `QuadTreeTileMaterial`. `PlaceholderTile` is now recognized as deterministic inside the loader — the server returns the same placeholder bytes every request — so it skips the fallback-URL and retry ladders. The failure log also now reports `after ${currentAttempt + 1} attempt(s)` instead of the misleading `after ${maxRetries} attempts`, which printed "after 0 attempts". Verified: destroying 27 dead placeholder tiles and re-refining re-marked all 27 dead with 0 new fetches.
- **Fixed a tripped imagery-service circuit breaker never recovering within a session** (`e9fd3f27`) (`src/nodes/CNodeTerrain.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeTerrainUI.js`). Once `ServiceAvailability` tripped (5 consecutive failures), its pre-flight rejected every subsequent request, so no success could ever be recorded and the service stayed "unavailable" until a full page reload — despite the error toast promising "Use the Refresh button in the Terrain menu to retry when connectivity is restored". `ServiceAvailability.reset()` existed but had zero callers. `CNodeTerrain.reloadMap()` — the chokepoint for Refresh (dynamic), map/elevation-type changes, and imagery un-suppression — now clears `badTextureUrls` and calls `ServiceAvailability.reset()`, and `doRefresh()` does the same for the non-dynamic branch. If the outage persists, real traffic re-trips the breaker after 5 failures, which is the intended behavior.

## Version 2.103.0 (2026-07-20)

### New Features
- **Set-aside (dismiss) gallery tiles** (`ee6e375c`) (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`). The **Analyze Traverse Methods...** gallery gains a per-tile X that "sets aside" a candidate: it flies out through the bottom (0.3 s) while the grid holds, then survivors slide into place (0.3 s FLIP). Set-aside tiles move below a separator rather than being deleted, stay fully readable, and keep their original ranking, so nothing is silently dropped; in that area the button becomes an up-arrow that returns the tile through the top. A **Restore set-aside** button (with a live count, placed next to Sync Scale) brings them all back. Setting aside the *selected* tile moves selection to whatever fills the gap (next in group → left → nearest on screen → stay put); group headings vanish when a group empties; respects `prefers-reduced-motion`; and clicks queue rather than being dropped mid-animation.
- **Deterministic Polynomial LSQ fit and polynomial-order sweep tiles** (`ee6e375c`, default revised by `2c63caa6`) (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `src/LOSFitting.js`, `benchmarks/losFitting.bench.test.js`). The deterministic `fitAlternatingLSQ` *Global Fit: Polynomial LSQ* is swept over polynomial orders 1–5 by default. The two Monte Carlo strategies are included across the same orders only when **Monte Carlo order sweep (diagnostic)** is enabled, avoiding ten expensive diagnostic fits in ordinary analyses. The sitch's single shared "MC Polynomial Order" control (`mcOrder`) is almost always left at its default 1 — where the curve is a straight line in time — so the effect of order was otherwise invisible and both Monte Carlo tiles were near-duplicates of the constant-velocity fit. `fitAlternatingLSQ` is deterministic (no RNG): the problem is nonlinear only in the range along each ray, so it fixes ranges, does an ordinary-least-squares polynomial fit, re-projects onto the rays, and repeats (time normalised to [−1,1] so t⁵ doesn't put ~1e14 into the solve). It is ~150–250× faster than Monte Carlo 2, lower-residual at nearly every order, and — unlike the two Monte Carlo fits, which sample range only within 0.9–1.1× of the constant-velocity seed — lets range move freely so a far/fast solution stays reachable. The tiles are method diagnostics, not object rankings, and carry the **Geometric Approximations** label.

### Improvements
- **Flat best-first traverse gallery with per-tile category labels** (`6944a0be`) (`src/TraverseRanking.js`, `tests/TraverseRanking.test.js`). The **Analyze Traverse Methods...** gallery (Traverse menu) is reordered into one flat screening list (`rankAllHypotheses` + a cross-category `makeComparator(true)`) instead of the 2.101.0 grouped sections that could bury a strong-screening candidate. Each tile carries a coloured category label from `RANKING_CATEGORIES` — **Physically based**, **LOS Constrained**, **Geometric**, **Geometric Approximations**, **Known Object** — assigned by `hypothesisCategory()` from the hypothesis key. Without usable truth, the cross-comparable gates are broad-screen pass, eligibility, completeness, tier, and active-pin count; category is only a tiebreak after those gates, never a class of truth or object probability. With usable truth, completed fits lead optimizer-incomplete fits and are then ordered by separation from the external reference. Each item still records its within-category rank (`groupIndex`/`groupSize`) so a tile can still say "#1 of 4 physically based".
- **Fit quality and motion ordinariness are separate judgements** (`6944a0be`) (`src/TraverseRanking.js`). Collapsing both into one "plausibility" tier produced false verdicts: a 6 kt / 0.09 g quadcopter drifting downwind — the closest of five candidates to the truth track — was branded "Implausible" for missing an absolute 0.5° residual threshold, while a 12 g solution that threaded the rays shared a badge and a sort slot with one that simply diverged. The tier is still the *worse* of `fitRank` (`err > 0.5 ? 0 : > 0.15 ? 1 : > 0.05 ? 2 : 3`) and `kinematicRank` (from peak g / peak speed), so ordering and eligibility are unchanged, but the LABEL now names whichever dimension binds: `tierForFitRank` gives "Poor fit" / "Weak fit" / "Fair fit" / "Passes broad screen"; `tierForRank` gives the kinematic wording ("Kinematically extreme", …). The slow-drone case therefore now reads **Weak fit**, not "Implausible". Both `fitRank` and `kinematicRank` are kept on the result record so callers can report which is binding.
- **Balloon-consistency ranking nudge** (`6944a0be`) (`src/TraverseRanking.js`, `tests/TraverseRanking.test.js`). New `balloonConsistency(track) = min(verticalDirectness, horizontalStraightness)` — a passive wind tracer needs BOTH a steady vertical trend and a one-direction drift, and taking the *weaker* of the two (not the average) means a monotonic climb cannot excuse a circling drift. Buoyant hypotheses (`BUOYANT_KEYS = {lantern}`) get a bounded `±BALLOON_CONSISTENCY_NUDGE` (=6) adjustment to `secondaryScore` (`nudge · (1 − 2·balloonC)`: promote when balloon-like, demote the same amount when the fitted motion reverses vertically or curves back on itself). Because the comparator consults `secondaryScore` only *after* the fit-quality tier ties, the nudge can reorder equally-good fits but cannot lift a balloon over a clearly better-fitting energetic or maneuvering solution. High (≥0.75) or low (≤0.45) consistency adds an explanatory line to the detail pane. Tests cover the five categories, flat ordering, the fit/kinematic badge split, and the consistency metric (high/low scoring, symmetric promote/demote, overturns-a-modest-edge, the tier-gate guardrail, hover-neutral).
- **Time-varying wind for the Sky Lantern / Balloon model, with geometric seed inversion** (`3102b28d` + disclosure follow-up) (`src/SkyLanternModel.js`, `src/LOSFitting.js`, `src/TraverseWind.js`, `src/DroneControlFit.js`, `tests/SkyLanternModel.test.js`, `tests/TraverseWind.test.js`). `SkyLanternModel` gains a smooth quadratic time-varying wind — new params `windDriftE/N` and `windCurveE/N` (the linear and quadratic *change* in each wind component across the whole clip, normalised on `clipDuration` so they are duration-invariant), priced by a variability prior (`_windVariationCost`, the "wind variability" cost term) — so a balloon can follow a roughly-straight-but-*curving* drift that a single constant wind cannot. A shared reconstruction helper now feeds both air-relative metrics and disclosure: tile/report prose gives start/midpoint/end wind and the profile table shows those three fitted winds across altitude, so the UI cannot silently display only the base wind. That extra freedom is unsearchable blind at the shipping differential-evolution budget, so `seedFromTrack`/`seedParams` invert a geometric track into the 12 balloon parameters. `fitPhysicsModel.fitMaxDt` still coarsens the search integration only; the final trajectory uses the model's native step.
- **Physical fits seed from the Kalman smoother; ~70× faster drone fit** (`443df13c`, completion disclosure follow-up) (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`). The balloon (free + measured wind) and drone control-input fits now seed from the Kalman smoother (`fitKalmanFilter`, a regularised, acceleration-penalising estimator) rather than whichever geometric track had the lowest LOS residual. Because LOS range is degenerate, the smoother's constant-velocity start has an explicit 500 m range floor; regularisation alone does not make collapse impossible. The seed carries no truth labels but can affect the retained local basin. The free quadcopter stays deliberately unseeded. Once seeded, the drone fit becomes a local-refinement problem: Nelder-Mead straight from the seed, `fitMaxDt` 1 s, `sampleStride` 20, `maxIter` 400 — about a second, down from tens of seconds. Reaching that cap is now disclosed as optimizer-incomplete, makes the tile ineligible, and places it after completed fits even in truth mode; it is no longer presented as convergence.
- **Both balloon wind reconstructions are now built and labelled by provenance** (`ee6e375c`) (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeDisplayWindField.js`). The two-mode balloon reconstruction shipped in 2.102.0 (`17f7d67a`) but was gated to `windField.source !== "manual"`, and almost every sitch uses manual wind, so the wind-pinned second hypothesis was never built and the split was invisible. The gate is dropped; the pinned variant now carries its provenance and is named for it — **(measured wind)** for a sounding/GFS profile, **(sitch wind)** for a hand-set constant — with notes stating plainly that the assumed-wind variant is an assumption and the free (wind-finding) fit is the stronger evidence (`windPolicy` taxonomy: free-diagnostic / measured-corrected / assumed-wind). It honours the existing "Wind for analysis" toggle (Zero wind suppresses the pinned variant entirely — verified 16 tiles vs 15), and `windField` source/status/version now join the analysis cache fingerprint since the field steers a hypothesis (previously the key covered only `targetWind`, a different node).
- **Fits disclose their soft priors; reported error is pure angular error** (`cf1f6f20`) (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `src/PhysicsModel.js`, `src/SkyLanternModel.js`, `src/FixedWingModel.js`, `src/QuadcopterModel.js`, `src/LOSFitting.js`, `src/TraverseAnalysis.js`, `tests/PhysicsPriorDisclosure.test.js`, `tests/TraversePlausibleIdentity.test.js`). Each physics model now itemises its soft priors (`params.priors`, per-term costs) and a tile shows a **Model priors cost** line — e.g. "0.043° (calm-wind preference …)" — whenever the total exceeds 0.005°; the reported `errDeg` is recomputed as pure angular error and deliberately *excludes* prior cost, so a soft prior can no longer help select a solution while leaving no trace in the printed number (a tile could otherwise say a wind was "inferred, not assumed" when a prior helped choose it). The itemised `extraCostTerms` is a parallel implementation, not the source of `extraCost`, so reporting cannot perturb the search (`tests/PhysicsPriorDisclosure.test.js` asserts the two agree across every branch); disclosure covers FixedWingModel's turn/climb priors (the anomaly-side counterpart) too. The same commit also closes two range-floor holes where a fit could collapse onto the sensor's own path (the closed-form perpendicular-distance objective reaches an exact zero there): `prepareSweep`'s constant-velocity seed now uses `minRange: 500` (that seed sets the ±10% sampling window for all ten Monte Carlo tiles), and `traversePlausible`'s `minDist` floor — previously gated on a `rangeFloor` flag the two hot callers never passed — now actually runs. A `trackMetrics` heading is magnitude-guarded (0.05 m/s) so a true hover reports zero turn rate instead of atan2 noise that fed a fictitious turn rate into `straightFlightScore` (the search objective, not just display). And the innermost `traversePlausible` solver is ~2.3–2.6× faster (scratch-buffer row assembly, cached b-spline basis), verified byte-identical — it is an IRLS solve, so a reordered accumulation could re-tune the slow-vs-fast regime gate; `tests/TraversePlausibleIdentity.test.js` hashes the raw float64 output across all five option-gated branches.
- **Physics-fit optimizers cooperate per evaluation; non-finite fits fail closed** (`5b2676ee`, Layer-2 follow-up) (`src/DifferentialEvolution.js`, `src/NelderMead.js`, `src/LOSFitting.js`, `src/TraverseAnalysis.js`, `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `tests/OptimizerCooperation.test.js`). `differentialEvolution`, `nelderMead`, and `patternSearchPolish` are now `async` and take an `onEvaluation` callback invoked after every objective evaluation — including the initial population/simplex — so `fitPhysicsModel`/`fitAircraft` yield and check `shouldCancel` between individual full-clip trajectory integrations on a ~60 ms wall-clock budget (`optimizerPulse`), not just at generation boundaries. (2.101.0 checked cancellation only per generation, and Nelder-Mead / pattern-search polish were synchronous with no cancellation.) One objective evaluation still runs to completion (atomic), but a physics or fixed-wing fit now stops within a fraction of a second regardless of clip length or generation cost (TA-26). The same pass makes the search fail closed rather than open (TA-02): every optimizer normalises a non-finite objective to `Infinity`; `fitPhysicsModel`'s `costFn` returns `Infinity` (was a `1e10` sentinel) when the mean angular error, a prior term, or the total is non-finite, and a **diverged ground-prior end-state integration now costs `Infinity`** instead of being silently caught and left the candidate spuriously cheaper; `fitPhysicsModel` returns `null` (a typed numerical failure) when `result.cost`, `errDeg`, or any non-excluded residual is non-finite; and `fitAircraft` throws — caught by `runTraverseAnalysis` as a `Fixed-Wing Aircraft` failure that keeps the rest of the gallery — when the optimizer yields a non-finite solution, trajectory, or residual. `tests/OptimizerCooperation.test.js` pins mid-search cancellation in all three optimizers and the non-finite→Infinity normalisation.

### Bug Fixes
- **Terrain finishing load mid-analysis is non-fatal** (`443df13c`, cache-tolerance follow-up) (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `src/TraverseAnalysisCache.js`). Terrain tiles can finish loading while Analyze Traverse Methods runs; the analysis records the local-ground and candidate-corridor samples that actually affect fitting/grading instead of aborting. A later equal-or-higher-resolution sample that changes by more than 0.1 m invalidates the cached grading; ordinary millimetre/centimetre interpolation differences between adjacent display LODs are treated as the same surface, preserving immediate cache hits. The pre-run guard for a genuinely half-loaded initial terrain remains.
- **Quadcopter fit no longer solves a physically absurd spin** (`aa871662`) (`src/QuadcopterModel.js`, `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `tests/QuadcopterModel.test.js`). The free Quadcopter physics fit was drifting with the wind while spending its free heading parameters on an enormous *unpriced* spin — measured on the Generated Orbit Test sitch: `turnRate` −23.9°/s with `turnAccel` −2.51°/s², a 574,464° heading sweep (~1,596 revolutions over 11 min, ending at ~4.7 rev/s) — invisible to the LOS residual because a craft circling at radius v/ψ′ is sub-metre once ψ′ is large, i.e. ~1e-5° at kilometre range. Nothing priced it (`turnRate` had no prior; the `turnAccel` prior was ~0.02 cost units against a data term of tens). A new root-mean-square turn-rate prior (`_turnEffortCost`, reference `TURN_EFFORT_REF` = 20°/s, closed-form mean square `(a²+b²+a·b)/3` in the two endpoint rates so it is duration-invariant) *prices* turning rather than bounding it (a bound tight enough to stop the spin would forbid a genuinely agile drone). Result on that sitch: 1,596 → 23.9 revolutions, end rate −1,700°/s → 1.07°/s, and the fit actually *improves* (0.5665° → 0.5408°) — the tell that the spin was unpriced noise occupying a direction the residual couldn't see, not fit quality; the solution is now a drone in ~4.8 m circles at 6.3 kt air speed carried by 24 kt wind at 0.09 g. `FixedWingModel` is deliberately untouched (it already penalises turn rate at both endpoints, which for a linear rate bounds the whole profile).
- **Truth Track dropdown no longer empties after a sitch reload** (`aa871662`) (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`). The **Truth Track** dropdown (2.102.0) was permanently empty from the second sitch load onward: its `tracksChanged` listener was registered behind a once-per-session guard, but `EventManager.removeAll()` on sitch dispose wiped it and the guard blocked re-registration, leaving only the build-time refresh (which runs *before* the sitch's tracks deserialize) to populate it. The listener is now re-registered on every rebuild, dropping the prior registration first so repeat calls can't accumulate.
- **Traverse graphs no longer alias a fast oscillation into a fake slow wave** (`aa871662`) (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`). Chart downsampling point-sampled every step-th frame (~29 frames / 0.97 s per sample), so a fast oscillation aliased into a slow "beat" that read as a real low-frequency wave. Downsampling now emits a per-bucket **min/max envelope** (two samples per bucket, min then max), collapsing to the original line where the data is smooth.
- **Traverse analysis survives a wind node with no grid** (`ee6e375c`) (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeDisplayWindField.js`). On some legacy sitches (e.g. agua) the wind node exists with source "manual" but its grid was never built, so `windU`/`windV` were null and `gridNx` was 0, and `sampleWind` indexed a null array with a NaN index and crashed the run. `sampleWind` now returns null when there is no grid or the inputs aren't finite (matching `sampleWindAtAltitude`'s documented contract), the one caller that dereferenced without checking is guarded, and the analysis treats any wind failure as "no wind-pinned hypothesis" rather than aborting the whole run.
- **Line Color picker works on balloon, synthetic and sonde tracks** (`cd1d0813`) (`src/TrackManager.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeDisplayTrack.js`, `src/i18n/en.js`). Three separate causes behind "Line Color does nothing": (1) `new Color({r,g,b})` silently returns white (Three's `Color.set()` handles only a Color, a number, or a string — a plain object leaves the default `0xffffff`), so both `CNodeDisplayTrack.recalculate()` fallbacks (bad point = red, outside A/B = dim grey) rendered white — now real Color objects; (2) balloon and synthetic display tracks lacked `ignoreAB: true` (which imported tracks set), so a sitch with `Sit.bFrame` at 0 painted the whole track in the out-of-range (white) colour while the node's own `lineColor` stayed correct — the picker only looked broken; (3) sonde tracks colour through `CNodeSondeColor` (computed per-frame from atmospheric data), which ignored the picker's `CNodeConstant`, so Line Color was a structural no-op — picking a colour now flips the node to `colorMode "none"` with that flat `fallbackColor`, and a new **Color Mode** dropdown (Flat / Temperature / Altitude / Pressure; i18n `track.colorMode`, round-trips through `modSerialize`/`modDeserialize`) restores the gradient. Also: gradient-track folder swatches now sample mid-track instead of frame 0 (surface air, so every sonde folder came out the same orange while the track was mostly the cold blue/cyan of altitude); Line Color now writes the value before setting the folder label (was one edit behind); and auto colours for user-created tracks step through `TRACK_PALETTE` via `nextPaletteColor()` instead of hardcoded orange (balloons) / traverse-yellow (synthetic). Serialized tracks always carry their own colour, so existing sitches are unaffected.

## Version 2.102.0 (2026-07-17)

### New Features
- **Physics → Scenarios menu with activation-gated scenarios** (`d31db80e`, `4f53bc98`, `27e079fc`) (`src/CScenarioManager.js`, `src/index.js`, `src/CustomManagerSetup.js`, `src/CustomManagerSerialize.js`). Football, Nimitz, Gimbal Analysis, and Flood Sim are now sequestered under a single **Physics → Scenarios** folder behind a common lifecycle (`CScenarioManager`). An un-activated scenario is a 100% no-op — no nodes, nothing in the Objects/Contents/Camera menus, no event listeners, no per-frame cost, no serialization footprint, and (via webpack dynamic `import()`) its code is not even fetched. Folder contents populate lazily on first open (`onOpenClose` → `populate()` builds only the Enable/Load buttons); activation creates everything else. A fresh custom sitch drops from 556 to 391 nodes and its camera dropdowns return to stock. `deserializeMods()` activates any scenario a loading save was actually *using* (detected from real-usage signals — Show flags, camera-switch choices, `floodEnabled` — not node-id presence) before the mod loop and before `remapLegacyTrackMods`, so a scenario-provided switch choice like "Fravor's Jet" survives load instead of being remapped to "orbitCamera".
- **Nimitz tic-tac encounter reconstruction** (`4f53bc98`, `d31db80e`) (`src/Nimitz.js`, `docs/Nimitz.md`). New **Physics → Scenarios → Nimitz** scenario: an interactive 4D reconstruction of the 14 Nov 2004 encounter as *verbally* described by Fravor/Dietrich (not the FLIR1 video) — two F/A-18Fs over a whitewater disturbance, a jinking tic-tac, ships (LCS placeholders for CVN-68/CG-59), and a CAP beacon. **Load Nimitz Encounter (Nov 14, 2004)** and a compressed (~1 min) variant configure location, 1430 PST date/time, a 420 s timeline, ships, and POV wiring. Selectable hypothesis modes — **Object Maneuvers (as described)** vs **Stationary Object (parallax)** — swap only the commanded turn rate; `CNodeNimitzJetTrack` integrates one C1 kinematic state (position/heading/turn-rate) at constant airspeed with a bounded turn-rate slew (max frame-to-frame speed change 0.0002 m/s over the flight), and the circle phase pins true LLA altitude to avoid a roll-out velocity spike. A read-only **Turn Bank / G** line shows the bank/load factor implied by the radius and intercept rate (ω·v = g·tan φ). Fravor/Dietrich camera POVs, a gated "Look At Tic-Tac" heading source, and a "Nimitz Pilot" FOV are provided, and both hypotheses ship as preset **Scripting** presentation tabs written in the DSL form. Every number is sourced in `docs/Nimitz.md`.
- **Football goal-kick simulator with Spidercam wire-strike replication** (`27e079fc`, `2744d734`, `48b92e63`) (`src/Football.js`, `docs/Football.md`, `data/images/footballPitch.png`, `src/nodes/CNodePositionLLA.js`). New **Physics → Scenarios → Football** scenario: launch a ball from a point on a selected track with ballistic physics (quadratic drag + Magnus from spin, 1/480 s substeps), a keyframed Spidercam cable-cam (dolly, aim, four sagging wires) with segment-segment wire collision, and a draped regulation pitch overlay (`footballPitch.png`, 105×68). **Load England-Norway Goal Kick (WC 2026)** reconfigures the whole sitch to replicate the Hard Rock Stadium wire-gate incident. **Wire Hit Offset (%)** slides the impact point sideways; both collide/miss outcomes are always simulated (the non-selected one draws as a grey ghost track + transparent ghost ball); a per-frame IMU-style **Ball G-Force** (kick ~360 g for one frame, wire graze tens of g, drag ~1.5 g) feeds a rolling 2 s strip-chart graph. A 720p-capped **Broadcast camera** view shares the look-view tile set and joins the Camera position/heading/FOV switches. The follow-up commit aims the broadcast camera midway between ball and pitch-center for steadier framing, adds a **Broadcast Camera Position** source selector (default Manual Position) that rides any imported track (`CNodeTrackSwitch` self-registers as a drop target; Manual Lat/Lon/Alt/AGL grey out while a track drives it), adds a **Wires in Look View** flag (default off), and re-fits the keys against the real-time 60 fps clip (heading 121.4°, kick at 0.583 s, ~14–15 m/s dolly). `CNodePositionLLA` gained a `locationTools: false` flag so the embedded broadcast camera gets a compact Lat/Lon/Alt/AGL folder (no Lookup/Geolocate/Go To); the Camera/Target location menus are unchanged (default on).
- **Add Balloon — synthetic wind-driven balloon target** (`0f4fe6d7`) (`src/BalloonPhysics.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeBalloonTrack.js`, `src/CustomManagerMenus.js`, `src/i18n/en.js`). New **Add Balloon** entry in the ground right-click (Drop Pin) context menu creates a rising, wind-advected balloon target — launch delay, buoyancy, and gust variability, with altitude-aware wind profiles (constant-wind fallback), rendered as a 0.5 m sphere. Serialized (save/load) and undoable. Backed by the new pure `BalloonPhysics.js` (unit-tested in `tests/BalloonPhysics.test.js`).
- **Tracking Wobble camera controller** (`0f4fe6d7`) (`src/nodes/CNodeControllerTrackingWobble.js`, `src/TrackingWobbleMath.js`, `src/i18n/en.js`). New **Tracking Wobble** folder (Camera menu, default off): a seeded, deterministic controller on the look camera that simulates imperfect manual tracking — the camera drifts off target and is imperfectly re-centered. Controls: **Amplitude (deg)**, **Drift Speed (deg/s)**, **Reaction Time (s)**, **Recenter Speed (deg/s)**, **Recenter Accuracy**, **Random Seed**. The wobble flows into both the rendered look view and the exported line-of-sight, and is kept last in the controller order when per-track angle controllers attach. Math pinned by `tests/TrackingWobbleMath.test.js`.
- **Synthetic truth import/export and Truth Track comparison** (`0f4fe6d7`) (`src/MISBUtils.js`, `src/MISBFields.js`, `src/TrackExportMath.js`, `src/TrackFiles/CTrackFileMISB.js`, `src/TrackManager.js`, `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `src/TraverseAnalysis.js`, `src/TraverseRanking.js`, `src/Chart3D.js`). The MISB CSV export (Lines of Sight) now also writes a FrameCenter ground track (terrain-raycast, MSL) and `truth_lat`/`truth_long`/`truth_alt`(ft)/`truth_heading`/`truth_speed` from the target track. `truth_*` columns re-import as a derived **Truth** track, with a per-track **Source Altitude is Meters** toggle (default feet). Traverse Analysis gains a **Truth Track** dropdown; when a truth track is selected, method ranking and rank-basis prose become truth-based, and the truth polyline appears in the 3D charts and the Full Report. Covered by `tests/MISBExportTruth.test.js`, `tests/ParseMISB1CSVTruth.test.js`, `tests/TraverseAnalysis.test.js`, `tests/TraverseRanking.test.js`.
- **Perspective (Homography) panorama projection** (`7400cc90`) (`src/CMotionAnalysisUI.js`, `src/i18n/en.js`). New **Projection** dropdown in **Video → Motion Analysis → Panorama → Motion Pano Options** with **Similarity (2D)** (default, unchanged) and **Perspective (Homography)**. A pan/tilt/zoom camera's true inter-frame mapping is a homography; the similarity-only stamping deviates ~0.4–1.2 px/frame at the corners, and that systematic error chains over hundreds of frames into visible misregistration between the live frame and the pano behind it in "Export Motion Pano Video". Perspective mode fits `cv.findHomography` (RANSAC) per frame to the full flow-vector set, chains from a middle-frame anchor with per-frame sanity gates and a blow-up fallback to the similarity layout, and stamps frames + the live overlay via `cv.warpPerspective` (canvas `setTransform` is affine-only). Stamps get a feathered alpha edge so warp-border sampling can't accumulate stripe/smear artifacts. Adds a `window.__motionPano` MCP/testing hook.
- **Rolling last-N-seconds window for custom graphs** (`4a344867`) (`src/CCustomGraphManager.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeCustomGraphView.js`). New per-graph **Show Last (secs)** setting (0 = whole clip): plot only the last N seconds up to the current frame, scrolling off to the left during playback. The x axis holds a constant N-second span (`view.fixedXRange`) and the y scale is fixed from the entire timeline so it doesn't jump as features scroll in/out. Serialized with the graph; 50 ms refresh throttle while windowed. Also registers the football ball g-force as a graph data source (only while "Show Football" is on, so it doesn't clutter unrelated sitches), and fixes `addGraph` building its dropdowns before refreshing sources — a freshly added graph never listed conditional sources because the registration's version bump was swallowed into its cached registry version.
- **Balloon traverse reconstruction: inferred (free) vs measured wind** (`17f7d67a`) (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `src/SkyLanternModel.js`). Reframes the Sky Lantern / Balloon hypothesis around the fact that a balloon traverse can be reconstructed with or without knowing the wind. The primary **FREE wind** reconstruction (the existing lantern fit, now explicitly labelled the inferred-wind reconstruction) fits wind + lift together with no measured-wind input. When a *measured* (non-manual) altitude-resolved wind field is loaded, a secondary **MEASURED wind** reconstruction is offered, softly pinning drift wind to the sampled sounding/GFS profile (via the existing `windPrior` term; SkyLantern's calm-air penalty is replaced by a prior toward the sampled wind). The detail pane adds an **"Inferred wind profile [vs measured]"** table — the wind the fit requires at each altitude alongside the measured wind — as the plausibility bridge. Both hypotheses share the "lantern" group key via an extracted `lanternHypothesis()` helper. The FREE-wind path and the inferred-vs-measured table are live and verified; the two-hypothesis MEASURED path is code-complete and gated on a measured wind field but still awaits a real-sounding traverse scene for side-by-side visual validation (first increment of the free/measured balloon redesign).
- **Zoom-to-tracks magnifier on the traverse 3D graphs** (`d6c030da`, `c586a489`) (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `src/Chart3D.js`, `tests/Chart3D.test.js`). Every interactive 3D volume graph (tiles, detail pane, fullscreen) gains a magnifying-glass button that reframes the box to the extents of the hypothesis track and truth track only — the platform/sensor path and ray endpoints no longer dictate the scale, which on long-baseline scenes left the tracks a tiny blob. `hypothesisVolumeScene` accumulates a second `zoomBounds` over just the track/truth polylines; `Chart3D.setZoom()` swaps to it and clips every series to the box (Liang-Barsky segment/AABB, exported + unit-tested) so LOS rays draw exactly where they cross the zoomed volume and polylines split into subpaths on exit/re-entry. The follow-up commit makes it a single group control (like Sync Orientation / Sync Scale): one click zooms — or restores — *every* graph to its own track extents, driven by a shared `groupZoomed` state that newly-created charts adopt. The button hides itself on scenes with nothing to zoom to (direction-only hypotheses without truth).
- **"Sync Duration to" (Time menu)** (`0f4fe6d7`) (`src/nodes/CNodeDateTime.js`, `src/i18n/en.js`). New **Sync Duration to** dropdown in the Time menu sets the sitch duration (number of frames) to match the duration of a loaded track.
- **"Hide Some LOS" (Show menu)** (`0f4fe6d7`) (`src/nodes/CNodeDisplayLOS.js`, `src/i18n/en.js`). New **Hide Some LOS** slider (Show menu, serialized) thins the displayed lines of sight: 0 shows all, n shows only every 2ⁿ-th line.

### Improvements
- **Wind now reaches balloon altitudes** (`54912a1e`) (`src/nodes/CNodeAtmosphericProfile.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeDisplayWindField.js`, `src/nodes/WindHelpers.js`). Two altitude-coverage fixes so wind-driven balloons get real wind up to burst (~100k ft) instead of reverting to constant fallback. GFS `WIND_LEVEL_TABLE` is extended from 200 hPa (~38.6k ft) to 10 hPa (~101k ft) with standard-atmosphere heights matching `fetch_wind.py`'s `LEVEL_ALT_FT`; the proxy already served these levels and fetching stays per-altitude, so the stratospheric levels load lazily. For soundings, the IDW blend now *drops* a station above its top valid-wind level (`CNodeAtmosphericProfile.computeTopWindAlt()`) instead of holding and blending its top wind, so a low-topped station can't pollute a high-altitude estimate; coverage degrades gracefully 3→2→1 stations. Verified: at 70k ft on a 3-station UWYO sitch the wind corrected from a polluted 52 kt @ 248° to a real 9 kt @ 79°.
- **Per-layer wind loading indicators and balloon altitude pre-load** (`91f9c803`) (`src/nodes/CNodeDisplayWindField.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeBalloonTrack.js`). Each GFS/custom pressure-level fetch now registers a corner loading indicator (`LoadingManager`, "Wind" category) — the same panel the file loader uses — so a cold layer (10–20 s from NOMADS) is visible rather than silent, and `_fetchLevel` dedupes concurrent fetches of the same level. `CNodeDisplayWindField.ensureLevelsUpToAltitude(ft)` pre-fetches every grid level from the surface up to the one bracketing `ft`; on each re-bake a balloon estimates its peak altitude (`startAlt + ascentRate·climbTime`, +10%) and requests those layers, so lengthening a sitch pulls in the higher layers instead of the balloon reverting to constant fallback. Idempotent; no-op for constant/sounding sources.
- **Faster UWYO sounding loading** (`41115a26`) (`sitrecServer/proxySounding.php`). Missing stations (no launch for the requested date/hour) return HTTP 404 from UWYO (a cold one ~16 s); the proxy used to map every 4xx to an uncached 502, so each missing station was re-fetched on every station walk, and the rate-limit counter incremented on cache hits too, so a walk of 15+ stations blew the 20/min budget and hit spurious 66 s waits. Now: a UWYO 4xx is negative-cached (~16 s → ~0.01 s on repeat), recency-tiered (well-past miss cached ~30 days, recent miss 1 h then recheck; a later success clears a stale miss), 5xx/empty/connection failures stay transient (still 502), and a rate-limit token is consumed only just before the actual UWYO fetch so cache hits never count.
- **Compact traverse comparison report tables** (`593505a5`) (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`). The Comparison table was unreadable — its last column duplicated each candidate card's full "Rank basis" sentence and a global `td:last-child` rule wrapped that prose at 320px, inflating rows and pushing the table past the 1020px page wrap into a horizontal scroll. The Rank basis column and the global last-column wrap rule are dropped (the caption now points to the cards), headers may wrap onto two lines (bottom-aligned) so a long unit-bearing header no longer forces its column wider than the values need, Interpretation/Group text columns are left-aligned, the LOS residual shows degrees only, and tiny mean altitudes no longer print as "-0.0". All four report tables now fit the 1020px wrap with single-line rows and no scrollbars.
- **Scripted-video preview keeps terrain LOD live** (`f7e157e3`) (`src/CScriptedVideo.js`). `startPreview()` used to freeze dynamic subdivision unconditionally, so the live preview showed missing tiles anywhere the scripted camera went that the user hadn't already looked at (e.g. a freshly relocated scenario site froze at ~10 tiles). Subdivision now stays live during preview (verified 10 → 394 tiles streaming in over a Nimitz preview); the old flicker-free freeze is available via a new **Freeze Terrain (Preview)** knob (default off) in the Render Quality folder. The offline MP4 render path is unchanged (it has its own warm-up/settle logic).
- **Add Balloon ~17× faster on long timelines** (`38f4d7c7`, `d3382969`) (`src/CManager.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeBalloonTrack.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeDisplayWindField.js`). Adding a balloon to a 195k-frame sitch took ~15–25 s of synchronous work. Two compounding O(frames × N) costs are removed: (1) `sampleWindAtAltitude()`→`_gatherSondeProfiles()` ran a full `NodeMan.iterate()` over the entire node graph on every per-frame wind query (~195k full-graph scans per bake); the profile-node *reference* set is now memoized against a new `CManager.listVersion` counter (data still read live). (2) One Add Balloon triggered ~5 unconditional full re-integrations; the bake is memoized on a `bakeKey`, so a byte-identical re-bake is skipped. Synchronous add drops 15.7 s → 0.9 s. The companion commit folds `CNodeDisplayWindField.sondeProfileSignature()` (profile count + summed valid-wind tops) into the balloon's wind fingerprint so a balloon saved with a sounding source correctly re-bakes when the asynchronously-loaded soundings arrive (without it, the memoized bake key was unchanged by arriving soundings and a loaded balloon stayed on constant fallback wind).
- **Ground overlay retains draped tile meshes across visibility flips** (`9ed2fe4d`) (`src/nodes/CNodeGroundOverlay.js`). Tile visibility churn (loads, LRU eviction, per-view activation) made `CNodeGroundOverlay` dispose and rebuild its draped meshes on every flip, dominating profiles at 73% of frame time. Built meshes are now retained and just have their layer mask toggled (real dispose only on `tileChanged` / full sync), and the render loop is re-armed only when something actually changed.
- **Eliminated three per-frame render-path hotspots** (`283c0f69`) (`src/CelestialMath.js`, `src/CustomSupport.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeTrackAir.js`). All byte-identical in output (full regression: no new diffs).
