## Version 2.98.1 (2026-07-07)

### Improvements
- **STANAG direct load auto-assigns camera = Platform, target = Ground by role** (`78b7a4cc`) (`src/TrackFiles/CTrackFile.js`, `src/TrackFiles/CTrackFileSTANAG.js`, `src/TrackManager.js`, `docs/Tracks.md`). New `CTrackFile.trackRoleHint(trackIndex)` (base returns `null`) lets a parser declare each sub-track's camera/target role; `CTrackFileSTANAG` maps `posHigh`→`camera`, `posLow`→`target`, `dynamics/pos`→none, and the hint transfers onto the surviving twin during de-duplication (a ground-locked `dynamics/pos` that coincides with `posLow` inherits the target role). `TrackManager.updateDropTargets` now selects the camera/target track switches by role hint when any track of the file declares one, falling back to the old positional −1/−2 `dropTarget` rule otherwise. Previously — after `df0c0561` made `dynamics/pos` the primary track 0 — the ordinal rule put `dynamics/pos` on the camera switch and `posHigh` on the target switch (backwards). `posHigh` is relabelled from **(LOS High)** to **(Platform)** (its per-frame-varying altitude and smooth lat/lon path are a real sensor flight track); `posLow` stays **(Ground)**. Verified live drag-drop: camera = Platform, target = Ground, camera heading = To Target.
- **Track auto-colour palette: 16 light hue-spaced tints** (`f33b3a73`) (`src/TrackManager.js`). Replaces the saturated-primaries `trackColors` palette with 16 light tints (every channel floored well above black) ordered so consecutive entries are far apart in hue, giving auto-coloured tracks better contrast against the dark 3D background. Pure yellow and pure white stay reserved (traverse and sonde colours). Only tracks flagged `paletteColored` are affected; serialized/user colours are untouched. The palette is shared by the initial assignment in `addTracks` and the deterministic `reassignTrackColors()`.
- **Object-menu folder labels: drop `_ob`, wider label, full-name tooltip** (`d4841007`) (`src/nodes/CNode3DObject.js`, `src/lil-gui-extras.js`). The per-object menu folder title strips the internal `_ob` node-id suffix (redundant — every entry in this menu is an object), raises the middle-truncation budget from 20 to 30 chars (the folder title has no CSS ellipsis and would otherwise wrap), and sets the full untruncated name (e.g. `elevated_track (Platform)`) as the folder tooltip. `GUI.prototype.tooltip` in `lil-gui-extras.js` now attaches the native tooltip to the folder title bar (`$title`) rather than the whole folder `domElement`, so the tooltip no longer bleeds over every child row of an open folder — improving every folder tooltip in the app.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed imported tracks and objects sitting at the wrong altitude (MSL vs HAE datum contract)** (`95083986`) — the marquee change. STANAG 4676 `<dynamics cs="WGS_84">` heights are ellipsoidal (HAE), but the MISB track pipeline (`CNodeMISBData` / `CNodeMISBDataTrack`) assumed every altitude was MSL and added the EGM96 geoid offset N on the way to ECEF, sinking such tracks by |N| (~19 m in Colorado). An audit found the same MSL/HAE confusion in several paths. A new serialized `altitudeIsHAE` flag marks sources whose raw altitude is already ellipsoidal; `CTrackFile.isAltitudeHAE(trackIndex)` lets each parser declare it (`CTrackFileSTANAG` reads the `cs` attribute via substring heuristics: egm/msl/orthometric/navd → MSL, wgs/ellipsoid/hae → HAE, absent/unknown → the 4676 ellipsoidal default). `needsGeoidToHAE()` is now the single gate for the MSL→HAE geoid add and returns false whenever the produced value is already HAE — flagged HAE columns, `useAGL` data (terrain HAE already added via `elevationAtLL` in `getRawAlt`), and active altitude locks (HAE per `AltitudeLock.js`) — fixing an AGL/altitude-lock geoid double-count (~16-30 m in CONUS) on both the data-display and export paths. HAE ingestion that was previously lost is recovered: MISB tags 75/78 (SensorEllipsoidHeight / FrameCenterHeightAboveEllipsoid) are used by `selectSourceColumns` as a fallback before AGL when the MSL column is empty (GPS-native KLV now positions correctly), and the Custom1 `TPHAE` column flags its parsed array. `getAltMSL` subtracts N for already-HAE values so `getAltHAE` and all exports stay datum-consistent. The `altitudeIsHAE` datum flag self-heals on reload — the source file is re-parsed and the flag re-derived, and `simpleDeserialize` skips keys absent from old saves — so no datum migration is needed (this covers the altitude datum only, not saved track selection/order). Verified: full Jest 1839 pass (9 new datum tests) + fast visual regression 40/40 unchanged.
- **Fixed KML/MISB-CSV export altitudes (KML absolute = MSL per OGC)** (`95083986`) — the export half of the datum contract. `CNodeTrack.exportTrackKML` and `CNode3DObject.exportToKML` now convert HAE→MSL (EGM96) for KML `absolute` altitude per OGC KML 2.2/2.3, so exports no longer land ~|N| low in Google Earth; KML LineString/Polygon feature import likewise passes `altitudeReference` MSL for absolute mode (matching the gx:Track import path). `exportMISBCSV` writes HAE values into the tag 75/78 columns (MSL column nulled) so a re-import auto-detects the datum instead of sinking ~16 m. `TrackFilterDialog` preview samples are datum-aware; wrong spec-citation comments and `docs/KMLDataSources.md` are corrected.
- **Fixed STANAG files with a ground-locked target loading a duplicate third track** (`df0c0561`) (`src/TrackFiles/CTrackFileSTANAG.js`). A GXP-InMotion STANAG track point carries up to three positions — the authoritative `dynamics/pos` plus proprietary `posHigh`/`posLow`, the high and ground endpoints of the sensor line-of-sight ray — and the parser emitted one Sitrec track per source, so a ground-locked target (where `dynamics/pos` is identical to `posLow`) produced three tracks, two of them duplicates. A new memoized `_distinctTracks()` drops any position source whose full sequence numerically duplicates an earlier one (~1 cm tolerance): ground-locked files now yield 2 tracks, genuinely elevated targets still yield 3. The enumeration is reordered so the authoritative `dynamics/pos` is track 0 (primary) — it now gets the spline smoothing, time-sync and bad-point check that previously keyed off an LOS endpoint, with the `posHigh`/`posLow` LOS endpoints demoted to supplementary geometry.
- **Fixed the first auto-coloured track getting the wrong palette colour** (`f33b3a73`) (`src/TrackManager.js`). The initial assignment used `trackColors[trackNumber % length]`, but `trackNumber` is the manager size AFTER the track is added (1-based), so the first track skipped palette index 0. It now uses `(trackNumber - 1) % length`, matching the 0-based rank used by `reassignTrackColors()`.

## Version 2.98.0 (2026-07-06)

### New Features
- **"Render Camera Use Traverse Track" — display-only traverse tracking in the look view** (working tree) (`src/CustomManagerSetup.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeView3D.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeView3DMouse.js`, `src/i18n/en.js`). A new `CNodeGUIFlag` id `renderCameraTrackTraverse` (label **Render Camera Use Traverse Track**, i18n `nodeLabels.renderCameraTrackTraverse`, default off) in the `cameraHeading` folder (Camera → Heading), created for custom sitches that have a traverse (`Sit.isCustom` + `lookView`/`lookCamera` + `LOSTraverseSelectTrack` or `LOSTraverseSelect`). Created if absent (the `lookCameraBankRoll` precedent), so pre-feature saves get it on load with stock behavior; post-feature saves round-trip it via `CNodeGUIFlag` modSerialize/modDeserialize. When on, the look view's rendered camera aims at the traverse solution — but the LOS, and hence every traverse method's solution, still comes from the selected Camera Heading, verified bit-identical with the flag on vs. off. How the isolation works:
  - `CNodeView3D.applyDisplayLookAt(frame)` / `removeDisplayLookAt(saved)`: save clones of the camera quaternion + up, set up from `getLocalUpVector(camera.position)`, `lookAt(target)`, update matrices; the remove restores the saved clones bit-exactly. Guards: a re-entrancy flag (`_displayLookAtActive`), non-finite targets, and a target within 1 mm of the camera (`distanceToSquared < 1e-6`).
  - Applied in try/finally around the `renderCanvas(frame)` body, so no code outside the render window (node updates, cascades, serialization, PTZ sync) can observe the display orientation. Mirrored into `prepareCameraForLOD`/`restoreCameraAfterLOD` (`_lodSavedDisplayLookAt`, applied before the Camera Tweaks xOffset/yOffset so composition matches render-time order) so terrain tile subdivision evaluates the displayed frustum — no tile gaps in the tracked direction.
  - The three pick entry points in `CNodeView3DMouse` — `_refreshCursorFromMouse`, `onMouseDown`, `onContextMenu` — are wrapped with the same apply/restore (wrapper + renamed `…Inner` pattern; nested calls no-op via the re-entrancy guard), so screen→ray picking, including right-clicking the traverse object, matches the displayed image.
  - `lookView.displayLookAtProvider` is a plain function property, not a node — no graph edges, so nothing can cascade from it. Primary target: `traverseObject._object.getWorldPosition()` — the yellow cube's rendered world position, already final after the node-update sweep (`moveTargetAlongPath`) and including the clampAboveGround offset, with zero node evaluation on this path. Fallback when the cube node is absent: `traverseSmoothedTrack ?? LOSTraverseSelectTrack ?? LOSTraverseSelect` read via `.p(frame)`.
  - The LOS never sees the display orientation because `CNodeLOSFromCamera` replays controllers on its private dummy camera, and every other camera reader runs outside these synchronous apply/restore windows.

## Version 2.97.0 (2026-07-05)

### Improvements
- **Traverse analysis recovers the truth on maneuvering-sensor scenes** (`c8f87b33`) (`src/TraverseAnalysis.js`, `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`). On a synthetic orbit scene (sensor circling a 43 kt straight target) most Analyze Traverse Methods candidates failed badly. Root cause: tracks that ride the LOS rays exactly inherit sensor pointing jitter as range- and fps²-amplified fake g-load, so the smoothness scorers preferred garbage loops over the truth. All ray-following methods (Constant Air Speed, Constant Altitude, Minimum Acceleration, Minimum Speed / saddle) now score and solve on a lightly smoothed off-ray copy (the `traverseMinSpeed` smoothing recipe) and report a small honest LOS residual; **Use This** still applies the exact-LOS live traverse. `plausibilityRating` grades ray-following tiles on a noise-floor-aware effective error (new `effectiveErrDeg`: `max(0, err − max(0.05, 1.5·errFloor))`) so honest noise can't demote badges — physics fits stay raw (a genuinely maneuvering target inflates the floor and would grade itself on a curve). Verified: the orbit scene now reads ~43 kt / level / ~4.4 NM on every method; Gimbal keeps its classic readings; Aguadilla lantern/min-speed unchanged.
- **Constant Air Speed sweep rewritten as a spline QP; slow objects now representable** (`c8f87b33`) (`src/TraverseAnalysis.js` `sweepConstAirSpeed`). The old per-frame ray walk (`traverseConstSpeed`) was an unstable shooting method that exploded into corkscrews at the correct combo on maneuvering-sensor scenes, so the truth could LOSE the sweep. Each (range, speed) combo is now solved as `traversePlausible` with a tight speed sigma (3 kt) on a stride-downsampled dataset (new `downsampleDataset`, ~2500 frames, per-frame wind displacement summed over each stride), lightly smoothed before scoring; a speed-fidelity term `(spdErr/10 kt)²` replaces `badFrames` as the infeasibility signal. The speed grid is now log-spaced 15–650 kt × 32 (the old linear 100–650 kt grid could not express slow truths at all — 43 kt was unrepresentable) and the sweep heatmap's y-axis is log-scaled. A new `constAirSpeedTrack()` builds the display/apply track by upsampling the downsampled QP's range profile (lambda) onto the full-res rays — re-solving at full n/fps re-amplifies jitter (measured 45.8±9.7 kt vs 43.5±0.4 kt on a 43.4 kt truth).
- **Constant Altitude fit reworked** (`c8f87b33`) (`src/TraverseAnalysis.js` `fitConstAltitude`, `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`). Scores the smoothed copy plus an LOS-residual term `(errDeg/sigmaLOSDeg)²` (a smooth-but-off-ray path can't win); an 8-pass bisection descent replaces the parabolic refine, which stalled ~1.5 km short of the best altitude on sharp score valleys; `startDist` is measured on the exact-ray twin (`trackExact`) so `applyHypothesis`'s startDistance → live-node altitude round-trip stays exact. When >20% of frames can't reach the plane (`failed`), the gallery shows a "Fit failed" tile: near-horizontal sightlines never cross a constant-altitude plane, so the hypothesis is meaningless.
- **Minimum Acceleration finds its range from geometry alone when the sensor's motion pins it** (`c8f87b33`) (`src/TraverseAnalysis.js` `fitPlausibleBestRange`/`traversePlausible`, `src/nodes/CNodeLOSFitPlausible.js`). `traversePlausible` gains three option-gated behaviors, all default off (existing callers byte-identical): `accelStride` (strided second-difference acceleration rows — the per-frame stencil is dominated by frame-scale LOS jitter), `rangeFloor` (soft floor at `minDist` 120 m with escalating IRLS weights), and `smoothOutput` (light B-spline post-smooth). `fitPlausibleBestRange` is now two-stage: Stage 1 runs a pure-smoothness (no speed target) coarse log sweep — when the sensor itself maneuvers, geometry alone pins the range and that valley is decisive (median-minus-best margin vs `decisiveMargin` 0.5); Stage 2 falls back to the soft speed-target sweep only on narrow-baseline scenes (e.g. Gimbal) where range is unobservable from geometry. Returns `usedSpeedTarget`/`decisiveness`; the results folder's Speed Target row shows *not needed (geometry)* when geometry picked the range.
- **Physics-model bounds raised to real physical limits, and pinned solutions are called out** (`c8f87b33`) (`src/TraverseAnalysis.js` `fitAircraft`, `src/SkyLanternModel.js`, `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`). The fixed-wing TAS floor drops 50 → 25 kt (FAR Part 103 caps ultralight power-off stall at 24 kt CAS — the old floor silently pinned slow scenes and forced multi-degree LOS errors) and the lantern wind bound widens ±13 → ±20 m/s per component (~39 kt each, 55 kt vector — 40–45 kt aloft is ordinary; the old bound encoded surface-wind intuition and made the fit fake faster drift via the shear multiplier). A solution sitting AT a bound is itself an implausibility signal, and `detailProse` now says so (`tasPinnedTxt`, `windPinnedTxt`: "the sightlines would prefer something slower than any fixed-wing can fly" / "faster drift than the lantern bounds allow").
- **"Least Maneuvering" renamed "Minimum Acceleration"** (working tree) (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `src/MakeTraverseNodesMenu.js`, `src/i18n/en.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeLOSFitPlausible.js`, `docs/TraverseMethods.md`). The old name claimed a result; the new one describes the algorithm's objective (it minimizes acceleration along the path). The gallery tile is **Minimum Acceleration** with subtitle "Acceleration-minimizing path at any range (geometry-picked / soft speed target)"; the Traverse menu displays **Global Fit: Minimum Acceleration** via the switch's display-only `labels` map — the serialized switch KEY "Global Fit: Plausible" is unchanged, so saved sitches load untouched; the results folder is **Minimum Acceleration Fit Results** (i18n `losFitPlausible.folder` + code fallback); detail prose and docs reworded to match.
- **"Chinese Lantern" renamed "Sky Lantern" everywhere** (working tree) (`src/ChineseLanternModel.js` → `src/SkyLanternModel.js`, `src/JetStuff.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeLOSFitPhysics.js`, `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `tests/ChineseLanternModel.test.js` → `tests/SkyLanternModel.test.js`, `docs/TraverseMethods.md`, `tools/SitrecBridge/sitrec-mcp-guide.md`). Class `SkyLanternModel` (`getName()` "Sky Lantern"); the `physicsModelChoice` switch key AND default are now "Sky Lantern" with NO backward-compat key kept — deliberate, the selector is not yet in circulating serialized settings; the gallery tile is **Sky Lantern / Balloon**, `applyHypothesis` selects the new model key, the progress overlay reads "Fitting sky lantern / balloon model...", and the verdict/report prose follows. The `window._traverseDebug` MCP hook now exposes `SkyLanternModel` (guide updated; SitrecBridge extension 1.0.55 → 1.0.56). Historical WhatsNew entries deliberately untouched.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed the analysis gallery on custom sitches missing every live-method contender tile, and "Use This" not switching the method** (working tree) (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`). Both the contender scan in `buildHypotheses` and `applyHypothesis` looked up the traverse switch by the hardcoded id `"LOSTraverseSelect"`, but the custom sitch names it `"LOSTraverseSelectTrack"` — so custom sitches silently got NO Global Fit CV/CA/Kalman/Monte Carlo/Straight Line tiles, and applying any candidate set the sliders but never re-selected the traverse method. New `resolveTraverseSelect()` tries both ids.
- **Fixed the "Best" badge sometimes crowning the wiggliest candidate** (working tree) (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js` `rankTieScore`/`rankHypotheses`). Within a plausibility tier, ties were broken by raw `errDeg` — but among ray-following methods LOS error is anti-correlated with quality (the less a track is smoothed, the tighter it hugs the jittery rays and the higher its fake g), so raw errDeg crowned the least-smoothed track over dead-straight zero-g reconstructions of the same scene. Ties now break on `straightFlightScore(metricsFull) + effectiveErrDeg/0.05` (kinematic cleanliness + floor-aware LOS error). The interactive gallery carried a duplicate copy of the old sort; `showResultGallery` now shares `rankHypotheses` with the report, so tiles and report always agree.
- **Fixed the Minimum Acceleration fit producing tracks that passed BEHIND the camera** (`c8f87b33`) (`src/TraverseAnalysis.js`). Without a range floor the QP could drive the range profile λ negative at close anchor ranges (observed on Aguadilla). `fitPlausibleBestRange` now solves with the new `rangeFloor` option (plus `accelStride` and `smoothOutput`), so both the gallery tile and the live **Global Fit: Minimum Acceleration** traverse are protected.
- **Fixed the Minimum Speed candidate's ±5 kt speed ripple at the clip ends** (`c8f87b33`) (`src/TraverseAnalysis.js` `traverseMinSpeed`). The B-spline's endpoint control points are data-starved, so the raw solution over/undershoots speed at the boundaries (a constant-speed truth read 35–45 kt). After the range-floor pass converges, a few IRLS iterations add speed-level rows over the first/last 15% of frames, pulling boundary air speed toward the interior trimmed median of the first converged iterate (frozen, so the target can't ratchet). Aguadilla accepted-path agreement improves 64 → 34 m.
- **Fixed the slow-object (saddle) explanation claiming all ranges fit equally via a bogus sub-second window** (`c8f87b33`) (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`). The "low-motion window" is now gated on being genuine — LOS rate must dip below 0.35× the median for ≥2 s. On a continuously rotating LOS no such window exists: previously the analysis picked a meaningless sub-second window over which every range trivially fits ("44/44 ranges"); now the solution family comes from the full-clip cost valley and the prose reports the narrow pinned band ("N of M sampled ranges; no low-motion window exists in this clip") — unusually for a slow-object reading, the sensor's own motion triangulates the range.
- **Fixed the sightline noise-floor estimate reading ~90° on straight-flying-sensor scenes** (`c8f87b33`) (`src/LOSFitting.js` `_solveSoftConstrained`, `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`). The free CV/CA fits minimize PERPENDICULAR distance to the rays, so the sensor's own path is a zero-residual trivial solution whenever the sensor flies a CV/CA-representable trajectory — the 2.96.0 noise-floor calibration could collapse onto the sensor itself and report garbage. `_solveSoftConstrained` now honors an opt-in `dataset.minRange` (violations re-targeted to the floor, same penalty machinery as the λ<0 case; existing callers unaffected), and the floor estimate passes `minRange: 500`.

### Documentation
- `docs/TraverseMethods.md` (Help → Documentation → Traverse Methods): the Plausible section is renamed **Global Fit: Minimum Acceleration** with an explicit serialization note (saved sitches still store the "Global Fit: Plausible" key), describes the two-stage geometry-vs-speed-target range search and the noise-robust QP options; the Physics section names **Sky Lantern**; the gallery notes explain the smoothed ray-following tiles, the **Best**-badge ordering, and the Constant Altitude "fit failed" case.

## Version 2.96.1 (2026-07-04)

### Improvements
- **Wrap-around sliders now wrap on arrow keys and the focused-input mouse wheel, not just drag** (`src/js/lil-gui.esm.js`). Controllers marked `.wrap()` — the Time menu's **Month/Day/Hour/Minute/Second/Millisecond** fields, which chain carries into each other (`CNodeDateTime.js`), plus receiver-less wraps like the PTZ **Pan (Az)** slider (`CNodeControllerPTZUI.js`) and the wind **From** sliders (`CNodeWind.js`) — previously wrapped and carried into their `_wrapReceiver` only in the drag path (`setValueFromX`); the `increment` helper serving ArrowUp/ArrowDown on a focused number input, and the mouse wheel while that input is focused (`_inputFocused`), called `_snapClampSetValue` and so stopped dead at min/max. A new `_snapWrapSetValue()` is the step-based counterpart of the drag wrap: on a wrapping controller with both bounds it reduces the value into range with period `max − min + step` — so the two endpoints sit one step apart, matching the integer date/time ranges (0–59, 1–31, …) — and carries ±1 per wrap into the `_wrapReceiver` (Minute 59→0 bumps Hour +1); `increment` now routes through it. Non-wrapping controllers fall through to the old `_snapClampSetValue` (so **Year** still clamps), and the wheel-over-slider handler is unchanged. Verified live: arrow-up on Minute at 59 rolls to 0 and carries Hour +1, arrow-down reverses it, Year clamps as before.

## Version 2.96.0 (2026-07-04)

### New Features
- **Per-solution frame-by-frame diagnostic charts in the traverse analysis** (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`). Every solution in the Analyze Traverse Methods gallery and full report now gets three per-frame charts: maneuvering g-force, speed (air speed, plus a muted ground-speed line whenever wind makes the two differ by more than 1 kt at any sampled point), and per-frame LOS fit error, with the dataset's empirical noise floor drawn as a reference line on the physics fits that carry one. Built by the new `hypothesisSeriesCharts` (per-frame LOS error from the new `losErrorSeriesDeg`; g/speed series read from the already-computed `metricsFull.series` — no extra fitting), rendered as PNG data-URLs through the existing `lineChart` pipeline. Series are trimmed `min(9, n/8)` frames at each end (velocity-smoothing-window edge artifacts) and downsampled to ≤700 points. Shown in the gallery Details pane under a new **Frame-by-frame behaviour** heading and in each of the report's per-solution articles (new `.solution-series` row).
- **Empirical sightline noise-floor calibration** (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`). `buildHypotheses` now fits a free constant-acceleration path to the sightlines (`fitConstantAcceleration` from `LOSFitting.js` — closed-form, deterministic, no object-type assumptions) and takes its mean angular error as the dataset's practical noise floor: a quadratic in time cannot follow camera-solve wander or pointing jitter, so its residual measures error in the sightlines themselves. On Aguadilla this floor reads 0.20° — matching the 0.20° scored by the hand-fitted accepted lantern path; on clean synthetic data both are ~0. Surfaced only when the floor is ≥ 0.02°, so clean sitches render exactly as before: the physics tiles' LOS-error stat row gains a multiple, e.g. the Aguadilla lantern tile reads "0.22° (1.1× floor)" (`hypothesisStats`, now shared by the in-app tiles AND the report cards, which previously built their own rows); the Details-pane/report prose gains a calibration sentence (new `floorContext`, e.g. "essentially AT that floor — the residual is dominated by data noise, not model failure"); the aircraft and lantern plausibility prose branches become floor-aware (lantern threshold `max(0.35, 1.4×floor)`, aircraft `max(0.05, 1.35×floor)`); and the head-to-head verdict paragraph (`buildVerdict`) and the report's methodology notes explain the floor. The plausibility badges and ranking are deliberately unchanged — only the prose and stat annotations calibrate.

### Improvements
- **Chinese Lantern physics model rebuilt as pure wind-drift kinematics** (`src/ChineseLanternModel.js`, complete rewrite). The old model integrated a stiff buoyancy-vs-gravity ODE (buoyancy up to 20 m/s² against 9.81 m/s² gravity, quadratic drag) with a free wind vector scaled by an UNCLAMPED altitude-shear multiplier (`1 + shear·z`, shear ∈ ±0.01/m — could go negative or reach 10–20× aloft) and no plausibility prior; the optimizer exploited that freedom into 60+ kt "lanterns" heading the wrong way, and on Aguadilla (the canonical sky-lantern case) missed the accepted hand-fitted lantern path by over a kilometer. The new model recognizes that a ~100 g lantern is a near-perfect wind tracer: horizontal velocity IS the wind at the lantern's current altitude — a solved wind vector bounded to ±13 m/s (~25 kt) per component with a linear altitude shear clamped to [0.25, 3]× so it can never reverse or blow up; vertical motion is the lantern life cycle, vz = vRise until flame-out time tBurn, then exponential decay (time constant tauCool) toward terminal sink −vSink, where tBurn may fall before, inside, or after the clip. Parameters are [initialRange, windE, windN, shearPerM, vRise, vSink, tBurn, tauCool] — the initialRange/windE/windN names are kept so the existing GUI wind/range-guess seeding still wires up. State is [x, y, z, z0] (the shear reference altitude rides along as a constant so `derivatives()` needs no out-of-band context); the smooth non-stiff kinematics let `maxDt` rise 0.02 → 0.25 s (~7× faster integration; the Aguadilla fit dropped ~8.7 s to ~1.3 s offline). A new `extraCost` adds gentle priors: a light-wind preference, a negative-shear penalty, and a soft sea-level floor evaluated on the closed-form altitude profile `_zAt` (wind never affects z, so z(t) is exact without integrating). Validated on Aguadilla: solved wind ~18 kt from ~65° (ENE), drifting WSW at ~11 kt mean while slowly descending, flame-out solved ≈80 s before the clip (a lantern already in its cooling descent); the fitted track lands within ~35 m of the sitch's hand-fitted lantern spline (old model: >1 km), and the gallery's aircraft-vs-lantern head-to-head verdict now favors the lantern (0.22° vs the fixed wing's 0.73°). A new Jest suite (`tests/ChineseLanternModel.test.js`) locks in the closed-form vertical profile, the shear clamp, the lantern-only parameter bounds, and DE recovery of a synthetic lantern's drift direction, speed regime, and life-cycle phases under a deterministic mulberry32 `Math.random` mock.
- **Global Fit: Physics uses global differential evolution for both models** (`src/nodes/CNodeLOSFitPhysics.js`). Previously the Chinese Lantern used single-start Nelder-Mead while only the Fixed Wing used DE + polish; both cost landscapes are multi-modal, and a single-start simplex reliably falls into the wrong basin. Both models now run DE + Nelder-Mead polish with the same settings as the analysis gallery's fits (`sampleStride 5`, `dePop 48`, `deGens 120`), so applying a gallery physics tile with **Use This** reproduces (statistically) the previewed track.
- **Lantern gallery texts rewritten data-driven** (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`). The old lantern tile/detail prose hard-coded skeptical wording ("far too fast for a lantern", "which no lantern does") written when the old model always produced absurd speeds. The lantern hypothesis' params now carry the solved wind/shear/life-cycle values (shearPerM, vRise, vSink, tBurn, tauCool, clipT) instead of the defunct buoyancy, and `detailProse` reports the solved wind speed and direction, the shear, and which life-cycle phase the clip covers (e.g. "flame-out is 80 s before the clip — a lantern already in its slow cooling descent"), with plausible/implausible wording conditional on the actual residual versus the floor-aware threshold. The range-pinned diagnosis follows the model's raised 30 km (16.2 NM) range ceiling, and the report's methodology paragraph now describes the wind-drift model.
- **Non-physical traverse methods are shown, ranked last** (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, commit 2029469a). A traverse-method contender whose current track is non-physical (finite but absurd speed/g — e.g. a manual Straight Line left at degenerate default endpoints) was previously returned as `null` by `methodNodeHypothesis` and silently hidden from the gallery. It is now shown for completeness, flagged `nonPhysical`, badged **Non-physical**, and ranked below everything else via a new rank −1 tier in `plausibilityRating`/`tierBadge`; **Use This** still applies it, preserving full method↔contender symmetry.
- **Re-running the analysis only recomputes global-fit methods whose inputs changed** (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, commit 2029469a). The gallery reads the live Global Fit nodes (Constant Velocity/Acceleration, Kalman, Monte Carlo) as contenders, but the recalculation cascade does not dirty an unselected fit node when its GUI params change, so the node's own `_dirty` flag can't be trusted — the previous guard force-recomputed every read node on every analysis, re-running the expensive Monte Carlo fits needlessly. Each node's last force-refresh input signature (LOS `analysisFingerprint` + `methodNodeParamSig`, the sorted numeric GUI inputs) is now remembered in a `_methodNodeSig` map and the node recomputes only when that signature changes: an unrelated re-run (e.g. a different A-B frame range) reuses the cached fit; changing a Monte Carlo/Kalman parameter, or the LOS, recomputes it.
- **Local-only traverse debug hook** (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`). `window._traverseDebug` exposes the module-scoped analysis internals (dataset builder, physics fitter, metrics) for console/MCP debugging, gated on `isLocal` at call time inside `addAnalyzeButton` (the binding is still false at module-init). Never present in production builds.

### Documentation
- `docs/TraverseMethods.md`: the Global Fit: Physics section is rewritten to describe both dynamics models, including the new lantern wind-drift kinematics, its life-cycle phases, and why its hard bounds make the residual an honest lantern-likeness measure.

## Version 2.95.1 (2026-07-04)

### Improvements
- **Traverse method names now display consistently in every sitch** (`src/MakeTraverseNodesMenu.js`, `src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`, `src/i18n/en.js`, `src/CFileManager.js`). The traverse switch has always used per-sitch key spellings for the same methods — "Constant Speed" (Aguadilla, GoFast, misb), "Const Ground Spd" (Gimbal), "Constant Ground Speed - " (custom), "Constant Ground Speed" (Nellis, FLIR1), "Const Air Spd" (Gimbal) — and saved sitches serialize the selected option by KEY, so the keys must never change. The switch definition in `MakeTraverseNodesMenu` now carries a display-only `labels` map (`CNodeSwitch` `v.labels`/`guiLabels`) that unifies what the user sees: all ground-speed spellings display as **Constant Ground Speed**, "Const Air Spd" as **Constant Air Speed**, and "Global Fit: Const Acceleration" as **Global Fit: Constant Acceleration** — keys, and therefore saved-sitch deserialization, are untouched (verified live in the custom sitch: selection round-trips store the legacy keys). The analysis gallery's Const Acceleration contender gets the same split via a new `display` field alongside its selection `label`/key in `extraMethods` (`methodNodeHypothesis` shows `display ?? label`; `params.methodLabel` stays the key). Same-pass renames: the Traverse-menu button is **Analyze Traverse Methods...** (new i18n key `traverseAnalysis.analyzeButton`), the tweaks subfolder is **Traverse Analysis Tweaks**, the progress overlay reads *Analyzing Traverse Methods*, the gallery tiles are **Least Maneuvering** (was "Least-Maneuvering") and **Minimum Speed** (was "Saddle Traversal"), the Plausible results folder is **Plausible Fit Results** (i18n `losFitPlausible.folder`, plus the code fallback string), the Min/Max Dist and wind-mode tooltips are reworded, and the File → Export subfolder holding the selected traverse track's KML/CSV export buttons is **Traverse of the Lines of Sight** (was "Traversal of the...", `CFileManager.makeExportButton`). Stale comments naming the old UI strings updated in `JetStuff.js`, `LOSFitting.js`, `CNodeLOSFitMinSpeed.js`, `CNodeLOSFitPlausible.js`.
- **The gallery's "Applied:" toast names the traverse method actually selected** (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`). `selectFirst` now returns the display label of the option it selected (`sel.guiLabels?.[key] ?? key`, or null if nothing matched) instead of a boolean, `applyHypothesis` returns it, and the **Use This** handler shows *Applied: \<candidate\> (method: \<selected\>)* whenever the selected method's name differs from the candidate tile's — e.g. *Applied: Least Maneuvering (method: Global Fit: Plausible)*, or an air-speed candidate landing on Constant Ground Speed in a sitch with no air-speed method.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed the analysis gallery's stationary candidates ("Ground Object", "Stationary Point in Space") applying as a zero AIR-speed traverse in most sitches, so the "held still" object drifted with the wind** (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`). `applyHypothesis`'s ground/fixedPoint case selected from `["Constant Speed", "Const Air Spd", "Constant Air Speed"]` — missing the "Constant Ground Speed" / "Constant Ground Speed - " / "Const Ground Spd" key spellings — so only sitches with the exact "Constant Speed" key (Aguadilla, GoFast, misb) got the ground method. The custom sitch ("Constant Ground Speed - "), Gimbal ("Const Ground Spd"), Nellis, and FLIR1 all fell through to a constant-AIR-speed method at speed 0, which holds zero airspeed and therefore drifts downwind instead of holding position. Selection now tries every ground-speed key spelling first (new `GROUND_SPEED_KEYS` list) with air-speed keys only as an explicit fallback. (The at-infinity "Fixed Point in the Sky" variant was never affected — it bails before selection.)
- **Hardened the "Constant Air Speed" candidate's ground-speed fallback against per-sitch key spellings** (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js`). The `constAir` case already tried both air-speed spellings before any ground spelling, but its ground fallback omitted "Constant Ground Speed" / "Constant Ground Speed - ", so in a sitch offering only those spellings and no air-speed method, **Use This** would silently select nothing. No built-in sitch currently hits this (every sitch with those spellings also offers an air-speed method), but the fallback now covers all spellings: `selectFirst(AIR_SPEED_KEYS) ?? selectFirst(GROUND_SPEED_KEYS)`.

### Documentation
- **docs/TraverseMethods.md corrected and extended** (Help → Documentation → Traverse Methods). The Global Fit: Plausible section wrongly said the fit is softly anchored to the *Tgt Start Dist* slider; in the code (`CNodeLOSFitPlausible` → `fitPlausibleBestRange`) that slider is only a wind-anchor hint — the fit finds its own start range via a coarse sweep refined around the winner, reports it as *Found Range* in the Plausible Fit Results folder, and respects the *Min Dist* / *Max Dist* limits in Traverse Analysis Tweaks; the prose now says so. Also: a new **Global Fit: Minimum Speed** section (the drifting-lantern / near-static reading, with the smoothing rationale); quick-reference rows added for **Plausible** and **Minimum Speed**; the Physics row updated to name both dynamics models (Chinese Lantern or Fixed Wing Aircraft) and the differential-evolution + Nelder-Mead solver; "Sequential Traversals" headings → "Sequential Traverses"; "Const Acceleration" → "Constant Acceleration"; the Analyze button name updated.

## Version 2.95.0 (2026-07-03)

### New Features
- **"Analyze Traversals" — one-button, every-interpretation analysis of the lines of sight** (`6c078782`, `3bc9272c`, `ebd4b30e`) (`src/AnalyzeTraverse.js` (new, UI/orchestration), `src/TraverseAnalysis.js` (new, compute core), `src/TraverseAnalysisData.js` (new, dataset builder), `src/Chart3D.js` (new), `src/DifferentialEvolution.js` (new), `src/FixedWingModel.js` (new), `src/SatelliteSearch.js` (new), `src/MakeTraverseNodesMenu.js`). LOS-only data never uniquely determines range: near-perfect fits exist at many distances if the object is allowed to maneuver. This feature makes that ambiguity explicit — an **Analyze Traversals...** button in the Traverse menu (added by `addAnalyzeButton` in `MakeTraverseNodesMenu`) evaluates every physical interpretation of the current sitch's lines of sight and ranks them by physical plausibility.
  - **Interpretations evaluated**: constant air speed (`sweepConstAirSpeed` grid search of start range × speed, scored by flight smoothness), constant altitude (`fitConstAltitude`), least-maneuvering at each assumed range (`rangeProfile` fast + slow variants — how much maneuvering does each distance *require*?), minimum speed (`traverseMinSpeed`, see below), fixed-wing aircraft (`fitAircraft`, differential-evolution parametric fit), Chinese lantern / balloon (`fitPhysicsModel` with the buoyancy+drag+wind `ChineseLanternModel`), ground object, stationary / sky-fixed point (`fitFixedPoint` converging vs `fitFixedDirection` parallel-rays-at-infinity), astronomical body (astronomy-engine ephemeris + apparent magnitude with a narrow-FOV brightness boost (`fovMagBoost`) + View-menu refraction via `applyRefractionECI`), and LEO satellite pass (`SatelliteSearch.js`: per-date catalogue via `proxyStarlink.php?type=LEO`, cached; SGP4 propagation + sunlit test).
  - **Symmetric contenders** (`ebd4b30e`): every method selectable in the sitch's traverse switch also competes — the global statistical fits (Constant Velocity, Const Acceleration, Kalman, Monte Carlo 1 & 2) and Straight Line are read straight off their live nodes (`methodNodeHypothesis`, re-scored in the analysis ENU frame, `_dirty` forced so a contender never reflects a stale array), and a sanity guard drops non-physical tracks (e.g. a manual Straight Line left at degenerate default endpoints: mean airspeed > 20,000 kt or max g ≥ 2000 is skipped).
  - **The gallery**: a full-screen two-pane overlay (`traverse-gallery-overlay`) — candidate tiles with key stats, a plausibility tier badge, and a rotatable 3D graph; selecting a tile opens a Details pane explaining how each number was derived, what constrains it, and where it sits in the solution space. **Use This** applies a candidate back to the scene: method-node contenders re-select that exact method in the traverse switch (`applyHypothesis` → `LOSTraverseSelect.selectOption`), fitted candidates set the matching sliders, ground/fixed-point map to a stationary traverse, and pure identifications (astronomical body, satellite, point at infinity) are flagged as "what it is, not a motion path" with Use disabled. **Open Full Report** in the footer opens a fully self-contained dark-theme HTML report (charts pre-rendered to PNG data-URLs, works offline) with per-candidate detail sections and 2D plan views for print. The run shows a cancellable progress overlay ("Analyzing LOS Traversals").
  - **Interactive 3D graphs** (`3bc9272c`, `src/Chart3D.js`): rotatable orthographic 3D volume rendered on a 2D canvas with a quaternion trackball, dark ground plane and back-plane grids, per-chart fullscreen (⛶). A `Chart3DGroup` ties all charts together — **Sync Orientation** (default on) rotates every chart together, **Sync Scale** (default off) locks them to one shared scale; re-enabling Sync Orientation no longer snaps charts to a stale matrix.
  - **Scope & inputs**: the analysis respects the A-B / In-Out frame range (`analysisFrameRange` → `buildAnalysisDataset` `frame0`/`frame1`; all frame→date/LOS mapping goes through the dataset). A **Traversal Analysis Tweaks** subfolder in the Traverse menu holds **Min Dist** / **Max Dist** start-range bounds (`analysisMinDist`/`analysisMaxDist` GUI nodes), a **Wind for analysis** selector (*Sitch wind* / *Zero wind*), and the hypothesis checkboxes — **AO: Stationary / sky-fixed object** (default on; cheap geometric test), **AO: Known object (this time)**, **AO: Known object (find time)**, and **Satellite: LEO pass for date** (all default off; each costs an ephemeris sweep or a catalogue load).
  - **Result cache**: the full run (sweep + several DE fits) takes ~15 s, so results are cached by a bit-level FP fingerprint of every LOS frame plus every input that steers the fits — frame range, wind mode, tweaks, speed/anchor sliders, sensor FOV, refraction on/pressure/temperature, simSpeed, and the live global-fit GUI params it reads (Kalman process/measurement noise; Monte Carlo trials / LOS uncertainty / order). Re-clicking with nothing changed reopens the gallery instantly.
- **Three new traverse methods in the Traverse menu** (`6c078782`, `ebd4b30e`) (`src/nodes/CNodeLOSFitPlausible.js` (new), `src/nodes/CNodeLOSFitMinSpeed.js` (new), `src/FixedWingModel.js` (new), `src/JetStuff.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeLOSFitPhysics.js`). **Global Fit: Plausible** (`fitPlausibleBestRange`/`traversePlausible`: range along each ray is a smooth cubic B-spline λ(f)) autonomously finds the least-maneuvering LOS-riding path treating the *Target Speed* slider as a loose target rather than an exact constraint, and reports its result in a "Plausible Fit Result" folder (i18n `losFitPlausible`: *Found Range*, *Speed Target*, *Plausibility (lower=better)*). **Global Fit: Minimum Speed** (`traverseMinSpeed` + a curvature-penalized low-order B-spline smoothing pass that sheds jet-track/FLIR pointing jitter — raw peak ~5.7 g smooths to ~0.2 g) finds the *slowest* object consistent with the sightlines — the drifting-lantern / near-static reading (recovers the accepted Aguadilla lantern path at ~12 kt where the old single-anchored-range saddle read ~48 kt); it is the same core the gallery's Minimum Speed / Saddle contender uses, so the applied path is byte-identical to the previewed one. **Global Fit: Physics** gains a **Fixed Wing Aircraft** dynamics model alongside Chinese Lantern, selectable via the new **Physics Model** switch (`physicsModelChoice`); its multi-modal cost landscape is solved with global differential evolution (`DifferentialEvolution.js`, pop 48 / 120 gens, strided cost sampling) plus a Nelder-Mead polish, with the solved wind softly pinned to the wind-guess sliders.

### Improvements
- **Global Fit: Physics now fits asynchronously without freezing the page or serving stale results** (`6c078782`) (`src/nodes/CNodeLOSFitPhysics.js`, `src/LOSFitting.js`, `src/PhysicsModel.js`). `fitPhysicsModel` is now `async`, yielding via a MessageChannel macrotask (`_macroYield`, immune to background-tab timer throttling) so the RK4+optimizer fit no longer blocks the main thread. Because a recalculate cascade reads the node synchronously the moment it becomes the selected traverse, `_doCompute` first serves a cheap placeholder track (points at the initial-range guess along each ray, or the previous fit) so downstream consumers always see valid positions; when the fit lands it recalculates from the node's *outputs* (not itself) so the finished track propagates to airTrack, the display track, and the g-force/distance graphs without re-entering the fit. Concurrent runs are guarded (`_computing` + re-dirty). The results folder now reports fit quality as *Avg Error* in degrees (mean angular error, separate from the composite *Fit Cost* that includes plausibility terms), shows the fixed-wing solution in aviation units (range NM, heading °, TAS kt, turn rate °/s, climb m/s + ft/min), and the *Physics Wind Speed / From* guess sliders now default from the sitch's target wind (`Sit.targetWindKnots`/`targetWindFrom`) with the speed range widened 0–60 → 0–150 kt.
- **The Monte Carlo global fits are now deterministic** (`ebd4b30e`) (`src/LOSFitting.js`). `fitMonteCarlo` / `fitMonteCarlo2` draw from a seeded PRNG (`mulberry32`, `options.seed`, fixed default seed) instead of `Math.random`, so the same LOS + parameters always yield the same trajectory — a stable, cacheable analysis contender and a repeatable on-screen traverse.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed the g-force graph showing a large spurious sawtooth with Global Fit traverse methods** (`6c078782`) (`src/LOSFitting.js`). Two compounding causes, both in code shipped since 2.42.0. (1) The fitted tracks were packed into `Float32Array` positions; ENU coordinates of tens of km quantize to ~1 cm at float32, and the g-force graph — a second difference amplified by fps² — turns that into a ~0.7 g residual sawtooth. All five fits (`fitConstantVelocity`, `fitConstantAcceleration`, `fitMonteCarlo`, `fitMonteCarlo2`, `fitKalmanFilter`) now emit `Float64Array` positions. (2) `buildLOSDataset` took sample times from `GlobalDateTimeNode.frameToMS`, which quantizes to integer milliseconds, so per-frame deltas jitter (33/34/33 ms at 30 fps); a fit places its output at P0+V·t, turning that jitter into ~0.15 m per-frame wobble at high fit speeds — tens of spurious g. Frames are uniformly spaced in real time for a constant-fps sitch, so times are now `f/fps`. A constant-velocity fit's acceleration graph now reads flat ~0 g, as it must.

### Documentation
- **docs/TraverseMethods.md** (Help → Documentation → **Traverse Methods**) gains a "Physically Plausible Analysis" section documenting the Analyze button, the soft-target philosophy (LOS-only data never uniquely resolves range), and the new Global Fit: Plausible / Minimum Speed / Fixed Wing methods (`6c078782`).

## Version 2.94.3 (2026-07-02)

### Improvements
- Recalculation cascades are ~1.2x faster with bit-for-bit identical output, covering the main user-driven paths: start-time scrubbing, Sitch Frames changes, and per-track edits (Alt offset / alt lock). Measured on a 6-aircraft ADS-B custom sitch (6287 frames, dev build): start-time tick 58→48 ms with the constant-speed traverse active (22→18 ms without), Sitch Frames change 38→30 ms, Alt offset 7.6→6.2 ms. The individual fixes: `checkDisplayOutputs` gating now uses an early-exit `anyVisibleDisplayOutputs()` instead of a full no-visited-set `countVisibleOutputs(0, true)` subgraph walk per track node per cascade (plus a visited set in `clearDepth` and pruned revisits in `markMaximumVisibleDepth`); `CNode.getValue` probes `getValueFrame(0)` once instead of twice and builds assert messages lazily, and `CNodeLOS` declares `returnsPosition` so LOS reads skip the shape probe (which ran a full camera update per read); `CNodeTerrain.getPointBelow` / `clampAboveGround` deduplicate a repeated `meanSeaLevelOffset` computation and an `ECEFToLLA` conversion via an optional out param; `CNodeLOSTraverseConstantSpeed` hoists per-iteration Sphere/Vector3 allocations (~1M objects per rebake) to node-level scratch, preserving the original position aliasing exactly; the new `CCachedCurveSampler` (`src/CachedCurveSampler.js`) is an arithmetically exact clone of `THREE.CatmullRomCurve3.getPoint` that caches segment cubic coefficients across consecutive samples, and `CNodeSmoothedPositionTrack`'s sparse dataTrack loop reuses freshly-baked upstream positions instead of re-deriving geoid + geodetic conversions (gated off for terrain-dependent AGL/alt-lock tracks); `CNodeMISBDataTrack` and sparse raw-data displays are flagged `framesInvariant` so `updateSitFramesChanged` skips rebaking them; `CNodeControllerObjectTilt.apply` drops from 6 identical track reads per frame to 2 and reuses a saved quaternion. Verified byte-identical: FP-bit hashes of all 41 node output arrays match the pre-change build across a 10-step operation protocol (414/414), including a real frame-count change; full Jest and 40-sitch fast-regression pass pixel-identical.

### Bug Fixes
- Fixed Render Viewport Video (Video menu), the saved-sitch thumbnail capture (`CFileManager.captureViewportScreenshot`), and the SitrecBridge MCP composite screenshot compositing views in ViewMan insertion order instead of the on-screen stacking order. In-app, `CViewManager.updateZOrder()` assigns `zIndex` by area (larger views lower, with alwaysOnTop/scriptZ exceptions), so a fullscreen look view with small main-view/video insets displays with the insets on top — but the three compositors drew in insertion order, so the fullscreen view painted over its insets and the export showed only the fullscreen view. All three (`src/VideoExporter.js`, `src/CFileManager.js`, `tools/SitrecBridge/extension/page-bridge.js`, extension 1.0.54) now call `ViewMan.updateZOrder()`, sort non-overlay views bottom-up by `zIndex`, and draw each view's overlay canvases immediately after their parent view — overlays live inside the parent's div in the DOM so they stack with the parent, whereas previously all overlays were drawn after every base view. Verified end-to-end with a real 16-frame export.
- Fixed double-clicking a floating menu tab that overlaps a view's header strip (e.g. the Time menu over the Main UIBar) triggering the view's fullscreen toggle instead of the menu's own double-click action. 23f7a064 made the document-level mouse router (`src/mouseMoveView.js`) defer to menus via `isTopMenuElementAt()` for pointerdown/pointermove/wheel but missed the capture-phase dblclick handler, which ran — and swallowed the event — before the menu's listener; `onDocumentDoubleClick` now has the same bail-out.

## Version 2.94.2 (2026-07-02)

### Improvements
- **Start-time scrubbing is ~5x faster** (measured 96ms → ~20ms per slider tick on a 6-aircraft ADS-B custom sitch, 6287 frames). Scrubbing the Time menu sliders triggers a full `recalculateCascade` that rebakes every track, smoother, and display; profiling found three hot spots, all fixed with output-identical optimizations. (1) `SlidingAverageX` in `src/smoothing.js` was O(n·window) — a ~200-wide interpolating inner loop per frame across ~10 sliding smoothers (~23M iterations per tick). Every sample in a frame's window shares the same fractional offset, so the sum decomposes exactly into two prefix-sum range queries — now O(n). The range-sum indices use `W = Math.ceil(window)` to match the original loop's `ceil(window)` sample count for fractional windows (the naive `prefix[x0 + window]` reads an undefined fractional Float64Array index and NaNs the whole output); the sum is still divided by the fractional `window`, so integer-window callers (all current ones) are bit-compatible in behavior, verified to ~1e-13 relative error. (2) `CNodeSmoothedPositionTrack`'s two spline-resample loops restarted their bracketing segment search from index 0 on every frame (O(frames·points)); the frame index is monotonic, so the bracket `idx` now persists across the loop. A new `_extractSourceXYZ()` also reads x/y/z straight from `this.sourceArray` (guaranteed to exist by `recalculate()`) instead of calling `source.p(i)` per frame, avoiding a full `getValue` dispatch, a wrapper, and a `Vector3` clone per frame. (3) `CNodeTrackFromMISB`'s per-frame resample loop built `assert()` message strings eagerly even when passing (dev builds only); hot-loop asserts now build messages only on failure. Also removed a duplicate per-frame `lla` array allocation and cached the MISB FOV `Number()` conversion per slot instead of per frame. The same paths serve the start-time sync-to-track, simSpeed changes, and the `;`/`'` nudge keys. Verified: full Jest suite passes; fast-regression harness 40/40 sitches with 0px diffs.

### Bug Fixes
- Fixed the Sitrec version panel (the "Sitrec" info GUI in the top-right corner, which shows the build version string) being painted over by the menu bar's black bar. Commit 23f7a064 (shipped in 2.92.2, the menus-win-clicks fix) raised `#menuBarBlackBar` to z-index 8999 / `#menuBar` to 9000, above the auto-placed `infoGUI` (z-index 1001). `CGuiMenuBar` in `src/lil-gui-extras.js` now pins `infoGUI.domElement.style.zIndex` at 9001, restoring its pre-regression always-on-top behavior.
- Fixed terrain and 3D-tile buildings failing to load in the area actually visible on screen when the Camera Tweaks **xOffset** / **yOffset** sliders (Camera → Camera Tweaks) are in use. The PTZ offsets rotate the camera at render time only (`applyCameraOffset` in `renderTargetAndEffects`), but both tile-loading systems evaluated visibility with the UN-rotated camera: Google photorealistic 3D-tiles LOD selection (`PerViewTiles.update` in `CNodeBuildings3DTiles`) and terrain elevation/texture tile subdivision (`CNodeView3D.prepareCameraForLOD`). With offsets comparable to or larger than the FOV (e.g. a 4° FOV with 2.8°/4° offsets), the rendered view barely overlapped the culling frustum, so on-screen tiles never loaded, leaving large gaps. `PerViewTiles.update()` now wraps its camera fingerprint + `TilesRenderer` update in `view.applyCameraOffset()` / `removeCameraOffset()` (try/finally, with `updateMatrixWorld`), and `prepareCameraForLOD()` applies the same offset rotation (saved quaternion restored in `restoreCameraAfterLOD`). Verified live: the previously-empty half of the view fully populates, and scrubbing offsets to -10°/-6° streams tiles into the newly visible area; fast-regression 40/40 at 0px.
- Fixed the browser freezing when dragging a control point of an editable synthetic track (**Edit Track** checkbox in the track's folder under Contents) while Google photorealistic 3D tiles are loaded. Each drag pointermove fires a full `recalculateCascade`, which reached the altitude label's ground-point node — `CNodeMeasureAltitude` creates a `CNodeMunge` (id `*_Below`) whose munge raycasts the loaded 3D tiles via `getTilesPointBelow` → `buildings3DTiles.groundBelow` (~2.5ms per call) — and `CNodeMunge.recalculate()` eagerly baked ALL `Sit.frames` values: measured 15,436ms for 6287 frames in one recalculate, per pointermove. Two fixes: (a) `CNodeMunge` gains an opt-in `lazyCache` mode — `recalculate()` just resets the per-frame cache and `getValueFrame()` computes frames on demand; the `_Below` munge opts in, since the label only ever reads the current frame. (b) `CNodeSplineEditor`'s `handleSplineEditorChange` now clears `splineEditor.dirty` after its immediate cascade — the same pointermove also set `dirty` via `updatePointEditorGraphics`, so `CNodeSplineEditor.update()` fired a second identical full cascade on the next render frame, doubling the cost of every drag move (other dirty setters like `addPoint` don't call onChange and keep their deferred cascade). Measured: simulated drag tick 15,458ms → ~8ms (~1900x); the altitude label still reads the correct AGL and the lazy cache stays consistent across cascades.

## Version 2.94.1 (2026-07-01)

### Bug Fixes
- Fixed the in-app AI assistant claiming to have performed actions (e.g. "Done — moved the camera to New York") without actually executing them when using the GPT-5 Mini or GPT-5 Nano models. Two root causes, both in `sitrecServer/chatbot.php`. First, `callOpenAI()` requested `reasoning_effort: "minimal"` for all models matching `/^gpt-5/i`; at minimal effort GPT-5 models skip the reasoning pass that drives reliable function calling and instead narrate the action in text with zero tool calls, so the client had nothing to execute. The effort is now `"low"` — still fast (~2–4 s per call) but the model actually emits the function calls. Second, the system prompt's `[Tool Results]` success rule told the model its job after a successful tool result was to "respond with brief confirmation TEXT (one sentence, no tool calls)"; smaller models over-applied this and stopped calling tools after ANY tool result — including intermediate query tools like `getCurrentDateTime` — dropping the remaining parts of multi-part requests such as "set the time AND go to New York". The prompt now has a "MULTI-PART REQUESTS (CRITICAL)" section requiring every part of a request to be performed (emitting all needed calls in one turn where possible, and never reporting done while a part is unperformed), and the success rule is reworded: THAT call succeeded, don't repeat it with the same args, but CONTINUE calling the remaining functions — a query tool returning is not the task being done. Verified live: with GPT-5 Mini, "12:21pm edt today, New York, NY" now runs `getCurrentDateTime` → `setDateTime` + `gotoLLA` (both executed); GPT-5 Nano emits both action calls in a single turn, and its attempted duplicate re-issue on continuation is caught by the existing server-side duplicate-call guard. Claude models were never affected.

## Version 2.94.0 (2026-07-01)

### New Features
- **HEIC/HEIF image support in all browsers** (`f6902531`) (`src/HEICUtils.js` (new), `src/CFileManagerParse.js`, `src/DragDropHandler.js`, `src/EXIFUtils.js`, `src/CFileManagerSave.js`, `src/TrackManager.js`). Sitrec previously could only decode HEIC/HEIF (the iPhone default photo format) in Safari, because no other browser natively decodes `image/heic`; dropping one elsewhere failed. New `src/HEICUtils.js` lazily `import("heic-to/csp")` — a libheif WASM wrapper — exposing `decodeHEICToImage` / `decodeHEICToBlobURL` / `decodeHEICToBlob`, code-split into its own on-demand chunk so the main bundle is unaffected until a HEIC is actually loaded. HEIC is wired through every image entry path: drag-drop (both as a video image source and as a ground overlay), the file-picker `accept` list, the URL-drop extension regex, `parseAsset`, and both save→reload restore paths (`heic`/`heif` added to `IMAGE_EXTENSIONS`, with a dedicated `HEIC_EXTENSIONS` guard that routes those bytes through libheif first). `extractJPEGImportMetadata` (`src/EXIFUtils.js`) now also parses HEIC EXIF, so geotagged HEIC photos get full metadata parity with JPEG — GPS location, heading, altitude, and focal-length→FOV are all applied on import. Because libheif already applies the `irot`/`imir` orientation transform when it decodes, the now-redundant EXIF rotation tag is stripped (new `stripImageRotationMetadata`) to avoid the image being rotated twice.

### Improvements
- **All native `confirm()` dialogs replaced with custom promise-based dialogs** (`f6902531`) (`src/showError.js`, `src/DragDropHandler.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeVideoView.js`, plus ~13 files). Native `confirm()` blocks the main thread — the video add/replace prompt in particular froze automation/MCP entirely while it was open. A new `showChoice(message, {title, options, cancelValue})` primitive in `src/showError.js` renders an N-button modal with optional per-option explanations, Escape/Enter/backdrop dismissal, and validation-mode auto-dismiss; `showConfirm` is reimplemented on top of it so every modal shares one code path and the page stays responsive. The video "add/replace" prompt (`promptAddOrReplace`) is now a custom **Replace / Add / Cancel** dialog with explanatory descriptions ("Keep both videos; switch between them with the selector." / "Replace the Video view's current video with the new one."), and callers abort cleanly on Cancel. The two previously hand-rolled `DragDropHandler` image dialogs were DRY'd onto `showChoice`, and the remaining ~18 native `confirm()` sites across 13 files were converted to `showConfirm`/`showChoice` (the bad-data filter uses a non-blocking fire-and-forget variant). Informational `alert()` calls were left unchanged.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed imported geotagged photos placing the look camera about one terrain-height too high** (`9dfdf4e4`) (`src/DragDropHandler.js`, `CNodeControllerTrackPosition`). On importing a geotagged image the look camera landed roughly one terrain-height above its true position (e.g. 277 ft MSL instead of the correct 214 ft) and only snapped down after a zoom. The cause was not the geoid: the camera is positioned by `CNodeControllerTrackPosition`, whose `forceAboveSurface` flag is on by default and clamps the camera *above* the currently-loaded 3D-tile surface. A ground-level photo whose true GPS altitude sits below the coarse (building-topped) tile surface got pushed up to that surface; zooming loaded finer tiles and re-clamped it lower, which is why interacting appeared to "fix" it. A photo's GPS altitude is absolute (MSL) and authoritative, so on import the camera node's `forceAboveSurface` is set to `false` and the position node's `agl` is set to `false` (altitude treated as absolute, not above-ground). The camera now lands at the photo's real altitude immediately, with no transient and no zoom needed. Applies to both HEIC and JPEG geotagged imports (shared apply path).

## Version 2.93.3 (2026-07-01)

### Internal
- **Retire the superseded Playwright visual-regression suite from the test registry to unbreak CI** (`test-registry.js`). Release 2.93.2 dropped `regression.test.js` from `playwright.config.js`'s `testMatch`, but the seven registry entries that still named `file: 'regression.test.js'` (`default`, the commented-out `wmts`, `agua`, `potomac`, `bledsoe`, `nightsky-permalink`, `demo-truck`) then pointed at a file no longer in the Playwright test set. That desync tripped the registry↔config sync assertion in `tests/driftChecks.test.js`, failing CI. Those entries are removed — the old Playwright screenshot suite is superseded by the fast-regression harness (`tests_regression/fast-regression/`) — so the drift check and CI pass again. The remaining self-comparing save→load round-trip entry (which references a different test file) is untouched. No user-facing behavior change; `test-registry.js` is not part of the app bundle.

## Version 2.93.2 (2026-07-01)

### Bug Fixes
- **Local Compute now produces the same Phase Correlation and ECC Euclidean results as the in-browser analyzer** (`tools/SitrecBridge/local-compute/motion_analysis_worker.py`, `src/CMotionAnalysisUI.js`). When 2.93.0 introduced Local Compute (native Python/OpenCV) motion analysis, two techniques disagreed with the browser path that produces the cache the rest of Sitrec consumes:
  - **Phase Correlation** — `compute_phase_correlation` used `cv2.phaseCorrelate`'s shift with the wrong sign convention (browser motion is the negative of the raw phase-correlation shift), so Local Compute reported motion in the opposite direction. Fixed by negating both components (`dx = -shift[0]/skip`, `dy = -shift[1]/skip`). It also now guards against a weak/ambiguous correlation peak: when the phase-correlation `response < 0.2` it falls back to `compute_sparse_consensus(...)`, matching the browser's behavior of not trusting a low-confidence global shift.
  - **ECC Euclidean** — `compute_ecc` ran a full `cv2.findTransformECC(MOTION_EUCLIDEAN)` solve, but Sitrec's browser OpenCV.js build effectively resolves this UI option to an Affine RANSAC fit on real footage. The native worker was therefore returning cache-incompatible results for the same nominal technique. `compute_ecc` now returns `compute_affine_ransac(...)` directly so Local Compute stays cache-compatible with the browser until/unless the browser ECC path is upgraded.
- **The Local Compute request now advertises the browser's OpenCV capabilities** (`src/CMotionAnalysisUI.js`). `buildLocalMotionAnalysisRequest()` adds `browserCvCapabilities: {phaseCorrelate, findTransformECC}`, each a boolean probing whether `getCV()` exposes the corresponding function, so the native worker can match the exact feature set of the browser build that generated the reference results.

### Internal
- **New value-tier differential regression test for motion analysis** (`tests_regression/fast-regression/scenarios/video/local-compute-motion-analysis.scenario.mjs`, `tests_regression/fast-regression/value-baseline/local-compute-motion-analysis.json`). Runs all five techniques (Linear Tracklet, Sparse + Consensus, Phase Correlation, ECC Euclidean, Affine RANSAC) both in-browser and via Local Compute over the Truck test clip and asserts the two paths agree within tolerance and that Local Compute is faster — directly guarding the fixes above. Supporting harness/tooling: `src/index.js` exposes a test-only `window.__sitrecRunMotionAnalysisForTesting` hook (gated to local or `?regression`), backed by `runMotionAnalysisForTesting`/`summarizeMotionAnalysisForTesting` in `src/CMotionAnalysisUI.js`; `tests_regression/fast-regression/run.mjs`, `run-scenarios.mjs`, and `README.md` are updated to run scenario tests; `tools/SitrecBridge/build-dist.mjs` now excludes `__pycache__`/`.pyc` from the bridge dist; and `playwright.config.js` drops the superseded `regression.test.js` from `testMatch`.

## Version 2.93.1 (2026-07-01)

### Security
- **The AI chatbot can no longer reach the JavaScript-executing Scripted Video API functions** (`src/CSitrecAPI.js`, `src/CDirectLLMClient.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeVIewChat.js`, `sitrecServer/chatbot.php`, `tests/CSitrecAPI.test.js`). The Scripted Video "script" is not a sandboxed DSL — its body is compiled with `AsyncFunction` and run as real JavaScript (see `scriptedVideo/ScriptJSRunner.js`). Two API entries execute it: `setScriptedVideoScript` (compiles + parses the script) and `previewScriptedVideo` (runs the active script body). The chatbot's tool surface was built from `getDocumentation()`, which advertised *every* API entry, so an indirect-prompt-injected chatbot could name these functions and run arbitrary code in the user's session (finding B1). Fix, defense-in-depth on both the advertise and enforce sides: (1) both entries are tagged `llmCallable:false`; (2) a new `getLLMDocumentation()` (via the shared `_documentationFor(predicate)`) returns the documentation with `llmCallable:false` entries removed, and `CNodeViewChat` now sends *that* filtered doc as `sitrecDoc` instead of `getDocumentation()`; (3) the tool builders re-deny the two names regardless of the doc handed to them — `buildTools()` in `CDirectLLMClient.js` skips `LLM_DENIED_FUNCTION_NAMES`, and `chatbot.php`'s `buildToolsFromDoc()` skips the matching `$llmDenied` list — so a tampered/legacy client sending the full doc still can't expose them; (4) `handleAPICall(call, source)` gained a `source` argument, and `CNodeViewChat` passes `"chat"`; a chat-sourced call to any `llmCallable:false` entry is refused with an error, so a *guessed* name can't reach a JS-executing function even though it was never advertised. Trusted UI / MCP / programmatic `call()` paths default to `source:"ui"` and are unaffected.
- **Scripted Video scripts now parse inside a sandboxed Web Worker instead of the page main world** (`src/workers/ScriptRunnerWorker.js` (new), `src/scriptedVideo/ScriptRunnerClient.js` (new), `src/CScriptedVideo.js`, `src/scriptedVideo/ScriptEditorWindow.js`, `src/scriptedVideo/ScriptCommands.js`, `src/scriptedVideo/ScriptJSRunner.js`, `tests/scriptRunnerWorker.test.js` (new)). `runScriptJS` compiles the user's script text with `new AsyncFunction(body)` and runs it once against a record-only API to build the timeline model; running that in the main world meant a malicious script (e.g. one delivered via B1 above) could read BYOK LLM keys, hit same-origin authenticated endpoints, or touch the DOM. The parse pass now runs in `ScriptRunnerWorker.js`, which structurally lacks DOM / `window` / `document` / `localStorage`, and which — at module-eval time, before any `onmessage` can compile user code — neuters every remaining network/storage/exfil vector: `fetch`, `XMLHttpRequest`, `WebSocket`, `importScripts`, `indexedDB`, `caches`, `EventSource`, `BroadcastChannel`, `Worker`/`SharedWorker` (the critical ones — a nested worker would otherwise start with a fresh, un-neutered global), and `navigator.sendBeacon`. The runner's imports (`ScriptCommands`/`ScriptSugar`/`ScriptJSCallSites`/`ScriptMath`) are pure, so neutering these cannot break a legitimate parse. `ScriptRunnerClient.js` (`runScriptViaWorker`) owns a single long-lived worker with `requestId` latest-wins correlation and a main-thread watchdog (`HARD_TIMEOUT_MS` 3000) that terminates and respawns the worker if a tight non-API loop (`while(true){}`) wedges it — the in-worker `MAX_RUN_MS`/`MAX_CALLS` guards only fire when the script calls the API. It falls back to running `runScriptJS` in-process where no `Worker` exists (Jest / non-browser), where the worker fails to spawn or crashes (`onerror` drains pending to in-process), or where `postMessage` throws; a hard-timeout deliberately does *not* fall back in-process (that would re-hang the page) and instead returns an error-shaped model. Supporting changes: `CScriptedVideo.parse()` is now async (a Worker round-trip / macrotask), so `CScriptedVideoManager` and `CScriptEditorWindow.setVisible()` now draw the timeline/backdrop from `parse().then(...)` instead of a `setTimeout(0)` that would race the reply and paint an empty/stale timeline; the `set` command in `ScriptCommands.js` now rejects a non-string `menu` argument so the parsed event model is always structured-cloneable (a function/object would throw `DataCloneError` when posted back from the worker); `ScriptJSRunner.js`'s header comment is updated to reflect that the Worker hardening is now done. Residual, documented in-file: dynamic `import()` can't be deleted (it's an operator, not a binding) — a document-level CSP `connect-src`/`worker-src` is the recommended orthogonal follow-up. There is no user-visible change to Scripted Video behavior.

## Version 2.93.0 (2026-06-30)

### New Features
- **Local Compute: full-range Motion Analysis can be offloaded to a native SitrecBridge Python/OpenCV worker** (`c66ffc17`, `fe48bd75`, `f11da09a`) (`src/LocalComputeBridge.js`, `src/CMotionAnalysisUI.js`, `tools/SitrecBridge/local-compute/motion_analysis_worker.py`). Browser-side motion analysis is CPU-bound and single-threaded; this adds an optional path that runs the whole analysis pass in native code and imports the result back into the existing `MotionAnalyzer`.
  - **Client bridge** (`src/LocalComputeBridge.js`, new): a `LocalComputeBridge` WebSocket client (singleton via `getLocalComputeBridge()`) that scans ports `9780`–`9799` for a SitrecBridge server advertising `capabilities.motionAnalysis`. It prefers a remembered port (localStorage key `sitrec.localCompute.port`), URL hints (`?localComputePort=` / `?lcPort=`), and preferred port `9796` before falling back to a full descending port probe (`/local-compute-probe`). Connect timeout 800 ms; per-request timeout 30 minutes; `request(method, payload, onProgress)` streams progress messages.
  - **Analysis hook** (`src/CMotionAnalysisUI.js`): `analyzeAllFrames()` now calls `analyzeAllFramesViaLocalCompute()` first, and only runs the browser pass if it returns false. `buildLocalMotionAnalysisRequest()` resolves the video's fetchable URL via `resolveURLForFetch()` (URL-backed sources only — `blob:`/`data:` videos throw "video source is only available inside the browser" and fall back), and sends `startFrame`/`endFrame`, `frames`/`fps`, `videoSpeed`, `effectiveRotation`, target `width`/`height`, a copy of `motionAnalyzer.params`, and the serialized motion mask (`maskData`) when the mask is enabled. `applyLocalMotionAnalysisResult()` repopulates `motionAnalyzer.resultCache` and `duplicateFrameCache` in the browser-compatible shape, then redraws the overlay/graph — so the existing overlay, graph, panorama, stabilization, and track-export paths keep working unchanged. Any failure sets a `Fallback: <reason>` status, resets derived state, and returns to the in-browser analyzer.
  - **Menu** (Motion Analysis folder): a new **Use Local Compute** boolean (internal `useLocalCompute`, default **on**) with tooltip "Use the local SitrecBridge Python/OpenCV worker for full-range Motion Analysis when available", plus a read-only, `.listen()`-driven **Local Compute** status field (`localComputeStatus.value`) that reports states like `Not checked`, `Connecting...`, `Bridge :9796`, `Done (N frames)`, and `Fallback: …`. Progress is mapped to the 4-step analyze UI (download → duplicates → analysis → fallback). Last-run stats are stashed on `window.__sitrecLocalComputeLastStats`.
  - **Native worker** (`tools/SitrecBridge/local-compute/motion_analysis_worker.py`, new, ~1180 lines): a Python (cv2 + numpy, ffmpeg/ffprobe) worker that downloads/decodes the source video and emits `resultCache`/`duplicateFrameCache`-compatible JSON. It mirrors the browser analyzer's motion techniques (`SPARSE_CONSENSUS`, `LINEAR_TRACKLET`, `PHASE_CORRELATION`, `ECC_EUCLIDEAN`, `AFFINE_RANSAC`) and duplicate-frame detection (identical-pixel ratio `0.93`, mean-abs-diff `0.15`), so results match the in-browser path.

### Improvements
- **SitrecBridge install/update flow for Local Compute** (`fe48bd75`, `f11da09a`) (`tools/SitrecBridge/extension/popup.{html,js}`, `tools/SitrecBridge/extension/background.js`, `tools/SitrecBridge/local-compute/install.sh`, `tools/SitrecBridge/local-compute/install.ps1`, `package.json`). The SitrecBridge extension popup gains an **Install/Update Local Compute** button (with a status line) that asks the connected Bridge to install/update the Local Compute dependencies (`python3`, `opencv-python-headless`/`cv2`, `numpy`, `ffmpeg`/`ffprobe`); it enables only when a connected Bridge advertises `localComputeCapabilities.localComputeInstall`, and streams `localComputeInstallProgress` messages. New cross-platform installers `install.sh` (macOS/Linux) and `install.ps1` (Windows — tries `py -3`, then `python`/`python3`; points at `winget` for Python/FFmpeg) are exposed as `npm run local-compute-install` / `npm run local-compute-install-win`. `SITREC_LOCAL_COMPUTE_PYTHON` selects a specific Python. `mcp-server.js` and `background.js` gained the matching `motion_analysis` capability, probe, and install plumbing.
- **The downloadable MCP Bridge now bundles the Local Compute worker** (`fe48bd75`, `cfec19f9`) (`tools/SitrecBridge/build-dist.mjs`, `src/i18n/en.js`). `build-dist.mjs` copies the `local-compute/` directory into `SitrecBridge.zip`, and the Help → Documentation → **Download MCP Bridge** tooltip (`downloadBridgeTooltip`) is updated from "…zero dependencies, just needs Node.js" to "Download the SitrecBridge MCP server, Chrome extension, and Local Compute worker".

## Version 2.92.3 (2026-06-30)

### New Features
- **"Sim Opacity" fades the look view's 3D building tiles so a Street View pano shows through** (`fe13cd8a`) (`src/StreetViewPanoUI.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeBuildings3DTiles.js`). A new `simOpacity` param/slider (Terrain → *Street View Pano* folder, label *Sim Opacity*, range 0–1) calls `setSimOpacity(v)` → `buildings.setLookSimOpacity(v)`, fading the Google/OSM 3D building tiles in the **look view only** so the panorama sphere behind them becomes visible; the main (map) view is left fully opaque. This is distinct from the shared *Terrain Opacity*, which fades terrain + buildings in all views.
- **Admin-only "Building Detail (SSE)" global LOD control for 3D building tiles** (`fe13cd8a`) (`src/nodes/CNodeTerrainUI.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeBuildings3DTiles.js`). `buildingErrorTarget` drives the 3D-tiles renderer's global screen-space-error target (clamped 8–40, default 20, **not** serialized); lower = sharper near geometry but more billed Google tile fetches. It is gated behind `isAdmin()` and lives in the Terrain → *Terrain Tweaks* folder. This uses ordinary frustum-based loading — an experimental camera-centred `LoadRegionPlugin`/`SphereRegion` foveation prototype was implemented in this change and then reverted (it force-loaded tiles outside the frustum, costing extra root-tileset requests without reliably buying near detail), so it is not part of the shipped behavior.

### Improvements
- **Street View panorama renders as a camera-tracking skybox (two concentric spheres)** (`fe13cd8a`) (`src/nodes/CNodeStreetViewPano.js`, `src/StreetViewPanoUI.js`). The panorama is now drawn as two concentric spheres sharing one texture, both re-centred on the look camera every frame so they track the observer like a skybox: a "sky" sphere drawn before the 3D scene (`renderOrder -1000`, `depthTest` OFF while opaque) so fading the sim reveals it, plus the z-sorted "registered" sphere; where both are visible they overlap exactly. The sphere radius slider range widened to `5–50000 m` (step 1).
- **"Opacity" renamed "Street Opacity"** (`fe13cd8a`) (`src/StreetViewPanoUI.js`). The existing panorama-sphere opacity control (`getNode().setOpacity`) is relabeled *Street Opacity* (tooltip: "Opacity of the Street View panorama sphere itself"), to disambiguate it from the new *Sim Opacity* control.
- **"Terrain Opacity" now also fades the 3D building tiles** (`fe13cd8a`) (`src/nodes/CNodeTerrainUI.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeBuildings3DTiles.js`). The shared `transparency` control (*Terrain Opacity*, Terrain → Terrain Tweaks) now applies to the 3D building tiles as well as terrain, as tinted glass: `depthWrite` stays ON so a faded tile still occludes its own hidden lower-LOD geometry instead of popping coarse tiles through.
- **3D-tiles LRU item-count cap raised 8000 → 16000** (`fe13cd8a`) (`src/nodes/CNodeBuildings3DTiles.js`). Raised alongside the byte cap so the byte cap is the binding memory bound rather than the item count, avoiding tile-admission-gate jams at low (sharp) error targets.
- **Street View panoramas persist across save + reload** (`63b64046`) (`src/nodes/CNodeStreetViewPano.js`, `src/CustomManagerSerialize.js`). The pano's location and tuning are serialized via `simpleSerials`, and a `deserializeMods` hook recreates and re-fetches the pano on load. The stitched equirectangular image itself is not stored — it is re-resolved from the saved lat/lon (so the saved sitch stays small and always reflects current imagery).

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed Street View panorama vertical misalignment on sloped streets** (`63b64046`) (`src/nodes/CNodeStreetViewPano.js`, `src/StreetViewPanoUI.js`). The pano dome was oriented from the metadata heading only, ignoring the capture pose, so a pano shot on a sloped street sat vertically offset from the 3D scene. The fix applies the metadata `tilt`: the true horizon sits `(tilt-90)°` above the equirectangular centre row, so the dome is pitched DOWN by `+(tilt-90)` about its right axis to land the horizon on the local horizontal (`roll` is already baked out of the published tiles, so it is stored for reference only). A new *Elevation Offset°* control (`elevationOffset`/`setElevationOffset`, range -20..20°, step 0.1, in the Terrain → *Street View Pano* folder) trims any residual offset (positive raises the dome).
- **Fixed the look camera drifting vertically after "Center Camera On Street View"** (`63b64046`) (`src/StreetViewPanoUI.js`). The action placed the look camera in AGL mode, whose world height rides the terrain LOD; Google's 3D tiles refine their ground for a few seconds after centring, so the AGL camera drifted vertically and the once-snapped panorama backdrop fell out of alignment. The camera is now forced to a fixed (non-AGL) absolute altitude at the pano's captured height (HAE→MSL via `meanSeaLevelOffset`), so it cannot drift and the sphere snap cannot go stale.
