## Version 2.92.2 (2026-06-29)

### Improvements
- **New Help-menu quick links: FlightRadar24 replay and Earth Nullschool (wind)** (`7b16cfc3`) (`config/config.js.example`). Two new generators are added to `extraHelpFunctions` and wired into the Help menu (via `CustomSupport.js`): `fr24Replay` (label *FlightRadar24 Replay Here*) opens `flightradar24.com/<YYYY-MM-DD/HH:MM>/10x/<lat>,<lon>/9` for the current frame's date/time and the look-camera position; `earthNullSchoolLink` (label *Earth Nullschool (Wind) Here*) opens an `earth.nullschool.net` 250 hPa wind view at the same time/location. The three existing links are relabeled to the terser "… Here" form (*ADSB Replay Here*, *Google Maps Here*, *In-The-Sky Here*). This lives in the config *template*; the live per-deployment `config.js` is gitignored, so a hosted instance picks these up when it adopts the new template (fresh installs / Docker get them by default).
- **Street View panorama backdrop now renders in the look view only** (`75c3337b`) (`src/nodes/CNodeStreetViewPano.js`). The pano is a first-person backdrop for the observer camera, but its default layer mask was `MASK_WORLD | MASK_MAIN | MASK_LOOK`, so the textured sphere also appeared in the main (map) view and any other view. The default is now `MASK_LOOK` alone — it stays out of `MASK_MAINRENDER` while the look view (`MASK_LOOKRENDER`) still includes `MASK_LOOK` — so the panorama no longer covers the map view or other views.
- **New custom graphs spawn in distinct, non-overlapping positions** (`23f7a064`) (`src/CCustomGraphManager.js`). The old placement stepped each new graph by `0.04 * (n % 6)` (~4%) while graphs are 0.4 (40%) wide, so successive "Add Custom Graph" results piled up ~90% on top of each other (and the 7th wrapped exactly onto the 1st), making it look like the button reused the existing graph. New graphs now cycle a five-position `ANCHORS` set (four corners + centre, which don't overlap for a 0.4×0.4 graph), nudging each further cycle by 0.03 so later graphs don't land exactly on earlier ones. Deserialized graphs still get their saved position restored over this initial one, so only fresh adds are affected.
- **Street View Pano menu hidden in serverless/desktop (no-PHP) builds** (`675f2fef`) (`src/CustomManagerSetup.js`). `setupStreetViewPanoMenu()` is now gated behind `!isServerless`, because the panorama feature depends on the PHP stitcher endpoint (`sitrecServer/streetview.php`) which is absent in serverless/desktop builds — so the menu is no longer dead UI there.
- **DAG (node-graph) debug view is draggable and pop-outable with live rendering** (`675f2fef`) (`src/nodes/CNodeViewDAG.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeView.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeViewCanvas.js`, `src/CustomManagerSetup.js`). The admin-only DAG view now sets `poppable`/`dockable`/`renderWhileWindowed` true and drops the `draggable:false` override at its admin creation site, so it drags and pops out like the Notes view. Because the DAG is a live per-frame 2D canvas (not static DOM like Notes), a naive pop-out would freeze as a fixed-size bitmap — the render-on-demand loop sleeps when its window is backgrounded, and the canvas would size from the now-hidden in-page div. The new `renderWhileWindowed` flag drives the popped view from the popup window's own `requestAnimationFrame` (calling `renderCanvas(par.frame)` each tick, stopped in `dockWindow()` via `_popRendering`), and `CNodeViewCanvas.adjustSize()` sizes the canvas from `_poppedWindow.innerWidth/innerHeight` while windowed — so the popped DAG stays fully live and interactive (pan/zoom/resize).

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed menu clicks (e.g. "Add Custom Graph") being silently swallowed by a graph view, and an on-screen graph making the whole UI unclickable** (`23f7a064`) (`src/lil-gui-extras.js`, `src/mouseMoveView.js`). `#menuBar` is a full-window (100%×100%) positioning container; menu panels and views competed in the root stacking context, so a free-floating view bumped to the menu "front" tier could cover a menu, and the document-level mouse router routed the press to that view, which then took pointer capture and stole the `pointerup` — every other click then did nothing. Fixed in two interlocking layers: (1) `#menuBar` gets a high `z-index` (9000) so menus stack above all views, but `pointer-events:none` so its empty area is not a screen-covering click shield (clicks fall through to views); `pointer-events:auto` is restored on the actual menu surfaces via a new `_markMenuSurface()` helper applied to every surface path (bar slots, detached folders, standalone/context menus, sidebar undock/restore), and the black bar (`#menuBarBlackBar`) becomes purely visual (`z-index` 8999, `pointer-events:none`). (2) `mouseMoveView.js` adds `isTopMenuElementAt(x,y)` (an `elementFromPoint().closest("#menuBar")` test); `onDocumentMouseDown`/`onDocumentMouseMove` and `onDocumentWheel` bail when the cursor is over a menu, so a view can no longer grab and pointer-capture a press meant for a menu.
- **Fixed an unwanted grey box around overlay and attached views** (`675f2fef`) (`src/nodes/CNodeView.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeViewCanvas.js`). The 1px 25%-grey inset outline added in 2.91.3 was being drawn on overlay/attached views too — putting a grey box around the compass HUDs (`relativeTo` views) and the attached video sub-panels (histogram/levels/curve/info), plus a redundant double-drawn outline on full-size overlay canvases. Both outline sites are now gated with the codebase's standard "is a real base window" predicate: the canvas outline (`CNodeViewCanvas`) by `!this.overlayView && !this.in.relativeTo`, and the div outline (`CNodeView`) by `!this.in.relativeTo`. Base views (main/look/video/graphs/editors/DAG/Notes) keep their border.
- **Fixed occasional blank/corrupt Street View panoramas and 502 load failures under concurrent use** (`6e6d479f`) (`sitrecServer/streetview.php`). The stitched panorama JPEG (`imagejpeg(... , $cacheFile)`) and the session-token file were written directly to their final paths, so a concurrent reader could catch a half-written JPEG — which still passed the `filesize > 0` cache-hit check, yielding a corrupt/blank pano — or a torn session-token file, failing `createSession` and returning 502s. Both are now written to a unique temp file (`<path>.tmp.<pid>.<hex>`) and `rename()`d into place (atomic on the same filesystem); a failed write/rename unlinks the temp and fails cleanly, so a reader always sees either the old/absent file or the complete new one.

### Security
- **Street View panorama generation is now rate-limited for every account; the "unlimited" tier is removed** (`75c3337b`) (`sitrecServer/streetview.php`). Admin, Members (group 14), and Plus (group 19) accounts previously bypassed all caps on this billed Google Map Tiles endpoint — one `op=image` stitch fans out to ~32 tile fetches at the default zoom (up to ~162 at max zoom / the 40 MP cap), all billed against the project key. The `unlimited` tier is gone: `$TIER_LIMITS` now caps fresh (cache-miss) stitches per caller per hour at admin 60 / logged-in 60 / anonymous 10, plus a `$GLOBAL_PANOS_PER_HOUR = 120` backstop across all callers (including admin), and a per-minute flood guard (`reqPerMin`) on all requests. Logged-in callers are keyed on their stable forum `user_id` (resistant to IP rotation / shared Cloudflare edge IPs); anonymous callers key on `REMOTE_ADDR`. Only `op=image` stitches are billed (metadata / panoId / session lookups are free). This bounds request rate only; the authoritative spend cap remains a Google Cloud billing budget + Map Tiles key quota.

---

## Version 2.92.1 (2026-06-29)

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed the WASD walk camera flinging the look camera ~225 km into the Pacific** (`f3806f40`) (`src/nodes/CNode.js`, `src/js/CameraControls.js`). Root cause was the display-output gate in `recalculateNodesBreadthFirstRecurse` (`CNode.js` ~line 972): `!node.checkDisplayOutputs || node.countVisibleOutputs(0,true) > 0` is meant to skip *downstream* nodes whose display chain is switched off, but it also skipped re-baking the *explicitly-cascaded root* node's own value. `fixedCameraPosition` (a `CNodePositionLLA`) is constructed from the `SitCustom` default LLA `[31.980814, -118.428486, 10000]` — a point ~225 km out in the Pacific at 10 km altitude — so its constructor bakes `ecef` to that ocean default. On deserialize the saved street `_LLA` is restored via the "Cam [C] Lat/Lon" GUI `onChange`, which calls `recalculateCascade()`, but because the camera-track switch sits on a different option the `fixedCamera` branch has zero visible display outputs and its `recalculate()` was skipped — leaving `_LLA` = street but `ecef` = stale ocean default. Pressing W forced the switch to `fixedCamera` and read the stale ocean `ecef`, flinging the smoothed look camera out to sea. The fix adds `depth === 0` to the gate so the explicitly-cascaded root node always recalculates (healing both the load-time stale bake and the WASD edit path). `updateWASDWalking` (`CameraControls.js`) was also hardened: it drops the fragile `getPointBelow` terrain re-anchor (redundant, since `CNodePositionLLA.recalculate()` already places the camera per its mode, and `getPointBelow` returns garbage when the tile under the camera hasn't loaded), and bails out (`forwardHorizontal.lengthSq() < 1e-6`) when the look direction is near-vertical to avoid a NaN movement vector.
- **WASD walk and AGL altitudes now ride the 3D-tile surface instead of the elevation map** (`e44ab3db`, plus the ground-detection/lever-arm follow-up fixes that made it actually work) (`src/nodes/CNodeBuildings3DTiles.js`, `src/threeExt.js`, `src/nodes/CNodePositionLLA.js`, `src/js/CameraControls.js`). WASD walking and "above ground level" positioning previously measured the ground from the smooth elevation map (EGM96/geoid grid), which ignores buildings and often disagrees with the rendered Google Photorealistic / OSM 3D tiles — so walking down a street floated above or sank into the visible geometry. Those tiles live in the `buildings3DTiles` node, not `TerrainModel`, so `getPointBelow()` / `raycastLocalGround()` never saw them. New `CNodeBuildings3DTiles.groundBelow(worldPos)` raycasts straight down along the geodetic `-up` against the loaded tile meshes (`this.group`, which recursively covers all per-view `TilesRenderers`; reused scratch `_groundRaycaster` to avoid per-frame allocation). Crucially it does **not** take the lowest (or highest) hit: the lowest dips through a building's watertight shell to a sub-surface skirt polygon *below* the surrounding tarmac, so an AGL witness / WASD walker ended up inside or under the building, while the highest grabs a roof. Instead `groundBelow` reads the buildings-free elevation-map ground at that column (`getPointBelow`) as a reference height and returns the tile hit whose height is *closest* to it, rejecting the result entirely (returns null → caller falls back to the elevation map) when even the nearest hit is more than `GROUND_TOLERANCE` (40 m) off — which weeds out building roofs and coarse-streaming-tile garbage (a coarse ancestor tile sampled off-centre can sit tens of km below the true surface, measured −50 km at Copenhagen). `threeExt.getTilesPointBelow(A)` is a thin wrapper that defers to `buildings3DTiles` or returns null when there are no 3D tiles. `CameraControls.updateWASDWalking` snaps an absolute-altitude camera to 5 ft (`WASD_EYE_HEIGHT`, 1.524 m) above that tile surface and, for an AGL camera, just moves horizontally and lets `recalculate()` hold it the user's AGL height above the tiles — falling back to a plain horizontal move (never the elevation map, never `getPointBelow`) when no tile is loaded below. `CNodePositionLLA._aglGroundPoint` rides the same tile surface, wired into all three AGL branches of `recalculate()` and scoped to position nodes so long aircraft tracks don't pay a per-frame tile raycast. Two bugs there broke WASD-on-buildings until fixed: callers pass `pos` at 100 km altitude (the elevation-map column query is lever-arm-immune), but raycasting the tiles *from* that height gave a local-up ~5e-5 rad off the surface normal, which over the 100 km lever arm became a fixed ~5 m horizontal miss — so the camera walked off in one direction regardless of the key pressed; `_aglGroundPoint` now first resolves the near-surface elevation ground (`getPointBelowCached(..., 0, ...)`) and raycasts the tiles from *there*, keeping the column directly below the camera. `CNodeMeasureAltitude` (`CNodeLabels3D.js`) now also reads `getTilesPointBelow` (falling back to `TerrainModel.getPointBelow` / `pointOnSphereBelow`) so the on-screen "ft AGL" readout measures the same surface the camera rides — previously it measured the elevation map and disagreed with the camera (e.g. "17 ft agl" with buildings on vs "0 ft agl" off).
- **Made the 3D-tile-riding AGL camera deterministic as tiles stream in** (`src/nodes/CNodePositionLLA.js`). An AGL camera is first positioned at sitch-load before the building tiles have streamed in (so it starts on the elevation map); the tiles then stream coarse→fine. A one-shot refine latched onto whatever LOD loaded first, so the witness landed in different spots run-to-run, or rocketed hundreds of feet up when it locked onto a coarse mid-stream tile. New `CNodePositionLLA._refineAGLToTiles()` (called from `update()`) re-anchors via per-node `recalculateCascade()` whenever the tile ground directly below changes by more than 5 cm, *converging* onto the fully-settled (deterministic) tile geometry and going quiet once the tiles stop refining. It deliberately uses per-node recalc, never the global `elevationChanged` broadcast — which would re-run every AGL track/label/MISB node on each of the hundreds of mid-stream tile events and starve the tile loader. The `groundBelow` `GROUND_TOLERANCE` gate (above) is what keeps this convergence from locking onto coarse-tile/roof garbage in the meantime.
- **Fixed a spurious "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'fps')" console error during initial sitch load** (`src/index.js`). On the async `?custom=` load path `setSit()` runs after an `await fetch(...)`, and the render-on-demand loop can be woken inside that window (visibilitychange / `setRenderOne` / `par.frame`), firing the `setTimeout`-scheduled `animate()` while `Sit` is still `undefined` and throwing at `Sit.fps`. `animate()` now bails out early (`if (Sit === undefined) return;`) when no sitch is established yet; `startAnimating()` re-arms the loop once it is. Harmless but noisy; no rendering was lost.

## Version 2.92.0 (2026-06-28)

### New Features
- **Scripting (cinematic camera) — renamed from "Scripted Video" and substantially expanded** (Video → Scripting). The text-script system that drives a cinematic camera on its own timeline and renders to a 1080p60 MP4 is renamed throughout: the `guiMenus.video` folder is now "Scripting" and its launcher item "Scripting Window…", and the editor window title changes from "Scripted Video" to "Scripting" (`src/CScriptedVideo.js`, `src/scriptedVideo/ScriptEditorWindow.js`). Scripts are JavaScript plus a flat one-line DSL (`src/scriptedVideo/ScriptCommands.js`, run via `ScriptJSRunner`).
- **Multiple named scripts as editor tabs** (`2fab7a0b`). The editor (`CScriptEditorWindow`) now owns a tabs model — several named scripts, each with its own text — persisted to `localStorage` under the new `TABS_KEY` (`sitrec_scripting_tabs`, `{tabs:[{name,text}], activeTab}`) and into the saved sitch. The serializer (`serializeScriptedVideo()`) calls `syncActiveFromEditor()` and is back-compatible with the old single-script payload (legacy `STORAGE_KEY` is migrated into a tab).
- **New DSL camera commands.** (`2fab7a0b`, `c7d0d2bb`, `63b52cce`)
  - `from <target> <bearing> <distance> <elevation> <secs>` — absolute, target-relative camera placement by bearing / distance / elevation (`from`'s elevation is wheel-editable, `role:deg`, since `c7d0d2bb`).
  - `moveto <pos> <secs> [lookAt]` — absolute camera placement to a world point (vs `from`'s target-relative placement), eased from the current pose over `<secs>` (0 = snap).
  - `follow <target> <secs> <distance> <height>` — third-person follow-cam that trails a moving target, staying behind it along its direction of motion and up by `<height>`, looking just ahead ("see what they see"); the camera swings around as the target rounds a corner.
  - `orbit` gains an optional helical `rise` argument (`role:dist`) that climbs the camera by that many metres along local vertical over the orbit — a "fly up and around" in one beat without a competing concurrent `rise`.
  - `linger` — alias for `wait` (`COMMAND_ALIASES`, alongside `title→text`, `on→show`, `off→hide`).
  - Target aliases `object` / `witness` / `camera`.
  - `view photo` dynamic preset — the witness photo (the video source) letterboxed to its own aspect and composited over the full-frame 3D main view, with the video faded in/out to dissolve to/from the real photo (`_photoOverlayLayout()`, registered as the `photo` view preset).
- **Capture button — author camera moves by flying** (`70283567`). A "Capture" toolbar button in the script editor (`ScriptEditorWindow._winButton`) calls `CScriptedVideoManager.captureCurrentView()`, which reads the live main-view camera pose and inserts a `moveto lat,lon,alt secs lat,lon,alt` line reproducing that exact pose (verified round-trip < 1 m). Ends the type-coordinates-and-guess loop for camera placement.
- **Scripts can address scene objects as visibility controls** (`4d145318`, `63155806`). `CSitrecAPI._resolveControl` now falls back to a 3D-object node (by id) when no menu control matches, presenting the object's visibility as a synthetic boolean controller. So the DSL `set` / `show` / `hide` (e.g. `hide Viewer`) — and `setMenuValue` over MCP / the in-app assistant — can toggle scene objects, not just menu controls; the manager's snapshot / apply-at-t / restore machinery is unchanged. Resolution order was hardened so an **exact** scene-object id beats a **partial** menu-control match (so `hide Viewer` toggles the object, not a menu button that merely contains "Viewer"), while exact menu controls still win.
- **`createWalker` API — a marker that walks a lat/lon path** (`63155806`). New assistant/MCP-callable `createWalker` builds a marker object (default a cylinder) that follows a linear track through lat/lon waypoints over a frame range, holding at the last waypoint, oriented upright to local vertical. Idempotent re-create tears down the object, its derived sub-nodes, and its track. It is intended as a moving subject for `follow` / `from` to film.
- **Assistant / MCP authoring API for cinematics** (`6a8f2513`). New `CSitrecAPI` methods `setScriptedVideoScript` / `previewScriptedVideo` / `stopScriptedVideo` let the in-app assistant (and MCP) author and play cinematic camera scripts. Documented in `docs/ScriptedVideo.md` and the MCP agent guide (`tools/SitrecBridge/sitrec-mcp-guide.md`) with a full "Scripting (Cinematic Camera)" section: DSL command tables, authoring rules, and a worked example.

### Improvements
- **Smooth, continuous camera motion + above-ground clamp** (`0221aab8`). A global `cameraSmoothing` (~0.35 s) in `applyCameraForTime` blends the camera across beat boundaries so the per-beat ease-in/ease-out no longer stops at every cut — one flowing, drone-like path, applied to both preview and render. `groundClearance` keeps the camera a few metres above the terrain. `follow` was reworked to ease OUT of the previous beat's pose (no snap-in), sample its heading over a wide frame baseline (gliding through waypoint corners), and use a deterministic fallback heading (the beat's overall direction) so the end pose is never vertical — fixing an underground snap that made the next `flyto` start from below ground.
- **Frame-accurate follow targets** (`63b52cce`). `follow` reads target velocity by frame-sampling a `createWalker` object's underlying track via `targetPos` — an object's `p(f)` returns its *current rendered* position, not a frame sample, which is useless for velocity. `localUp` is passed into the sample context so `follow` can flatten the heading (`ScriptCameraEngine.js`).
- **Timeline interaction rework** (`40a5dbaa`, `2a9d4f3c`) (`src/scriptedVideo/ScriptTimelineWidget.js`). A dedicated top "ruler" strip (~22% height) always scrubs and holds the playhead handle, with view-cut labels moved below it. Cmd/Ctrl+wheel zooms centred on the cursor's time (scroll up = zoom in); plain wheel still pans / edits a segment's duration over its edge. A fat ±6 px playhead grab zone takes top priority so you can drag the playhead even when it sits on a segment (brightens on hover). Scrub-drag snaps to view-cut / segment edges within ~5 px (Cmd bypasses). Per-zone cursors and a discoverability tooltip were added. The number-edit wheel (`adjustNumberToken`, `ScriptEditorWindow.js`) was flipped to treat positive `deltaY` as increment so scroll-up increases a number under macOS natural scrolling (and the drag synth updated so dragging up still increments).
- **Inline error/warning underlines + parse warnings** (`bf2fa189`, `c7d0d2bb`). Parse errors and the new `parseWarnings` now mark their offending line in the editor backdrop with a wavy underline — red for errors, amber for warnings — parsed from the `line N:` message prefix (`ScriptEditorWindow.js`). The two silent footguns now warn in the editor status: overlapping camera beats (they fight for the camera) and targets that did not resolve (the beat silently holds its start pose).
- **Render at best 3D-tiles LOD with per-frame cross-fade** (`a59e053d`) (`src/scriptedVideo/ScriptRenderer.js`, `src/CScriptedVideo.js`). The offline render previously looked coarser than the live preview because `tame3DTiles` forced the moving view's tile `errorTarget` from 20 (fine) to 80 (coarse) and zeroed the fade to settle. The render now runs at the preview's best LOD (`renderTilesError=20`). Each output frame is classified: *warmup* (first frame / view cut / secs-0 snap / camera jump > `cutJumpThreshold`) renders fade-off with a full settle for a crisp keyframe; *continuous* frames stream new tiles then advance the wall-clock cross-fade (`FadeManager._lastTick`) by exactly one video-frame and capture, so LOD swaps fade in over video time instead of popping or being waited out. `renderMaxFrames>0` renders only the first N frames for fast test renders.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed Google Photorealistic 3D Tiles ground-level terrain dropping out after timeline scrub** (`e345e751`) (`src/nodes/CNodeBuildings3DTiles.js`). Near-field terrain would drop to a flat gray void (or stay low-LOD) after scrubbing the timeline forward and back in a ground-level view. Primary cause: the 3d-tiles-renderer LRU cache's default byte budget (`maxBytesSize` 0.4 GB) is too small for a ground-level dense-city working set (~0.8 GB / ~1500 tiles). `TilesRendererBase.update()` only requests downloads while `!lruCache.isFull()`; once byte-full with every loaded tile in-frustum (none evictable) it requests nothing, so the current frame's near-field tiles never download — scrubbing fills the cache with tiles from the camera's *other* positions, locking out the return frame's tiles. The per-renderer byte budget is raised to 1.5 GB max / 1.0 GB min (a ceiling, not an allocation: used tiles are never evicted and the cache holds only the real working set, so views that don't need the headroom pay nothing). Complementary fix: honor the library's "needs-update" event, which fires from async completion callbacks (tile parsed, root tileset loaded, nodes processed) *outside* `update()`. `PerViewTiles.update()` skips `renderer.update()` once the camera is static and the tileset looks settled; a tile finishing in that window would otherwise be stuck loaded-but-invisible. The signal is latched, the render-on-demand loop woken, and one rescue `update()` run; events stop once streaming stops, so this never defeats the idle GC optimization.
- **Fixed the scripted-video preview being left stuck at coarse terrain detail after a render** (`a59e053d`). `tame3DTiles` never restored the moving view's tile `errorTarget` after a render, leaving the live preview coarse. The renderer now saves and restores `errorTarget` and `fadeDuration` in a `finally` block.

## Version 2.91.3 (2026-06-27)

### Improvements
- **A thin border now outlines every view so its edges are always visible.** `CNodeView` (`src/nodes/CNodeView.js`) and `CNodeViewCanvas` (`src/nodes/CNodeViewCanvas.js`) apply a 1px 25%-transparent grey inset outline (`outline: '1px solid rgba(128,128,128,0.25)'` with `outlineOffset: '-1px'`). It is drawn as an *inset outline* rather than a CSS border so it paints inside the view bounds and does not shift content or change layout. The outline is duplicated on the canvas in `CNodeViewCanvas` because the full-size canvas fills the div and would otherwise paint over the div's outline.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed video filters not re-filtering the current frame.** Two related cache-staleness bugs in the video filter pipeline (`src/nodes/CNodeVideoView.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeVideoViewFilters.js`):
  - **Stale frame during playback.** `applyConvolutionToImage()` (Convolution: sharpen / edge-detect / emboss) and `applyLevelsToImage()` (Levels) keyed their result cache on the input-image object identity plus their own params, but not the frame number. During playback the decoder reuses the same image object across frames, so identity alone returned a stale filtered result and the filtered video appeared frozen. Both functions now take a `frame` argument and add `_convLastFrame` / `_levelsLastFrame` to their cache checks, matching the existing curves/tonal/invert pattern.
  - **Stale downstream stage on a filter change.** Each cached stage in the chain (convolution → levels → curves → tonal → invert) keys its cache on the *identity* of its input canvas, but every stage reuses the same output-canvas object across renders. So changing an upstream filter setting while paused left a downstream stage serving pixels computed from the old upstream input (same input-canvas identity, same frame, same own-params). New `getFilterStateKey()` builds a fingerprint of every cached-stage filter setting; `getAdjustedVideoFrameSource()` compares it against `_lastFilterStateKey` at the head of the chain and, on any change, calls `invalidateAllFilterCaches()` to flush every stage so the current frame is fully re-filtered. The key is stable across frames, so playback's per-frame caches are untouched and there is no spurious flushing.

## Version 2.91.2 (2026-06-27)

### Bug Fixes
- Fixed the "Center Camera On Street View" button (Terrain → Street View Pano) placing the look camera far too high when the camera was in AGL (above-ground-level) mode. The panorama centre altitude is HAE (height above the ellipsoid), but `fixedCameraPosition` stores its altitude in a mode-dependent datum: in AGL mode `_LLA[2]` is height *above terrain* (its recalc cascade adds the ground back), otherwise it is MSL (the cascade adds the geoid offset). `centerCameraOnPano()` in `src/StreetViewPanoUI.js` passed the raw HAE straight to `setLLA`, so in AGL mode the ground was double-counted and the camera ended up ~ground-level too high (dramatic over high terrain — about 1.3 km up over the Mexican highlands). It now converts to the node's datum first: in AGL mode it subtracts the terrain ground via `elevationAtLL(lat, lon, true)`, otherwise it subtracts the geoid offset via `meanSeaLevelOffset(lat, lon)`, so the camera lands at the panorama's actual height either way.
- Fixed the "Street View Pano" folder jumping to the top of the Terrain menu after a sitch reload. The folder is built once and marked permanent so it survives reloads, but `setupStreetViewPanoMenu()` early-returned (`if (svFolder) return`) on every subsequent load and so never re-asserted its position; meanwhile the Terrain menu is rebuilt per sitch and re-appends its own folders *after* the permanent one, leaving Street View Pano floating to the top. A new `positionStreetViewFolder()` helper now runs on every load: it places the folder immediately after "Remove Geometry" (falling back to the bottom when that folder is absent — "Remove Geometry" only exists for Google Photorealistic 3D Tiles), then re-asserts "Terrain Tweaks" as the last entry. This required a new folder-aware `GUI.prototype.moveAfter(name)` in `src/lil-gui-extras.js`, because the existing `Controller.prototype.moveAfter` only matched a sibling controller's `.name` and could not target a sibling folder by its `.title`.

---

## Version 2.91.1 (2026-06-27)

### Bug Fixes
- Fixed variable/odd-timescale MP4s loading with the wrong frame count and playing back jerky/slow-motion. `MP4Source` in `src/js/mp4-decode/mp4_demuxer.js` passed mp4box's `sample.cts`/`sample.duration` straight into `EncodedVideoChunk`, but mp4box reports those in the track's media timescale while WebCodecs `EncodedVideoChunk.timestamp`/`duration` are defined in microseconds. The two coincide only when the timescale is exactly 1,000,000, which is why most videos worked; a track with any other timescale (real example: a 30 fps phone/social re-encode at timescale 9,000,000) got per-frame timestamps scaled by `timescale/1e6` (9x too large). The inflated `framePTSus` made `CVideoPatchedData.shouldWrap()` see giant inter-frame gaps and pad a 432-frame 14.4 s / 30 fps stream out to 3880 virtual frames (89% held copies), shown with the debug red "held frame" squares. The fix converts `cts`/`duration` to integer microseconds using `sample.timescale`, rounded to an integer so the decoder echoes the same timestamp back on the output `VideoFrame` and `timestampToChunkIndex` still matches. VFR / odd-timescale MP4s (including this representative 29.9–30.0 fps case) now load with the correct frame count and play smoothly.
- Fixed patched (irregular-cadence) videos disappearing after save + reload. `CVideoPatchedData` (the wrapper that gives dropped-frame videos a uniform virtual timeline) delegated many calls to its wrapped `source` but never exposed the `videoDroppedData`/`videoDroppedURL` *properties*. Save-time rehosting reads `videoData.videoDroppedData` directly off the live `videoData` — which is the wrapper once a video is patched — so the raw bytes looked absent, rehosting was skipped, and the saved sitch stored only a bare local filename that could not be restored on reload. Added delegating getters/setters for `videoDroppedData` and `videoDroppedURL` to the wrapped source, making the wrapper transparent to every consumer (save rehost for S3 and local-folder via `rehostDynamicLinks`/`rehostVideoNodeLocal`/`findHeldBytesForSource`, object tracking, and the export-detection in `CFileManagerParse`).
- External-URL image/video sources (e.g. a Facebook CDN signed link dragged in from a web page) now have a permanent copy rehosted at save time. Previously such sources kept the remote URL as their `staticURL` and were not flagged `dynamicLink`, so the existing dropped-data rehost path skipped them; when the signed URL later expired (403) the saved sitch showed a black view. `rehostDynamicLinks()` in `src/CFileManagerSave.js` now rehosts a permanent copy when Sitrec still holds the original bytes, via new helpers `isRehostableExternalURL()` (remote http(s) not already on our upload area), `findHeldBytesForSource()` (exact-key lookup, then this entry's `videoData.videoDroppedData`, then a scheme+host+path fuzzy match gated by `hasVolatileSignedQuery()` so distinct resources differing only by a meaningful query like `image?id=1` vs `image?id=2` are never conflated), and `deriveExternalRehostFilename()` (commit `d9757007`).
- Fixed a garbage file extension on the rehosted object key when cropping a long (>100 char) dropped-video filename that contains a literal `?` (e.g. "...UAP o IA?.mp4"). The crop path used `getFileExtension()`, which routes through `stripURLSuffixPreservingHashParameters()` and treats the `?` as a URL query separator, stripping the real ".mp4" and deriving the extension from an earlier dot. The crop now derives the extension from the last dot directly and crops the base, keeping a clean ".mp4". Cosmetic — the `sitrec://` reference still resolved, so no data was lost.

---

## Version 2.91.0 (2026-06-27)

### New Features
- **Street View panorama background sphere** (Terrain → Street View Pano). New `CNodeStreetViewPano` (`src/nodes/CNodeStreetViewPano.js`) renders a Google Street View panorama as an inside-viewed textured sphere (BackSide material + flipped texture, `renderOrder -1000` / `depthWrite:false` background layer) centred at its ECEF location and oriented to the pano's metadata heading via a local ENU basis, with a lazy fetch guarded by generation/disposed checks. Imagery is fetched and stitched entirely server-side by the new `sitrecServer/streetview.php` (session-token caching, panoId resolve, metadata, per-pano Zmax tile-grid stitch into one equirectangular JPEG via GD, disk cache) **so the Google Map Tiles API key never reaches the browser**; the endpoint sends the key's required `Referer` and is hardened with a CORS origin allow-list, zoom clamp, output-pixel cap, complete-stitch-or-fail caching, and per-IP + global stitch rate limits. The "Street View Pano" folder (`src/StreetViewPanoUI.js`, wired via `CustomManagerSetup.js`) provides Latitude / Longitude / Altitude (m) fields, *Use Camera Position*, *Zoom (detail)*, *Fetch Panorama*, *Fetch At Camera* (copy the camera position then fetch the nearest pano), *Center Camera On Street View* (moves the look/observer camera to the pano's exact captured location, correcting the offset from Google snapping to the nearest pano), *Heading Offset°*, *Sphere Radius (m)*, *Opacity*, *Visible*, and a read-only *Status*. Per-group rate limiting (`5fd69102`) tiers the endpoint by forum group (reusing `getUserInfo()` / `isAdmin()` / `user_groups`, as `rehost.php` does): admins (group 3) and Sitrec Members/Plus (14/19) are unlimited; other logged-in users get 120 requests/min and 60 fresh stitches/min per IP; anonymous callers get one-tenth (12 req/min, 6 stitches/min), with a 60/min global cache-miss stitch backstop applied to all non-privileged callers as anti-IP-rotation defense.

### Improvements
- **"Edit Geometry (Trees)" renamed to "Remove Geometry"; manual brush strokes are now undoable** (Terrain → Remove Geometry). `buildTreeRemovalGUI()` (`src/nodes/CNodeTerrainUI.js`) renames the folder to "Remove Geometry" and the brush toggle from "Manual Edit" to "Manual Remove" (`TREE_FLATTEN_DEFS`, `src/TilesTreeFlatten.js`), and is now built in two passes so the manual-remove controls and the "Restore Geometry" reset sit at the folder root while the tree-specific "Automatic Tree Removal" heuristics drop to a sub-menu at the bottom. Each manual brush gesture (pointer-down→up, plus window-blur) is now a single undo unit: the brush (`src/TreeManualBrush.js`) snapshots the append-only dab list at pointer-down (`snapshotDabs()`) and commits an `UndoManager` action at pointer-up (`commitStrokeUndo()`). Because dabs apply idempotently per tile mesh (DAB_COUNT stamp, tail-only), undo/redo swap the dab list then restore original geometry and replay the chosen set via `restoreDabsState()` (`src/nodes/CNodeBuildings3DTiles.js`), gated by "Apply Edits" so an undo while edits are hidden stays hidden. Ctrl/Cmd+Z already routes to the global `undoManager`.
- **Advanced terrain controls grouped into a "Terrain Tweaks" sub-folder** (Terrain → Terrain Tweaks). `CNodeTerrainUI` (`src/nodes/CNodeTerrainUI.js`) collects the assorted tweak/debug controls — debug elevation grid, elevation scale, terrain opacity, texture/elevation detail, dynamic-subdivision toggles, building edges, tile edges, building material mode + flat colour, and the ellipsoid Earth-model toggle — into a new collapsed `terrainTweaks` sub-folder pinned to the bottom of the Terrain menu via `moveToEnd()`, decluttering the top level. It is recreated per sitch so it self-heals on reload.

## Version 2.90.2 (2026-06-27)

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed a sitch-load race where a superseded load could corrupt the next sitch — black "look" and "video" views and a blank left sidebar.** Loading a saved/custom sitch, switching to another sitch (e.g. *Tree Test*) before the first finished loading, then loading the first again could leave the new sitch with hidden look/video views and an empty sidebar. In-session loads serialize via the 500ms `newSitchObject` poll, but two pieces of a load's work are deferred and *not* awaited by that serialization, so they outlive the transition: (1) `CFileManager.loadAsset()`'s `add()` happens in a late `.then()` long after its call-time, filename-keyed dedup checks (`#loadingPromises`/`exists(id)`), so a stale or duplicate-id load reaching `add()` either asserted "adding X twice" or, in production (asserts stripped), silently clobbered the live entry; (2) `CustomManagerSerialize.deserializeMods()` awaits `waitForPendingActions()` between mods (e.g. while a video decodes) and is launched fire-and-forget, so when parked across a transition it resumed and applied the OLD sitch's mods (including a doubled/fullscreen `mainView`) onto the NEW sitch's nodes, hiding look/video. The fix adds a monotonic `Globals.loadGeneration` counter bumped by `disposeEverything()` on every teardown (`src/Globals.js`, `src/index.js`): `deserializeMods()` snapshots the generation, bails after each await if it changed (returning `false`), and `finishDeserialization()` is skipped for a superseded load (`src/CustomManagerSerialize.js`); `loadAsset()`'s late `.then()` now reuses an existing entry instead of re-adding (`src/CFileManager.js`); and `newSitch()` is wrapped in `try/finally` so `isTransitioning` always clears — a throw mid-transition previously left `renderMain()` permanently short-circuited into all-black views with a blank sidebar and no recovery but a reload (`src/index.js`). Verified via MCP repro (assert and sampled fullscreen-leak gone) and fast-regression (40 pass, 0 real regressions).

---

## Version 2.90.1 (2026-06-26)

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed a crash on every default app load (`?action=new`) introduced by the 2.90.0 UI redesign.** `CNode.applyEarlyMods()` (`src/nodes/CNode.js`) dereferenced the global `Sit` unconditionally (`if (Sit.mods && Sit.mods[this.id])`). The 2.90.0 lazy-editor refactor made the Scripted Video editor a `CNodeView` created on first use, and `CScriptedVideoManager.setupMenu()` runs an initial `parse()` at app-init time, which now calls `getScriptText()` → `ensureEditor()` and constructs the editor `CNodeView` **before any sitch is loaded**. The `CNodeView` constructor calls `applyEarlyMods()` while `Sit` is still `undefined`, throwing `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'mods')`. The guard is now `if (Sit?.mods && Sit.mods[this.id])` — with no sitch loaded there are no mods to apply, so early-mod application is simply skipped. The error was non-fatal to rendering (so the pixel-only fast visual-regression suite never caught it), but it surfaced as an uncaught page error on load, which the Docker-image Playwright smoke test (which asserts zero console errors) flagged.

---

## Version 2.90.0 (2026-06-26)

### New Features
- **Per-view header bars (UIBar) — a Blender-style "area header" on every view.** New `src/CUIBar.js` (`CUIBar`) plus `CNodeView.createViewHeader()` (`src/nodes/CNodeView.js`) give each non-HUD view an overlay header strip drawn *above* the canvas (it never insets the viewport, so showing/hiding it changes no 3D rendering). The bar lays out as `[ title menu | extra menus ][ spacer ][ icons | pin | close ]`. The title is itself a lil-gui menu named with a friendly capitalised view name ("Main", "Look", "Video", "Assistant", …, via `friendlyViewName()`) and becomes the home for per-view options (replacing the old custom-graph tab menu). Standard chrome icons, left→right: fullscreen (⛶), pop-out (⧉, where applicable), pin (📌), close (✕). The big content views (`mainView`, `lookView`, `video`, `video2` — `HEADER_DEFAULT_OFF`) default to an unpinned hover-reveal header; editor/panel views default pinned. Pin state is serialized per view (`pinHeader`); the bar itself is pure runtime chrome and is not serialized. The header strip is also a drag handle (drag it to move the view, no modifier needed), with `HEADER_DRAG_MOVE_THRESHOLD` (5 px) before it starts moving.
- **Tiling layout with draggable shared-edge seams.** New `src/CLayoutManager.js` (`CLayoutManager` / `LayoutMan`) replaces the old guillotine split-tree with an adjacency-based model (Strategy C): views stay free fractional rectangles, and once per frame (`updateSeams()`, called from `src/indexRender.js`) it scans visible top-level view pixel rects for *shared edges* — where one view's edge is flush against another's (`EDGE_TOL` 6 px, `MIN_OVERLAP` 16 px). Every shared edge (even a partial-length one) gets a draggable grab strip (`GRAB_PX` 8 px) on an absolute seam layer; dragging it (`_onSeamPointerDown` → `_applySeam`) moves the coupled views' edges together — one grows while the other shrinks, clamped so the shrinking side never drops below `MIN_VIEW_PX`. This lets a small view share a partial edge with a larger one and still resize both from one handle, which the old full-length cuts could not. The retired tree API (`setLayout`/`clearLayout`/`serialize`/`dockViewAt`/…) is kept as no-op stubs so old saved sitches with a serialized `layout` tree load unchanged (views just come back as free rects and the seam engine re-couples flush edges).
- **Reset Layout (View menu).** `guiMenus.view` gains a "Reset Layout" item (`src/index.js`) wired to `LayoutMan.resetLayout()`: snaps the open tileable views into a clean default grid — `mainView` to the left half, the rest stacked in the right half (or an even horizontal split when there's no main view).
- **Pop a DOM panel out into a real separate browser window (⧉).** New shared path on `CNodeView`: `popOut()` / `dockWindow()` / `togglePopout()` (commit `b57b4699`). A `poppable` flag adds the ⧉ "Pop out into a window" header icon; `popOut()` `window.open`s a popup and moves the view's content (the div's children except the CUIBar header) into it, setting `--sitrec-header-h:0` there so the content fills the window — so a panel can be dragged onto a second monitor. While popped out the `windowed` flag makes the in-page view "closed for layout": `_computeEV` forces it invisible (`src/CViewManager.js`), `_collectRects` excludes it from seams (`CLayoutManager`), and its div hides. Closing the popup (or the main window, via `pagehide`) docks it back. Made poppable: `CNodeNotes` and `CNodeViewText` (chat / debug / ephemeris). Charts are deliberately not poppable yet.
- **Detach a tiled view by dragging its header.** Dragging a view's header past `HEADER_DRAG_DETACH_THRESHOLD` (30 px) pops it out of the layout into a free-floating window (`CNodeView` header-drag handling, Blender-style detach).
- **Double-click a view's header to toggle fullscreen.** Double-clicking the header strip (`src/mouseMoveView.js` document `dblclick` listener, registered in the capture phase so a hover-revealed bar can't swallow it) toggles fullscreen for that view — for both pinned and hover-revealed headers — and no longer moves the camera. Double-clicking the view *content* no longer fullscreens. `src/js/CameraControls.js` `handleMouseUp` skips the double-tap zoom-to-point when the pointerup lands in the header strip (the real "camera jumps to origin" cause).
- **Close a view from its header, with Undo.** The header ✕ calls `CNodeView.closeViewWithUndo()` which hides the view and records an undoable action (`UndoManager`), so Undo reopens it and Redo closes it again.

### Improvements
- **Scripted Video editor is now a tileable, always-on-top view.** `CScriptEditorWindow` (`src/scriptedVideo/ScriptEditorWindow.js`) was converted from a body-level floating panel into a `CNodeView` subclass (commit `fba7e3a6`), so it joins the unified window system: standard CUIBar header titled "Scripted Video", header-drag + Q-body-drag + edge-resize, participation in `LayoutMan` seams, and `alwaysOnTop` so it stays above other views. Because views are sitch-scoped (disposed on reload), the editor is created lazily via `ensureEditor()` and sets `modSerialize=undefined` so it is never saved. Its bespoke "⧉ New Window" toolbar button and the redundant "Script Window (New Window)" Video-menu item were removed in favour of the shared ⧉ pop-out path. `CViewManager.updateZOrder` now checks `alwaysOnTop` before `scriptZ` so tool windows stay above scripted-preview layouts.
- **DOM panels consolidated onto the single base chrome path; 128 px minimum view size.** `CNodeNotes` and `CNodeChartView` dropped their own bespoke tab/close/drag/resize code and now use the standard `createViewHeader` → CUIBar + `makeDraggable` + `makeResizable` (commit `604a95b7`). New shared constant `MIN_VIEW_PX = 128` in `src/DragResizeUtils.js`: both edge-resize and seam-drag clamp to it, so a view can't be shrunk into a sliver (was 20 px / 40 px respectively). The CUIBar title menu width is `max-content` instead of lil-gui's fixed 245 px, so the icons abut the title as a view narrows instead of being shoved off the right edge.
- **AI Model dropdown mirrored into the Assistant view header.** New `src/MenuMirror.js` (`registerMirrorSource` / `mirrorMenuItem`) surfaces an existing lil-gui controller inside another menu without duplicating its logic — both bind to the same `object[property]` and stay in sync, including async option-list refreshes (the model-fetch `options()` call propagates to every mirror). First use (commit `192e0f9f`) mirrors the Settings "AI Model" dropdown into the Assistant view's header menu. `clearMenuMirrors()` is called on sitch teardown.
- **Terrain refines after a resize/fullscreen/layout change without needing a camera move.** `src/nodes/CNodeTerrainUI.js` now folds `widthPx`/`heightPx` into the camera fingerprint so a viewport size change re-arms the LOD subdivision grace (screen-space error is measured in real pixels, so a resize genuinely changes it). `CNodeView.changedSize()` arms `setRenderOne(true)` on an actual dimension change so the deferred renderer resize actually repaints under render-on-demand (commit `2e223147`).

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed render-on-demand stalling on a visible but OS-unfocused tab.** Commit `a45c2518`. The render loop's focus gate (`shouldSleepAnimationLoop` did `if (paused && !focused) return true`; `wakeAnimationLoop` bailed on `paused && !windowFocused`) suppressed work on a tab that was visible but not the focused OS window — the common case when Sitrec is driven alongside another window. Result: an armed `renderOne` (toggling a layer, FOV, fullscreen) never painted until the window was refocused (e.g. a camera-moving mousedown), and `hasPausedBackgroundWork()` (terrain LOD subdivision, building tiles) was skipped so tiles never finished loading. The original ~600% CPU runaway the focus gate was meant to stop had two other roots, both already fixed and retained (the `setRenderOne(false)` no-op clear, and `CNodeTerrainUI` keeping `updateWhilePaused` true forever), so the focus gate was now pure downside. The loop is now gated only on `document.hidden` (`src/renderLoopControl.ts` drops the `focused` arg and the `paused && !focused` clause; `src/index.js` removes the focus listener and the `_mcpDebug` carve-out, keeps `visibilitychange`). A visible tab always runs requested renders and finite background work; an idle paused tab still sleeps because the `updateWhilePaused` producers self-terminate. Shared render-loop code, so this benefits the main build too.
- **Fixed a scripted-video deserialize crash that mispointed the look camera and left the scene unsettled.** Commit `7ff389a9`. After the editor became lazily created (`this.editor = null` until `ensureEditor()`), `deserializeScriptedVideo()` (`src/CScriptedVideo.js`) still dereferenced `scriptedVideo.editor.textarea`, throwing a `TypeError` for any sitch with a saved script. Because `finishDeserialization()` runs un-awaited, the throw became an unhandled rejection that aborted the rest of deserialization — skipping the camera-controller gating recalc (so the look camera was clobbered by ungated celestial/horizon-flare controllers) and all post-load finalization (the scene never settled, hitting a 90 s settle timeout). The fix routes through `ensureEditor()` like `getScriptText()` does.
- **Hardened header/view drags.** `makeDraggable` (`src/DragResizeUtils.js`) now also ends the drag on `pointercancel` (commit `6e6dcc72`), so an interrupted drag can't leave orphaned drag state or document listeners.
- **Suppressed the per-view header under regression mode** so the hover-reveal bar's timing-dependent visibility no longer flakes the visual baselines (`CUIBar.setShown` short-circuits when `Globals.regression` is set; commit `7ff389a9`). Test-only; changes no normal use or 3D rendering.

---

## Version 2.89.2 (2026-06-26)

### Improvements
- **Faster startup and a 55% smaller main download.** Commit `91958497` cuts the production main JS chunk from 3.66 MB to 1.66 MB gzip (9.0 MB → 5.78 MB raw). The biggest lever was the EGM96 geoid: `egm96-universal` embedded the 721×1440 int16 grid as a 2.77 MB base64 string (~1.87 MB gzipped) that was parsed on the main thread at startup. It is now shipped as `data/egm96/egm96-15.bin` = `gzip(planar(rowDelta(grid)))` (849 KB), generated by `scripts/extractEGM96Geoid.js` (round-trip validated, max error 0 over 3332 points). `src/EGM96Geoid.js` is rewritten as a self-contained lazy loader (`fetch` + native `DecompressionStream` + the exact bilinear algorithm), gated behind `ensureGeoidLoaded()` awaited at the top of `SituationSetupFromData` (`src/SituationSetup.js`) and in `src/EXIFUtils.js` image import. The grid is fetched only when a sitch actually needs elevation (never on a bare browser load) and has a stable filename, so it is cached across deploys instead of re-downloaded inside every contenthashed bundle. Additional code-splits: `mathjs` → the `mathjs/number` entry (`CNodeMath`, `CClientNLU`) drops BigNumber/decimal.js, Complex, Fraction and any-type matrix/unit machinery while keeping every scalar function the slider/value expressions and the degrees-trig calculator use; `jszip` moves from 5 static imports to dynamic `import()` at its already-async call sites (and a dead import is removed from `CFileManager`); `chrono-node` is lazy-imported inside `parsePartialDateTime`, its only caller. Supporting changes: `scripts/babelDynamicImportToRequire.cjs` (test-env Babel plugin so Jest transforms dynamic `import()`), updated `CNodeTrack`/`EXIFUtils` test mocks for the async geoid loader, `webpackCopyPatterns.js` includes `data/egm96` in serverless builds, and `generateThirdPartyNotices.js` adds the public-domain EGM96 data attribution. Verified: full fast-regression 39/39 pass; affected Jest suites green.
- **Edit Geometry (Trees) snap-to-ground now reaches the real street, even on trees.** In commit `246be914`, trees live in separate tile meshes from the ground, so the old within-mesh snap search only found the tree's own trunk base (roughly half-way down). `TreeFlattener.lowestGroundPoint` + `CNodeBuildings3DTiles._groundFloorH` now compute the ground floor as the lowest *original* vertex within a fixed 20 m horizontal cylinder (`GROUND_SEARCH_RADIUS`) searched **across all loaded meshes**, computed once at paint time and persisted on the dab as ground altitude `g` (stable as tiles stream and on reload; idempotent because it's the lowest original vertex over a fixed neighbourhood). `_brushMesh` converts the persisted world-height floor into each mesh's local frame and falls back to a within-mesh search for legacy dabs, with an 80 m depth guard (`MAX_COLUMN_DEPTH`) so it won't snap across a cliff or down a hole. Snap now operates on the brush's vertical **cylinder** footprint (so even a small brush flattens a full column to the ground) rather than a sphere.
- **Hold Option/Alt while painting to RESTORE geometry to its original height.** New in `246be914`: `CNodeTreeManualBrush` tracks `_altKey` from the pointer events and `effectiveAction()` returns `"restore"` when Option/Alt is held, overriding the *Edit Action* dropdown. Restore puts the brushed (sphere-footprint) vertices back to their pristine backed-up positions — e.g. to recover a building a snap accidentally flattened — and is persisted as a `"restore"` dab. It requires a matching pristine backup with the original vertex layout (snap preserves it; a delete that rebuilt the geometry does not, so restore bails there). The *Manual Edit* tooltip in `TilesTreeFlatten.js` (`TREE_FLATTEN_DEFS`) is updated to document this. Reached via **Terrain → Edit Geometry (Trees) → Manual Edit**.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed Delete leaving behind sparse "wall"/spike sliver triangles.** In `246be914`, the brush delete previously removed a triangle only when ≥2 of its vertices were inside the brush sphere, which left the thin pillar/spike-wall triangles that have just one vertex in the brush. Delete now removes any triangle with **≥1 vertex** in the brush sphere. (Known remaining: very small or boundary snaps can still leave wall slivers; triangle-closure cleanup is the next step.)
- **Fixed the hover wireframe preview showing a mismatched double "ghost" across views.** Each view holds the 3D tiles at a different LOD, so previewing in every view overlaid two ghosts at different resolutions. The hover preview (`previewBrush`) is now restricted to the **hovered view's renderer only**. Preview and edit also now use the correct footprint per action — a vertical **cylinder** for snap and a **sphere** for delete/restore — matching what the commit actually applies.

## Version 2.89.1 (2026-06-26)

### Improvements
- **Env-var custom map sources gain an optional `_MAPPING` (EPSG number) tile-projection selector.** Follow-up to the `_ZOFFSET` / `_MIN_ZOOM` env-var custom-source work. In `src/nodes/CNodeTerrainUI.js`, the custom-map scan loop (`SITREC_CUSTOM_MAP_<NAME>_URL`) now reads `SITREC_CUSTOM_MAP_<NAME>_MAPPING` via `parseInt(Globals.env[prefix + 'MAPPING'])` and, when present, sets it as the source def's `mapping` — mirroring a `config.js` source's `mapping`. Default remains web-mercator (`3857`); set `4326` for an equirectangular / CRS84 tile source (e.g. a WMTS GoogleCRS84Quad layer such as the NRL BlueMarble layers). The map and elevation sources in simultaneous use must share the same mapping. Operator/config-facing only — there is no GUI control or i18n key; the value flows through the existing `SITREC_CUSTOM_*` env-var pipeline. Documented in `config/shared.env.example` (added to the `_URL, _NAME, _MAX_ZOOM, _MIN_ZOOM, _MAPPING, _ATTRIBUTION, _TERMS_URL, _ZOFFSET` list, with a worked commented NRL WMTS example using `_MAPPING=4326` and `{z}/{y}/{x}` order).
- **Developer/debugging aid: `window.__sitrecForceRender(true|false)` keeps the render loop running in a backgrounded tab.** Not user-facing — there is no menu or UI. A hidden tab's render loop normally force-sleeps (so terrain LOD subdivision and tile loading stall), which makes it impossible to inspect rendering via the SitrecBridge in a backgrounded tab. `src/renderLoopControl.ts` `shouldSleepAnimationLoop` gains a `forceRender` arg that returns `false` (never sleep) ahead of the hidden/paused checks; `src/index.js` adds a module-level `forceRenderLoop` flag wired into `shouldSleepAnimationLoop`, the `globalThis.__sitrecForceRender` toggle (which also `setRenderOne(true)` + `wakeAnimationLoop()`), and a `window.__sitrecForceRenderLoop` mirror for querying — and `wakeAnimationLoop` now bypasses its `document.hidden` and `paused && !focused` gates when the flag is set. A hidden tab still throttles the `setTimeout` scheduler (~1 fps in Chrome), but that is enough to drive subdivision/tile loading over a few seconds. Call `__sitrecForceRender(false)` to restore normal render-on-demand. Covered by a new case in `tests/renderLoopControl.test.js` (hidden + paused + unfocused + `forceRender:true` → does not sleep).

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed equirectangular (EPSG:4326 / GoogleCRS84Quad) map sources rendering as blank/black terrain that never subdivided.** Root cause in `src/WMSUtils.js`: `CTileMappingGoogleCRS84Quad.getNorthLatitude()` and `lat2Tile()` computed the vertical tile-row count as `Math.pow(2, z-1)`, which is `0.5` at zoom 0. That made the dynamic root tile's south-edge latitude compute as `90 - (1/0.5)*180 = -270°`, which `LLAToECEF` wraps back to the north pole, collapsing the whole root tile to a zero-radius point. The globe-horizon cull then marked it (and every descendant) invisible, so a dynamic 4326 map rendered as empty terrain and never subdivided. The fix clamps the row count to `Math.max(1, Math.pow(2, z-1))` in both methods, so z0 (and z1) is a single full-height row spanning `[90°, -90°]` — exactly the row count the tile-URL `maxY` assert and `subdivideTile`'s z0→z1 special case already assume. Fixes every 4326 map source (e.g. the NRL WMTS BlueMarble layers), not just one. Such sources are selected via **Terrain → Map Type**.
- **Fixed the `Layer` dropdown leaving coarse, low-detail terrain on a static camera.** In `src/nodes/CNodeTerrainUI.js`, the **Layer** dropdown's `onChange` (`updateLayersMenu`) called `unloadMap` / `loadMap` — rebuilding the texture map down to its coarse root — but did not re-arm the subdivision "grace" window. With the camera still, the already-decayed static-camera grace gate then blocked any further subdivision, so the new layer stayed coarse until the user moved the camera or clicked **Refresh**. Added `requestSubdivisionPass()` to the handler, mirroring the **Map Type** handler, so the new layer's tiles refine immediately.
- **Fixed the `Dynamic Subdivision` toggle having the same omission.** Toggling **Dynamic Subdivision** (Terrain) on calls `reloadMap`, rebuilding the map to its coarse root, but likewise did not re-arm the subdivision grace — so on a static camera it stayed coarse. Added the same `requestSubdivisionPass()` to the toggle's `onChange`; it is harmless when toggling the option off, because the subdivision driver ignores the grace window when not dynamic.

---

## Version 2.89.0 (2026-06-26)

### New Features
- **"Edit Geometry (Trees)" — remove trees from Google Photorealistic 3D Tiles, automatically and with a manual brush.** This release ships the entire terrain tree-editing feature for the first time (both `src/TilesTreeFlatten.js` and `src/TreeManualBrush.js` are brand new; `CNodeBuildings3DTiles` and `CNodeTerrainUI` are extended). It only applies to Google Photorealistic 3D Tiles, and its GUI is a new folder under the **Terrain** menu (`guiMenus.terrain`), `buildTreeRemovalGUI()` building it data-driven from the `TREE_FLATTEN_DEFS` table so the controls and algorithm never drift.
  - **Automatic pass.** A *Flatten Trees* toggle plus its tuning live in an **Automatic Tree Removal** sub-menu (with *Ground Plane*, *Tree Detection*, *Building Detection*, *Clustering* sub-sub-folders and a *Re-run* button → `applyTreeFlattenParams()`). `TreeFlattener` analyses each loaded tile: RANSAC-fit ground plane, ExGR/ExG green-vegetation tests, and a per-vertex normal-coherence + max-height building test, then either flattens tree foliage down to the ground plane or deletes its triangles while protecting coherent man-made surfaces (roofs/walls). Per-tile `Cull Distance (m)` and `Min Tile Verts` gates limit which tiles are processed.
  - **Manual Edit brush.** A *Manual Edit* toggle (`setManualEditEnabled()`) suspends the automatic pass and turns on a paint brush: left-click-dragging over the tiles applies the current *Edit Action* (`Snap to ground` / `Delete triangles`) to the geometry within *Brush Radius (m)* (default 8, range 1–20; default action *Snap to ground*) via `TreeFlattener.applyBrush()` / `_brushMesh()`, reusing the same snap/delete + backup/restore machinery as the automatic pass. `src/TreeManualBrush.js` owns capture-phase pointer handlers that pre-empt the camera drag when a stroke starts on a tile hit, screen→ray picking, and per-view application. Picking is robust across all views including the look view's video-matched pixel zoom: NDC comes from the canvas client rect, view selection uses `_effectivelyVisible` (full-screen safe), and the ray is built from the DISPLAYED projection via `prepareCameraForLOD()` / `restoreCameraAfterLOD()` (the live/render camera is ~2× too wide).
  - **Live wireframe "ghost" hover preview.** While *Manual Edit* is on and you hover without painting, the triangles the brush would affect are temporarily hidden from the solid tiles and redrawn as wireframe line segments, so you see exactly what would be removed before clicking. `TreeFlattener.previewBrush()` / `_restorePreview()` collapse the covered triangles to degenerate and log the originals for restore (handling both indexed and the actual Google-tile NON-indexed geometry); `TreeManualBrush` owns one growable `LineSegments` ghost and uses a mouse+camera fingerprint to skip idle per-frame rebuilds so the render loop can settle. Fully non-destructive — restored on move-away, window blur, or *Manual Edit* off; nothing commits until a click.
  - **Persistent edits across tile streaming and save/load.** Each stroke is recorded as a deduped sphere "dab" `{lla:[lat,lon,alt], r, a}` — the resolved hit in lat/lon/alt (frame-independent, ellipsoid-robust), radius, and action — with a new dab skipped if within `0.3 × radius` of the previous, so a drag stores a sparse trail, not hundreds of spheres. `TreeFlattener.reapplyDabs()` is a per-frame, budgeted pass that brings each loaded mesh up to date, stamped with a `DAB_COUNT` so the append-only list only re-applies its tail (with a bounding-sphere reject). `CNodeBuildings3DTiles.applyManualBrush()` records the dab (world→LLA) and `rebuildDabsWorld()` caches the world-space form (LLA→ECEF), rebuilt on load and on ellipsoid change. Edits serialize through the existing `treeFlattenParams` path and `modDeserialize` rebuilds the world cache (round-trip verified). An *Apply Edits* master toggle (default on, `setApplyEdits()`) re-applies the saved edits to tiles as they load — turn it off to temporarily reveal the original geometry without discarding edits; *Restore Geometry* (`clearAllEdits()`) resets everything, dropping the saved dab list and restoring all tiles. A click also applies synchronously to currently-loaded tiles for immediate feedback (idempotent via the `DAB_COUNT` stamps, so the per-frame reapply pass doesn't double-apply).

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed shell scripts (`*.sh`) breaking a Windows-to-Linux build-from-source workflow by silently converting to CRLF on checkout.** `.gitattributes` previously had only `* text=auto`, which honours `core.autocrlf` on checkout; on Windows (git's default `core.autocrlf=true`) a `git pull` rewrote `*.sh` to CRLF, and a CRLF shebang then failed on Linux with `bad interpreter: No such file or directory` — `install.sh` had to be `dos2unix`'d before it would run on a Linux server. `.gitattributes` now pins `eol=lf` for `*.sh`, `*.bash`, `Dockerfile*`, and `*.env` / `shared.env*` (and `eol=crlf` for Windows-only `*.cmd`); `eol=lf` overrides `core.autocrlf`, so these files stay LF regardless of the user's git config. Build/deploy-from-source only — no in-app behaviour change. Distinct from the 2.88.3 fix, which strips CRLF from the env DATA the bake scripts read; this fixes CRLF in the script FILES themselves. Verified by simulating a Windows checkout (`core.autocrlf=true`): before = CRLF (`install.sh` 617 CR lines), after = clean LF (0 CR).

## Version 2.88.6 (2026-06-25)

### Improvements
- **Env-var custom map/elevation sources gain an optional `_MIN_ZOOM` floor.** Follow-up to the 2.88.5 `_ZOFFSET` feature. In `src/nodes/CNodeTerrainUI.js`, both the custom-map scan loop (`SITREC_CUSTOM_MAP_<NAME>_URL`) and the custom-elevation scan loop (`SITREC_CUSTOM_ELEVATION_<NAME>_URL`) now read a per-source `SITREC_CUSTOM_MAP_<NAME>_MIN_ZOOM` / `SITREC_CUSTOM_ELEVATION_<NAME>_MIN_ZOOM` via `parseInt(Globals.env[prefix + 'MIN_ZOOM']) || 0` (missing/non-numeric defaults to `0`) and set it as the source's `minZoom`. `minZoom` is the lowest zoom Sitrec will request from the source; below it the terrain renders a placeholder tile (custom map) or a zero-elevation placeholder (custom elevation) rather than issuing a request. The custom-elevation source previously hardcoded `minZoom: 0`; that literal is replaced by the configurable value. Operator/config-facing only — there is no GUI control or i18n key; the value flows through the existing `SITREC_CUSTOM_*` env-var pipeline and the unchanged Docker env path. Documented in `config/shared.env.example` (added to the `_URL, _NAME, _MAX_ZOOM, _MIN_ZOOM, _ATTRIBUTION, _TERMS_URL` list for both source types, with worked `SITREC_CUSTOM_MAP_ESRI_MIN_ZOOM` / `SITREC_CUSTOM_ELEVATION_AWS_MIN_ZOOM` examples) and in `docs/dev/CustomTerrainSources.md`.
- **Cheaper, faster repeat turns from the in-app AI assistant via prompt caching.** Commit `4e46313e` adds two Anthropic `cache_control: ephemeral` breakpoints, mirrored across both the server proxy (`sitrecServer/chatbot.php` `callAnthropic`) and the browser bring-your-own-key path (`src/CDirectLLMClient.js` `callAnthropic`, with a new `withCacheBreakpoint` helper): one on the system block — since tools render before system, a single marker caches the large, byte-stable tools+system prefix across turns — and one on the final message, so each of the up-to-5 tool-loop iterations reads the growing conversation prefix at ~0.1x instead of full input price. The BYOK client now wraps `system` in block form and builds a shallow message copy with only the last message cache-marked (it must not mutate the reused `messages` array, or stale breakpoints would accumulate and exceed the 4-breakpoint cap). `chatbot.php` surfaces `cacheReadTokens` / `cacheWriteTokens` / `inputTokens` / `outputTokens` in its debug output for verification (full prompt size = `inputTokens + cacheWriteTokens + cacheReadTokens`). The two paths carry cross-reference "parity" banners so future edits stay in sync; covered by `tests/CDirectLLMClient.test.js` and `tests/CSitrecAPI.test.js`. Full-server and BYOK only (no AI chat in serverless). Verified live: a ~43K-token tools+system prefix caches and repeat requests read ~99.8% from cache (~10x cheaper input on Opus 4.8).
- **The AI assistant now fetches the real current date/time on demand instead of carrying it in the prompt.** Part of commit `4e46313e`, and the enabler for the caching above. The volatile wall-clock timestamp was removed from the system prompt (`chatbot.php`'s `{$date}` line and `src/CDirectLLMClient.js`'s `BASE_SYSTEM_PROMPT` `{{dateTime}}` substitution; `buildSystemPrompt` no longer takes `dateTime`) because it changed the cached prefix every request and defeated cross-turn caching. The model now calls a new `getCurrentDateTime` tool only when a request actually depends on the present moment ("right now", "tonight", "in an hour") or needs the user's local timezone. It is implemented in both paths: server-side in `simulateToolCall` (returns the client-reported `$date`, i.e. `$data['dateTime']`, as an ISO 8601 string with timezone, distinct from the simulation time) and client-side as `CSitrecAPI.getCurrentDateTime` (returns `GlobalDateTimeNode.timeWithTimeZone(now)` plus `GlobalDateTimeNode.getTimeZoneOffset()`), and is added to the chatbot tool allow-list. The simulation date/time stays in the prompt (it changes infrequently and is core context). User-visible effect: the assistant still answers correctly about the real "now" and the user's timezone; it just retrieves them via a tool call rather than a static prompt value.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed a negative `_ZOFFSET` on an env-var custom map/elevation source requesting an invalid negative `{z}` tile at low zoom.** The 2.88.5 `_ZOFFSET` feature substituted `z + zOffset` straight into the `{z}` placeholder, so at a low tile zoom a negative offset (e.g. `_ZOFFSET=-2` at `z=1`) produced a request like `-1/0/0`. In `src/nodes/CNodeTerrainUI.js`, both source loops now (a) auto-raise the effective floor — `if (zOffset < 0) minZoom = Math.max(minZoom, -zOffset)` — so the terrain turns any `z < minZoom` tile into a placeholder (no request) before an out-of-range zoom can be asked for, and (b) clamp the substitution as a final guard: `template.replace('{z}', Math.max(0, z + zOffset))`. Any `_ZOFFSET` is now safe regardless of `_MIN_ZOOM`; an operator no longer needs to hand-set `_MIN_ZOOM` just to make a negative offset valid. Documented alongside the `_MIN_ZOOM` notes in `config/shared.env.example` and `docs/dev/CustomTerrainSources.md`. Verified live across map offsets -2/0/+1 and elevation -3 (computed `minZoom` 2/3/0/3; no negative `{z}` emitted).

## Version 2.88.5 (2026-06-25)

### Improvements
- **Env-var custom map/elevation sources gain an optional `_ZOFFSET` zoom-level shift.** The env-var custom-source feature builds a tile-URL function from a template containing `{z}`/`{x}`/`{y}` placeholders, substituting the tile coordinates verbatim — which fails for a server whose tiling is offset by a fixed number of zoom levels from Sitrec's standard slippy-map zoom. In `src/nodes/CNodeTerrainUI.js`, both the custom-map scan loop (`SITREC_CUSTOM_MAP_<NAME>_URL`) and the custom-elevation scan loop (`SITREC_CUSTOM_ELEVATION_<NAME>_URL`) now read a per-source `SITREC_CUSTOM_MAP_<NAME>_ZOFFSET` / `SITREC_CUSTOM_ELEVATION_<NAME>_ZOFFSET` via `parseInt(Globals.env[prefix + 'ZOFFSET']) || 0` (so a missing or non-numeric value defaults to `0`) and ADD it to the zoom in the `mapURL` closure: `template.replace('{z}', z + zOffset)`. `0` is a no-op; e.g. `_ZOFFSET=-1` requests one zoom level coarser. Only `{z}` is affected — `{x}`/`{y}` are unchanged. This is operator/config-facing only: there is no GUI control or i18n key — the value is supplied through the existing `SITREC_CUSTOM_*` env-var pipeline and forwards to the browser through the unchanged Docker env path (no `docker/entrypoint.sh` change needed). Documented in `config/shared.env.example` (added to the `_URL, _NAME, _MAX_ZOOM, _ATTRIBUTION, _TERMS_URL` list for both source types, with worked `SITREC_CUSTOM_MAP_ESRI_ZOFFSET` / `SITREC_CUSTOM_ELEVATION_AWS_ZOFFSET` examples) and in `docs/dev/CustomTerrainSources.md` (noting the declarative `_ZOFFSET` mirrors the `z - 1`-style shift a `config.js` `mapURL` function can do by hand). Verified end-to-end: applies on the local build and forwards cleanly into the 2.88.4 container.

## Version 2.88.4 (2026-06-25)

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed the Docker image shipping with no PHP `vendor/`, so every endpoint that `require`s `vendor/autoload.php` fataled inside the container.** `sitrecServer/vendor/` is gitignored and the Docker images never ran `composer install`, so the AWS SDK (`Aws\S3\S3Client`) and Guzzle (`GuzzleHttp\Client`) were entirely absent from the published/local image. As a result every server PHP endpoint that does `require 'vendor/autoload.php'` — S3 uploads plus `settings.php`, `object.php`, `rehost.php`, `metadata.php`, `getsitches.php`, and `admin_*` — fataled in the container, which broke the S3-backed storage the Docker install actively recommends for persistence. Non-Docker server deploys were unaffected, because their rsync deploy step runs `composer install` on the server. The fix adds a dedicated `FROM composer:2 AS phpdeps` build stage to **both** `Dockerfile` (local builds) and `Dockerfile.release` (CI builds): it copies in `sitrecServer/composer.json` + `composer.lock` and runs `composer install --no-dev --prefer-dist --optimize-autoloader --ignore-platform-reqs` from the committed lock (reproducible; `--ignore-platform-reqs` installs exactly what the lock pins on the alpine composer image, and the only lock extensions it lacks — `awscrt`/`pcntl`/`sockets`/`intl` — are optional and unused here). Each Dockerfile then `COPY --from=phpdeps /sitrecServer/vendor /var/www/html/sitrecServer/vendor`, placing the built `vendor/` alongside the PHP files (the build's `dist/` carries `composer.json`/`composer.lock` but no `vendor/`). The runtime `php:8.4-apache` image independently provides the PHP extensions S3 actually uses (curl/json/simplexml/mbstring/openssl). This is an operator/deployment-only fix with no end-user-visible app behavior change; it affects only the Docker image, which is rebuilt on the next version-tag push. Verified on a full representative build that PHP endpoints and `vendor/` coexist and that `Aws\S3\S3Client` + `GuzzleHttp\Client` autoload correctly inside the image.

---

## Version 2.88.3 (2026-06-24)

### Improvements
- **"Local" elevation source now self-prunes when its tiles are missing, instead of tripping the cross-service offline-fallback error.** "Local" elevation tiles are served from Sitrec's own origin (`SITREC_TERRAIN`), so a load failure means the data simply isn't present (e.g. no `sitrec-terrain` volume mounted), not a transient network outage — yet a failed elevation fetch previously went through `ServiceAvailability.recordFailureByUrl`, treating it like a down third-party tile service and surfacing the user-facing "service unavailable" offline-fallback path. In `src/QuadTreeTile.js`, `loadElevation`'s catch block now checks whether the failing source is the selected "Local" elevation and, if so, routes to the terrain UI's new `handleLocalElevationMissing()` rather than `ServiceAvailability`. `src/nodes/CNodeTerrainUI.js` gains `handleLocalElevationMissing()` (deletes the `"Local"` entry from `elevationSources`, logs a single `console.warn` — not a modal — and falls back to `"Flat"` if Local was selected; the now-missing source is itself the guard, so repeat calls from other in-flight tiles are no-ops), `rebuildElevationMenu()` (rebuilds the lil-gui dropdown via `.options()` and re-applies listen/name/tooltip/onChange after lil-gui destroys the controller), and an extracted `onElevationTypeChanged()` shared by the dropdown's `onChange` and the programmatic fallback (unloads the old elevation map via `reloadMap`, updates attribution, and forces a `requestSubdivisionPass()` so new tiles load even when paused). `src/nodes/CNodeTerrain.js` `elevationURLDirect` adds a defensive `if (!sourceDef) return null;` so a pruned source mid-flight yields "no elevation" rather than throwing.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed Windows (CRLF) env files corrupting baked/configured Docker values.** A Windows-saved `shared.env` baked into an image via `install.sh` / `sitrec.sh --bake` left a trailing `\r` *after* the closing quote on each value. The bake parsers' quote-strip is a glob anchored to end-of-string, so the `\r` defeated it — literal quotes plus the CR were baked into the `ENV` values. Symptoms for operators: custom map source names showed a trailing `"`, custom map/elevation tile URLs were invalid, and `DOCKER_MAP_TYPE` / `SITREC_ENABLE_DEFAULT_MAP_SOURCES` / `SITREC_ENABLE_DEFAULT_ELEVATION_SOURCES` were silently ignored. The fix strips a trailing CR before the quote-strip at every layer:
  - `install.sh` / `sitrec.sh`: strip a trailing CR at the top of the bake parser (portable `CR=$(printf '\r')` — `$'\r'` silently no-ops under dash/busybox-ash), with a friendly "CRLF detected" notice and a `SITREC_BAKE_DRY_RUN` test hook.
  - `docker/entrypoint.sh`: strip CR before the quote-strip in *both* value loops (this defends the docker-compose `env_file:` / `docker run --env-file` paths, which bypass the bake parser and feed raw env straight into the container). Also: the `window.__SITREC_ENV__` `<head>` injection was switched from `sed` to pure shell parameter expansion, because a custom map URL containing `&` (e.g. `...?token=a&style=b`) was being corrupted — `&` is the whole-match on `sed`'s replacement side, so it spliced `<head>` into the URL. And `SITREC_DOCKER_INTERNAL_PORT` now tolerates a trailing CR. Paths (`SITREC_HTML_FILE` / `SITREC_ENV_PHP_FILE`) were made overridable so the entrypoint can be exercised end-to-end by tests.
  - `src/envUtils.js`: `getEnv()` now strips a trailing CR/newline at one chokepoint that feeds `getEnvBool` and exact map-type key lookups — including serverless build-time values from dotenv-webpack — so `getEnvBool("true\r")` and `sources["NoClouds\r"]` no longer miss and fall back to defaults.
  - `src/configUtils.js`: `setupConfigPaths()` CR-normalizes every string value in the `Globals.env` config returned by `config_paths.php` before anything reads it (the docker-compose `env_file:` path again).
  - New tests: `tests/dockerEnvCRLF.test.js` runs the real `install.sh` / `sitrec.sh` / `entrypoint.sh` parsers against a CRLF fixture, and `tests/envUtils.test.js` unit-tests `getEnv` / `getEnvBool`. Verified end-to-end by baking an image from a mixed-CRLF `shared.env` onto 2.88.2 via the client method (banner, custom map source, and disabled default sources all crossed clean).

---

## Version 2.88.2 (2026-06-24)

### Bug Fixes
- Fixed a spurious duplicate entry in the terrain/elevation map-source dropdowns when a custom source defines a terms-of-use link. In `CNodeTerrainUI.js`, the custom-source scanner matched env keys against `^SITREC_CUSTOM_MAP_(.+)_URL$` (and the `SITREC_CUSTOM_ELEVATION_` equivalent). Because a source's optional terms-of-use property is named `SITREC_CUSTOM_MAP_<NAME>_TERMS_URL` — which also ends in `_URL` — the greedy `(.+)` captured `<NAME>_TERMS`, so the terms link was misread as a *second*, distinct custom source (e.g. an extra "Custom Map (ESRI_TERMS)" entry). Both the map loop and the elevation loop now guard with `&& !key.endsWith('_TERMS_URL')`, so `_TERMS_URL` is treated only as a per-source property and the menu shows just the single intended source. Pre-existing since the custom-source parser was written; only surfaces for sources that define a `_TERMS_URL`. Verified end-to-end on a real build.
