Fix: the load callback now captures the `model` it was started for and bails if it is no longer current — `if (model !== this.currentModel) { Globals.pendingActions--; return; }` — discarding the stale result (with a `console.warn`) while keeping the `Globals.pendingActions` counter balanced. This is the model-load analogue of the video selection race.
  - Same change also adds an assert-safety guard in the old-model cleanup branch: `FileManager.exists(this.currentModel.file) &&` now precedes `FileManager.isUnhosted(this.currentModel.file)`, so rapidly switching away from a still-loading, not-yet-registered model no longer asserts on a missing file inside `isUnhosted`.
- **Fixed a multi-video/multi-image restore intermittently selecting (and mis-pairing) the wrong source after an out-of-order async load.** `src/nodes/CNodeVideoView.js`: restoring a saved sitch with several videos/images drove a sequential restore through `continueVideoRestore`, which paired each async load *completion* with a *positional* slot (`videos[loadedCount]` plus a running `skippedCount`). Because images load via mixed paths — a fast in-memory `Image` path vs. a `newVideo` URL fallback — and can share S3 URLs, completions arrived out of order or with an extra, so a completion-order slot could (a) pair one video's saved label/`staticURL` with a *different* video's decoded `videoData` (a slot swap), and (b) let a late straggler push a duplicate `videos[]` entry that then hijacked the selected index. User-visible symptom: a sitch saved with multiple images/videos could load with the wrong image/video selected — non-deterministic, roughly 1 in 3 loads — e.g. the "Atremis Starlink" sitch intermittently showed the wrong Earth image. Fix makes the restore **identity-based** instead of completion-order-based:
  - `_preCreateRestoreSlots()` pre-creates one `videos[]` output slot per saved entry up front (in saved order, each with `videoData: null`), so every saved entry owns a fixed slot before any load completes, and the saved `currentVideoIndex` (`pr.targetIndex`) is applied immediately. Called from the first `loadVideoFromEntry` of a restore (guarded by `pr._slotsCreated`).
  - Every restore-originated load now carries the source index of the slot it fills (`restoreIndex`), threaded through `newVideo` / `loadVideoFromEntry` / `makeImageVideo` and stamped onto the created `videoData` as `_restoreIndex` (also copied onto the `CVideoPatchedData` wrapper in `wrapWithPatchedIfNeeded` so a wrapped completion still routes correctly).
  - `continueVideoRestore` now reads `loadedVideoData._restoreIndex` and writes the result *only* to its own slot (and only if that slot is still empty), dropping stale/duplicate completions. It advances/starts the next load only when the slot it is currently waiting for (`pr.loadingIndex`) is resolved, so a straggler for a different slot can't advance the chain or kick off a duplicate load.
  - The add-on-load fast paths (`newVideo`, `makeImageVideo`) only call `addVideoEntry` when `restoreIndex === undefined && !this.pendingVideoRestore`, so a restore-originated straggler can never push a duplicate entry — even if it completes after restore has finished and `pendingVideoRestore` is gone.
  - `skipCurrentVideoRestore` marks the in-flight slot `_restoreFailed` (instead of bumping a `skippedCount`) and advances the same index-based way; `_finalizeVideoRestore` compacts out failed slots, shifts the saved target left by any removed-before-target slot, and re-applies the selection authoritatively (`currentVideoIndex = -1` then `selectVideo(finalTarget)`) so the saved selection — never a late add — wins. Auto-tracking re-apply (`deserializeAutoTracking`) moves into `_finalizeVideoRestore` unchanged. This is the video-selection analogue of the 3D-model load race fixed above.

## Version 2.74.2 (2026-06-04)

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed sustained ~600% CPU on a loaded, paused sitch (broken render-on-demand).** Render-on-demand could never go back to sleep, so heavy scenes (Google photorealistic 3D tiles + terrain + night sky) full-rendered every tick forever even while paused. The core bug was in `setRenderOne()` (`src/Globals.js`): its truthy-coalescing guard `if (!par.renderOne)` (which exists to avoid redundant wake-ups) silently neutralised `setRenderOne(false)`, so the clear never applied and `par.renderOne` stayed `true` permanently — `renderMain` never took its paused early-out. Fixes span the whole render path:
  - `src/Globals.js`: `setRenderOne(false)` now writes `par.renderOne = false` and returns immediately (a clear always applies and never wakes the loop); the truthy path keeps its coalescing guard.
  - `src/indexRender.js`: `renderMain` clears the flag with a direct `par.renderOne = false` (unambiguous), and the `Globals.fixedFrame` (URL-pinned-frame) block only re-arms `par.frame`/`setRenderOne` when `par.frame !== Globals.fixedFrame`; previously it re-set `par.frame` every tick, which re-armed `renderOne` via the `par.frame` setter and defeated the paused early-out.
  - `src/threeExt.js`: `DebugArrow` only re-applies a layer mask when it actually changed (`DebugArrows[name].layers.mask !== layerMask`), and `propagateLayerMaskObject` / `setLayerMaskRecursive` only call `setRenderOne(true)` when a child mask actually changed. These run from per-frame redraw paths (camera frustum rebuild, flow indicator), so the old unconditional `setRenderOne(true)` re-armed the loop every frame.
  - `src/nodes/CNodeCompassUI.js`: `lastElevation` is now updated unconditionally in `renderCanvas`. Previously it was only stored when elevation was displayed, so the change-guard kept failing every frame (stuck at its init value), redrawing the compass and calling `removeText()`→`setRenderOne(true)` every frame.
  - `src/nodes/CNodeBackgroundFlowIndicator.js`: the two expensive near-horizontal terrain raycasts are now cached and skipped when frame + both camera positions + both LOS directions are unchanged (the arrows are persistent named debug objects, so they stay drawn). The cache is invalidated in `remove()` so a later `show()` recomputes.
  - `src/nodes/CNodeFloodSim.js`: `setRenderOne(true)` now fires only when a flood physics step actually runs (the throttled ~20/s path), not unconditionally every 16 ms.
  - `src/nodes/CNodeContrail.js`: `update()` only requests a render when `frame !== this._lastContrailFrame` (the ribbon rebuild is frame-deterministic; GUI edits already request a render via their onChange handlers).
  - `src/nodes/CNodeBuildings3DTiles.js`: `PerViewTiles.update` skips `TilesRenderer.update()` (which re-traverses the whole tileset and churned ~119 KB/frame across two views on a Google-photorealistic scene) when the camera is static (a per-view `_lastCamFingerprint` over the world matrix + fov/zoom/aspect) and the tileset is fully settled (`_isUpdatePending()` checks queued/downloading/parsing/fading), with a ~1s grace window after the camera stops. A new `tile-visibility-change` listener calls `setRenderOne(true)` so late-arriving / LOD-swapped / fading tiles get drawn, and the node self-disables `updateWhilePaused` once settled so the render loop can actually sleep.
  - `src/nodes/CNodeTerrainUI.js`: the subdivision keep-alive grace is now gated on `pendingTileLoads.size` (real in-flight network/decode) instead of `_dirtyParents.size`, which created a feedback loop (a coverage pass flips a tile's active state as a side effect, refreshing the grace, running another pass forever — churning at ~600% CPU on a static camera). The node also self-disables `updateWhilePaused` once the terrain is fully settled (no grace, nothing loading via `pendingTileLoads` or per-tile `isLoadingElevation`, no pending recalc/refresh), so the loop sleeps; a camera move or async tile-load completion wakes it.
  - `src/nodes/CNodeTerrain.js`: the debounced `elevationChanged` dispatch now calls `setRenderOne(true)` after late elevation tiles mutate the mesh, so freshly-arrived elevation is drawn instead of showing stale flat/coarse terrain until the user nudges the camera. This same render-on-demand fix also corrects the camera frustum occasionally rendering at the wrong FOV in video sitches that match the video aspect (the frustum rebuild's per-frame layer-mask reapply was part of the self-sustaining loop, and the static-camera early-outs now fold camera fov/aspect into the settle fingerprint).
- **Fixed stuttering and ~1-second hangs when playing high-resolution (e.g. 4K) video.** `src/CVideoDecodeWorker.js`: the WebCodecs decode worker decoded a whole group of chunks (80–90) in one synchronous loop with no backpressure, flooding the pipeline with GBs of external memory in flight (each 4K `VideoFrame`/`ImageBitmap` is ~33 MB), which made V8 fire back-to-back Major GCs that reclaimed almost nothing — the worker spent its time collecting instead of decoding. Added decode backpressure: the decode loop awaits a drain whenever `decoder.decodeQueueSize + inFlightFrames` exceeds `MAX_IN_FLIGHT_FRAMES` (12, a ~400 MB ceiling at 4K), with `inFlightFrames` decremented in the bitmap-post `.finally()` (`frameDrained`). A `decodeGeneration` counter aborts and wakes a suspended decode loop on decoder error (`recoverDecoder`), `reset`, `configure`, and `dispose`, so a fatal/corrupt-stream case can't wedge the loop until the main-thread 45s group timeout.
- **Fixed a Three.js console warning on synthetic buildings.** `src/nodes/CNodeSynthBuilding.js` no longer passes `flatShading: true` to a `MeshBasicMaterial` (which is unlit and warns about the unsupported property); `flatShading` is now only applied for non-`basic` (lit) materials.

### Improvements
- **Quality performance preset no longer decodes full-resolution video by default.** `src/CustomSupport.js`: the Quality preset's `videoMaxSize` changed from `"None"` (full source) to `"1080P"`, so even the highest preset caps decode resolution at 1080P and no preset decodes full 4K by default (complementing the decode-worker backpressure fix above). The other presets are unchanged (Balanced 720P, Fast 480P, Potato 360P).

## Version 2.74.1 (2026-06-03)

### Bug Fixes
- **Deterministic auto-assigned track colours** (`src/TrackManager.js`): auto-palette track colours were indexed by `TrackManager.size()` at track *creation* time. Imported tracks are created in async load-completion order (each file calls `addTracks` as it finishes parsing), so the palette index per track varied run-to-run — the whole palette rotated and every track was recoloured. This caused run-to-run track-colour flicker for users loading the same multi-track sitch, and intermittent visual-regression failures on multi-track sitches (e.g. the Potomac collision sitch). Fix: the palette is extracted to a module const `TRACK_PALETTE` (yellow deliberately skipped — it's the traverse colour), and a new `reassignTrackColors()` runs at the end of `addTracks`, *before* `NodeMan.recalculateAllRootFirst()`, so the colour constants are stable before the line geometry rebuilds. It collects every track flagged `paletteColored` with a `shortName`, sorts them by `shortName` (order-independent → deterministic and distinct), and assigns `TRACK_PALETTE[rank % length]`, updating the per-track colour `CNodeConstant`s (`colorData_<short>` / `colorTrack_<short>`) and the display nodes' `dropColor` (track colour × 0.75 on `trackDisplayDataNode` / `trackDisplayNode`). Only auto-palette tracks are touched: sonde (radiosonde) tracks stay white and aren't marked `paletteColored`, and user/serialised colours are left alone. The provisional `size()`-indexed colour is still set at creation but is now always overridden by the deterministic pass (tracks also now carry `paletteColored` and `shortName`).

### Internal / Refactor
- **Fast-regression harness defaults to headless** (`tests_regression/fast-regression/run.mjs`, `README.md`, test-only): the harness now launches Chrome headless by default — `headless: !flag('headed')`. New-headless Chrome uses the *same* ANGLE Metal GPU backend as headed (verified `ANGLE Metal Renderer` in both) at the same speed and shares the same persistent profile/cache, but draws no on-screen window, so repeated local runs no longer steal keyboard focus while you work. A new `--headed` flag opts back into a visible window (e.g. to watch a single sitch); `--headless` is kept as an accepted no-op. NOTE: headed and headless ANGLE rasterize ~0.1–0.5% differently, so baselines are mode-specific — regenerate the (local-only) baselines if you switch modes. `README.md` flag table updated to match.

## Version 2.74.0 (2026-06-03)

### New Features
- **Tear-out menu sub-folders (floating / sidebar-docked sub-menus)** — the menu-bar drag system (`CGuiMenuBar` in `src/lil-gui-extras.js`) now lets any *folder* (sub-menu) inside a menu-bar menu be dragged out into its own floating window, in addition to the existing tear-out of whole top-level menus.
  - **Interaction:** `wireFolderTearout(folder)` (idempotent, guarded by `folder._tearoutWired`) wires each folder's `$title`. Dragging the title past a small movement threshold tears the folder out at the cursor into a floating container; a plain click below threshold still just expands/collapses the folder (the gesture temporarily locks `lockOpenClose` to disambiguate). Dropping on a screen edge or the center divider docks the folder into the left/right/center sidebar, reusing the same drop indicators as top-level menus. A sidebar-docked folder pops back into a cursor-following floating window on first drag (`removeMenuFromLeftSidebar`/`Right`/`Center`).
  - **Restore (= "close"):** double-click the title (`dblclick` on `$title`, plus an Android double-tap fallback mirroring the top-level menu handling), drag off the top of the screen, or drop it mostly off-screen (`isMenuOffScreen`). All paths call `restoreFolderToParent(folder)`.
  - **Design:** only the folder's `domElement` is relocated into `folder._detachedContainer`; the GUI object stays in its parent's `children`/`folders` arrays, so `gui.root`, `.listen()`, path-based serialization, nested tear-out and `destroy()` cascades keep working. A comment-node placeholder (`document.createComment("torn-out-submenu")`, stored as `folder._detachPlaceholder`) marks the exact DOM slot to restore into without defeating lil-gui's `.children:empty` styling. The menu bar tracks torn-out folders in a `detachedFolders` Set and a `folder.mode` of `"DOCKED"` / `"DRAGGING"` / `"DETACHED"`. `_isFolderDetached()` excludes torn-out folders from a parent menu's content accounting.
  - **Serialization:** each folder's saved state gains an optional `detached:{mode, left, top, zIndex, sidebarIndex?}`. `modDeserialize` re-tears-out folders (sidebar order merged with that of top-level menus via `getLeftSidebarMenuIndex`/etc.) and `_restoreAllDetachedFolders()` restores every torn-out folder first on reset/destroy/deserialize so no floating/sidebar containers leak across sitch loads. Round-trips with multiple floating and sidebar-docked sub-menus, including nested ones.
  - **Re-entrancy guard:** sidebar show/hide dispatches a synchronous window `resize`. Dragged folders use `mode === "DRAGGING"` and batch operations bump a depth counter so the off-screen auto-restore check (`_checkFloatingMenusOnResize`) only acts on settled `"DETACHED"` folders and can't re-enter on a half-placed folder. (Commit `9478956b`.)

### Internal / Refactor
- **LLA→ECEF conversion cleanup** (`src/LLA-ECEF-ENU.ts`, no user-facing change): removed vestigial machinery that derived the EUS origin and ENU rotation matrix from `Sit.lat`/`Sit.lon` — dead since the ECEF transition, where `LLAToECEFRadians` returns a raw ECEF position with no origin subtraction or rotation. The private `_updateSitConstants` is renamed `_updateEarthModelConstants` and now computes only the two ellipsoid constants (`_llaecef_e2`, `_llaecef_ratio`) that depend solely on the earth-model radii; its change-detection cache key drops from `(Sit.lat, Sit.lon, equatorRadius, polarRadius)` to `(equatorRadius, polarRadius)`. The dead pre-computed origin (`_originEcef*`) and rotation-matrix (`_m00`…`_m22`) globals and the `Sit` import are gone. The long-deprecated, always-ignored optional `radius` parameter (and its `assert`s) is removed from `LLAToECEFRadians` / `LLAToECEF` / `LLAVToECEF` — no caller anywhere passed it. The now-dead exported `updateLLAToECEFConstants()` (zero callers) is deleted. Verified numerically bit-for-bit identical ECEF output (2205-sample test across multiple earth models) and visually via the full fast-regression suite (0 scene diffs).
- **Fast-regression harness: current-worktree base URL** (`tests_regression/fast-regression/run.mjs`, test-only): the harness no longer defaults its base URL to a now-removed `/regress/` deployment. `currentWorktreeBase()` derives the base from the current git worktree's build folder via `config/config-install.js`'s `buildFolder` (`main` → `local.metabunk.org/sitrec/`, any other branch → `local.metabunk.org/<branch>/`), with a git-branch fallback when the gitignored config is absent (e.g. fresh clone). `--base=` / `SITREC_REGRESS_BASE` still override. `README.md` updated to match.
- **Fast-regression harness: per-sitch frame overrides** (`tests_regression/fast-regression/run.mjs`, test-only): adds a `FRAME_OVERRIDES` map and `frameFor(sitch)` helper so individual sitches can be captured at a sitch-specific frame instead of the global `CONFIG.frame` (10). Default entry `'Mosul Orb': 62` (the orb is only in a representative position by frame 62, as the old Playwright suite captured it); a `--frame-overrides="Name=62,Other=120"` CLI option merges in more. Wired into `buildLoadUrl` and the frame-mismatch note.
- **Trim legacy Playwright visual-regression registry** (`test-registry.js`, `tests_regression/regression.test.js`, test-only): removes the `testquick`, `ocean`, `gimbal`, `starlink`, `orion`, and `mosul` entries, now covered by the fast-regression harness (dropping `gimbal` also follows the project's no-gimbal-testing convention).

## Version 2.73.1 (2026-06-03)

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed Video Info Display labels drifting and growing when the video is zoomed or panned.** The optional on-video readouts (filename, frame number, timecode, timestamp, dates, etc., drawn by `CNodeVideoInfoUI`) had two problems under zoom/pan: (a) each label's font was scaled by the live, zoom-inflated destination height (`rect.h`), so text grew as you zoomed in; and (b) labels were pinned to the screen rather than to the video content, so they slid off the point they annotated when the video was panned. The fix treats each stored label position (`timecodeX/Y` etc.) as a fraction (0–100) of the *video frame* rather than the canvas, and routes reads/writes through the video view's existing source→dest transform via two new helpers, `pctToCanvas` / `canvasToPct` (which call `CNodeVideoView.videoToCanvasCoords` / `canvasToVideoCoords`, covering both the source-crop zoom and the pos-based mouse zoom). `drawInfoToContext` now takes a `videoView` argument and, when one is present (`live`), maps positions through a `mapPos` closure and sizes the font/padding off `baseHeight` — the unzoomed fit-to-view height computed from the source-vs-view aspect ratio — instead of `rect.h`, so labels keep a constant on-screen size at any zoom. The drag handlers (`dragOffsetX/Y` setup and the drag-move position write) were updated to use the same `pctToCanvas` / `canvasToPct` conversion. The stabilized-video exporter passes no `videoView`, so it keeps the prior native-resolution percent-of-frame layout (`widthPx`/`heightPx`) and is unchanged.

### Internal
- The fast-regression harness (`tests_regression/fast-regression/run.mjs`) now enumerates "Regression"-labelled sitches across multiple test-user accounts via a new `--user=1,99999999` option (default users 1 and 99999999), taking the union of labelled sitch names and resolving the owning user per sitch. Test-harness only; no user-facing effect.

## Version 2.73.0 (2026-06-03)

### New Features
- **Custom Graph** (Show → Graphs → *Add Custom Graph*): a new fully-configurable graph window. `CCustomGraphManager` adds the *Add Custom Graph* button and, per graph, a subfolder of controls (`graphControls` i18n keys: Title / Show / Dark / Toggle Legend / X / Y1 (left) / Y2 (right) / Y3 (right) / Remove) that serialize round-trip. `CNodeCustomGraphView` (extends `CNodeOSDGraphView`) renders Y1 on the left axis and Y2/Y3 on the right, with dark/light theme, legend toggle, on-canvas title, per-series fixed ranges, an empty-state message, drag-to-scrub of the frame line, and a Frame A→B range option. Data comes from a new runtime registry, `CGraphDataManager` (pull-only, version-polled), populated with Camera Motion, Point Track, Analyze Motion (raw + smoothed), Horizon, all OSD series, and per-track heading/speed. Wired into `CustomManager` setup/serialize with `dispose` teardown.
- **Sun-vs-LOS angle series** (Custom Graph data sources): for each `CNodeDisplayLOS` node, `CCustomGraphManager` registers three series under a new *Sun* group — `sunLOS.<id>.angle` = acos(F·S), the true angle between the look camera's per-frame line-of-sight forward F and the direction to the Sun S; `sunLOS.<id>.up` = signed elevation offset (atan2 in the LOS vertical plane); `sunLOS.<id>.left` = signed azimuth offset. LOS-display ids vary per sitch and may be plural, so they are enumerated like tracks/OSD. The up/left basis is roll-independent (built from world-up at the camera); the Sun direction is cached per frame and cleared on dispose.
- **Y-compress** (View menu): a slider (range 1–20, default 1) on the mainView that anamorphically squashes the vertical view frustum. `CNodeView3D` divides `projectionMatrix.elements[5]` (the vertical NDC scale, 1/tan(fovV/2)) by the factor so straight lines stay straight; it is applied both in the render pass (patched onto `updateProjectionMatrix`, composed over the video-pan patch and restored LIFO so it survives `matchVideoAspect` re-apply after `renderSky`) and in `prepareCameraForLOD()` so terrain tile culling sees the widened frustum and loads the newly-exposed tiles (without this the new vertical band showed black gaps). A top-left "Y-compress=N.Nx" DOM overlay (`updateYCompressIndicator()`) appears when active and is restored on sitch load (`addSimpleSerial("yCompress")` + `modDeserialize`).
- **SHF Starlink-flare predictor — real satellites by default** (`tools/shf/`): the standalone flare-prediction tool now downloads the current Starlink TLE in the background at startup and uses it whenever fresh (< 1 day old), falling back to a synthetic constellation only when offline/unavailable or when the requested date is outside the TLE's useful range (the synthetic set is anchored to that date so the seasonal geometry stays meaningful). When real data is in range but unavailable, the in-results synthetic note becomes a *CLICK HERE TO LOAD CURRENT SATELLITES* button. The live scanning view now uses the same 3-line header (Flares / Look / place) and compass/wide-view layout as the final screen so nothing jumps on completion, renders the wide view in replay mode with a dim streak backdrop, and fires a one-shot dot burst per newly-found flare (a "flurry"). "Flares visible" is renamed *Flares* and dead space between sections is tightened.

### Improvements
- **Camera-motion smoothing supports even windows** (Camera Motion): `smoothMotion` (`CameraMotionFromVideo.js`) now accepts even smoothing windows via integer offsets, which cancel a 2-frame source cadence (interlacing/pulldown) outright; the default window changes from 3 to 4. The Smoothing-window slider now allows even values and re-smooths the cached raw motion live (`setMotion` + `recalculateCascade`) with no video re-read.
- **Faster "Open in Sitrec" handoffs on hours-long sitches.** App handoffs build 88k–432k-frame sitches; dragging the camera loaded new elevation tiles and each tile re-cascaded the inherited LOS-analysis graph, baking O(frames) arrays. `CNodeTerrain` now coalesces per-tile `elevationChanged` into a single `scheduleElevationChanged` dispatch per frame (N tiles → 1 cascade). `fromApp` generalizes the existing lightweight gating (which hid unused target/traverse/measurement leaves, formerly `appFlight`-only) to the fixed-observer scene via a new persisted `Sit.appFromApp` marker, and hides the camera display track in fixed mode (a fixed observer's "track" is a single point). `CNodeDisplayTrack` caps sampled line points at 4000 so `recalculate()` stays bounded on very long sitches (normal-length tracks unaffected). Full recalc 276 ms → 146 ms at 88k frames.
- **Static-observer fast path: no per-frame baking for a constant camera.** A fixed-camera fromApp scene baked O(frames) arrays of identical points and rebuilt them on every elevation-tile load. `CNodePositionLLA` gains `isConstantOverFrames` (fixed `_LLA`, no wind → identical every frame; `getValueFrame` already serves `this.ecef` on demand) and skips baking the per-frame array in that case. `CNodeSmoothedPositionTrack` gains a matching constant-source fast path (gated to `copyData===false` with no `dataTrack`): it caches one `{position}` (re-derived each `recalculate` so C-drag/terrain updates flow through), serves it for all frames, skips the bake, and sets `lazyInterpolated=true` so chained tilt/bank-roll smoothers also passthrough. Provably equivalent (a constant source's baked array is N identical entries). Measured: camera cascade 65 ms → 0.8 ms at 88,020 frames.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed all Starlink flares being culled for sea-level / ground observers.** `rayIntersectsEllipsoid` (in `src/threeExt.js`, and its SHF mirror `rayHitsEllipsoid` in `tools/shf/geo.js`) returned `t1>0 || t2>0`, which is always true when the ray origin is on or inside the ellipsoid. A ground observer's altitude is geoid/terrain-relative, and at sea level the geoid sits below the WGS84 ellipsoid, so a camera "50 ft AGL" can land a few metres inside it (~9.5 m at MIA). With the origin inside, every look direction (even straight up) reported a horizon intersection, so every satellite read as below the horizon and all flares were silently culled. This was a regression since 8c123f53 (the sphere→ellipsoid horizon check); the old sphere test used the smaller polar radius, so the same camera stayed safely outside it. Fix: when the origin is on/inside the ellipsoid, project it out to just above the surface before the intersection test and use an epsilon on the roots; the satellite→sun shadow ray (origin always far outside) and below-horizon culling (incl. horizon dip) are preserved.
- **Fixed the on-screen data overlay staying hidden after "Show All".** In `CNodeOSDDataSeriesController.js`, the Show-All handler set each series' show flag but never re-evaluated the video-info overlay's visibility, so turning all series on left the overlay hidden if it had been hidden. It now calls `updateVideoInfoVisibility()`, matching the per-series Show toggle.

### Internal
- New `tests_regression/fast-regression/run.mjs` visual-regression harness driving the installed Chrome (GPU) over every sitch tagged "Regression" (enumerated dynamically), with agent-friendly `report.json` and `npm run test-fast[/-update/-list]`. As part of this, `CNodeDisplayWindField` now pins the streamline dash shader's `uTime` to `Globals.fixedFrame` in fixed-frame/regression mode (it previously used a free-running per-render counter, making locked-frame screenshots land at varying animation phases); live playback is unchanged. Concurrency, `--sitches=` selection, and settle behavior were subsequently tuned, and local baseline PNGs were moved out of git.
- The build-time version string now derives its time-zone label from the build machine via `Intl.DateTimeFormat` (`timeZoneName:'short'`) instead of hardcoding " PT" (`webpack.common.js`), so UTC CI runners no longer mislabel UTC digits as PT. End users see no behavioral change; the version-compare regex in `src/index.js` ignores the trailing zone text.

## Version 2.72.0 (2026-06-02)

### Improvements
- **FMV data analyzer overhaul: renamed *Analyze Video FMV Data*, now accepts single files and drag-and-drop, accumulates results across actions, and adds a per-frame CSV export and a combined Summary Report** (File → *File Analysis* → *Analyze Video FMV Data...*). The 2.71.0 *Analyze Video Folder* tool is substantially reworked in `src/analysis/VideoFolderAnalysisUI.js` and `src/analysis/AnalyzeVideoFile.js`:
  - **Rename / framing.** The menu action (`analyzeVideoFMV`, *Analyze Video FMV Data...*, tooltip *Open the FMV timing/MISB analyzer — drag in files or a folder, or choose them*) and the dialog title are now *Analyze Video FMV Data* (FMV = Full Motion Video). The modal fills the viewport (`width: 98vw; height: 98vh`).
  - **More input paths.** Alongside *Choose Folder* (`runFolderScan` via `showDirectoryPicker`), a new *Choose File* button (`runChooseFile`) picks one or more files via `showOpenFilePicker` (with an `<input type=file multiple>` fallback). The whole dialog is now a drop target (`wireDragAndDrop`): dropping video/KLV files **or** a folder analyzes them. A dashed *Drop video / KLV files or a folder to analyze* overlay (`dropHint`) shows on dragover; the `drop` handler calls `stopPropagation()`/`preventDefault()` so Sitrec's global drag-drop does **not** also load the dropped file as a sitch. Dropped folders are walked recursively via `webkitGetAsEntry()` (`collectFsEntry` / `entriesFromDataTransfer`), honoring the existing *Recursive* checkbox. All three paths funnel into one `analyzeEntries(state, entries)` core that uses a uniform `entry.getFile()` accessor (folder handles, file-picker handles, dropped `FileSystemEntry`s, and plain `File`s all normalize to it).
  - **Accumulate + clear.** Results now **append** across every folder/file/drop action instead of replacing the table; rows are keyed by an incrementing `entry.key` in `state.rowsByKey` (replacing the old path-keyed `rowsByPath`), so re-adding the same path is allowed. A new red *Clear Results* button (`clearResults`) empties the table and `state.results`. Control enable/disable is centralized in `refreshControls(state)`.
  - **Per-frame "Frames" CSV export.** A new *Frames* table column gives each file (with video PTS rows) an *Export* button that downloads `<path>-frames.csv`. This is a new format keyed on the **virtual (patched) frame timeline**, not the raw PES rows. In `AnalyzeVideoFile.js`, `buildVirtualFrames()` reconstructs the §12 `CVideoPatchedData` virtual timeline directly from the video PES PTS array (no decoder): it computes the nominal frame duration as the median positive PTS interval (`nominalFrameDurationUs`), steps a virtual frame index `V` at that cadence from the first PTS to the last, and maps each `V` to the latest source frame at/under its target PTS — so the synthesized gap-fill "held"/duplicate frames are materialized as their own rows. `buildFrameRows()` then emits one row per virtual frame with: `frame`, `isDuplicate` (1 when the row repeats the previous source frame, i.e. a gap-fill held frame), `sourceFrame`, timing (`virtualTimeS`, `virtualPtsUs`, `sourcePtsUs`), `pairingMode` (`"pts"` when the KLV records carry PES-header PTS — synchronous/PCR-locked — else `"uts"`, paired on `UnixTimeStamp` relative to the recording's first record — asynchronous fallback, the same dichotomy as `CNodeTrackFromMISB`), the paired `klvRecordIndex` + `klvPesPtsUs`/`klvUtsUs`/`klvDeltaUs`, and then a named column for **every MISB tag populated in that file** (ordered by tag number, via `MISB` from `MISBFields`). Pairing is to the **nearest** record on the chosen axis, raw values, no interpolation. Frame rows are **memory-safe**: `analyzePtsSync` is now called with `includeRows:false` (summary stats only), and the heavy per-frame rows are built only on demand through a new `includeFrameRows` option threaded into `analyzeVideoFileLike` → `analyzeTransportStreamFile`/`analyzeStandaloneKlvFile`/`analyzeKlvBuffer` — the folder scan keeps each file's summary, but `exportFramesForFile()` re-analyzes the single clicked file with `{includeFrameRows:true}` at click time. Results now carry `frameRows`/`frameMisbColumns` instead of `ptsRows`.
  - **Summary Report.** A new *Summary Report* button (`buildSummaryReport` → `showTimingAnalysis`) opens a text report with overall stats (files with MISB metadata, synchronous PES-PTS file count, total/PES-PTS record counts, warnings/errors/gaps/no-MISB/unsupported counts, mean KLV UTS CV), a short plain-English *ANALYSIS* section (how many files are synchronous vs UTS-fallback, gap/large-span-diff/warning/error callouts), and a fixed-width *VIDEOS* table (`summaryTableText`) mirroring the dialog columns.
  - **Removals / renames.** The old *Export PTS Rows* button and its `ptsRows`/`allPtsRows`/`ptsRowsToCsv`/`stripPtsRowsForJson` machinery are gone (replaced by `stripForJson`). Aggregate export file names changed to `sitrec-fmv-data-analysis.{json,csv}`, `sitrec-fmv-data-reports.txt`, and the new `sitrec-fmv-data-summary.txt`.
  - **Tooltips.** Per-control tooltips added in commit `1d8fea11` (buttons, summary tiles, table headers, recursive checkbox, status/progress, dialog title — set via `title`/`aria-label`) are carried forward and expanded to cover the new controls (*Choose File*, *Clear Results*, *Summary Report*, the per-row *Export*, and the *Frames* column), and the *File Analysis* folder/action tooltips were updated to describe the broader FMV scope.

---

## Version 2.71.0 (2026-06-02)

### New Features
- **Analyze Video Folder: batch MISB/KLV timing analysis across a whole folder of videos, with CSV export** (File → *File Analysis* → *Analyze Video Folder...*). New analysis subsystem under `src/analysis/`, wired in through `CFileManager.setupResourcesMenu` via `addFileAnalysisMenu()` from `VideoFolderAnalysisUI.js`, which adds a permanent, collapsed *File Analysis* folder (tooltip *Batch file analysis tools*) to the File menu and one action, *Analyze Video Folder...* (tooltip *Scan a local folder for MISB timing statistics*). The dialog (`openVideoFolderAnalysisDialog`, titled *Analyze Video Folder*) uses the File System Access API to pick a local folder, recursively scans for candidate video/KLV files (`isVideoAnalysisCandidateName`), and analyzes each one (`analyzeVideoFileLike` in `AnalyzeVideoFile.js`) without loading it into the sitch. `TSMetadataScanner.js` walks the MPEG-TS container (`scanTransportStreamForMetadata` / `scanTransportPackets`) to enumerate program PIDs, collect per-stream PES PTS entries, and pick the primary video stream / first video PES origin (`getPrimaryVideoStream`, `getPrimaryVideoPTSus`); the embedded KLV is decoded through `parseKLVFile`. `MisbTimingStats.js` then computes the timing statistics: `analyzeTimelineValues` (mean interval, coefficient of variation `cv`, gap count/size from the `UnixTimeStamp` timeline), `analyzePtsSync` (PES-PTS pairing coverage — `ptsRecordCoverage` = fraction of KLV records carrying a PES PTS, `ptsFrameCoverage` = fraction of video frames paired), an FPS cross-check (`computeFpsAnalysisForTiming`: PCR-derived vs wall-clock fps), and a roll-up `analyzeMisbTiming` that emits flags and a one-line `verdict`. Results render in a sortable table — columns *File, Status, Verdict, Records, PES PTS Rec, Frame Pair, KLV Span, Gaps, CV, Video PTS, Diff, Report* — with per-file progress (*Analyzing N of M…*) and a *Cancel* button. Four export buttons write to disk: *Export JSON*, *Export CSV* (`sitrec-video-folder-analysis.csv`, one row per file with the summary metrics from `summarizeTimingAnalysis`), *Export PTS Rows* (`sitrec-video-folder-pts-rows.csv`, the raw per-record PES-PTS rows), and *Export Reports* (the full per-file text reports from `renderMisbTimingReport`). Supporting change in `MISBUtils.parseKLVFile`: a new `{silent}` option routes parse errors through `console.warn` instead of `showError`, so batch analysis of dozens of files no longer fires a blocking error dialog per malformed stream. Unit coverage added in `tests/MisbTimingStats.test.js`.

### Improvements
- **Timing Analysis report now shows which KLV stream each derived track binds to, and whether it paired on PES PTS or fell back to UTS wall-clock** (date/time folder → *Timing Analysis...* button → new *TRACK PAIRING* section). `CNodeTrackFromMISB.recalculate()` now stamps each derived MISB track with an in-memory `this.pairingInfo = {mode, hasRealVideoPTS, klvHasRecordPTS, wrapped, misbNode, records}`, where `mode` is `"pts"` for PES-PTS pairing (synchronous, PCR-locked per-record) or `"uts"` for the `UnixTimeStamp` wall-clock fallback (asynchronous, nominal rate); it is a diagnostic only and is never serialized. `CNodeDateTime._timingAnalysisAction()` reads those stamps (detected by the presence of `.pairingInfo`, so subclasses like `CNodeLazyMISBFlightTrack` are included without an `instanceof` import) and appends a *TRACK PAIRING* block to the report prelude: it groups tracks by which loaded stream they bound to — labelled positionally as *Stream N* from `misbNodes.indexOf(info.misbNode)`, never by node id — and the resulting pairing mode, plus the shared video-frame-PTS state (*real (honors dropped frames)* vs *synthetic (uniform)*) and frame-patching state (*WRAPPED* vs *not wrapped*), and a verdict explaining the sync/async implications and pointing at §6c / §12 of the dev doc. The section is structural-only (positional labels + track counts, no tag values) and is shown only for multi-PID files (`misbNodes.length > 1`) or when any track uses the UTS fallback, so single-stream PES-PTS files are unaffected. Backing dev documentation `docs/dev/misb-timing.md` was corrected in the same change: the pairing model is now described as the two modes that actually exist in code — PES-PTS pairing and the UTS wall-clock fallback — rather than a fictional "three tiers"; all "Tier 1/2/3" references were renamed to the PTS/UTS mechanism names, several arithmetic/diagram/date errors were fixed, and §6c (pairing modes) and §7 (this report section) were added.
- **Image-set export orbit distance and altitude now use one consistent distance unit (Nautical Miles by default), and Vertical FOV goes down to 0.001°** (Export Image Set folder; Physics → *Units*). In `ExportImageSet.js` the *Orbit Altitude* slider (`imageSetOrbitAltitude`) was changed from `unitType: "small"` (feet/metres) to `unitType: "big"`, so it now displays in the same big distance unit as *Orbit Distance* (`imageSetOrbitDistance`, already `"big"`) — Nautical Miles under the default `CUnits("Nautical")`, or km in metric. To suit camera offsets measured in NM the altitude default/range were rescaled (value 30000→5, range 0→500, `maxMax` 10000) and the *Orbit Distance* range end was widened 100→500. If you prefer feet, switch Physics → *Units* to a feet-based mode. Separately, the *Vertical FOV (deg)* control's minimum was lowered from `0.1` to **`0.001`** (step `0.1`→`0.001`) for very long-focal-length / high-zoom shots. (Note: the commit subject says "0.01°", but the diff sets `0.001` — the diff is authoritative.)

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed sustained elevation-tile refetching on a completely static camera at very narrow FOV** (`QuadTextureSphere` / elevation quadtree). A coarse tile's bounding sphere has roughly twice the radius of each child's, so for a very narrow FOV (e.g. a ~3° look view aimed up a mountain) the parent's fat sphere could graze the frustum edge while all four children's lean spheres fell entirely outside it. `shouldSubdivideTile` then returned true, the four children were created, immediately deactivated as not-visible, and (with a short inactive-tile prune timeout at high `maxDetails`) deleted along with their fetched elevation data — whereupon the still-visible parent re-subdivided and re-fetched them, looping forever (~5 s period) even with a static camera. The fix adds `anyProspectiveChildVisible()`: before pushing a leaf to subdivide, it builds the four prospective child culling spheres and runs them through the same dilated-frustum + horizon/globe-occlusion test PASS 3 uses; if none would be visible the tile is treated as a leaf and the doomed children are never created. This is the sphere-broad-phase analogue of the OBB narrow phase that texture tiles get once they measure their own bounds (elevation tiles never build a mesh, so `altitudeBounds.measured` stays false and they never get OBB culling). The per-child verdict matches PASS 3 exactly for unmeasured tiles, so it can only skip subdivisions whose children would all be deactivated anyway — it cannot remove visible detail. Verified on the Volcano sitch (`maxDetails 25`): static-camera elevation fetches drop from ~232/6 s to 0/16 s; rotating still refines fully to z15 (124 tiles, each fetched once) and settles back to 0.
- **Fixed a second (twovid) video hijacking the global timeline and being able to overwrite the first on a local save.** *(C1)* Loading a second video (`video2`) went through the same path as the primary and rewrote `Sit.frames` / `Sit.fps` / `Sit.videoFrames` and reset `par.frame`, so a clip of different length/fps silently redefined the sitch timeline and desynced the primary video, tracks, and slider. An `ownsTimeline` flag (default true; false only for `video2`) is threaded view → `videoData`, and every global `Sit.*` timeline write (`CVideoMp4Data`, `CVideoWebCodecData`, `CVideoH264Data` including `updateFPS`) and every `par.frame` / `par.paused` load/stop reset is gated on it; single-video behavior is unchanged. *(C2)* `chooseLocalRehostPath` cached resolved paths keyed only by the preferred path, so a second dropped video sharing a basename reused the first's path — overwriting the first file and pointing both entries at one clip (data loss). The cache is now identity-aware (stores `{result, sourceBuffer}` and only hits when the source `ArrayBuffer` matches), so a distinct dropped file misses and the existing collision-suffix loop assigns it a distinct path; the pre-population in `CFileManager.js` was updated to match. Verified live on `sitch=custom`: a 7028-frame `video2` left the 1033-frame timeline and playhead untouched; the cache hits the same buffer and misses a different one.

## Version 2.70.0 (2026-06-01)

### New Features
- **Camera Motion (Background): recover a camera flight path from a near-nadir video's moving background** (Video → *Camera Motion (Background)* folder). New analysis in `src/CameraMotionFromVideo.js`, wired in via `setupCameraMotionMenu()` from `CustomManagerSetup.js`. For roughly straight-down (nadir) footage where the ground/cloud-tops slide past, it estimates the camera's egomotion from the background and renders a plausible camera path over the terrain. Pipeline: each frame is converted to grayscale and fed to OpenCV.js `goodFeaturesToTrack`, tracked forward with pyramidal Lucas-Kanade (`calcOpticalFlowPyrLK`) plus a forward-backward consistency check to drop bad tracks, then fitted with a robust complex-similarity model (hand-rolled, because `estimateAffinePartial2D`/`cornerSubPix` are absent in this OpenCV build) yielding per-frame translation/rotation/scale (~993/1000 inliers, ~0.31px RMS in testing). A static feature mask removes the burned-in redaction blocks (pixels that never exceed a dark-luma threshold over the clip) and a central box for the reticle, so those fixed elements don't pollute the fit. Duplicate/held-frame detection and gap/low-quality interpolation (motion distributed from adjacent real frames, as Motion Analysis does for panoramas) keep the path smooth across repeated or untrackable frames. The per-frame motion is integrated into an ECEF flight path via `CNodeCameraMotionTrack`, displayed with `CNodeDisplayTrack` as a ground "curtain" plus a moving marker. The path can drive the look camera: selecting *Camera Motion Path* in the `cameraTrackSwitch` option positions `lookCamera` along it (through `cameraTrackSwitchSmooth`), and `CNodeControllerCameraMotionOrientation` orients it using either the recovered roll or a fixed depression (*Look orientation*: Off / Recovered roll / Fixed depression, with a *Depression° (fixed)* and *Roll direction* control). A live optical-flow overlay (*Visualize motion*) draws the tracked feature vectors on the video. Menu actions: *Analyze & Build Path* (shows live "Analyzing… N%" / "Masking… N%" progress in its own label and reports errors inline), *Rebuild Path (no re-analyze)*, and *Clear Path*. Frame decode is gated on `isFrameCached` and `par.frame` is advanced sequentially to avoid decoder thrashing; the analysis loop yields so the UI stays responsive.
- **Secondary video view for side-by-side comparison** (drop a second video; choose *Second Video View (side by side)*). Adds a hidden `video2` view, created for every sitch that has a primary video view (`SitVideo.js` / `SituationSetup.js`, which also restore `videos2`/`videoFile2` on load), so two recordings of the same event can be compared and synced. Dropping a second video while one is already loaded raises a choice dialog (`showSecondVideoChoiceDialog` in `DragDropHandler.js`, titled *Second Video*) offering: *Second Video View (side by side)* (only when `video2` exists), *Add to Video View (keep both, selectable)*, or *Replace Video View video*. Choosing side-by-side reveals the layout via the new *TwoVideos* view preset (`CustomManagerSetup.js`, keypress **8**) with both videos filling the screen, falling back to a manual split when no presets exist; `CustomSupport.js` hides `video2` for any other preset. The secondary view round-trips on reload through `CustomManagerSerialize.js` as `videos2` / `currentVideoIndex2` / `videoFile2`, and `CFileManagerSave.js` rehosts dropped content for both the primary and secondary video on local saves (shared logic refactored into `rehostVideoNodeLocal`). `CNodeVideoView.js` adds a per-view *Lock to In Frame* control on secondary views, which plays the second clip relative to `Sit.aFrame` (its own frame 0 is shown when the global playhead reaches the in point); shared Video-menu controls are suffixed per view (`viewMenuSuffix()`) to disambiguate the two.

### Improvements
- **Camera Motion now auto-scales the path from FOV and altitude** (Video → *Camera Motion (Background)* → *Auto scale (FOV/alt)*). Ground metres-per-pixel are derived from the sitch field of view, camera altitude, and the background (cloud-top) altitude (*Background alt (m)*) instead of a fixed manual guess; the manual slider is retained as *Manual m / pixel* and used only when *Auto scale* is off. Robustness hardening in the same change: cumulative path math is guarded against NaN/non-finite fit terms so a single bad frame can't poison the whole track or bake NaN into the camera; the orientation controller clamps its frame index to the motion-array length; the inlier set/count/RMS are recomputed from the final similarity model; a re-entrancy guard prevents concurrent Analyze runs from corrupting state; the flow-visualization overlay/hook is reset when the video view is replaced; cached motion data whose frame count doesn't match the video is rejected; the grayscale `Mat` is freed in a `finally`; and analysis bails cleanly on fewer than 2 frames or a bad first-frame decode.

## Version 2.69.2 (2026-06-01)

### Bug Fixes
- **SHF app no longer serves a stale Starlink constellation from the service worker cache** (`tools/shf/sw.js`). The standalone Starlink Horizon Flares (SHF) PWA — native ES modules under `tools/shf/`, not part of the webpack bundle — has its own service worker whose `fetch` handler runtime-cached *every* same-origin GET that returned 200, cache-first. That included the live TLE fetch the app makes through the Sitrec proxy at `/sitrecServer/proxy.php?request=CURRENT_STARLINK`. Because a service worker intercepts `fetch` before the HTTP cache layer, this silently defeated the app's `{cache:"no-store"}` on that request in `fetchCurrentTLE` (`tools/shf/app.js`): once the proxy response landed in Cache Storage, subsequent runs were served the stale orbital data instead of reaching the server, so flare predictions could be computed from out-of-date TLEs. The fix adds `const SCOPE = new URL("./", self.location).pathname;` (the directory the worker was registered from, e.g. `/tools/shf/`) and an early `if (!url.pathname.startsWith(SCOPE)) return;` *before* the cache-first `event.respondWith` block. Only the tool's own version-stamped/static assets under its scope are runtime-cached now; any other same-origin URL — above all the TLE proxy — passes straight through to the network, so `no-store` reaches the server. `SCOPE` is derived from `self.location` (rather than a hardcoded path) so it stays correct when the tool is opened under a different-case path on a case-insensitive filesystem (e.g. `/tools/SHF/`). For the same case-insensitivity reason `fetchCurrentTLE`'s proxy base-path strip was made case-insensitive to match `sitrecBaseURL()` — `window.location.pathname.replace(/\/tools\/.*$/, "")` → `…/\/tools\/.*$/i`.
  - **Supporting internals (no user-facing behavior change):** to let the headless end-to-end test (`tools/shf/tools/verify-browser.mjs`) reliably detect when the *final* results screen has rendered, `app.js` now sets `els.results.dataset.state` to `"scanning"` in `setupLiveResults` and flips it to `"final"` in `renderResults` / `renderNoFlares` / `renderError`. This is an unambiguous "results are final" signal; previously the test waited on `.r-when`, a class the live scaffold's status line also uses, which could fire mid-scan. Relatedly, the dev-only per-flare debug detail list is now gated on `isLocalHost && !navigator.webdriver` (was just `isLocalHost`), so automation sees the production-clean results page — the same `navigator.webdriver` skip already used for service-worker registration. The README (`tools/shf/README.md`) gained a service-worker caveat on the `__BUILD_V__` note (an un-stamped, directly-served install pins one Cache Storage name and can serve stale JS/CSS until the SW or cache is cleared) and corrected the documented look-ahead from 3 days to 1 day to match the app's `LOOK_AHEAD_DAYS = 1`.

## Version 2.69.1 (2026-06-01)

### Improvements
- **"SHF App" link added to the main menu, beneath the live Starlink Horizon Flares sitch** (`src/index.js`, `rootSitches` loop in `initializeOnce`). The root-sitch button-building loop now emits an extra menu item immediately after the per-sitch button when `sitch === "starlink"`: `_gui.addExternalLink("SHF App", SITREC_APP + "tools/shf/").perm()` with the tooltip *"Open the standalone Starlink Horizon Flares (SHF) predictor app"*. Because the SHF (Starlink Horizon Flares) predictor is a separate standalone PWA served as raw ES modules under `tools/shf/` — not a Sitrec sitch — it is added via `addExternalLink` (which does `window.open(url, '_blank')`, opening it in a new tab) rather than as a `newSitch` button. The result is a direct shortcut to the standalone SHF app sitting directly below the in-app "Starlink Horizon Flares (LIVE)" sitch entry. The target resolves through `SITREC_APP`, so it points at the SHF app of whichever deployment is running.

## Version 2.69.0 (2026-06-01)

### New Features
- **Replay mode — animated, time-driven wide-view playback (new default)** (`tools/shf/app.js` `startReplay`/`stopReplay`, `tools/shf/skyview.js` `horizonProjection`/`flareBrightnessAt`/`skyBodiesSVG`/`MOTION_SAMPLE_MS`). The Starlink Horizon Flares tool's "view from here" horizon panorama now defaults to an animated replay that plays the prediction the way it will actually look. `horizonMode` (module-level, persists across renders this session) cycles `dots → streaks → replay` via the ★ button (`.hv-toggle`), with `replay` as the initial value. A single `requestAnimationFrame` loop (`startReplay`) advances replay time at 1× real time; each satellite brightens/holds/fades over its real duration via the shared `flareBrightnessAt(f, t)` and drifts along its real apparent path (`az = f.azDeg + dAzDeg·(t−peakMs)/MOTION_SAMPLE_MS`, same for el), with the faint timelapse streaks drawn behind at ~12% opacity (`streakMul`) for context. Playback starts at `peakMs` — the **busiest minute** of the visible set (`renderResults` bins the shown flares into 1-minute buckets and picks the fullest), loops `t2→t1`, and skips long dead gaps (`anyActive`/`nextStart`: if nothing is visible and the next flare start is >1.5 s away, jump to ~0.6 s before it, so each flare still plays at 1×). A draggable Sun under the horizon (`.replay-sun` / `.replay-sun-hit`, a 32px-wide hit rect with pointer capture) sits at the Sun's true azimuth (`sunAzimuthAt`, shared with the hourly markers) and scrubs time: `xToTime` maps pointer-x to the nearest of 120 pre-sampled instants across the window. A monospace `HH:MM:SS` readout (`.replay-time`, top-left above the horizon) and the stars/planets (`.replay-bodies`, redrawn by `skyBodiesSVG`) update once per second (throttled on `Math.floor(t/1000)`), so the sky rotates with replay time. Replay flare dots are soft-edged via a `objectBoundingBox` radial gradient (`#rflareDot`: solid to ~88% of the radius, feathering to transparent over the outer ~12% — no real bloom), sized `r = 1.0 + 2.0·b` by brightness. `renderScreen` calls `stopReplay()` whenever the page leaves results, and a fresh scan tears down any prior animation.
- **Timelapse-streak mode** (`tools/shf/skyview.js` `horizonView` streak branch; `tools/shf/flareEngine.js` core tracking; `tools/shf/flarePhysics.js` `flareCoreAngle`). The ★ `streaks` mode draws each flare as a "timelapse exposure" — a 2px white line tracing the satellite's apparent path over the flare (peak ± apparent-motion × time-offset, `back`/`fwd` in `MOTION_SAMPLE_MS` units), with a `userSpaceOnUse` linear-gradient brightness profile along the line: 0 at both ends, peaking at the run's brightest point. The profile is a **ramp-HOLD-ramp** with a full-brightness plateau across the cone "core" rather than a single triangle peak: `flareEngine` now records `coreStartMs`/`coreEndMs` on every flare (first/last time the glint sits inside the full-brightness core, where `glint < flareCoreAngle(spread)` — the new `flareCoreAngle` = `spread·(1−rampFraction)`, the plateau of `flareRamp`), carried through `makeFlare`; when a core is present the gradient repeats the bright stop at both core edges (flat top), otherwise it falls back to a single peak stop. The shared `flareBrightnessAt` uses the same control points, so replay and the streak agree on each flare's curve.
- **LIVE / real-time toggle** (`tools/shf/skyview.js` `liveButton`/`liveBadge`, `tools/shf/style.css` `.replay-live-btn`/`.replay-live`, `tools/shf/app.js` `renderHorizon`). A red "LIVE" badge above the time readout doubles as a button (`.replay-live-btn`), drawn only when "now" falls inside the flaring window (`nowInWindow = Date.now() ∈ [t1,t2]`, passed as `liveButton`/`opts.liveButton`). Toggling it on re-renders the horizon with `realtime` and anchors replay time to the wall clock (`ctl.realtime` → `ctl.t = clamp(Date.now())` each frame, scrubbing disabled), so the view shows what is flaring right now; a second red indicator (`liveBadge`, hidden by default) appears below the Sun and moves with it. Both `.replay-live` elements are shown only while real-time mode is active. This `liveButton` explicitly **replaces the old clock button**; colours are CSS-driven (grey with the dot at opacity 0 when off so nothing shifts, red box/dot/text when `.on`) and it shares the 46×16 geometry of the passive badge so the two read identically.
- **Zoom button (1× → 2× → 4×)** (`tools/shf/app.js` `horizonZoom`/`ZOOMS`/`zoomWin`, `tools/shf/style.css` `.hv-zoom`). A magnifier button stacked just above the ★ cycles the wide view through 1×/2×/4×. Zoom narrows the projection window (`zoomWin`: `halfWidthDeg`/`elMaxDeg ÷ factor`) so flare/star/Sun **positions** spread out around the same centre while every drawn element (text, dots, lines — all absolute px) stays the same size. `renderResults` rebuilds the SVG and the matching `horizonProjection(zoomWin(win))` so the animated replay layer maps identically to the static scene, preserving the current dots/streaks/replay mode and real-time state across a zoom change.

### Improvements
- **Shared flare-brightness model extended and single-sourced** (`tools/shf/flarePhysics.js`, `tools/shf/skyview.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeDisplayNightSky.js`). `flarePhysics.js` (added in this release window as the canonical cone-ramp / penumbra-fade / brightness-constants module with `isFlareVisible`) gains `flareCoreAngle`, and `skyview.js` extracts `flareBrightnessAt`, `skyBodiesSVG`, `horizonProjection`, and `MOTION_SAMPLE_MS` so the live replay layer, the timelapse streaks, the static dots, and the redrawn sky all derive position and brightness from one set of functions and can't drift apart. The main-app live night sky (`CNodeDisplayNightSky.js`) now imports `FLARE`/`flareRamp`/`penumbraFade` from `tools/shf/flarePhysics.js` instead of inline-duplicated constants (`0.04` base, `0.3` darkness multiplier, the `spread·0.25` cone ramp) and math — verified output-identical — so the standalone SHF tool and Sitrec's rendered flares share a single brightness definition. (The model lives under `tools/shf/` because that tool is served as raw ES modules and can only import from there; the webpack bundle imports down into it.)
- **Visibility cutoff applied throughout the wide view** (`tools/shf/app.js`, `tools/shf/flareEngine.js`). Only flares bright enough to actually be seen — the glint outshines the satellite's base brightness, per the shared `isFlareVisible`/`f.visible` flag introduced this release window — are counted and plotted. The live count filters on `f.visible`; `renderResults` plots `shown` (= the `f.visible` subset, falling back to all only if none qualify); the streak/dot/replay drawing clamps `intensity` and replay skips dots dimmer than `b ≤ 0.01`. Faint sub-base glints no longer inflate the result or clutter the panorama.
- **Motion arrow length now reflects flare duration** (`tools/shf/skyview.js` dots branch). In `dots` mode the amber motion arrow is now scaled by how far the satellite travels over the whole run (apparent motion × duration, `durDt = (endMs−startMs)/MOTION_SAMPLE_MS`, capped at `MAXL = 80`px), replacing the old fixed 15px length, so a long, fast pass reads as a longer arrow.
- **Hourly Sun-azimuth markers on the horizon panorama** (`tools/shf/app.js` `sunHourMarkers`/`sunAzimuthAt`, `tools/shf/skyview.js`, `tools/shf/astro.js` `sunEquatorial`). The "view from here" panorama draws a labelled amber down-arrow per whole local hour at the Sun's azimuth for that hour, in a headroom band above the sky. Because the flares track the Sun's direction, the markers turn the azimuth axis into a rough time axis. `sunAzimuthAt` is shared by these markers and the replay Sun so the draggable Sun lands on the same azimuths as the ticks above it.
- **Single-pass scan progress bar** (`tools/shf/app.js` `renderLoading`). The scanning loading bar was rewritten as one monotonic pass driven by the worker's real filter/refine progress — it starts at 0, never resets or jumps to ~25%, and (for fixed-location searches) caps the fill just below 100% to leave headroom for a possible extra dark window. Phase labels narrate each step above the bar.
- **Corrected limb/shadow explanatory diagram** (`tools/shf/index.html`). The info page's limb/shadow illustration was redrawn to show parallel sunlight grazing the limb, the satellite's flat panel held parallel to the ground beneath it, and a proper terminator plus Earth's-shadow umbra with the satellite sitting at the shadow edge.
- **"Open in Sitrec" launch camera reframed** (`src/fromApp.js` `frameMainView`). Launching a prediction into the main Sitrec app now places the overview camera up in space (~6000 km from the observer, on the anti-Sun side) looking down at the observer/aircraft at ~45°, so Earth's limb and the Starlink shell wrapping it fill the frame with the observer in the middle. Previously it was a low third-person establishing shot from ~14 km behind and ~7 km above the look camera.

### Bug Fixes
- **Off-view flares are clipped to the sky rectangle instead of squashed against its edges** (`tools/shf/skyview.js`). The horizon projection's `yOf` previously clamped elevation to `[0, elMax]`, which collapsed any streak or motion arrow running above the top (or below the horizon) onto the rim. `horizonProjection.yOf` is now **unclamped** and all flare drawing (streaks, dots, arrows, and the replay layer) is wrapped in `<g clip-path="url(#skyClip)">` — a `skyClip` rect covering the sky region — so overflowing geometry is cropped at the edge with its true direction preserved. This is most visible when zoomed in, where positions spread out and more geometry runs off-view.

## Version 2.68.3 (2026-06-01)

### Improvements
- **Browser/mouse Back and Forward now navigate between the Starlink Horizon Flares tool's screens** (`tools/shf/app.js`). The standalone SHF (Starlink Horizon Flares) tool is a single-page app with three screens — form, results, info — that previously toggled purely via the `hidden` attribute with no History API integration, so after running a search the browser (and mouse) Back button did nothing. The screen togglers were refactored into a single `renderScreen(name)` that flips visibility only (no history mutation) and preserves the old side effects — terminating the flare Web Worker (`activeWorker`) when landing on the form, and building the info-page SVG/SMIL visuals once via `buildInfoVisuals()`. Two thin wrappers, `navigateResults()` and `navigateInfo()`, push a `history.pushState({screen:'...'}, "")` entry before rendering (stateless, same URL); `navigateResults()` uses `replaceState` when already on results so re-running a search doesn't stack duplicate entries and a single Back always reaches the form. `init()` now seeds the form as the base entry with `history.replaceState({screen:'form'}, "")` and registers a `popstate` listener that renders `e.state.screen` (defaulting to `"form"`). The on-screen "‹ Edit" (results) and "‹ Back" (info) buttons now call `history.back()` instead of toggling directly, and the "i" info buttons call `navigateInfo()`, so manual and browser navigation share one path and never desync the stack — including Back from info, which pops to whichever screen it was opened from. The old `showForm`/`showResults`/`showInfo`/`hideInfo` functions and the `infoReturnScreen` module variable were removed; the history stack reproduces the info-return target losslessly, so no return-screen bookkeeping is needed. `onSubmit` now calls `navigateResults()` in place of the old `showResults()`.
- **The "Install App" suggestion in the Starlink Horizon Flares tool is now clearly separated and dismissable** (`tools/shf/index.html`, `tools/shf/style.css`, `tools/shf/app.js`). The install button lives outside the screen divs so it appears at the bottom of every screen, and sat directly below the results where users expect a Back control — reported as feeling like a dark pattern. The button is retained on every screen, but `.install-wrap` now sets `position: relative`, a hairline `border-top: 1px solid var(--line)` divider, a larger `margin: 32px auto 0`, and `padding: 20px 0 0` (previously `margin: 22px auto 0` with no divider), so it is visually set apart from the content above on every screen. A new dismiss "×" control (`#install-dismiss` in index.html, styled `.install-dismiss` top-right) hides the suggestion for the rest of the session: `installDismissed()` (a new try/catch-guarded helper) reads the `shfInstallDismissed` flag from `sessionStorage`, `updateInstallButton()` now gates the wrap on `isStandalone() || installDismissed()`, and `wireInstall()` wires the × to set the flag and re-gate. It reappears in a fresh tab/session. (The SHF tool is a standalone PWA, not part of the main Sitrec webpack bundle.)

## Version 2.68.2 (2026-05-31)

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed the production deploy build failing after the `tools/starlink` → `tools/shf` rename** (`webpack.common.js`): 2.68.1 renamed the standalone tool directory `tools/starlink` → `tools/shf` but missed one path reference — the `TerserPlugin` `exclude` regex in the production-only minimizer config still read `/(Sit.*|tools[\\/]starlink[\\/].*)\.js$/`. That exclude exists because the SHF (Starlink Horizon Flares) tool is copied verbatim as native ES modules (`import`/`export`), which Terser's script-mode parser can't minify. After the rename the regex no longer matched the tool's files, so `npm run deploy` (which minifies) hard-failed with `ERROR in tools/shf/flareWorker.js from Terser plugin — Unexpected token: name (import)`. Dev builds (`npm run build`) were unaffected because they don't run the minimizer — which is why 2.68.1's dev builds passed but its production deploy build was broken. The fix updates the exclude to `tools[\\/]shf[\\/]` (and the adjacent comment). Verified with `npx webpack --config webpack.prod.js` compiling cleanly.

## Version 2.68.1 (2026-05-31)

### Improvements
- **Standalone tool directory renamed `tools/starlink` → `tools/shf`** (42 git renames; served URL `/<base>/tools/starlink/` → `/<base>/tools/shf/`). Pure rename, no behavior change. All path references were updated to match: `webpackCopyPatterns.js` (the `["index.html","sw.js"]` build-stamping loop's `from`/`to` that injects `__BUILD_V__`), `tools/shf/app.js` `sitrecBaseURL()` (regex now strips `/\/tools\/shf\/.*$/` so the *Open in Sitrec* handoff still resolves the right host/base), the `tools/index.html` launcher link (`href="shf/"`), `tools/shf/README.md` documented URLs, and the path comment in `src/fromApp.js`. Intentionally **not** touched, because they are unrelated to the tool directory: the `starlink` *sitch* (`data/starlink/SitStarlink.js`, `Sit.name === "starlink"`) that the handoff builds on, the `docs/Starlink` help link, the Celestrak `GROUP=starlink` query param, and the internal identifiers `sw.js` cache name `starlink-flares-<build>` and `TLE_CACHE_KEY`. Note: the historical 2.68.0 changelog entries above still reference the old `tools/starlink/` paths and are deliberately left unchanged.
- **Home-screen app name is now "SHF"** (`tools/shf/manifest.webmanifest` `short_name` "Flares" → "SHF"; `tools/shf/index.html` `apple-mobile-web-app-title` "Flares" → "SHF"). "Flares" is generic and collides with unrelated home-screen apps; "SHF" (initialism of *Starlink Horizon Flares*) is short enough never to truncate under an icon. The full manifest `name` "Starlink Horizon Flares" is unchanged, so the install dialog still shows the long name. Both `short_name` and `apple-mobile-web-app-title` are read at install time, so only newly-installed instances pick up the new label; already-installed copies keep "Flares" until reinstalled.
- **Accurate, platform-aware PWA install instructions** (`tools/shf/app.js`). The manual install-instructions path was rewritten. The old code used a single `isIOS()` check and showed Safari-only "Add to Home Screen" steps to *all* iOS users (and a generic message to everyone else) — wrongly telling iOS-Chrome users to use Safari, even though since iOS 16.4 every iOS browser can Add to Home Screen. A new `platformInfo()` detector distinguishes iOS Safari, iOS Chrome (`CriOS`), other iOS browsers (Firefox/Edge/Opera wrappers), Android, macOS Safari, desktop Firefox, and desktop Chrome/Edge (with an iPadOS `MacIntel`+`maxTouchPoints` fallback for the desktop-UA case). A new `manualInstall()` returns per-platform steps: iOS branches point at the correct Share-button location for that browser (Safari toolbar at bottom vs Chrome address-bar/⋯ menu vs the generic ⋯ menu, with an inline share-glyph SVG and a 16.4+ note for non-Safari); Android shows ⋮ → *Install app*; macOS Safari shows *File → Add to Dock* (Sonoma+ note); desktop Firefox explains it can't install PWAs and points to Chrome/Edge/Safari/mobile; desktop Chrome/Edge shows the address-bar install icon and the *Install Starlink Horizon Flares…* menu item. The automated `beforeinstallprompt` path (`onInstallClick`: confirm → `bip.prompt()` → `userChoice`) is unchanged — `platformInfo()` only drives instruction text, never which path runs.

## Version 2.68.0 (2026-05-31)

### New Features
- **"Open in Sitrec" handoff from the Starlink Horizon Flare tool** (`src/fromApp.js`, `src/CustomManagerSetup.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeControllerVarious.js`): The standalone flare tool's results screen gains an *Open in Sitrec* button that opens the main app at `?fromapp=1&…` with the prediction parameters. Sitrec builds a situation on top of the existing `starlink` sitch: a real-time (1×) timeline spanning the full flare window (first→last flare) starting on the peak flare at 30 fps (a 4-hour `MAX_FRAMES` backstop guards pathologically long inputs, then centers on the peak); a real origin→destination flight track built as MISB data and loaded through the normal `TrackManager` pathway so it becomes a `CNodeTrackFromMISB`, appears in the Contents menu, and wires into `cameraTrackSwitch` with no special-casing (fixed-location mode instead raises the ground camera 50 ft); a new *Horizon Flare Region* camera lock (`CNodeControllerHorizonFlareRegion`) that aims at the Sun's azimuth ~9° above the horizon and follows it, selected automatically by the launch; LOS display off; and the main 3D view framed from behind/above the look camera so the flare band and region fill the shot. The tool title became "Starlink Horizon Flare".
- **war.gov UAP case-code tile in the Sitch Browser** (`src/CSitchBrowser.js`): Typing a war.gov UAP case code (e.g. `PR050`) into the Sitch Browser search box surfaces a green synthetic tile ("Open UAP case record") as the first card; activating it loads that case's video exactly as if its war.gov URL had been dragged into Sitrec. `renderThumbnails()` prepends the tile when `extractWarGovPRCode()` matches the search text, with `_thumbCardOffset` keeping the DOM-child↔filtered-index mapping correct for selection, rubber-band, and scroll-into-view. The tile is selectable (blue border) but never added to `this.selection`, so context menu / delete / select-all ignore it; Down-arrow from the search box selects it as grid position 0, and Enter activates it like a double-click. `_triggerWarGovPRRecord` routes to the SAME handler a dragged URL uses, keyed on `NodeMan.exists("video")`: with a video node present it calls `DragDropHandler.handleDroppedText()` directly (no reload), otherwise it loads the custom sitch first so the dropped video has a node to land in — replacing the old `pendingDropTexts` + `setNewSitchObject` route that forced a full `disposeEverything()`/rebuild and failed on sitch-to-sitch swaps.
- **Starlink Horizon Flare tool — About page** (`tools/starlink/index.html`, `app.js`, `skyview.js`, `style.css`, new `tools/starlink/video/DOD_110779879-480p.mp4`): An "i" info button (upper-right of every screen) opens an About page describing the phenomenon, with a floated animated "what it looks like" naked-eye flare simulation (`flareSimSky()` in `skyview.js`), a live animated compass-rose direction preview reusing the results-screen `compassRose()`, an embedded full-screenable AARO explainer video ("Starlink Horizon Flares explained by AARO"), a detailed "how it works" section with two inline SVG geometry diagrams, and external links (incl. the AARO Satellite Flaring information paper, Sitrec on GitHub, Metabunk, CelesTrak, Wikipedia). The animated visuals are refactored onto a shared `twinkleFlare()` SMIL generator extracted from `flareDots()`, with new `upOnly` (drift never heads downward) and `shrinkFade` (fade by radius rather than opacity) options for the naked-eye sky preview. The About copy follows the AARO paper: the reflector is the satellite bus's flat, mirror-like, ground-facing underside (nadir, tangential to the surface); flares appear in a "flare window" 0–10° above the horizon toward the set Sun while the Sun sits roughly 38°–46° below the horizon; they are NOT a twilight-only effect, NOT a moving "train" (each flash is a different satellite), and are often brighter than Venus.
- **Starlink Horizon Flare tool — Install App button** (`tools/starlink/index.html`, `app.js`): An *Install App* button appears at the bottom of every screen (hidden via `isStandalone()` when already running as an installed app). An early-capture inline script in `index.html` stashes the `beforeinstallprompt` event (`window.__bipEvent`) and re-fires `bip-ready`/`app-installed` for the async `app.js` module. On Chrome/Edge/Android the button confirms ("Install the Starlink Horizon Flares app on this device?") then triggers the native prompt; on iOS/iPadOS (`isIOS()`) and other browsers without an automated prompt it opens a modal with platform-specific manual steps (Safari Share → "Add to Home Screen", or the browser's install menu item). This builds on the 2.67.0 PWA/service-worker work — it adds the explicit in-app install affordance the tool previously lacked.

### Improvements
- **AARO Official UAP Imagery cases PR1–PR18 now resolve** (`src/WarGovUFOUtils.js`, `src/CSitchBrowser.js`): The browser tile and dropped-URL paths already resolved war.gov "DOW-UAP-PRxx" cases (PR019+) via the bundled WARGOV CSVs, but AARO's Official UAP Imagery (PR-001..PR-018) predates that release and is hosted on DVIDS, absent from those CSVs, so typing/dropping PR1–PR18 previously failed. A fixed `AARO_PR_DVIDS_IDS` table (PR-001..PR-018 → DVIDS video ID, sourced from the matching dvidshub.net videos) plus `getAaroDvidsIdForPrCode()` are added; `getWarGovUFODvidsId()` and `getWarGovUFODvidsIdForPrCode()` check the AARO table first and fall back to the war.gov CSV catalog, so both the URL pathway (`resolveWarGovUFOVideoURL`) and the prCode pathway share one resolver. `extractWarGovPRCode` was widened to 1–3 digits so single-digit codes (PR1..PR9) are recognised (all normalise to zero-padded `PRNNN`), and the tile label was genericized to "Open UAP case record" (PR1–18 AARO, PR19+ war.gov). All 18 mappings are pinned by `tests/WarGovUFOUtils.test.js`.
- **Starlink Horizon Flare tool — "next flares" search capped at 24 hours** (`tools/starlink/app.js`): The fixed-location forward search look-ahead (`LOOK_AHEAD_DAYS`) was reduced from 3 days to 1. The flare geometry recurs on a ~daily cycle (Sun position + the constellation's ground track), so if there's no flare in the first day there won't be one in the next two — searching further just wasted time.
- **Lazy MISB flight-track for the synthetic app flight** (new `src/nodes/CNodeLazyMISBFlightTrack.js`, `src/TrackManager.js`, `src/fromApp.js`, `src/CustomManagerSerialize.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeMunge.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeSmoothedPositionTrack.js`): The synthetic "Open in Sitrec" airplane flight no longer bakes a ~414k-entry per-global-frame array (and then re-smooths it). `CNodeLazyMISBFlightTrack` (extends `CNodeTrackFromMISB`, keeps full MISB/KML export and the time-offset wrapper) stores only the sparse MISB samples (≤1200) as a chordal `CatmullRomCurve3` and evaluates the camera position on demand at the fractional global frame, mirroring the existing `method:"spline"` sparse path but lazily. It is chosen in `TrackManager` strictly when `FileManager.getInfo().isAppFlight` is set (via `info.isAppFlight` in `buildAppFlightTrack`), so no other track path changes. `CNodeSmoothedPositionTrack` gains a passthrough mode: when its (switched) source resolves to a `lazyInterpolated === true` track it delegates `getValue`/`getValueFrame`/`exportArray` to the source instead of baking, sets its own `lazyInterpolated` flag so chained smoothers (e.g. the ObjectTilt orientation smoothers off `cameraTrackSwitchSmooth`) also take the passthrough path, and re-evaluates every cascade so switching camera tracks turns it on/off dynamically. `CNodeMunge` now skips its per-frame bake when `countVisibleOutputs(0, true) === 0` (default-on `checkDisplayOutputs`, opt out with `checkDisplayOutputs:false`); `getValueFrame()` falls back to a live `munge()` compute and nothing reads `.cachedValues` directly, so this is behavior-identical and only changes WHEN the work happens. `fromApp.js` / `CustomManagerSerialize.js` add `applyFlightLightweightGating()`, which hides the unused `traverseDisplayTrack`/`altitudeLabel2`/`distanceLabel`/`traverseObject`/`moveTargetAlongPath`/`orientTarget` display leaves (it hides, not deletes, so referencing node ids don't break and a real dropped track restores them) on both live launch (`finishFromApp`) and saved-sitch reload (pre-recalc in the deserialize path) so the `checkDisplayOutputs` gate skips their expensive per-frame bakes — gated on `Sit.appFlight`. The `node-smoke` skip-list was updated for the new node's nested ESM path.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed a WebGL context-loss crash on sitch swaps** (`src/nodes/CNodeView3D.js`): `dispose()` called `forceContextLoss()` (which fires `webglcontextlost` asynchronously) and then nulled `this.renderer`, so the never-removed listener fired on a disposed view → "Cannot read properties of null (reading 'info')". The lost/restored handlers are now stored as references and `removeEventListener`-ed at the top of `dispose()` before `super.dispose()` nulls the canvas. Genuine context-loss recovery on live views is unaffected.
- **Fixed a duplicate Three.js runtime copy** (`src/QuadTreeTile.js`, `webpack.common.js`): `QuadTreeTile.js` used `require("three")` in two spots, which resolves via the package's "require" export to `three.cjs` — a second runtime copy alongside the ESM `three.core.js` — triggering the runtime "Multiple instances of Three.js being imported" warning and disjoint prototypes (broken `instanceof`). Those inline requires were replaced with the already-imported `Vector3` and `LLAToECEFInto`. The `DetectDuplicateThreeModules` webpack plugin was upgraded to detect runtime builds by content (presence of the `window.__THREE__` init guard) rather than by path, and now pushes to `compilation.errors` (hard build failure) naming the offending source module, so a future duplicate-runtime import can't ship silently.
- **Fixed the synthetic "Open in Sitrec" flight being saved as a rehosted file** (`src/fromApp.js`, `src/CustomManagerSerialize.js`): A custom sitch built by the handoff used to persist the synthetic flight as a rehosted MISB file (and left a dead filename reference on local saves). The flight is fully regenerable from ~8 scalar parameters, so `buildAppFlightTrack(p)` was extracted from `finishFromApp` and shared by both the live launch and the reload path (identical node graphs); the generated "App Flight.kml" FileManager entry is flagged `skipSerialization` so it is never rehosted/embedded and is dropped from the `loadedFiles` save loop (and its per-file track metadata is skipped); the generator params are stashed on `Sit.appFlight`, written as `out.appFlight`, and regenerated on deserialize before the mods pass so `Track_Flight` et al. exist for their mods to attach (guarded against double-create for legacy saves that still embed the file). Verified by a save+reload round-trip: the saved sitch carries only the params and the flight regenerates as an identical `CNodeTrackFromMISB` in the Contents menu, selected in `cameraTrackSwitch`, with all node mods applied.
